P·L LIBER LEVITICUS 113

Patrologia Latina · Vol. 113 ·

LIBER LEVITICUS

One book of the Glossa ordinaria — the whole gloss, book by book.

PL 113, coll. 295–380  ·  Latin from the Migne printing  ·  New English translation  ·  column numbers follow the original plates, not the Garnier reprint

Anselm of Laon and his school

Anselmus Laudunensis et schola

twelfth century

The masters of the cathedral school of Laon, gathered around Anselm (c. 1050–1117), to whom the making of the Glossa ordinaria on the Bible is traditionally credited. The gloss is a school's work rather than one man's: it draws its comment from the Fathers — Rabanus, Isidore, Bede, Jerome, Gregory — and names them as it goes, and it grew by accretion over generations. Migne prints it under the name of Walafrid Strabo, an ascription now known to be mistaken.

Hebraice VAIICRA ( ויקרא ).

In Hebrew VAIICRA ( ויקרא ).

PRAEFATIO.

  • 295D

(ISICHIUS in Levit. ) Liber iste Leviticus dicitur quia scilicet in hoc nomine totius libri argumentum manifestatur. Tribus Levi tabernaculi ministerium sortita est, in quo sacrificia, et primitias, et primogenitorum 296Doblationes offerri praeceptum est, quia omnia sacrificia ad Levitas pertinent (Num. XVIII). A quibus liber iste congruum sortitus est nomen, qui haec omnia digerit per ordinem.

Latine Offertorius vel sacrificativus dicitur, quia 297Ade sacrificiis et caeremoniis agit. Hic bene post Exodum ponitur: in eo enim tabernaculum ordinatur, in hoc ea continentur quae Dominus Moysi de tabernaculo loquebatur, et tabernaculi caeremoniae.

Si quis autem quaerat quare libri legis divisi sunt, cum historia tempore indivisa sit, respondendum est: ut fastidium scilicet lectori auferret librorum divisio, sicut et viae spatia dividit viator, ut tanquam divisus minuatur labor.

Rabanus ab Isichio Hierosolymorum episcopo, qui Leviticum satis plene exposuit, multa collegit, et ab aliis quoque Patribus, qui ejus aliquas sententias exposuerunt, quaedam operi suo apposuit, et ex confuso sententiarum ordine quemdam expositionis ordinem multorum doctorum sententias alternando 297Bcomposuit.

(ISICHIUS in Levit. ) Prae omnibus necesse est interpretationem legis ad anagogen trahi, et spiritualem intelligentiam in littera prescrutari. Hoc enim non contradicit legi, sed eam defendit. Spiritualem quippe intellectum diligenter esse inquirendum etiam ipse Christus ostendit, qui in parabolis suis (Matth. XIII) agrum, mundum; agricolam seminantem semen bonum, justos; zizania, malignos; messores quoque, angelos; et inimicum superseminantem, diabolum appellavit. Quis ergo vetat bovem eum qui operatur justitiam, appellare; sicut ovem, simplicem; et innocentem, columbam: et turturem sublimius agentes et contemplationi vacantes? Nunquid non Ezechiel Hierusalem leaenam dixit: et catulum ejus, 297CEliachim, qui ductus est in Aegyptum, et alium catulum Joachim in Babylonem ductum? (Ezech. XIX.) Non solum quae in parabolis dicta sunt, sed et quae secundum historiam sunt, ad sublimiorem ducenda esse intellectum Paulus ostendit, dicens: Scriptum est quoniam Abraham habuit duos filios, etc.; post quae addidit: Quae sunt secundum allegoriam dicta: haec enim sunt duo Testamenta, etc. (Gal. IV). Fortassis autem noxia erit littera spoliata spirituali intelligentia, et variis reprehensionibus obnoxia. Unde per Ezechielem et bonam legem, et non bonam Dominus appellat, dicens: Dedi eis praecepta mea, et judicia mea ostendi eis, quae faciat homo et vivat in eis. Et alibi: Dedi eis mandata non bona, et judicia 297Din quibus non vivant. Patet ergo bona esse et non bona; et in quibus vivant et in quibus non vivant: qui enim custodit sanctum Spiritum, vivit in eis; qui autem secundum carnem, moritur, quia vitam non cognoscit in eis. Deinde legislator non semper, sed certo tempore voluit custodiri legis superficiem, ut reprimeret duram Judaeorum cervicem; unde: Scio quod populus iste durae cervicis sit (Exod. XXXII); Osee autem vocat Judaeos vitulam consternantem, quae scilicet nullo modo domatur. Et alibi: Ephraim vitula docta diligere trituram, transivi super pulchritudinem colli ejus (Osee. X); in quo obedientia significatur. Jugum enim legis imposuit, ut humiliati libenter susciperent leve et suave jugum Evangelii (Matth. XII).

298A(ORIG., homil. 1, in Levit.) Si secundum quosdam etiam nostrorum simplicem intellectum sequamur, et absque ulla, ut ipsi nos ridere solent, stropha verbi et allegoriae nubilo vocem legislatoris excipiamus, ego ecclesiasticus sub fide Christi vivens, et in medio Ecclesiae positus, ad sacrificandum vitulos vel agnos, et ad offerendam similam cum thure et oleo, divini praecepti auctoritate compellor: ipse ergo Dominus, ipse Spiritus sanctus deprecandus est, ut omnem nebulam peccati quae visum cordis nostri obscurat, auferre dignetur, ut spiritualem intelligentiam intueamur, secundum illud: Revela oculos meos et considerabo mirabilia, etc. (Psal. CXVIII).

(ISID.) Omnis hostiarum diversitas Christi hostiam praefigurabat. Unde, veritate oblata, cessavit umbra 298B(I Cor. XV), verum sacrificium veri et aeterni sacerdotis praefigurans. Ideo autem hostiis animalium propter emundationem oblatis, una vera hostia Christi promittebatur, in qua fieret remissio peccatorum contractorum de carne et sanguine, qui regnum Dei non possidebunt: quia substantia corporalis in coelestem mutabitur qualitatem. Christus in vitulo propter virtutem crucis offerebatur; in agno, propter innocentiam; in ariete, propter principatum; in hirco, propter similitudinem carnis peccati; in turture et columba, quia Deus et homo. Mediator enim Dei et hominum in duarum substantiarum conjunctione monstrabatur; in similaginis conspersione, credentium per aquam baptismi collectam esse Ecclesiam, id est corpus Christi, ostendebatur. Nos autem moraliter 298Cofferimus vitulum, cum carnis superbiam vincimus; agnum, cum irrationabiles motus corrigimus; haedum, cum lasciviam superamus; columbam, dum simplices sumus; turturem, dum casti sumus, vel dum castitatem servamus; panes azymos, cum non in fermento malitiae, sed in azymis sinceritatis et veritatis epulamur.

(RAB.) Singula sacrificia, imo singulas pene syllabas, et vestes Aaron, et totum Leviticum ordinem coelestia dicit spirare sacramenta.

Origenes quoque ait: Quia sicut in novissimis diebus Verbum Dei ex Maria virgine carne vestitum processit in mundum; cujus caro ab omnibus videbatur, divinitas paucis et electis cognita tegebatur: sic verbum Dei per prophetas profertur ad homines, 298Dibi carnis, hic litterae velamine tectum. Littera tanquam caro aspicitur: spiritualis sensus tanquam divinitas sentitur.

PREFACE.

  • 295D

(ISICHIUS in Levit. ) This book is called Leviticus, namely because in this name the argument of the whole book is made plain. The tribe of Levi obtained by lot the ministry of the tabernacle, in which it was commanded that sacrifices, and firstfruits, and 296Dthe offerings of the firstborn be offered, because all the sacrifices pertain to the Levites (Num. XVIII). From these this book has obtained by lot a fitting name, a book which sets all these things in order.

In Latin it is called Offertorius or sacrificativus, because 297Ait treats of sacrifices and ceremonies. It is rightly placed here after Exodus: for in that book the tabernacle is ordained, in this are contained the things which the Lord was speaking to Moses concerning the tabernacle, and the ceremonies of the tabernacle.

But if anyone should ask why the books of the law were divided, since the history is undivided in time, this must be answered: that the division of the books might take away weariness from the reader, just as a traveller too divides the stretches of the road, so that the labor, being as it were divided, may be lessened.

Rabanus gathered many things from Hesychius, bishop of Jerusalem, who expounded Leviticus fully enough, and from other Fathers too, who expounded some of his opinions, he added certain things to his own work, and out of the confused order of the opinions, by alternating the opinions of many doctors, he composed 297Ba certain order of exposition.

(ISICHIUS in Levit. ) Before all things it is necessary that the interpretation of the law be drawn to anagoge, and that the spiritual understanding be searched out within the letter. For this does not contradict the law, but defends it. That the spiritual understanding is to be diligently sought out Christ himself also shows, who in his parables (Matth. XIII) called the field, the world; the husbandman sowing the good seed, the just; the tares, the wicked; the reapers too, the angels; and the enemy oversowing, the devil. Who then forbids to call him who works justice an ox; as a sheep, the simple; and the innocent, a dove; and a turtledove, those who conduct themselves more loftily and are at leisure for contemplation? Did not Ezekiel call Jerusalem a lioness, and her whelp 297CEliachim, who was led into Egypt, and another whelp Joachim, led into Babylon? (Ezech. XIX.) That not only the things which are said in parables, but also the things which are according to the history, are to be led to a loftier understanding, Paul shows, saying: It is written that Abraham had two sons, etc.; after which he added: Which things are said according to allegory: for these are the two Testaments, etc. (Gal. IV). [Vulg. Gal 4:24 has per allegoriam, "by allegory," where Migne prints secundum] But perhaps the letter, stripped of the spiritual understanding, will be harmful, and liable to various reproaches. Whence through Ezekiel the Lord calls the law both good and not good, saying: I gave them my precepts, and I showed them my judgments, which if a man do, he shall live in them. And elsewhere: I gave them commandments not good, and judgments 297Din which they shall not live. [Vulg. Ez 20:25 has praecepta, "precepts," where Migne prints mandata, and the future vivent for vivant] It is plain, therefore, that they are good and not good; and that there are those in which they live and those in which they do not live: for he who keeps the Holy Spirit lives in them; but he who lives according to the flesh dies, because he does not know the life in them. Then the lawgiver willed the surface of the law to be kept not always, but for a set time, that he might restrain the hard neck of the Jews; whence: I know that this people is of a stiff neck (Exod. XXXII); [Vulg. Ex 32:9 has Cerno, "I perceive"] but Hosea calls the Jews a heifer that shies away, one namely that is in no way tamed. And elsewhere: Ephraim is a heifer taught to love threshing, I passed over the beauty of her neck (Osee. X); in which obedience is signified. For he imposed the yoke of the law, that being humbled they might willingly take up the light and sweet yoke of the Gospel (Matth. XII).

298A(ORIG., homil. 1, in Levit.) If, following certain men even of our own, we were to follow the simple understanding, and were to receive the voice of the lawgiver without any twist of the word (as they themselves are wont to laugh at us) and without the cloud of allegory, then I, a churchman living under the faith of Christ and set in the midst of the Church, am compelled by the authority of the divine precept to sacrifice calves or lambs, and to offer fine flour with frankincense and oil: the Lord himself, therefore, the Holy Spirit himself, is to be entreated, that he may deign to take away every cloud of sin which darkens the sight of our heart, so that we may behold the spiritual understanding, according to that saying: Open thou mine eyes, and I shall consider the wondrous things, etc. (Psal. CXVIII).

(ISID.) Every diversity of victims prefigured the victim of Christ. Whence, the truth being offered, the shadow ceased 298B(I Cor. XV), prefiguring the true sacrifice of the true and eternal priest. And therefore, when the victims of animals were offered for cleansing, the one true victim of Christ was promised, in which there should be made the remission of the sins contracted from flesh and blood, which shall not possess the kingdom of God: because the bodily substance shall be changed into a heavenly quality. Christ was offered in the calf, because of the strength of the cross; in the lamb, because of innocence; in the ram, because of headship; in the he-goat, because of the likeness of the flesh of sin; in the turtledove and the dove, because he is God and man. For the Mediator of God and of men was shown in the conjunction of two substances; in the sprinkling of the fine flour it was shown that the Church, that is, the body of Christ, has been gathered out of believers through the water of baptism. But we morally 298Coffer a calf, when we conquer the pride of the flesh; a lamb, when we correct irrational motions; a kid, when we overcome wantonness; a dove, while we are simple; a turtledove, while we are chaste, or while we keep chastity; unleavened loaves, when we feast not in the leaven of malice, but in the unleavened bread of sincerity and truth.

(RAB.) He says that the several sacrifices, nay almost the several syllables, and the vestments of Aaron, and the whole Levitical order, breathe out heavenly mysteries.

Origen too says: That just as in the last days the Word of God, clothed with flesh from the Virgin Mary, went forth into the world, whose flesh was seen by all, while his divinity, known to few and to the elect, was covered over: so the word of God is brought forth to men through the prophets, 298Dthere covered by the veil of flesh, here by the veil of the letter. The letter is looked upon as flesh: the spiritual sense is perceived as divinity.

CAPUT PRIMUM.

VERS. 1.-- Vocavit, etc. (ISICH. in Levit. ) Per conjunctionem sequentia jungit praecedentibus; initium, scilicet libri hujus cum fine praecedentis: unius enim et ejusdem diei opus in utroque continetur. Liber quoque Numeri, quia nihil habet medium inter se et Leviticum, simile sortitur prooemium; hoc est: Locutusque est Dominus ad Moysen in deserto Sinai, etc. (Num. I). Nihil ergo post historiam Levitici ante historiam Numeri populus egit: neque enim locum mutaverat, quia necdum tabernaculum 299Adedicaverat, nec multum temporis praeterierat. In primo quippe mense secundi anni, ejusdem mensis initio agere coeperunt. In Exodo autem et Genesi, quia multum temporis medium, mortis Joseph et egressionis filiorum Israel, fluxerat, conjunctio non obtinet principium, sed opportunum dispensationi eorum dispensatur exordium: Haec sunt nomina filiorum Israel, qui ingressi sunt Aegyptum.

(ISICH.) Secundum LXX. Revocavit. Sicut secundum eos revocavit Deus Beseleel ex nomine, quando praecepit fieri tabernaculum secundum figuram, quae ostensa est in monte: ergo et nunc quamdam sacrificiorum intelligibilium imaginem describere praecepit; haec revocatio quippe quamdam dispensationem rerum vel verborum ad superiora respicientium 299Binnuit.

(ORIG. hom. 1 in Levit.) Vocavit autem, etc. In principio, etc., usque ad qui ad haec audienda interiores aures afferunt mundas.

(ISICH. in Levit. ) Quod non diligat Deus Judaeorum sacrificia, etc., usque ad et aperte haec vocatio dispensationem verborum vel rerum respicientium ad superiora innuit.

(ORIG. homil. 1 Exod.) Hominem quem primo posuit ad offerendum Domino, etc., usque ad sacrificium quoque duplex est quod et terrestria et coelestia salvet.

(ID., ibid.) Homo qui ad imaginem Dei et similitudinem factus rationabiliter vivit, etc., usque ad et collum ejus sicut turturis.

299C(ISICH. in Levit. ) Simplicem designat hominem, etc., usque ad jumentis etiam salus conceditur.

(ID.) Sacrificium Deus exigit ut nobis proficiat, non sibi, etc., usque ad quae tandem pro nobis aut contra nos testimonium dicant.

VERS. 3.-- De armento, etc. (ISID.) Primum sacrificium est vitulus, id est Christus, de armento, id est de patriarcharum stirpe progenitus, qui aratro suae crucis terram carnis nostrae perdomuit, et Spiritus sancti semen virtutum fruge ditavit.

(ORIG., ut supra. ) Vitulus superbus, caro nostra, etc., usque ad oleum autem charitatis et pacis et aliarum virtutum non habuerunt.

Ad placandum, etc. Juxta LXX autem hoc acceptum dicit offerenti, scilicet si enim acceptum nobis 299Dvere sit, et cor nostrum recte judicans, nullique adhaerens passioni, munus ad suscipiendum dignum probaverit: tunc orare in conspectu Domini, id est remissionem possumus obtinere. Unde coram ipso suadebimus corda nostra, quia si reprehenderit nos cor nostrum, scimus quia major est Deus corde nostro, et cognoscit omnia, etc.

VERS. 4.-- Ponetque, etc. Quod nos hostiam dicimus, Graece καρπώμα dicitur, in quo intelligibile holocaustum significatur. Spiritualiter enim fructificat, qui per spiritualem conversationem efficitur holocaustum: propter quod qui offert, manum in caput hostiae ponit, quod significat initium praedictae conversationis, quam qui offert, ipse sibi causa promissionis 300Aest. Sponte enim offert, quia donum est, et omne donum voluntarium est. Si enim promissionem suam trangressus fuerit, seipsum accusat, cum poenas luerit. Sic autem acceptabilis erit, et ad expiationem suam proficiens, cum positione manus manifestaverit, quia sponte non necessitate obtulerit.

VERS. 5.-- Immolabitque, etc. (ISICH. in Levit. ) Non solum enim propriam conscientiam et conversationem Deo debemus offerre, etc., usque ad sicut Christus ostium appellatur, sic doctrina apostolica quam largitur.

Filii Aaron. (ORIG., ubi supra. ) De genere Aaron erant Anna et Caiphas, et alii qui Christum pronuntiaverunt esse reum mortis, et effuderunt sanguinem 300Bejus ad basim altaris: ibi enim est occisus, ubi altare erat et basis ejus, unde: Non capit prophetam perire extra Hierusalem.

VERS. 6.-- Detractaque, etc. (ORIG., ibid.) Sacerdos pellem detrahit, etc., usque ad terrena coelestibus et divinis humana sociavit.

(ID) Intestina cum pedibus jubet aqua dilui, in significationem baptismi. Intestina lavat, qui conscientiam purgat; pedes abluit, qui consummationem suscipit sacramenti. Qui enim mundus est, non indiget nisi ut pedes lavet, sed est mundus totus (Joan. XV): nec quisquam potest habere partem cum Jesu, nisi laverit pedes ejus.

(ISICH. in Levit. ) Pellis detrahitur cum divitiis et quibuscunque saecularibus exspoliamur, etc., usque 300Cad Christum imitantes sicut possumus.

(GREG. lib. I Moral., cap. 40.) Pellem hostiae detrahimus, etc., usque ad nihil lubricum in ara suae orationis imponat.

VERS. 10.-- Quod si, etc. (ISICH. in Levit. ) Paucis immutatis eadem dicit, etc., usque ad unde: Filius non portabit iniquitatem patris, etc.

Anniculum, etc. (ISID.) Agnus oblatus, Christus innocens crucifixus. Unde: Ecce agnus Dei, ecce qui tollit peccata mundi. Bene haedus offertur, quia per mortem Christi jugulatur diabolus auctor peccati.

(ISICH.) Hoc de bobus non praecepit, etc., usque ad propterea holocaustum dicitur etiam sine operatione 300Djustitiae.

VERS. 11.-- Ad latus, id est Gentilitatem altare respicit, quia pro gentibus celebrata est passio Christi. Nos vero ad aquilonarem partem altaris sacrificium offerimus, dum memoriter passionem Christi pro gentibus factam credimus.

VERS. 13.-- Omnia adolebit. Haec omnia super altare offerentem ponere jubet ut omnem nostrum hominem, spiritualibus scripturis quae de intelligibili altari Dominici corporis dictae sunt, coaptemus, et ad imitationem ejus vivere studeamus.

Et odorem. Quando sacrificium nostrum holocaustum fit, et offerimus omnia super intelligibile altare, tanquam imitatores Christi conversationem 301Avitae nostrae conformantes ei, tunc offertur Domino odor suavissimus.

VERS. 14.-- Si autem. (ISID.) Turtur, caro Christi, secundum illud Salomonis: Pulchrae sunt genae tuae sicut turturis: Columba Spiritum sanctum significat. Unde: Super quem videris Spiritum descendentem sicut columbam, hic est Filius meus. Turtur ergo columba caro Christi est Spiritui sociata: haec tria sacrificia offert homo, id est Christus, de bobus scilicet, pecoribus, et avibus.

(ISICH. in Levit. Admirabilis verborum subtilitas, etc., usque ad, contemplantur et sapiunt coelestia, ubi Christus est in dextera Dei sedens.

CHAPTER ONE.

VERS. 1.-- He called, etc. (ISICH. in Levit. ) By a conjunction he joins what follows to what precedes; the beginning, namely, of this book with the end of the preceding: for the work of one and the same day is contained in both. The book of Numbers too, because it has nothing intervening between itself and Leviticus, obtains a like proem; that is: And the Lord spoke to Moses in the desert of Sinai, etc. (Num. I). Nothing, therefore, did the people do after the history of Leviticus and before the history of Numbers: for it had not changed its place, because it had not yet 299Adedicated the tabernacle, nor had much time gone by. For in the first month of the second year, at the beginning of that same month, they began to act. But in Exodus and Genesis, because much intervening time had flowed by, that of the death of Joseph and of the going forth of the sons of Israel, a conjunction does not hold the opening, but a beginning suited to their dispensation is dispensed: These are the names of the sons of Israel, who went into Egypt.

(ISICH.) According to the Seventy, He called back. Just as, according to them, God called back Beseleel by name, when he commanded the tabernacle to be made according to the figure which was shown on the mount: so now too he has commanded a certain image of the intelligible sacrifices to be described; for this calling back intimates 299Ba certain dispensation of things or of words looking to the things above.

(ORIG. hom. 1 in Levit.) And he called, etc. In the beginning, etc., as far as those who bring clean inward ears to the hearing of these things.

(ISICH. in Levit. ) That God does not love the sacrifices of the Jews, etc., as far as and openly this calling intimates the dispensation of words or of things looking to the things above.

(ORIG. homil. 1 Exod.) The man whom he first set to offer to the Lord, etc., as far as the sacrifice too is twofold, which saves both earthly things and heavenly.

(ID., ibid.) The man who, made to the image and likeness of God, lives reasonably, etc., as far as and his neck is as a turtledove's.

299C(ISICH. in Levit. ) He designates the simple man, etc., as far as salvation is granted even to beasts of burden.

(ID.) God requires sacrifice that it may profit us, not himself, etc., as far as which at the last may speak testimony for us or against us.

VERS. 3.-- Of the herd, etc. (ISID.) The first sacrifice is the calf, that is, Christ, of the herd, that is, begotten of the stock of the patriarchs, who with the plough of his cross subdued the earth of our flesh, and enriched the seed of the Holy Spirit with the fruit of the virtues.

(ORIG., ut supra. ) The proud calf, our flesh, etc., as far as but the oil of charity and of peace and of the other virtues they had not.

To appease, etc. But according to the Seventy he says this to be acceptable to the offerer, namely, if indeed it be to us 299Dtruly acceptable, and our heart, judging rightly and cleaving to no passion, shall have approved the gift as worthy to be received: then we can pray in the sight of the Lord, that is, obtain remission. Whence before him we shall persuade our hearts, because if our heart reprove us, we know that God is greater than our heart, and knoweth all things, etc.

VERS. 4.-- And he shall put, etc. What we call a victim is in Greek called καρπώμα, in which the intelligible holocaust is signified. For he fructifies spiritually who by a spiritual manner of life is made a holocaust: for which cause he who offers puts his hand upon the head of the victim, which signifies the beginning of the aforesaid manner of life, of which he who offers is himself to himself the cause of the promise 300A. For he offers of his own accord, because it is a gift, and every gift is voluntary. For if he shall have transgressed trangressus his promise, he accuses himself, when he shall have paid the penalties. And so he will be acceptable, and profiting toward his own expiation, when by the placing of the hand he shall have made it manifest that he offered of his own accord, not of necessity.

VERS. 5.-- And he shall immolate, etc. (ISICH. in Levit. ) For not only our own conscience and manner of life ought we to offer to God, etc., as far as as Christ is called the door, so is the apostolic doctrine which he bestows.

The sons of Aaron. (ORIG., ubi supra. ) Of the race of Aaron were Annas and Caiaphas, and the others who pronounced Christ to be guilty of death, and poured out his blood 300Bat the base of the altar: for there was he slain, where the altar was and its base, whence: It cannot be that a prophet perish outside Jerusalem.

VERS. 6.-- And being drawn off, etc. (ORIG., ibid.) The priest draws off the skin, etc., as far as he joined earthly things to heavenly and human things to divine.

(ID) He commands the entrails with the feet to be washed with water, in signification of baptism. He washes the entrails who purges his conscience; he washes the feet who receives the consummation of the sacrament. For he who is clean needeth not save to wash his feet, but is clean wholly (Joan. XV): [Vulg. Jn 13:10 has Qui lotus est, "he that is washed," where Migne prints Qui enim mundus est] nor can anyone have part with Jesus, unless he wash his feet.

(ISICH. in Levit. ) The skin is drawn off when we are despoiled of riches and of whatsoever things are worldly, etc., as far 300Cas imitating Christ so far as we can.

(GREG. lib. I Moral., cap. 40.) We draw off the skin of the victim, etc., as far as let him lay nothing slippery upon the altar of his prayer.

VERS. 10.-- But if, etc. (ISICH. in Levit. ) With a few things changed he says the same, etc., as far as whence: The son shall not bear the iniquity of the father, etc.

A yearling, etc. (ISID.) The lamb offered is Christ the innocent crucified. Whence: Behold the Lamb of God, behold him who taketh away the sins of the world. [Vulg. Jn 1:29 has the singular peccatum, "the sin of the world"] Well is a kid offered, because through the death of Christ the devil, the author of sin, is slain.

(ISICH.) This he did not command concerning oxen, etc., as far as therefore it is called a holocaust even without the working 300Dof justice.

VERS. 11.-- At the side, that is, the altar looks toward the Gentile world, because the passion of Christ was celebrated for the Gentiles. But we offer the sacrifice at the northern part of the altar, while we mindfully believe the passion of Christ to have been wrought for the Gentiles.

VERS. 13.-- He shall burn all things. He commands the offerer to place all these things upon the altar, that we may fit our whole man to the spiritual scriptures which have been spoken concerning the intelligible altar of the Lord's body, and may study to live to the imitation of him.

And the odor. When our sacrifice becomes a holocaust, and we offer all things upon the intelligible altar, as imitators of Christ conforming the manner 301Aof our life to him, then is there offered to the Lord a most sweet odor.

VERS. 14.-- But if. (ISID.) The turtledove, the flesh of Christ, according to that saying of Solomon: Fair are thy cheeks as a turtledove's: the dove signifies the Holy Spirit. Whence: Upon whom thou shalt see the Spirit descending as a dove, this is my Son. [Vulg. Jn 1:33 continues et manentem super eum, hic est qui baptizat in Spiritu Sancto, "and remaining upon him, this is he that baptizeth in the Holy Spirit"] The turtledove, therefore, the dove, is the flesh of Christ joined to the Spirit: these three sacrifices man offers, that is, Christ, namely of oxen, of cattle, and of birds.

(ISICH. in Levit. Wonderful is the subtlety of the words, etc., as far as, they contemplate and savor heavenly things, where Christ is sitting at the right hand of God.

CAPUT II.

301BVERS. 2.-- Anima, etc. A meliori parte totum hominem significat: requirit enim spiritualem et perfectum auditorem, quia anima est sapientiae capax, non caro.

(ISID.) Anima, etc. Quartum sacrificium offert anima. Simila Ecclesiam significat, quae de multis personis quasi granis est collecta, legis et Evangelii mola inter litteram et spiritum separata, per aquam baptismi adunata, chrismate peruncta et solidata spirituali igne; per humilitatem fit accepta hostia.

(ORIG.) hom 2 in Lev. Anima. Quae scilicet nec vitulum habet, etc.; usque ad oleo divinae misericordiae vel gratiae.

Mystice, significat, sicut ibi: Cunctae animae quae 301Cingessae sunt cum Jacob in Aegyptum, etc. (Gen. XLVI.) Lucas autem ait: Eramus autem omnes animae in navi ducentae septuaginta sex (Act. XXVII). Forsitan per animam, Gentilem significat. Supra enim filiis Israel praecepit, ut sacrificia offerrent cum sanguine. Hic autem qui est sine lege, offerre praecipiens, non dicit homo, sicut supra, sed anima. Animalis erat Gentilis, non intelligens ea quae sunt Dei, sed vocatur ad oblationem misericordia Dei, ut percipiat salutem; cum pro peccatis sacrificia disponit, qui nec habet ovem nec capram, turturem vel columbam offerre praecipitur; qui nec habet illud, similam. Non enim jam ex operibus, ut pauper virtutibus, sed sola cognitione Dei salvatur, quae licet principium sit salutis, tamen thus et 301Doleum, supponendum, ut gratia de eleemosynis et orationibus misceatur. Unde angelus ad Cornelium ait: Orationes tuae et eleemosynae tuae ascenderunt ad Dominum (Act. X). In thure interiores curantur passiones, et fumo ejus sanantur occuli ex infirmitate lacrymantes. Oratio quippe animae passiones sanat, oculos interiores curat ab ignorantia lacrymas ex aegritudine peccati accendentes fugat.

VERS. 2.-- Filios, ministros Ecclesiae; quia quidquid offerimus, cum sanctis Ecclesiae doctoribus conferre debemus. Unde Paulus cum apostolis contulit Evangelium, ne in vacuum cucurrisset (Gal. II).

Quorum. Unitas Ecclesiae pugillum similae tollit, 302Aquia pro capacitate sensus unusquisque fidelis Scripturae scientiam debet appetere. Unde: Non plus sapere quam oportet, sed sapere ad sobrietatem. Quorum unus, etc. (Rom. XII). Notandum quod pugillus similae et olei, totum vero thus offerri jubetur; quia sapientia quae in simila, et charitas vel misericordia quae in oleo signatur, in hac vita non possunt esse plena. Unde: Videmus nunc per speculum in aenigmate: et, nunc cognosco ex parte, etc. (I Cor. XII). Olei quoque pars offertur, quia misericordia nunquam impletur, quia etsi miserearis quantum potes, plus tamen debes velle. Charitas etiam quae oleo figuratur, in praesenti inchoatur, in futuro perficietur. Thus autem totum crematur, quia semper orationi insistitur.

302BSimilae. Scientiae vel cognitionis Dei. Accepta enim est scientia a Deo, quam fides comprehendere potest, et sicut qui pugillo vult plus comprehendere, nihil proficit, totum quippe decurrit: sic fides est mensura scientiae Dei. Credere enim oportet accedentem ad Deum, et quod non est ex fide, peccatum est (Heb. XI).

Memoriale, etc., cognitionis Dei perfectam scientiam, cujus memores erimus, quae nunquam destruitur; et eam quae nunc est, non ut contrariam, destruit, sed partem minimam esse per plenitudinem suam ostendit: Cum enim venerit quod perfectum est, evacuabitur quod ex parte est (I Cor. XIII).

VERS. 3.-- De sacrificio, etc. (ISICH. in Lev. ) De illa scilicet perfecta et in coelis abscondita, in Christoque 302Creposita scientia, in quo sunt omnes thesauri sapientiae et scientiae absconditi (Col. II).

Aaron, montanus interpretatur, id est, altiora de Deo intelligens; vel arcanus, quasi in se legem Dei et eloquia habens: hic sacrificium offerre potest. Sanctum sanctorum, est de oblationibus Domino: partile enim est quod reliquum est, omnes tamen scientias superans et ad perfectionem exaltans

VERS. 4.-- Cum obtuleris sacrificium, etc. (ISICH., ibid. ) Sacrificium Christus pro nobis immolatus.

Coctum in clibano virginis, scilicet utero. Clibanus enim panem suscipit et ignem: sic uterus virginis panem vitae, id est, Dei verbum, et ignem Spiritus sancti de coelo suscepit. Unde: Spiritus sanctus superveniet in te, etc. (Luc. I). Si ergo offers munus 302Dincarnationis Christi, quae facta est in ventre virginis, non est acceptum Deo, nisi de simila, id est, scientia vel notitia Dei fiat. De hac re siquidem locutionem ab apostolis et a prophetis suscipimus, Verba apostolorum sunt panes, lagana prophetarum. Quanto enim panes laganis, ad nutriendum aptiores, tantum praecellunt verba apostolorum verbis prophetarum. Utrique autem azymi sine fermento scientiae saecularis. Utraque tamen in oleo fiunt, concorditer enim ab illis de Christi miseratione scriptum est, quia apostoli et prophetae scripserunt, sed apostoli plenius scripserunt, qui completum ostenderunt, quod illi nuntiabant futurum.

Lagana. Panis latus et tenuis, qui prophetiam et 303Alegem significat, quantum enim distat inter soliditatem panis et tenuitatem lagani, tantum inter Evangelium et verba legis et prophetarum: haec tamen oleo lita, quia lex et prophetia charitatem Christi praedixerunt, qua in salutem humani generis venit.

VERS. 5.-- Si oblatio tua fuerit, etc. (ISICH. in Lev. ) Oblatio de sartagine, etc., usque ad et ideo ab ejus scientia recessit.

Similae conspersae, etc. Quia mortuus est et misertus nostri.

Absque fermento, id est, sine macula.

VERS. 6.-- Divides eam minutatim: ut sermonem passionum ejus, qui per partes processerunt, dividamus, alapas, scilicet colaphos, sputa et vulnera 303Bdiligenter distinguamus.

Et fundes super, etc., ut quemadmodum misericorditer pro nobis mortuus est, nos quoque pro fratribus usque ad mortem misereamur.

De sartagine. Sicut enim sartago media inter ignem et cibum, sic quodammodo crux inter impassibilem divinitatem et humanitatem, quae igne passionis est assata. Et sicut in sartagine mollia durescunt, dura mollescunt, sic in passione crucis lapidea corda mollita, mollia et fluxa solidata. De sartagine ergo sacrificium offerre est de mysterio crucis cum veneratione disputare.

VERS. 7.-- Sin autem de, etc. (ISICH. in Lev. ) Sicut in sartagine crucem, etc., usque ad ex misericordia quippe mortem sustinuit, et resurrectionem 303Cdispensavit.

Sin autem, etc. Moraliter, similam offert qui in aliquibus communis vitae usibus bene conversari studet. Haec oleo conspergenda est, quia omnis anima oleo eget misericordiae, nec vitam praesentem evadere potest, nisi misericordia coelestis affuerit. Clibanus autem pondus tentationis exprimit, sartago constantiam et robur animi, craticula multiplicem impugnationem.

VERS. 11.-- Omnis oblatio. (ORIG. homil. 2 in Lev.) Dicat aliquis, etc., usque ad hoc modo verius et perfectius secundum Evangelium sacrificia offers, quae jam secundum legem non potest offerre Israel.

Fermenti ac mellis. Nihil voluptuosum, nihil suave placet Deo, sed quod habet aliquid mordacitatis et 303Dseveritatis. Unde pascha cum amaritudinibus manducatur.

Ac mellis. Gentilis eloquentiae, quae voce suavis est, non re. Unde: Favus distillans labia meretricis (Prov. V). Quod autem dicitur in Canticis: Mel et lac sub lingua tua (Cant. IV), praesentibus non contradicit. Sub lingua enim dicitur, non in lingua, ut ostendatur paganorum sapientia, et Judaeorum littera linguae Ecclesiae, id est doctrinae subjecta esse. Horum non vult integram doctrinam offerre. Unde ait: Non adolebitur in sacrificium, id est, holocaustum: primitias tantum eorum offeretis. Primitias enim illius sapientiae offerimus, non totam, sicut Paulus ad Athenienses, inquit: Sicut vester poeta dixit: hujus 304Aet genus sumus. Quod enim invenit congruum scientiae Dei, non respuit: non tamen ultra testimonia multiplicavit, quia placuit Deo per stultitiam praedicationis salvos facere credentes; unde Moyses ait: Super altare vero non imponentur, et cum liceret dici non ponentur, dicit: Non imponentur in odorem suavitatis: significans, quia si tentaverimus de exterioribus divinitatis adipisci notitiam, tale sacrificium ad altare non ascendit, sed deprimitur in terrenis, quia de terra est.

(BEDA. in Lev., tom. II). Quod mel in Dei sacrificium non offerretur, etc., usque ad imperfecta quaeque omittimus.

VERS. 13.-- Sale condies nec auferes sal foederis. Apostolica sapientia. Unde: Vos estis sal terrae, etc. 304B(Matth. V). Sicut omnis cibus sale conditur, ita apostolica doctrina sermo praedicationis. Ideo praecipitur sal in omni sacrificio offerri, ut secundum Apostolicam doctrinam et institutionem et imitationem operis vivamus. Unde Paulus, ait: Imitatores mei estote, sicut et ego Christi (I Cor. IV). Et alibi: Superaedificati super fundamentum apostolorum et prophetarum (Eph. II). Unde et legislator non dixit simpliciter sal: sed, sal foederis Dei tui. Sicut enim foedus rationale, sic etiam sal rationale. Testamenti ergo Evangelici et salis Apostolici nos commonere voluit. Oleum in sacrificium offertur, si cum hilaritate offeramus, non ex tristitia aut ex necessitate. Hilarem enim datorem diligit Deus (II Cor. IX).

Sal qui stypticam vim habet, et contrarius est 304Cmelli, jubetur addi sacrificiis: sal symbolum est prudentiae, quae restringit et exsiccat voluptatem.

In omni oblatione, etc. Placere enim Deo non poterit quod non ad normam voluntatis ejus correctum fuerit. Nam si in rebus humanis tanta cautela agendum admonuit quidam de mundi hujus sapientibus, ut diceret: Omnis actio temeritate et negligentia vacare debet: quid de Dei rebus aestimandum est?

Si autem obtuleris, etc. (ORIG., lib. I in Levit.) Secundo in loco primitiarum, etc., usque ad quia littera separabatur a spiritu.

(ISICH.) Confringes in morem, etc. Non modo praecepit eos Dei notitiam, etc., usque ad per eleemosynam quippe et orationem scientia divina nobis perficitur.

CHAPTER II.

301BVERS. 2.-- A soul, etc. From the better part he signifies the whole man: for he requires a spiritual and perfect hearer, because the soul is capable of wisdom, not the flesh.

(ISID.) A soul, etc. The fourth sacrifice a soul offers. The fine flour signifies the Church, which is gathered out of many persons as out of grains, separated between the letter and the spirit by the millstone of the law and of the Gospel, made one through the water of baptism, anointed with chrism and made solid by spiritual fire; through humility it becomes an accepted victim.

(ORIG.) hom 2 in Lev. A soul. Which, namely, has neither a calf, etc.; as far as with the oil of the divine mercy or grace.

Mystically it signifies, as there: All the souls that went in 301Cwith Jacob into Egypt, etc. (Gen. XLVI.) But Luke says: And we were in the ship, all the souls, two hundred seventy-six (Act. XXVII). Perhaps by "soul" he signifies the Gentile. For above he commanded the sons of Israel to offer sacrifices with blood. But here, commanding him who is without the law to offer, he does not say "man," as above, but a soul. The Gentile was animal, not understanding the things which are of God, but he is called to the oblation by the mercy of God, that he may receive salvation; when he appoints sacrifices for sins, he who has neither sheep nor she-goat is commanded to offer a turtledove or a dove; he who has not even that, fine flour. For he is now saved not out of works, as one poor in virtues, but by the knowledge of God alone, which, though it be the beginning of salvation, yet frankincense and 301Doil must be laid under it, that grace may be mingled from alms and prayers. Whence the angel says to Cornelius: Thy prayers and thine alms are gone up unto the Lord (Act. X). [Vulg. Acts 10:4 reads ascenderunt in memoriam in conspectu Dei, "are gone up for a memorial in the sight of God"] In the frankincense the inward passions are cured, and by its smoke are healed the eyes that weep from infirmity. For prayer heals the passions of the soul, cures the inward eyes from ignorance, puts to flight the tears that kindle from the sickness of sin.

VERS. 2.-- The sons, the ministers of the Church; because whatsoever we offer, we ought to confer with the holy doctors of the Church. Whence Paul conferred the Gospel with the apostles, lest he should have run in vain (Gal. II).

Of which. The unity of the Church takes up a handful of the fine flour, 302Abecause according to the capacity of his understanding each of the faithful ought to seek after the knowledge of Scripture. Whence: Not to be more wise than it behoveth, but to be wise unto sobriety. Of which one, etc. (Rom. XII). It is to be noted that a handful of the fine flour and of the oil, but the whole of the frankincense, is commanded to be offered; because the wisdom which is signified in the fine flour, and the charity or mercy which is signified in the oil, cannot in this life be full. Whence: We see now through a glass in a dark manner: and, now I know in part, etc. (I Cor. XII). A part also of the oil is offered, because mercy is never fulfilled, since even if thou show mercy as much as thou canst, yet thou oughtest to will more. Charity too, which is figured by the oil, is begun in the present, and in the future shall be perfected. But the whole of the frankincense is burned, because prayer is always persisted in.

302BOf the fine flour. Of the knowledge or acquaintance of God. For that knowledge is accepted from God which faith can comprehend; and just as he who wishes to grasp more with his fist gains nothing, for the whole runs out: so faith is the measure of the knowledge of God. For he that cometh to God must believe, and whatsoever is not of faith is sin (Heb. XI). [Vulg. Heb 11:6 runs credere enim oportet accedentem ad Deum quia est; the second clause is Rom 14:23]

A memorial, etc., the perfect knowledge of the acquaintance of God, of which we shall be mindful, which is never destroyed; and that which now is it destroys not as contrary to itself, but shows it to be a least part through its own fullness: For when that which is perfect is come, that which is in part shall be done away (I Cor. XIII).

VERS. 3.-- Of the sacrifice, etc. (ISICH. in Lev. ) Of that, namely, perfect knowledge hidden in the heavens and laid up in Christ, 302Cin whom are hid all the treasures of wisdom and knowledge (Col. II).

Aaron is interpreted "mountainous," that is, understanding higher things concerning God; or "hidden," as it were having in himself the law and the oracles of God: he can offer the sacrifice. The holy of holies is of the oblations to the Lord: for that which is left over is a portion, yet surpassing all knowledges and exalting to perfection

VERS. 4.-- When thou shalt offer a sacrifice, etc. (ISICH., ibid. ) The sacrifice is Christ immolated for us.

Baked in an oven, namely in the womb of the Virgin. For an oven receives bread and fire: so the womb of the Virgin received the bread of life, that is, the Word of God, and the fire of the Holy Spirit from heaven. Whence: The Holy Spirit shall come upon thee, etc. (Luc. I). If therefore thou offerest the gift 302Dof the incarnation of Christ, which was wrought in the womb of the Virgin, it is not accepted by God unless it be made of fine flour, that is, of the knowledge or acquaintance of God. Of this matter indeed we receive the utterance from the apostles and from the prophets, The words of the apostles are the loaves, the wafers those of the prophets. For by as much as loaves are fitter than wafers for nourishing, by so much do the words of the apostles excel the words of the prophets. Yet both are unleavened, without the leaven of secular knowledge. Both, however, are made in oil, for concordantly it has been written by them concerning the compassion of Christ, since both apostles and prophets wrote, but the apostles wrote more fully, who showed to be fulfilled what those were announcing as to come.

Wafers. A broad and thin bread, which signifies prophecy and 303Athe law; for as great as is the distance between the solidity of bread and the thinness of a wafer, so great is that between the Gospel and the words of the law and of the prophets: yet these are smeared with oil, because the law and prophecy foretold the charity of Christ, by which he came unto the salvation of the human race.

VERS. 5.-- If thy oblation shall be, etc. (ISICH. in Lev. ) The oblation of the frying-pan, etc., as far as and therefore he departed from the knowledge of him.

Of fine flour sprinkled, etc. Because he died and had mercy on us.

Without leaven, that is, without stain.

VERS. 6.-- Thou shalt divide it into little pieces: that we may divide the discourse of his passions, which went forward by parts, and may carefully mark off the buffets, that is, the blows, the spittings and the wounds 303B.

And thou shalt pour upon, etc., that as he mercifully died for us, so we too may have mercy on our brethren even unto death.

Of the frying-pan. For as the frying-pan is midway between the fire and the food, so in a certain manner is the cross between the impassible divinity and the humanity, which was roasted by the fire of the passion. And as in a frying-pan soft things harden and hard things soften, so in the passion of the cross stony hearts are softened, and soft and flowing ones made solid. To offer sacrifice, therefore, from the frying-pan is to dispute with veneration concerning the mystery of the cross.

VERS. 7.-- But if of, etc. (ISICH. in Lev. ) As on the frying-pan the cross, etc., as far as for out of mercy he underwent death, and dispensed the resurrection 303C.

But if, etc. Morally, he offers fine flour who studies to behave well in some of the usages of the common life. This is to be sprinkled with oil, because every soul needs the oil of mercy, nor can it escape the present life unless heavenly mercy shall have been at hand. But the oven expresses the weight of temptation, the frying-pan the constancy and strength of the mind, the gridiron the manifold assault.

VERS. 11.-- Every oblation. (ORIG. homil. 2 in Lev.) Let someone say, etc., as far as in this way thou offerest more truly and more perfectly according to the Gospel the sacrifices which Israel can no longer offer according to the law.

Of leaven and of honey. Nothing voluptuous, nothing sweet pleases God, but what has something of sharpness and 303Dof severity. Whence the pasch is eaten with bitter herbs.

And of honey. Of Gentile eloquence, which is sweet in voice, not in fact. Whence: A honeycomb distilling are the lips of a harlot (Prov. V). But what is said in the Canticles: Honey and milk are under thy tongue (Cant. IV), does not contradict what is here said. For it is said "under the tongue," not "in the tongue," that it may be shown that the wisdom of the pagans and the letter of the Jews are subject to the tongue of the Church, that is, to her doctrine. Of these he does not will the whole doctrine to be offered. Whence he says: It shall not be burned in sacrifice, that is, as a holocaust: ye shall offer only the firstfruits of them. For we offer the firstfruits of that wisdom, not the whole of it, as Paul to the Athenians says: As your own poet said: of him 304Awe also are the offspring. [Vulg. Acts 17:28 reads sicut et quidam vestrum poetarum dixerunt: ipsius enim et genus sumus, "as certain also of your own poets said"] For what he found agreeable to the knowledge of God, he did not reject: yet he did not multiply testimonies beyond this, because it pleased God by the foolishness of preaching to save them that believe; whence Moses says: But upon the altar they shall not be laid, and though it might have been said "they shall not be placed," he says: They shall not be laid for an odor of sweetness: signifying that if we shall have attempted to obtain the knowledge of the divinity from outward things, such a sacrifice does not ascend to the altar, but is pressed down among earthly things, because it is of the earth.

(BEDA. in Lev., tom. II). That honey should not be offered in the sacrifice of God, etc., as far as we omit whatsoever things are imperfect.

VERS. 13.-- Thou shalt season with salt, nor shalt thou take away the salt of the covenant. The apostolic wisdom. Whence: Ye are the salt of the earth, etc. 304B(Matth. V). As all food is seasoned with salt, so is the word of preaching by apostolic doctrine. Therefore it is commanded that salt be offered in every sacrifice, that we may live according to apostolic doctrine and instruction and the imitation of its work. Whence Paul says: Be ye imitators of me, as I also am of Christ (I Cor. IV). And elsewhere: Built up upon the foundation of the apostles and prophets (Eph. II). Whence also the lawgiver did not say simply "salt," but the salt of the covenant of thy God. For as the covenant is rational, so also the salt is rational. He willed, therefore, to admonish us of the evangelical Testament and of the apostolic salt. Oil is offered in sacrifice, if we offer with cheerfulness, not out of sadness or out of necessity. For God loveth a cheerful giver (II Cor. IX).

Salt, which has a styptic force and is contrary to 304Choney, is commanded to be added to the sacrifices: salt is the symbol of prudence, which restrains and dries up pleasure.

In every oblation, etc. For that will not be able to please God which shall not have been corrected to the rule of his will. For if in human affairs a certain one of the wise men of this world admonished that we must act with so great caution, so that he said: Every action ought to be free of rashness and of negligence: what is to be judged concerning the things of God?

But if thou shalt offer, etc. (ORIG., lib. I in Levit.) In the second place, of the firstfruits, etc., as far as because the letter was separated from the spirit.

(ISICH.) Thou shalt break after the manner, etc. Not only did he command them the knowledge of God, etc., as far as for through almsgiving and prayer the divine knowledge is perfected in us.

CAPUT III.

  • 304D

VERS. 1.-- Quod si hostia, etc. (ORIG. homil. 3 in Levit.) Transmissis caeteris, de salutaribus sacrificiis dicit, quae de animalibus, id est bobus et capris et ovibus offeruntur, et nihil ultra ad immolandum substituitur: nec aves quidem, quae in offerendis muneribus substitutae sunt superius. Qui enim salutares offert hostias salutis suae conscius est: necesse ergo habet ut magna et perfecta offerat, unde Apostolus: Perfectorum autem est cibus solidus (Heb. V).

(ISICH.) Quod si hostia, etc. Sunt secundum leges diversae hominum conversationes, etc., usque ad oportet enim eas digne conversari, ut perveniant ad salutem.

305AMarem sive feminam, etc. (ISICH.) Superius in holocaustum tantum mares offeruntur, etc., usque ad id est infirma opera cum fortioribus.

VERS. 3.-- Adipem qui, etc. (ID.) Oportet intelligibilem adipem offerri, etc., usque ad et per deliberationem massam sanctificemus.

Vitalia. LXX: Ventrem, id est cor, cujus secundo facit mentionem juxta LXX, quia duplicem habet operationem secundum medicos, quia per unam partem alimentum trahit, quam vocant ventrem superiorem: per alteram purgationem discernit, quam vocant ventrem inferiorem. Renes sunt genitalium seminum ministeria, cum ventre et femoribus communicantes. Primum ergo adipem, qui supra ventrem est, et duos renes, et adipem qui super eos est, 305Bid est super femora, quae renibus sunt vicina, jubet offerri: in medio enim femoris et ventris sunt positi.

VERS. 4.-- Reticulum jecoris: Id est, epatis, in quo delectatio vel concupiscentia. Unde juxta LXX pinna epatis cum renibus auferetur: jecur enim in renibus vim operationis ministrat.

VERS. 6.-- Si vero de ovibus fuerit, etc. (ISICH). Quem praecipiat offerre bovem, etc., usque ad in lumbis quoque operatio generationis.

VERS. 9.-- Et caudam. LXX: Lumbum sine macula integrum, ne scilicet aliud offeratur Deo, aliud adversario. Sufficit enim huic ad veniam quae virtutis sunt non agere; sin autem mala operatur, veniam non consequitur. Unde: Estote simplices sicut columbae. 305CColumbae enim nimiam simplicitatem habent, malitiam nullam. Qui de bobus sacrificium offert, lumbum Deo offerre non eget: quem in bonis operibus constringit et exercet. Qui de capris immolat, non habet lumbum immaculatum quem offerat, quia eum olim peccato maculavit. Qui ergo non fuit in peccatis, immaculatum lumbum offerre potest, non qui peccator fuit. Hac causa in sacrificio pacificorum capras ab agnis distinxit.

VERS. 17.-- Nec adipem. Propriam concupiscentiam, id est carnalem habere non debemus: Quisque enim tentatur a concupiscentia sua abstractus et illectus. Sanguinem prohibet comedi, ne quemquam interficiamus: sanguinem enim edere, bestiarum, 305Did est crudelium, est. Ideo aut omnino additur: ut et proximis parcamus et nobis. Deo autem debet anima immolari, unde jussit sanguinem fundi circa basim altaris.

CHAPTER III.

  • 304D

VERS. 1.-- But if the victim, etc. (ORIG. homil. 3 in Levit.) The rest being passed over, he speaks of the peace-offering sacrifices, which are offered from animals, that is, from oxen and goats and sheep, and nothing further is substituted for immolation: not even birds, which above were substituted in the offering of gifts. For he who offers peace offerings is conscious of his own salvation: he has need, therefore, to offer great and perfect things, whence the Apostle: But solid food is of the perfect (Heb. V).

(ISICH.) But if the victim, etc. There are, according to the laws, diverse manners of life among men, etc., as far as for they must live worthily, that they may come to salvation.

305AMale or female, etc. (ISICH.) Above, for a holocaust, males only are offered, etc., as far as that is, weak works together with stronger.

VERS. 3.-- The fat which, etc. (ID.) The intelligible fat must be offered, etc., as far as and by deliberation let us sanctify the lump.

The vitals. The Seventy: The belly, that is, the heart, of which he makes mention a second time according to the Seventy, because it has a twofold operation according to the physicians, since through one part it draws nourishment, which they call the upper belly: through the other it separates off the purgation, which they call the lower belly. The kidneys are the ministries of the generative seeds, communicating with the belly and the thighs. First, therefore, he commands the fat which is above the belly, and the two kidneys, and the fat which is upon them, 305Bthat is, upon the thighs, which are near to the kidneys, to be offered: for they are set in the midst of the thigh and of the belly.

VERS. 4.-- The caul of the liver: That is, of the epar, in which is delight or concupiscence. Whence according to the Seventy the lobe of the epar shall be taken away with the kidneys: for the liver ministers in the kidneys the force of operation.

VERS. 6.-- But if it shall be of the sheep, etc. (ISICH). Whom he may command to offer an ox, etc., as far as in the loins also the operation of generation.

VERS. 9.-- And the tail. The Seventy: The loin whole and without blemish, that is, lest one thing be offered to God and another to the adversary. For it suffices this man, for pardon, not to do the things which are of virtue; but if he works evils, he does not obtain pardon. Whence: Be ye simple as doves. 305CFor doves have exceeding simplicity, and no malice. He who offers a sacrifice of oxen has no need to offer the loin to God: him he binds and exercises in good works. He who immolates of the goats has not an unspotted loin which he may offer, because he once stained it with sin. He therefore who has not been in sins can offer an unspotted loin, not he who has been a sinner. For this cause, in the sacrifice of the peace offerings, he distinguished the she-goats from the lambs.

VERS. 17.-- Nor the fat. We ought not to have our own concupiscence, that is, the carnal: For every one is tempted, being drawn away and enticed by his own concupiscence. He forbids blood to be eaten, that we may kill no one: for to eat blood belongs to beasts, 305Dthat is, to the cruel. Therefore either it is added altogether: that we may spare both our neighbors and ourselves. But the soul ought to be immolated to God, whence he commanded the blood to be poured out about the base of the altar.

CAPUT IV.

VERS. 1-2.-- Locutusque etc. Anima quae peccaverit, etc. (ORIG., hom. 2 in Levit.) Recte animam dicit, etc., usque ad et spiritus dux ad virtutem, si eum sequi velit.

(ISICH.) Anima cum peccaverit, etc. Nihil necessarium legislator omisit, etc., usque ad unde, Qui nescibat peccatum, pro nobis peccatum factus est.

VERS. 3.-- Si sacerdos, etc. (ORIG. Observandum est quod in peccato pontificis legislator non addit 306Aquod ignorans aut nolens peccaverit: neque enim ignorantia in eum cadere potest, qui ut doceret caeteros provectus est.

(ID.) Si sacerdos, etc. Terror simul et misericordia, etc., usque ad qui conscientia infirmitatis mordetur.

(Id.) Si sacerdos, etc. Pontifex, sensus pietatis et religionis, etc., usque ad Christi morte reparatur.

VERS. 3.-- Vitulum immaculatum Domino, etc. (ID.) Secundo invenimus offerri a pontifice vitulum in holocaustum, etc. usque ad in quo passus est Christus.

VERS. 5.-- Hauriet quoque de sanguine, etc. Sanguinem vituli in tabernaculum, id est, in coelum intulit: cum post resurrectionem coelos conscendens, 306Bvultui Patris astitit, quasi offerens sanguinem passionis, interpellans pro nobis.

VERS. 6.-- Septies: pro dimissione, scilicet peccatorum et sub ministratione spiritalium donorum. Sanguis autem passionem significat, secundum illud: Mundus ego sum a sanguine justi hujus. Et alibi: Sanguis ejus super nos, etc. De hac aspersione Petrus ait: In obedientiam et aspersionem sanguinis Christi, etc.

Velum, humanitatem Christi. Nam sicut velum erat inter sancta sanctorum et exterius tabernaculum, ita caro Christi est inter nos, qui exterius tabernaculum sumus, et divinitatem, quae per sancta sanctorum significatur. Contra velum ergo testimonii sacerdos sanguinem vituli aspersit, quia Christus 306Ccarnem suam sanguinem passionis cruentavit.

Contra velum, etc. Multis nominibus carnem Domini appellat, quia operatio ejus multa. Unde ipse Dominus seipsum, modo sacrificium (Luc. XXI), modo pastorem, rursus vitem, deinde panem vitae et granum tritici, translatis vocabulis nominavit. (Joan. X, XV, VI, XII) Sic et Moyses carnem ejus velum appellavit. Unde Paulus: Initiavit nobis viam per velamen, id est carnem suam (Heb. X). Velum quoque sanctuarii exhinc appellatur, quia in eo sancti sanctorum caro figuratur.

VERS. 7.-- Super cornua. Hoc altare sedem Dei, id est Seraphin significat, unde Esa: Volavit ad me unus de Seraphin, et in manu ejus calculus, quem forcipe tulit, de altari (Isa. VI). Thymiama, secundum 306DLXX, compositiones omnium virtutum. Ubi enim decet compositas esse virtutes, nisi in sede Dei? de coelo namque ad nos descendunt. Unde Jacobus ait: Quae autem desursum sapientia (Jac. III). Quatuor autem virtutes, sapientia, justitia, fortitudo, temperantia, quia caeterarum virtutum genera continent, nominantur cornua: has inungit Deus proprio sanguine confortans, scilicet majori et perfectiori virtute, ut possint contra adversarias potestates fortitudinesque praevalere.

Altaris, etc. Angelicarum virtutum. Si enim homines gratum Deo odorem bonorum operum reddunt, quanto magis sancti angeli in puritate naturae permanentes? Si autem peccatum quod Christi sanguine 307Apiaretur, non admiserunt, per sanguinem tamen ejus nobis pacificati sunt, et numerus eorum reparatus.

In basim. Basis, pars altaris inferior, infirmos homines significat. Sanguine enim Christi non solus numerus angelorum restitutus est, sed et homo redemptus: ideo angelorum quoque redemptio Christus dicitur (Col. I).

Altaris holocausti. Corporis Christi. Ipse enim est sacerdos, ipse sacrificium, ipse et altare. Sicut enim multa operatur, ita convenienter multis nominibus appellatur. Unde Paulus: Habemus altare de quo non habent potestatem edere qui tabernaculo deserviunt (Heb. XIII): hoc est, corpus Christi, de quo non licet edere Judaeis; hoc altare est in introitu 307Btabernaculi, id est coeli, quia per ipsum ingressum habemus in coelum.

Et adipem. Mystice, delectationes et desideria carnis; quae tamen omnia non sunt in Christum recipienda, sicut gulae, et coitus desiderium. Quae autem habuerat, habuit ex nobis, ut sitim et esuriem, et hujusmodi. Haec autem Patri obtulit, ad cujus transtulit gloriam, et nobis in exemplum protulit, ut ad Deum desideria nostra referamus.

VERS. 9.-- Duos renunculos et reticulum, quod est super eos juxta ilia. (ISICH.) Quae fiunt et in sacrificio pacificorum, vel salutari. Et in duo sacrificium dividit, ut sacrificium Christi liceat omnibus imitari tam his scilicet qui inferioris sunt conversationis, 307Cquibus sacrificium pacificorum convenit, quam his qui altiora sequuntur, quibus sufficit si sequentia imitantur.

VERS. 11.-- Pellem vero et omnes carnes cum capite et pedibus et caetera. Hoc praecipit ut Christum non dividas, sicut quidam dividunt dicentes: Qui de Maria passus, qui de Deo passus non est; Nobis autem unus est Deus Jesus Christus per quem omnia (I Cor. VIII). Caro enim in Domino omnis humanitas est, secundum quod dicitur: Verbum caro factum est (Joan. I). Unde ait: Carnes ejus cum capite et pedibus vel extremis secundum alios, ut per haec verba, eum qui pro nobis passus est, primum et novissimum esse credamus: ipse enim est ante omnia, et in ipso constant omnia. Cum intestinis et fimo, 307DLXX: cum ventre et stercore. Venter autem significat mentem; eum ergo qui pro nobis passus est, mentem humanam habuisse minime dubitemus, quod quidam haereticorum negant. Fimus, peccatum humani generis, signat quod crucifixus in se suscepit. Fimus, cum sit immundus, gerentem non polluit; neque naturae nostrae est, quamvis in nobis sit: sic peccata nostra, quae Dominus portavit, aliena ab illo fuerunt, nec eum polluerunt.

Intestinis et fimo, etc. (ORIG.) Forte ad comparationem, etc., usque ad plenius legislator in sequentibus figurat ejus mysterium.

VERS. 13.-- Quod si omnis turba. (ISICH.) Idem praecipit in sacrificio turbae et sacerdotis, etc., usque 308Aad pro ipsis enim crucifixoribus oravit: Pater, ignosce illis quia nesciunt quid faciunt.

VERS. 21.-- Vitulum efferet extra castra (ID.) Sufficit nobis conclusio legis ad manifestanda praesentia, nihil enim praecipitur hic mutari pro peccato multitudinis. Manifestum est quod idem peccatum plebis et sacerdotis intelligit, ideo in utroque idem sacrificium ordinavit.

VERS. 22.-- Si peccaverit princeps et fecerit. (ID.) Sicut in spontaneis sacrificiis, etc., usque ad sabbatum enim ad otium malorum datum est.

(ORIG.) Princeps iste potest dici vis rationis, quae intra nos est, quae si peccet, ut aliquid stultum agamus, sententiam illam timeamus: Vos estis sal terrae. Si vero sal infatuatum fuerit, ad nihilum valet 308Bnisi projiciatur foras, et conculcetur ab hominibus. (Matth. V.)

VERS. 23.-- Hircum de capris. Non capram, facilem scilicet vel mediocrem, sed difficilem et asperam poenitentiam. Quanto enim quis intellectu praeeminet, tanto, si peccat, pro majori culpa majorem poenam sustinet.

VERS. 24.-- Ponet manum, etc., per semetipsum peccatum manifestans, et offerens confessionem suam. Ipse autem juxta LXX, non occidit, sed alii; quia illi est necessarium aliorum auxilium, ut poenitentia suscipiatur, nec sacerdos occidere praecipitur, ait enim:

Cumque immolaverit, etc., nec addidit, quis immolare debeat, licet enim in sacrificio poenitentiae 308Calterius uti adjutoriis: unde Jac.: Orate pro invicem, ut salvemini.

Ubi solet mactari, etc. Quia similiter placent Deo digna peccatorum poenitentia et fortia justorum opera.

VERS. 25.-- Cornua altaris, etc. Super crucem offeruntur primitiae sanguinis qui pro peccato offertur, quia opus crucis et effectus peccatorum nostrorum perfecta remissio est.

Et reliquum fundens. Omnem scilicet poenitentiam evangelicae praedicationi tribuens cujus intentio est ut se corrigat et salvetur qui poenitet. Unde in principio Evangelii Dominus inquit providenter: Poenitentiam agite, appropinquat enim regnum coelorum. 308D(Matth. III.)

VERS. 27.-- Quod si peccaverit, etc. (ORIG. hom. 2. in Levit.) Faciens unum ab omnibus mandatis Domini, etc., usque ad cum agnoscit immolare sacrificium laudis.

De populo, etc. (ORIG.) Ad distinctionem ejus, etc. usque ad propter nos necessarium ducit.

Si autem de pecoribus. (ISICH.) Oblationi caprae quam offerunt qui de populo sunt, etc., usque ad de capra et ove similia praecipit.

CHAPTER IV.

VERS. 1-2.-- And he spoke etc. The soul that shall have sinned, etc. (ORIG., hom. 2 in Levit.) Rightly does he say "the soul," etc., as far as and the spirit is a leader unto virtue, if he be willing to follow it.

(ISICH.) When a soul shall have sinned, etc. The lawgiver omitted nothing necessary, etc., as far as whence, He who knew not sin was made sin for us. [Vulg. 2 Cor 5:21 reads eum qui non noverat peccatum pro nobis peccatum fecit, "him who knew no sin he made to be sin for us," where Migne's verb is passive]

VERS. 3.-- If the priest, etc. (ORIG. It is to be observed that in the sin of the high priest the lawgiver does not add 306Athat he sinned unknowing or unwilling: for neither can ignorance fall upon him who has been advanced that he might teach the rest.

(ID.) If the priest, etc. Terror and mercy together, etc., as far as who is gnawed by the consciousness of his infirmity.

(Id.) If the priest, etc. The high priest, the sense of piety and of religion, etc., as far as is repaired by the death of Christ.

VERS. 3.-- A calf without blemish to the Lord, etc. (ID.) In the second place we find a calf offered by the high priest for a holocaust, etc. as far as in which Christ suffered.

VERS. 5.-- He shall also take of the blood, etc. He bore the blood of the calf into the tabernacle, that is, into heaven: when after the resurrection, ascending the heavens, 306Bhe stood before the countenance of the Father, as it were offering the blood of the passion, making intercession for us.

VERS. 6.-- Seven times: for the remission, namely, of sins, and under the ministration of spiritual gifts. But the blood signifies the passion, according to that saying: I am clean from the blood of this just man. [Vulg. Mt 27:24 reads Innocens ego sum, "I am innocent"] And elsewhere: His blood be upon us, etc. Concerning this sprinkling Peter says: Unto obedience and the sprinkling of the blood of Christ, etc.

The veil, the humanity of Christ. For as the veil was between the holy of holies and the outer tabernacle, so the flesh of Christ is between us, who are the outer tabernacle, and the divinity, which is signified by the holy of holies. Against the veil of the testimony, therefore, the priest sprinkled the blood of the calf, because Christ 306Cmade his own flesh bloody with the blood of the passion.

Against the veil, etc. By many names does he call the flesh of the Lord, because its operation is manifold. Whence the Lord himself named himself, now a sacrifice (Luc. XXI), now a shepherd, again a vine, then the bread of life and the grain of wheat, by transferred words. (Joan. X, XV, VI, XII) So Moses too called his flesh a veil. Whence Paul: He hath consecrated for us a way through the veil, that is, his flesh (Heb. X). The veil of the sanctuary too is named from this, because in it the flesh of the holy of holies is figured.

VERS. 7.-- Upon the horns. This altar signifies the seat of God, that is, the Seraphim; whence Isaiah: There flew to me one of the Seraphim, and in his hand a coal, which he took with the tongs from the altar (Isa. VI). The incense, according to 306Dthe Seventy, the compositions of all the virtues. For where does it befit the virtues to be composed, if not in the seat of God? for from heaven they descend to us. Whence James says: But the wisdom that is from above (Jac. III). But four virtues, wisdom, justice, fortitude, temperance, because they contain the kinds of the other virtues, are named horns: these God anoints with his own blood, strengthening them, namely with a greater and more perfect strength, that they may be able to prevail against the adverse powers and forces.

Of the altar, etc. Of the angelic powers. For if men render to God a pleasing odor of good works, how much more the holy angels, remaining in the purity of their nature? But if they did not commit the sin which was to be expiated by the blood of Christ, 307Ayet through his blood they were made at peace with us, and their number was repaired.

Unto the base. The base, the lower part of the altar, signifies weak men. For by the blood of Christ not only was the number of the angels restored, but man also redeemed: therefore Christ is called the redemption of the angels too (Col. I).

Of the altar of holocaust. Of the body of Christ. For he himself is the priest, he himself the sacrifice, he himself also the altar. For as he works many things, so fittingly is he called by many names. Whence Paul: We have an altar whereof they have no power to eat who serve the tabernacle (Heb. XIII): that is, the body of Christ, of which it is not lawful for the Jews to eat; this altar is at the entrance 307Bof the tabernacle, that is, of heaven, because through it we have entrance into heaven.

And the fat. Mystically, the delights and desires of the flesh; all of which, however, are not to be received into Christ, such as the desire of the gullet and of coition. But those which he had, he had from us, as thirst and hunger, and things of this kind. And these he offered to the Father, to whose glory he transferred them, and set them forth for us as an example, that we may refer our desires unto God.

VERS. 9.-- The two little kidneys and the caul that is upon them near the flanks. (ISICH.) Which things are done also in the sacrifice of the peace offerings, or of salvation. And he divides the sacrifice into two, that it may be permitted to all to imitate the sacrifice of Christ, both to those, namely, who are of a lower manner of life, 307Cto whom the sacrifice of the peace offerings is suited, and to those who follow after higher things, for whom it suffices if they imitate what follows.

VERS. 11.-- But the skin and all the flesh with the head and the feet and the rest. This he commands, that thou divide not Christ, as certain men divide him, saying: He who suffered was of Mary, he who was of God did not suffer; But to us there is one God Jesus Christ, through whom are all things (I Cor. VIII). [Vulg. 1 Cor 8:6 reads nobis tamen unus Deus Pater ex quo omnia … et unus Dominus Iesus Christus per quem omnia, distinguishing the Father from the Lord Jesus Christ] For the flesh in the Lord is the whole humanity, according to what is said: The Word was made flesh (Joan. I). Whence he says: His flesh with the head and the feet, or the extremities according to others, that by these words we may believe him who suffered for us to be the first and the last: for he is before all things, and in him all things consist. With the entrails and the dung, 307Dthe Seventy: with the belly and the excrement. But the belly signifies the mind; let us therefore in no wise doubt that he who suffered for us had a human mind, which certain of the heretics deny. The dung signifies the sin of the human race, which the crucified took upon himself. Dung, though it be unclean, does not pollute him who carries it; nor is it of our nature, although it be within us: so our sins, which the Lord bore, were foreign to him, nor did they pollute him.

With the entrails and the dung, etc. (ORIG.) Perhaps by way of comparison, etc., as far as the lawgiver figures his mystery more fully in what follows.

VERS. 13.-- But if all the multitude. (ISICH.) The same thing he commands in the sacrifice of the multitude and of the priest, etc., as far 308Aas for he prayed for his very crucifiers: Father, forgive them, for they know not what they do. [Vulg. Lk 23:34 reads Pater, dimitte illis, non enim sciunt quid faciunt]

VERS. 21.-- He shall carry the calf outside the camp (ID.) The conclusion of the law suffices us for making plain the present matters, for nothing is here commanded to be changed for the sin of the multitude. It is manifest that he understands the sin of the people and of the priest to be the same, and therefore in both he ordained the same sacrifice.

VERS. 22.-- If a prince shall have sinned and done. (ID.) As in the voluntary sacrifices, etc., as far as for the sabbath was given for rest from evils.

(ORIG.) This prince can be called the force of reason which is within us, which if it sin, so that we do something foolish, let us fear that sentence: Ye are the salt of the earth. But if the salt shall have become insipid, it is worth nothing 308Bbut that it be cast forth and trodden underfoot by men. (Matth. V.) [Vulg. Mt 5:13 reads quod si sal evanuerit in quo sallietur ad nihilum valet ultra nisi ut mittatur foras, "if the salt lose its savour, wherewith shall it be salted?"]

VERS. 23.-- A he-goat of the goats. Not a she-goat, that is, an easy or middling penance, but a hard and rough one. For by as much as a man excels in understanding, by so much, if he sins, does he undergo a greater punishment for a greater fault.

VERS. 24.-- He shall put his hand, etc., manifesting his sin through himself, and offering his own confession. But he himself, according to the Seventy, does not slay it, but others do; because the help of others is necessary to him, that his penance may be received, nor is the priest commanded to slay it, for he says:

And when he shall have immolated, etc., nor did he add who ought to immolate, for it is lawful in the sacrifice of penance 308Cto use the helps of another: whence James: Pray one for another, that ye may be saved.

Where it is wont to be slain, etc. Because there please God alike a worthy penance for sins and the strong works of the just.

VERS. 25.-- The horns of the altar, etc. Upon the cross are offered the firstfruits of the blood which is offered for sin, because the work of the cross and its effect is the perfect remission of our sins.

And pouring out the rest. Attributing, namely, all penance to the evangelical preaching, whose intention is that he who repents may correct himself and be saved. Whence in the beginning of the Gospel the Lord says providently: Do penance, for the kingdom of heaven draweth near. 308D(Matth. III.) [Vulg. Mt 3:2 reads the perfect adpropinquavit, "hath drawn near"]

VERS. 27.-- But if he shall have sinned, etc. (ORIG. hom. 2. in Levit.) Doing one thing out of all the commandments of the Lord, etc., as far as when he acknowledges it, to immolate a sacrifice of praise.

Of the people, etc. (ORIG.) For the distinguishing of him, etc. as far as he holds it necessary for our sake.

But if of the cattle. (ISICH.) To the oblation of the she-goat which those who are of the people offer, etc., as far as he commands like things concerning the she-goat and the sheep.

CAPUT V.

VERS. 1.-- Si peccaverit anima, etc. (AUG. quaest. 1 in Levit.) Videtur dicere peccare hominem, etc., 309Ausque ad sed statim subjunxit: Anima quae tetigerit aliquid immundum, etc.

(ISICH.) Si peccaverit, etc. Non repetit quae superius dixit, etc., usque ad quia alterius peccatis vel errori non debemus consentire, sed arguere.

VERS. 2.-- Anima qui tetigerit. (ISICH.) Non corporis, etc., usque ad sed potest dicere cum David: Anima nostra sicut passer erepta est de laqueo venantium (Psal. CXXIII).

(GREG. lib. XXXII Moral., cap. 4 tom. II.) Juramentum proferre est voto nos divinae servitutis alligare, etc., usque ad igni doloris ardemus.

VERS. 4.-- Anima quae juraverit, etc. (AUG., quaest. 2 in Levit.) Quid est quod pro tacito perjurio, vel tacto morticino, vel aliquo immundo nullum sacrificium 309Bpraecipitur? Pro eo vero quod quisque falsum nesciens jurat, agnam vel capram offerri decernit? an pro omnibus supradictis hoc sacrificium oportet intelligi? Omnibus enim enuntiatis intulit sacrificium expiationis.

Intellexerit delictum. (ISICH.) Notandum quod delictum, etc., usque ad sicut sequentia ostendunt.

VERS. 7.-- Sin autem non potuerit, etc. (ID.) Hoc adjiciamus, ut si quid horum princeps peccaverit, hircum offerat de capris, condignam scilicet poenitentiam sibi, qui nec peccare debuit, nec communicare aliis, nec subditos ad peccandum provocare. Quod ergo evenire non debuit, indiscussum reliquit.

Sin autem, etc. (ID.) Qui non potest affligere se continue, etc., usque ad quia seipsum plenissime 309CDeo sacrificat.

VERS. 8.-- Retorquebit caput (GREG., hom. in Ezech. ) Mors Christi ad conjunctionem sui corporis, etc., usque ad omnes nos sibi verius in ipsam mortem suam conjunxit.

(GREG.) Retorquebit, etc. In sacrificio Dei turtur esse debemus, ut caput nostrum ad pennulas retorqueatur, id est, sensus ad virtutes. Per caput, mentem intelligimus, quia sicut caput corpus, ita mens actiones regit. Sed caput collo inhaeret, nec funditus abscinditur, quia mens nostra a carnis delectatione est separanda, non a carnis necessaria cura, nec curam carnis faciamus in desideriis (Rom. XIII). Caput ergo turturis partim abscindatur, et partim collo inhaereat.

309DAbrumpatur, etc. Vult nos non servire corpori, sed abrumpi vel retorqueri, ne lex peccati regnet in membris nostris. Quia vero corpus nostrum non debemus odio habere, nec diaboli facturam secundum haereticos existimare, sed menti supponere, non praecipit separari corpus a collo, utrumque enim unius ejusdemque creatura est.

VERS. 9.-- Fundamentum, etc. LXX, basim, quod est Evangelium, quia, secundum intentionem sacrificii Christi et Evangelii, debet sacrificium pro peccatis offerri. Unde addit: Quia pro peccato est, nihil enim sic a peccato eripit, quemadmodum imitatio Christi et obedientia Evangelicae praedicationis.

310AQuod si non quiverit. Quia non omne tempus ad poenitentiam facile, quando scilicet ipsa poenitentia abutimur, et fructum ejus, ad peccatum redeuntes, abolemus, legislator innuit dicens: Quod si non quiverit manus ejus, etc.

(AUG. quaest. 8 in Levit., tom. III.) Quaeritur utrum ubique accipiendum sit quod dictum est, etc., usque ad ut caetera omnia ab istis ad illam generalitatem pertinerent.

VERS. 11.-- Ephi. (ISICH.) Mensura est trium mensurarum, etc., usque ad non perire eos qui sub manu ejus fuerint.

Non mittet in eum, etc. Quasi: non est haec perfecta poenitentia, ideo oleum non habet, id est, spiritualem laetitiam hilaritatis, nec thus, quia nec 310Bconfidendum est ei in orationibus: sed tradat, qui offert oblationem, sacerdoti, id est, spem salutis committat divinae pietati.

Qui pro peccato. Quia nescit utrum boni odoris sit oratio ejus qui frequenter praevaricatur. Unde: Quis scit si convertatur et ignoscat Deus, et relinquat post se benedictionem (Joan. III)? Non sinit ergo desperare nec tamen praesumere, ne iterum negligentes sint.

VERS. 12.-- Pugillum. In pugillo Scriptura significatur, quo scribitur. Scriptum est autem: Non dico tibi septies, sed usque septuagies septies (Matth. XXVIII). Per pugillum ergo innuit Deum exorandum esse, ut meminerit scriptae misericordiae suae, ut eam nobis donet: sicut fratribus donari jubet; hoc 310Cenim orantem mundabit sacerdos magnus.

VERS. 13.-- Anima si praevaricans, etc. (ISICH.) Ab omni peccato abstinendum est, sed maxime ab eo quod contra sancta committitur: quantum autem sit horrendum oblatio testatur. Nusquam enim a principio historiae, nisi hoc in loco, aries offerri jubetur, in quo necessitas et utilitas sacrificii perhibetur: non offertur hic hircus, non capra, non ovis, non par turturum, aut duo pulli columbarum.

Caeremonias per, etc. (ORIG.) Multae species sanctificationis sunt. Est oleum, quo ungitur solum tabernaculum; et thymiama, de quo dicitur: Quicunque talem compositionem fecerit, peribit de populis suis: panes quoque propositionis soli mares comedunt, qui sunt de stirpe Aaron. Mystice autem in 310Dhis quae sanctificata sunt, peccat, qui in sacro loco aliquid irreverenter usurpat, vel res divinas indigne tractat.
