VERS. 15.-- Duobus siclis. Duo sicli, duo praecepta charitatis sunt, quibus peccator emit sibi confidentiam remissionis. Nihil enim valet poenitentia sine gratia remissionis; unde juxta LXX aries quinquaginta siclis emitur, quia per poenitentiam et geminae charitatis observantiam perfecta requies acquiritur, quando remissio peccatorum datur. LXX, argenti quinquaginta siclis, siclo sancto. Nemo enim acquirit Domini sacrificium nisi per Spiritum sanctum, quia nemo potest dicere Dominus Jesus nisi in Spiritu sancto (I Cor. XII). Quinquaginta vero Spiritus sancti adventum 311Asignificat, quia die Pentecostes, id est, quinquagesima ad apostolos venit. LXX: siclo sancto. In siclo sancto, Spiritus sancti virtutem et operationem significavit. Siculus 20 obolorum est, vicenarius obnoxius peccati non est: unde qui de Aegypto exierunt, cum essent viginti annorum, peccati populi poenam non susceperunt (Num. XIV); mundus quippe est ab omni peccato spiritualis homo.

VERS. 16.-- Et quintam partem, etc. (ISICH.) Qui vase sancto vel cibo, etc., usque ad sic enim pro peccato nostro deprecabitur.

VERS. 17.-- Anima si peccaverit, etc. (ID.). Videtur oblitus quod praeceperat: pro quibus enim peccatis capram vel ovem offerri praecepit, nunc arietem offerri praecipere videtur; sed nec oblitus 311Best anteriora, nec contraria praecepit: ibi enim per animam, Gentilem significavit: hic autem eum qui per ignorantiam transgreditur mandata legis.

(AUG. quaest. 7 in Levit.) Quaeritur quomodo discernitur hoc delictum ab eis quae generaliter complexus est, etc., usque ad vel aliqua in sacris servitiis.

VERS. 18.-- Offeret arietem, etc. Superius praecepit ut poenitens capram offerret, hic arietem immolari jubet, quia nihil prodest asperitatem poenitentiae immolare, nisi statueris in Christi immolatione plenam confidentiam habere.

CHAPTER V.

VERS. 1.-- If a soul shall have sinned, etc. (AUG. quaest. 1 in Levit.) He seems to say that a man sins, etc., 309Aas far as but he straightway subjoined: The soul that shall have touched anything unclean, etc.

(ISICH.) If he shall have sinned, etc. He does not repeat what he said above, etc., as far as because we ought not to consent to the sins or the error of another, but to reprove them.

VERS. 2.-- The soul who shall have touched. (ISICH.) Not of the body, etc., as far as but he can say with David: Our soul hath been delivered as a sparrow out of the snare of the fowlers (Psal. CXXIII).

(GREG. lib. XXXII Moral., cap. 4 tom. II.) To utter an oath is to bind ourselves by vow to the divine servitude, etc., as far as we burn with the fire of grief.

VERS. 4.-- The soul that shall have sworn, etc. (AUG., quaest. 2 in Levit.) What is it that for a perjury kept silent, or for a carcass touched, or for anything unclean, no sacrifice 309Bis enjoined? but that for the fact that anyone swears a falsehood unknowingly, he decrees a she-lamb or a she-goat to be offered? or is this sacrifice to be understood for all the things aforesaid? For when all had been set forth, he brought in the sacrifice of expiation.

He shall have understood his offence. (ISICH.) It is to be noted that an offence, etc., as far as as the following words show.

VERS. 7.-- But if he shall not have been able, etc. (ID.) Let us add this, that if a prince shall have sinned in any of these things, he should offer a he-goat of the goats, namely a penance worthy of himself, who ought neither to have sinned, nor to have shared it with others, nor to have provoked his subjects to sinning. What therefore ought not to have happened, he left undiscussed.

But if, etc. (ID.) He who cannot afflict himself continually, etc., as far as because he sacrifices himself most fully 309Cto God.

VERS. 8.-- He shall turn back the head (GREG., hom. in Ezech. ) The death of Christ unto the joining of his own body, etc., as far as he joined us all to himself more truly in that very death of his.

(GREG.) He shall turn back, etc. In the sacrifice of God we ought to be a turtledove, that our head may be turned back to the little wings, that is, the senses to the virtues. By the head we understand the mind, because as the head rules the body, so the mind rules the actions. But the head cleaves to the neck, and is not cut off utterly, because our mind is to be separated from the delight of the flesh, not from the necessary care of the flesh, and let us not make provision for the flesh in its desires (Rom. XIII). Let the head of the turtledove therefore be partly cut off, and partly cleave to the neck.

309DLet it be broken off, etc. He wills that we should not serve the body, but be broken off or turned back, lest the law of sin reign in our members. But because we ought not to hold our body in hatred, nor to reckon it, after the heretics, a making of the devil, but to set it under the mind, he does not command the body to be separated from the neck, for both are the creature of one and the same.

VERS. 9.-- The foundation, etc. LXX, the base, which is the Gospel, because, according to the intention of the sacrifice of Christ and of the Gospel, the sacrifice for sins ought to be offered. Whence he adds: Because it is for sin, for nothing so snatches a man from sin as the imitation of Christ and obedience to the Gospel preaching.

310ABut if he shall not have been able. Because not every time is easy for penance, when namely we abuse penance itself, and destroy its fruit by returning to sin, the lawgiver hints at this, saying: But if his hand shall not have been able, etc.

(AUG. quaest. 8 in Levit., tom. III.) It is asked whether what has been said is to be taken everywhere, etc., as far as that all the rest should pertain from these to that generality.

VERS. 11.-- An ephi. (ISICH.) It is a measure of three measures, etc., as far as that they should not perish who shall have been under his hand.

He shall not put upon it, etc. As if to say: this is not perfect penance, and therefore it has no oil, that is, the spiritual gladness of cheerfulness, nor frankincense, because neither 310Bought he to have confidence in his prayers: but let him who offers the oblation hand it over to the priest, that is, let him commit the hope of salvation to the divine mercy.

Which is for sin. Because he knows not whether the prayer of him who often transgresses is of a good odour. Whence: Who knoweth if God will turn and forgive, and leave behind him a blessing (Joan. III)? He does not therefore suffer them to despair, nor yet to presume, lest they be negligent again.

VERS. 12.-- A handful. In the handful Scripture is signified, wherewith it is written. And it is written: I say not to thee seven times, but unto seventy times seven times (Matth. XXVIII). By the handful therefore he hints that God is to be entreated, that he may remember his written mercy, that he may grant it to us: as he bids it be granted to our brethren; for this 310Cshall the great priest cleanse the one who prays.

VERS. 13.-- If a soul transgressing, etc. (ISICH.) From every sin abstinence must be had, but most of all from that which is committed against holy things: and how horrible it is, the oblation testifies. For nowhere from the beginning of the history, save in this place, is a ram commanded to be offered, in which the necessity and the usefulness of the sacrifice is set forth: here no he-goat is offered, no she-goat, no sheep, no pair of turtledoves, or two young pigeons.

The ceremonies, through, etc. (ORIG.) There are many kinds of sanctification. There is the oil, wherewith the tabernacle alone is anointed; and the incense, of which it is said: Whosoever shall make such a compound, shall perish from among his people: the loaves also of proposition only the males eat, who are of the stock of Aaron. But mystically he sins in 310Dthose things which have been sanctified, who usurps anything irreverently in a holy place, or handles divine things unworthily.

VERS. 15.-- With two sicles. Two sicles are the two precepts of charity, wherewith the sinner buys for himself the confidence of remission. For penance is worth nothing without the grace of remission; whence, according to the LXX, the ram is bought with fifty sicles, because through penance and the observance of the twofold charity perfect rest is acquired, when the remission of sins is given. LXX, with fifty sicles of silver, by the holy sicle. For no one obtains the sacrifice of the Lord save through the Holy Spirit, because no man can say the Lord Jesus, but by the Holy Ghost (I Cor. XII). But fifty 311Asignifies the coming of the Holy Spirit, because on the day of Pentecost, that is, the fiftieth, he came to the apostles. LXX: by the holy sicle. In the holy sicle he signified the power and the working of the Holy Spirit. A siculus is of 20 obols, a man of twenty is not liable for sin: whence they who went out of Egypt, when they were of twenty years, did not undergo the punishment of the people's sin (Num. XIV); for the spiritual man is indeed clean from every sin.

VERS. 16.-- And the fifth part, etc. (ISICH.) He who with a holy vessel or food, etc., as far as for so shall he make entreaty for our sin.

VERS. 17.-- If a soul shall have sinned, etc. (ID.). He seems to have forgotten what he had commanded: for the sins for which he commanded a she-goat or a sheep to be offered, now he seems to command a ram to be offered; but he has neither forgotten 311Bthe former things, nor commanded contrary things: for there by the soul he signified the Gentile: but here him who through ignorance transgresses the commandments of the law.

(AUG. quaest. 7 in Levit.) It is asked how this offence is distinguished from those which he has generally embraced, etc., as far as or some things in sacred services.

VERS. 18.-- He shall offer a ram, etc. Above he commanded that the penitent should offer a she-goat, here he bids a ram be immolated, because it profits nothing to immolate the harshness of penance, unless thou shalt have determined to have full confidence in the immolation of Christ.

CAPUT VI

VERS. 1, 2.-- Locutus est, etc. Anima quae peccaverit, 311Cetc. (ISICH.) Idem peccatum est quod in sancta, etc., usque ad simile est rapinae.

(ORIG. hom. 4 in Levit.) Anima quae peccaverit, etc. Ego puto quod animam nostram, etc., usque ad: Nemo enim mittit vinum novum in utres veteres.

Rem perditam, etc. (ORIG.) LXX: perditionem. Laborant haeretici in Scripturis perditionem invenire, unde possint errorem astruere: sed forte ecclesiastica verba audientes resipiscunt, et quod invenerant, perditionem esse intelligunt et reddunt, id est, auctori suo dimittunt.

VERS. 5.-- Et quintam insuper partem, etc. Linguam, scilicet in qua unus de quinque sensibus: haec offertur, ut de caetero non peccet, quia per ipsam unumquodque praedictorum commissum est.

311D(ORIG.) LXX: non quintam partem, sed quintas, ut bis quinque, etc., usque ad unum Deum referamus.

VERS. 8.-- Locutusque est Dominus ad Moysen, dicens. (ISICH.) Hic de jugi sacrificio loquitur, etc., usque ad et totam vitam suam ad perfectionem componat.

VERS. 10.-- Lineis, non laneis, tunicam et feminalia linea vult esse, non lanea (Exod. XXVIII). Vestis enim linea habilis et mollis est, et adhaerens corpori; lanea autem aspera, quia habiles multumque cohaerentes sunt animae justitia et castitas. Linea quoque vestis facile potest haberi, sic et virtutes, si quis voluerit eas consequi.

312ATolletque cineres, etc. Cinis consummatio boni operis. Cineres extra castra efferet Aaron, qui post hanc vitam bona opera per Christum pontificem magnum Deo Patri praesentant. Et ibi in favillam rediguntur, dum assistit vultui Dei pro nobis. Castra enim sunt praesens vita, in qua semper contra spirituales nequitias pugnamus (Heb. IX). Juxta altare ponuntur, dum consummatio bonorum operum in arca cordis reconditur. Altare enim in hoc loco cor significat. Gloria nostra haec est testimonium conscientiae, etc. ( Eph. ult. )

VERS. 11.-- Indutusque aliis. (ORIG.) Observandum quod aliis indumentis utitur sacerdos in sacrificiorum mysterio, et aliis quando procedit ad populum. Unde: Sapientiam loquimur inter perfectos 312B(II Cor. I). Et alibi: longe inferiori stola indutus, nihil judicavi, inquit, me scire inter vos, nisi Jesum Christum, et hunc crucifixum (II Cor. I).

VERS. 12.-- Ignis in. (AUG. quaest. 12 in Levit.) Non vult ignem prorsus exstingui, sed cum usque mane arserit holocaustum, ablatis reliquis iterum renovati, quo ardeant alia quae imponuntur.

Quem nutriet sacer. Allegorice. Mane judicatus est Christus, vespere crucifixus. Ignis ergo mane et vespere subjicitur: quia charitas Dei in nobis nutritur, dum Christum mane judicatum, vespere crucifixum, recolimus.

(GREG. lib. XXV Moral., cap. 7.) Altare Dei cor nostrum est, etc., usque ad ut quo magis videtur Deus, magis diligatur.

312CEt imposito holocausto desuper adolebit, etc. (AUG., quaest. 13 in Levit.) Videndum est utrum nullus dies praetermitteretur, etc., usque ad quod mirum est si taceret, nec admoneret talia vespere offerri.

VERS. 14.-- Haec est. Videtur legislator iterare quae dixit, memoriam renovans repetitione mandati, et ostendens utilitatem et necessitatem legis. Interponit tamen aliquid novi in quo mandatum necessarium et inexcusabile innuit. Sicut ergo de holocaustis sacrificia iteravit, permiscens eis assiduitatem, ut est illud: Ignis in altari semper ardeat et nunquam deficiat: sic in sacrificio similae, id est theologiae addit, quia oportet in loco tantum sancto, et a masculo comedi.

Et coram. Secundum quod Christus revelaverit, 312Dqui est verum altare. In his ergo theologiam constituamus quae nobis tradidit Christus incarnatus, ut Patrem in eo, et ipsum in Patre cogitemus, et quia Spiritus est Filii et a Patre procedit.

VERS. 16.-- Reliquam, etc., ut non ex toto a theologia cessemus, quia summa scientiae in futurum conversatur.

In loco. Ecclesia, quae est introitus et vestibulum coeli, in ea oportet de divinitate loqui, unde psal. LXVII: In ecclesiis benedicite Deo, est quisque sibi domestica ecclesia, dum a terrenis negotiis cessat: hic sibi de Deo loqui et theologiam exercere potest. Tales sunt quibus pars sacrificii datur, de Deo, scilicet, meditari et loqui.

313AVERS. 17.-- Sicut pro peccato. Simile innuit hoc sacrificium ei, quod et pro peccato et pro delicto: sermo enim de Deo maxime ad expiationem et ad emundationem animae habetur, quando oleo et thuri, id est, eleemosynae et orationi, sacrificium verbi Dei, id est, theologiae, conjunguntur.

VERS. 18.-- Mares tantum. Non omnium est de Deo disputare, sed eorum qui nihil femineum, nihil fluxum in cogitationibus vel operibus habent. Legitimum ac sempiternum est. De Deo enim disputare praesumere non debemus, dum per saeculares phantasias a masculorum dignitate declinamus.

Omnis qui tetigerit. (ISICH.) Hoc ad litteram stare non potest: nunquid enim si tetigerit homicida aut profanus sanctificabitur? nullus enim excipitur. Sed 313BChristus, in cujus typo haec praecesserunt, immolatur: hujus carnem qui tetigerit, id est, qui fideli corde totam spem salutis in passione ejus et resurrectione posuerit, continuo sanctificabitur.

(ID.) Omnis qui, etc. Omnis commemoratio Dei sanctificationem praestat. Unde: Sanctificatur autem per verbum Dei et orationem. Sed cum dicit, tangentem sanctificari, plane ostendit quia disputando de Deo, id est, theologizando, sufficit nobis tantum tangere: qui enim vult calefieri, si manum in ignem miserit, pro calefactione adustionem sustinebit.

Haec est oblatio. (AUG., quaest. 14 in Levit.) Alia sunt sacrificia quae commemoravit in Exod., quibus per septem dies sanctificantur sacerdotes, ut fungantur 313Csacerdotio. Aliud est quod commemorat, quod offerat summus sacerdos, quando constituitur, id est quando ungitur: unde addit: In die qua unxerit eum, non eos, cum etiam secundos sacerdotes ungi praecipiat; hoc sacrificium est sempiternum per successionem, scilicet sacerdotum. Vel quia quod signat est sempiternum.

Haec est, etc. Aaron mons fortitudinis, id est, Christus; filii ejus omnes fideles; dies unctionis, tempus incarnationis, quo unctus est oleo laetitiae prae participibus suis.

Ephi. Christi humanitatem. Ephi quidem tres modios capiens significat Trinitatem, si tamen ephi Domini caro est, ut demonstretur divinitatis eminentia, et Dominicae carnis cum ea inseparabilitas, nihilominus 313Dsacrificium perpetuum est. Una enim oblatione consummavit in sempiternum sanctificatos: unde et salvare in perpetuum potest.

In sacrificio. (ORIG.) Lex Moysi sacrificium est sacerdotis, etc., usque ad in quo nihil sit remissum, nihil luxuriosum (Matth. XVI).

Mane. Mane coepit passio Domini ex quo in atrio Caiphae alapis caesus est, et usque ad vesperum protelata est, tenebris enim eam interpolantibus, a sexta hora usque ad horam nonam in mane et vespere dividitur.

VERS. 21.-- Quae in sartagine. (GREG. hom. 12 in Ezech.) Nullum Deo tale sacrificium est, etc., usque ad holocaustum Deo deputatur.

VERS. 22.-- Sacerdos. LXX: Sacerdos unctus pro 314Aeo ex filiis ejus faciet ea. Christus enim pro Aaron est, non in loco ejus, quia transtulit in se sacerdotium, et abstulit Aaron. Ex filiis ejus, quia de Jacob per Mariam descendit et ex eadem tribu.

Qui patri. Unus Dominus Jesus Christus, per quem omnia (Rom. XI). Dominus enim gloriae crucifixus est, et incarnati Verbi passio est, quae fuit in carne Christi: Qui semetipsum totum exinanivit, formam servi accipiens et obediens patri usque ad mortem crucis.

VERS. 23.-- Omne enim. Quaecunque in sacrificio pacificorum in hoc quoque jubet fieri.

Nec quisquam. Quod comeditur comminuitur: sacrificium Christi comminuit, qui pro peccatis mortuum dicit, cum peccatum non fecerit.

314B(ORIG.) Nec quisquam comedet. Sacrificium in hoc loco Verbum ipsum accipiendum est, et doctrina, de qua nullus edit, id est, disputat vel retractat; sed holocaustum est. Quidquid enim dixit, quidquid statuit, aeterna consecratione perdurat.

Sacerdos qui, etc. (AUG. quaest. 18 in Levit.) Non quod imponit, etc., usque ad hoc et modo breviter commemorat.

(ORIG. homil. 5 in Levit.) Quaedam hostiarum sunt solius Dei, etc., usque ad melius ista igni reservamus.

VERS. 27.-- Quidquid tetigerit. Quamvis oblatio sit alterius, id est poenitentis, sanctificabitur etsi non poeniteat. Similiter si parvulus baptizatus fuerit, 314Cqui per se nondum peccavit, mundatur ab originali

Si de sanguine, id est, si substantia carnis peccatis fuerit maculata. Unde: Vestimentum mistum sanguine erit in combustione, et cibus ignis (Isa. IX) infernalis, scilicet lavabitur in loco sancto, id est spem habebit in baptismo, qui non solum praeterita abluit, sed instruit ut de futuris poenitere possit.

VERS. 28.-- Vas autem fictile, etc. Infirmus: scilicet, imbecillis, qui peracta poenitentia relabitur ad peccata, his confringendus est terrore judicii.

VERS. 29.-- Omnis masculus, etc. Sacerdos integrum sacrificium offerens et comedens, Christus est, qui nostram poenitentiam offert, et comedit peccatorum salutem. Carnes autem sacrificii, quia pertinent 314Dad poenitentiam, omnis masculus de sacerdotibus comedit, fortis, scilicet et strenuus, cui dicitur: Viriliter age, et confortetur cor tuum, et sustine Dominum (Psal. XXVI).

VERS. 30.-- Non comedetur, etc. Quia ad Christum pertinet, qui propria auctoritate, et propria divinitatis virtute remissionem tribuit. Ejus enim passio, per quam nobis procuratur remissio, non fuit hominis puri, sed Dei incarnati. Ideo non editur ab hominibus, sed igne consumitur, quia remissionem dare Dei solius est, qui per ignem significatur, Deus enim noster ignis consumens est (Deut. IV).

CHAPTER VI

VERS. 1, 2.-- He spoke, etc. The soul that shall have sinned, 311Cetc. (ISICH.) It is the same sin which is committed against holy things, etc., as far as it is like to robbery.

(ORIG. hom. 4 in Levit.) The soul that shall have sinned, etc. I think that our soul, etc., as far as: For no man putteth new wine into old bottles.

The thing lost, etc. (ORIG.) LXX: perdition. The heretics labour to find perdition in the Scriptures, whence they may build up their error: but perhaps, hearing the words of the Church, they come to their senses, and understand that what they had found is perdition, and restore it, that is, they let it go back to its own author.

VERS. 5.-- And the fifth part besides, etc. The tongue, namely, in which is one of the five senses: this is offered, that for the rest he may not sin, because through it each one of the things aforesaid was committed.

311D(ORIG.) LXX: not the fifth part, but fifths, as twice five, etc., as far as we may refer them to the one God.

VERS. 8.-- And the Lord spoke to Moses, saying. (ISICH.) Here he speaks of the continual sacrifice, etc., as far as and let him frame his whole life unto perfection.

VERS. 10.-- Of linen, not of wool: he wills the tunic and the drawers to be of linen, not of wool (Exod. XXVIII). For a linen garment is fitting and soft, and clinging to the body; but a woollen one is rough, because justice and chastity are fitting to the soul and cleave much to it. A linen garment also can easily be had, so also the virtues, if anyone shall wish to attain them.

312AAnd he shall take up the ashes, etc. The ash is the consummation of a good work. Aaron shall carry the ashes outside the camp: they who after this life present their good works to God the Father through Christ the great high priest. And there they are reduced to embers, while he stands before the face of God for us. For the camp is the present life, in which we always fight against spiritual wickednesses (Heb. IX). They are placed beside the altar, while the consummation of good works is laid up in the ark of the heart. For the altar in this place signifies the heart. Our glory is this, the testimony of our conscience, etc. ( Eph. ult. )

VERS. 11.-- And being clothed with others. (ORIG.) It is to be observed that the priest uses certain garments in the mystery of the sacrifices, and others when he goes forth to the people. Whence: We speak wisdom among the perfect 312B(II Cor. I). And elsewhere, clothed in a far lower robe: Nothing did I judge, he says, myself to know among you, but Jesus Christ, and him crucified (II Cor. I).

VERS. 12.-- Fire on. (AUG. quaest. 12 in Levit.) He does not will the fire to be wholly extinguished, but that, when the holocaust shall have burned until morning, the remains being taken away, it be again renewed, whereby the other things which are laid on may burn.

Which the priest shall feed. Allegorically. In the morning Christ was judged, in the evening crucified. Fire therefore is laid under morning and evening: because the charity of God is nourished in us, while we call to mind Christ judged in the morning and crucified in the evening.

(GREG. lib. XXV Moral., cap. 7.) Our heart is the altar of God, etc., as far as that the more God is seen, the more he may be loved.

312CAnd the holocaust being laid on above he shall burn it, etc. (AUG., quaest. 13 in Levit.) It is to be seen whether no day was passed over, etc., as far as which is a wonder, if he were silent and did not admonish that such things be offered in the evening.

VERS. 14.-- This is. The lawgiver seems to repeat what he has said, renewing the memory by the repetition of the commandment, and showing the usefulness and the necessity of the law. Yet he inserts something new, in which he hints that the commandment is necessary and inexcusable. As therefore he repeated the sacrifices concerning the holocausts, mingling assiduity with them, as is that: Let the fire on the altar always burn and never fail: [Vulg. Lv 6:12–13 has the futures ardebit … numquam deficiet, "shall always burn … shall never fail"] so in the sacrifice of fine flour, that is of theology, he adds that it must be eaten only in a holy place, and by a male.

And before. According as Christ shall have revealed, 312Dwho is the true altar. In these things therefore let us establish theology which Christ incarnate delivered to us, that we may think of the Father in him, and of him in the Father, and that the Spirit is of the Son and proceeds from the Father.

VERS. 16.-- The remainder, etc., that we may not wholly cease from theology, because the sum of knowledge is conversant with the world to come.

In the place. The Church, which is the entrance and the vestibule of heaven; in it one must speak of the Godhead, whence in Psalm LXVII: In the churches bless ye God, each man is a household church to himself, while he ceases from earthly business: here he can speak to himself of God and exercise theology. Such are they to whom a part of the sacrifice is given, namely, to meditate and to speak of God.

313AVERS. 17.-- As for sin. He hints that this sacrifice is like to that which is both for sin and for offence: for discourse concerning God is had chiefly unto the expiation and the cleansing of the soul, when to oil and frankincense, that is, to almsgiving and prayer, the sacrifice of the word of God, that is, of theology, is joined.

VERS. 18.-- The males only. It belongs not to all to dispute of God, but to those who have nothing womanish, nothing flowing, in their thoughts or their works. It is lawful and everlasting. For we ought not to presume to dispute of God, while through worldly imaginings we turn aside from the dignity of males.

Everyone that shall touch. (ISICH.) This cannot stand according to the letter: for shall a murderer or a profane man be sanctified if he shall have touched? for none is excepted. But 313BChrist, in whose figure these things went before, is immolated: he who shall have touched his flesh, that is, who with a faithful heart shall have placed the whole hope of salvation in his passion and resurrection, shall straightway be sanctified.

(ID.) Everyone who, etc. Every commemoration of God bestows sanctification. Whence: But it is sanctified by the word of God and prayer. But when he says that the one touching is sanctified, he plainly shows that in disputing of God, that is, in theologizing, it is enough for us only to touch: for he who wishes to be warmed, if he shall have put his hand into the fire, shall suffer burning instead of warming.

This is the oblation. (AUG., quaest. 14 in Levit.) Other are the sacrifices which he commemorated in Exodus, by which through seven days the priests are sanctified, that they may discharge 313Cthe priesthood. Another is that which he commemorates, which the high priest offers when he is appointed, that is, when he is anointed: whence he adds: In the day in which he shall anoint him, not "them," although he commands the second priests also to be anointed; this sacrifice is everlasting by succession, namely of priests. Or because that which it signifies is everlasting.

This is, etc. Aaron is the mountain of strength, that is, Christ; his sons are all the faithful; the day of anointing, the time of the incarnation, in which he was anointed with the oil of gladness above his fellows.

An ephi. The humanity of Christ. An ephi indeed, holding three measures, signifies the Trinity, if however the ephi is the flesh of the Lord, so that the eminence of the divinity may be shown, and the inseparability of the Lord's flesh with it; none the less 313Dthe sacrifice is perpetual. For by one oblation he hath perfected for ever them that are sanctified: whence also he is able to save for ever.

In the sacrifice. (ORIG.) The law of Moses is the sacrifice of the priest, etc., as far as in which there is nothing slack, nothing wanton (Matth. XVI).

In the morning. In the morning the passion of the Lord began, from when in the hall of Caiphas he was smitten with blows, and it was drawn out even to the evening; for since the darkness interposed, from the sixth hour to the ninth hour it is divided into morning and evening.

VERS. 21.-- Which in the frying-pan. (GREG. hom. 12 in Ezech.) No such sacrifice is to God, etc., as far as it is reckoned a holocaust to God.

VERS. 22.-- The priest. LXX: The anointed priest for 314Ahim, of his sons, shall do these things. For Christ is for Aaron, not in his place, because he transferred the priesthood into himself, and took it away from Aaron. Of his sons, because he descended from Jacob through Mary and from the same tribe.

Who to the father. One Lord Jesus Christ, through whom are all things (Rom. XI). For the Lord of glory was crucified, and it is the passion of the incarnate Word, which was in the flesh of Christ: Who wholly emptied himself, taking the form of a servant, and obedient to the father even unto the death of the cross. [Vulg. Phil 2:7–8 has neither totum, "wholly," nor patri, "to the father," and reads factus obediens usque ad mortem, mortem autem crucis]

VERS. 23.-- For every. Whatsoever things are in the sacrifice of the peace offerings, these also he bids to be done herein.

Nor shall any one. What is eaten is broken small: he breaks small the sacrifice of Christ who says that he died for sins, whereas he did not commit sin.

314B(ORIG.) Nor shall any one eat. The sacrifice in this place is to be taken as the Word himself, and the doctrine, of which none eats, that is, disputes or handles it over again; but it is a holocaust. For whatsoever he said, whatsoever he appointed, endures by an eternal consecration.

The priest who, etc. (AUG. quaest. 18 in Levit.) Not that he lays on, etc., as far as this too he now briefly commemorates.

(ORIG. homil. 5 in Levit.) Certain of the victims are of God alone, etc., as far as better do we reserve these things for the fire.

VERS. 27.-- Whatsoever shall touch. Although the oblation be another's, that is, the penitent's, it shall be sanctified even if he do not repent. Likewise if a little one shall have been baptized, 314Cwho has not yet sinned of himself, he is cleansed from the original

If of the blood, that is, if the substance of the flesh shall have been stained with sins. Whence: A garment mingled with blood shall be for burning, and food for the fire (Isa. IX) of hell, namely: it shall be washed in a holy place, that is, he shall have hope in baptism, which not only washes away things past, but instructs him that he may be able to repent of things to come.

VERS. 28.-- But the earthen vessel, etc. The infirm: namely, the feeble, who after penance is performed slips back to sins; he must be shattered by these, by the terror of judgment.

VERS. 29.-- Every male, etc. The priest offering and eating the whole sacrifice is Christ, who offers our penance and eats the salvation of sinners. But the flesh of the sacrifice, because it pertains 314Dto penance, every male among the priests eats, the strong, namely, and the vigorous, to whom it is said: Do manfully, and let thy heart be strengthened, and wait thou for the Lord (Psal. XXVI).

VERS. 30.-- It shall not be eaten, etc. Because it pertains to Christ, who by his own authority and by the proper power of his divinity grants remission. For his passion, through which remission is procured for us, was not that of a mere man, but of God incarnate. Therefore it is not eaten by men, but is consumed by fire, because to give remission belongs to God alone, who is signified by fire, for our God is a consuming fire (Deut. IV).

CAPUT VII.

  • 315A

VERS. 1.-- Delicto. (ISICH.) Delictum aliquando dicit, etc., usque ad oblatio autem renum et pinnulae epatis et femorum sanctimonia est.

(ORIG. quaest. 5 in Levit.) Quidam dicunt esse delictum cum non facimus quod debemus: peccatum vero cum non facimus quod non debemus, sed non ubique hoc observatur.

VERS. 2.-- Sanguis ejus, etc. (ISICH.) Quaecunque fiunt in sacrificio pacificorum, in hoc quoque jubet fieri.

VERS. 7.-- Sicut pro peccato, etc. (AUG., quaest. 20 in Levit.) Peccatum est perpetratio mali, delictum desertio boni. Sicut enim aliud est declinare a malo, 315Baliudque facere bonum, ita et aliud abstinere a bono, et aliud facere malum, et illud delictum hoc peccatum sit. Vel delictum est quod ignoranter, peccatum quod scienter committitur.

(ORIG., ubi supra. ) Sicut pro peccato, etc. Similiter jubentur adipes arietis, usque ad id est, quod a via peccati peccatorem convertunt.

Ad sacerdotem, etc. Christum, qui pro nobis propitiatio factus est. In ipso ergo omne sacrificium ministratur, et agitur, et quod ex poenitentia consequitur, ad eum refertur. Ipse enim, ut nos salvaret, venit dicens: Non veni vocare justos, sed peccatores (Matth. IX).

Holocausti victimam. (ISICH.) Non bonum scilicet aut ovium, etc., usque ad cujus respectu patientiam 315Chabemus in passionibus.

VERS. 9.-- Habebit pellem. Circumstantiam divitiarum, quas debent offerre vero sacerdoti, qui volunt holocaustum fieri. Sathan circumstantia divitiarum pellem nominavit, dicens: Pellem pro pelle, et omnia quae habet homo, dabit pro anima sua (Job II). Hanc dedit Job ut pellem, id est patientiam retineret, quae pellis dicitur ex qua fiunt multa utilia.

Et quidquid in craticula, etc. Quia haec theologiae incarnationis proxima sunt, necessario offerentis sacerdotis sunt, id est Christi nati, passi, et qui resurrexit.

VERS. 10.-- Sive oleo conspersa, etc. (ORIG. hom. 5 in Levit.) Sacrificium salutare jubetur fieri in oleo etc., usque ad alter in sartagine, tertius in craticula.

315D(ISICH.) Conspersum oleo sacrificium de simila dicit, etc., usque ad filiis Aaron deputavit.

VERS. 12.-- Si pro gratiarum actione fuerit oblatio. Ad litteram pro gratiarum actione fit oblatio, cum quis de magna tribulatione liberatus gratias agit, sicut Moyses post transitum maris Rubri (Exod. XV). Mystice quoque, qui majora crimina non commiserit, Dei misericordiae laudes persolvit, qui se immunem ab horrendis criminibus custodivit.

Et lagana azyma uncta oleo. Panem, scilicet tenuem et latum, qui scientiam prophetarum significat. Quantum autem distat inter soliditatem panis et teneritudinem lagani, tantum inter scientiam veteris et Novi Testamenti; panes ergo sparguntur oleo, lagana 316Aunguntur, quia majus est lumen et gratia Novi Testamenti quam veteris.

Coctamque similam, et collyridas olei, etc. Divinam scilicet sapientiam, quae coquitur igne charitatis. Nam Christus Dei sapientia propter nimiam charitatem se tradit morti.

VERS. 14.-- Offeretur Domino, et erit sacerdotis, etc. LXX: Et offeret de omnibus muneribus suis laudationem Dei sacerdoti profundenti sanguinem salutaris illi erit. Non enim volentis neque currentis, sed Dei miserentis est (Rom. IX). Christo autem ministranti et perficienti sacrificium primitiae offerendae sunt.

VERS. 15.-- Cujus carnes eadem comedentur die, etc. (ORIG., ubi supra.) Alius animae cibus est lactis, 316Betc., usque ad: Sint, inquit, nova et vetera.

Usque mane. Futurum saeculum, ubi erit verus dies qui nocti hujus saeculi succedit, non vult nostram vitam imperfectam relinqui per quam nos oportet salvari: in futuro enim non licebit operari.

VERS. 16.-- Si voto vel sponte, etc. Omne sacrificium pacificorum, vel salutare spontaneum est, unde inter dona superius positum est. Sed gratiarum actionis vel laudis sacrificium, quamvis species ejus sit, quia rationabile est, voluntarium non dicitur: quia, cum rationales sumus, debitum quodammodo praecipuum Creatori debemus rationabile sacrificium.

Eadem similiter. Quia quidam post hujus vitae finem aliam talem descripserunt, introducentes quoddam 316Csaeculum mille annorum in quo Jerusalem aedificatur et templum ejus, et sancta sanctorum restaurabuntur, et sacrificia offerentur. Sed si quis hoc credit, et quodammodo in mente comedit, sperans se eo tempore comedere, quod est impossibile, oblatio ejus non suscipitur, quia polluta, peccatum quoque contrahit, unde ait: Quaecunque anima tali se edulio contaminaverit, etc.

Sed et si quid, etc. (ORIG., ubi supra. ) Duos dies, quibus licet vesci carnibus sacrificii, etc., usque ad non ergo assumam nobis cunctorum scientiam.

VERS. 17.-- Tertius invenerit dies. Futurum saeculum in quo generalis resurrectio, quam Christi resurrectio significavit. Mane facto tertii diei, tunc quod reliquum est igne consumetur: quodcunque 316Denim salutare imperfectum reliquerimus, aut malum fecerimus, tanquam ligna, fenum, aut stipula, igne consumetur: nos autem detrimentum patiemur (I Cor. III).

VERS. 19.-- Caro quae, etc. (ORIG., hom. 5 in Levit.) Carnes sanctae divina verba intelliguntur, usque ad et aliena immunditia pollutus est.

VERS. 21.-- Vel jumenti. Jumenta immunda sunt qui immundis et terrenis actionibus intenti sunt, unde fornicatores equi emissarii dicti sunt.

VERS. 23.-- Adipem ovis, etc. Idest nec fortis, nec innocentis, nec poenitentis, bona intentio vel desiderium sinistra interpretatione depravandum tanquam per hypocrisim sit factum, ne quis velit meritum 317Aalterius exterminare, et quasi comedendo imminuere.

(AUG., quaest. 21 in Levit. tom. III.) Dixit superius, etc., usque ad sibi elegerunt mortem peccati.

VERS. 24.-- In varios usus. Non solum bonorum imitando opera, sed etiam malorum nobis in exemplum trahendo. Legimus enim Herodem natalis sui diem celebrasse, et epulas sanguine foedasse (Matth. XIV): tale desiderium in usum habemus ut caveamus, sed econtrario nostri natalis diem quo in paradiso fuimus, cum luctu et afflictione memoremus. Pharisaeus superbiens ait: Gratias ago tibi, quia non sum sicut caeteri homines, etc. (Luc. XVIII.) Nos econtrario dicamus: Deus, miserere mei, quia non sum similis servis tuis, qui angelicam ducunt 317Bvitam in terris.

VERS. 25.-- Si quis adipem, etc. (ORIG.) Quasi: nemo scandalizet unum ex his minimis, qui in me credunt, etc. Sanguis comedi prohibetur, quasi, ne glorieris adversus fractos ramos, ne tu excidaris, et illi, si non permanserint in incredulitate, inserantur (Rom. XI). Sanguis autem Judaeorum populus recte intelligitur: non enim ex fide neque ex spiritu Abrahae, sed tantum de sanguine ejus descendit.

VERS. 29.-- Qui offert victimam. (ORIG. hom. 5 in Levit.) Alia littera, etc., usque ad et salutis suae conscius gratias Domino refert.

(ID., ibid. ) Sacrificium quod dicitur salutare, etc., usque ad qui portant vasa Domini.

VERS. 30.-- Tenebit manibus, etc. (HIERON., epist. 317Cad Fabiolam, tom. I.) De victimis salutaribus adipem offerunt sacerdotes, etc., usque ad ut discat contemnere quae se videt in secessum projicere.

LXX: Offerens sacrificium salutare Domino, offeret munus suum Domino de sacrificio salutari ejus. Quod exponens adjungit: Tenebit manibus adipem hostiae. Vel juxta LXX: manus ejus offerent ex ea Domino: Ipsum enim oportet quae ad salutem suam pertinent operari: quia justitia justi super eum erit, et impietas impii erit super eum (Ezech. XVIII).

Pectusculum autem erit, etc. (ISID.) Pectus datur sacerdoti, ut cogitationem mundam et doctrinae habeat notitiam. Brachium dextrum, ut bona opera habeat, et ad pugnam contra diabolum armata manu procedat, et quod corde conceperit, operum exemplo 317Dperficiat. Datur et de privato maxilla, ut eloquentia polleat: datur et venter, ut luxuriam exstinguat, et gulam contemnat.

VERS. 32.-- Sacerdotis. (AUG.) Christi, in sacerdote figurati. Unde solus sacerdos potest sacrificium hoc perficere. Incipere enim bonum nostrum est: perficere et ad finem ducere, divinae gratiae est. Non est volentis neque currentis, sed Dei miserentis dirigere actiones: unde et brachium Dei nominatur. Unde recte armum vel, juxta LXX: brachium accepit.

VERS. 33.-- Qui obtulerit sanguinem, etc. Christus est, qui verum sanguinem pacificorum et adipem, animas scilicet et desideria salutarium offert, qui est 318Ade filiis Aaron sacerdos scilicet singularis et in omnibus praecipuus, cui cedit armus dexter in portionem, id est, omnis actio bona et perfecta.

VERS. 35.-- Haec est unctio. (ISICH.) Videntur ergo haec ad unctionem sacerdotum pertinere, etc., usque ad quid autem per praedictam unctionem intellexerit in sequentibus ostendit.

VERS. 37.-- Ista est lex holocausti, etc. (ORIG., hom. 5.) Cum proposuerit legislator, etc., usque ad in qua figurarum veritas conservatur.

Ista est lex, etc. (ISICH.) Vides differentes conversationes quae Deo conjungunt, honorem scilicet, sapientiae perfectum, qui significatur per holocaustum; vel justitiam mediocrem, quae operatur ea quae sunt ad salutem. Sacrificia enim pacificorum vel per holocaustum 318Bvel per oblationem recte esse utilia ad sacerdotalem dignitatem lex intellexit. Unde in die qua legem unctionis mandavit, subdidit ea quae sunt de sacrificiis simulque omnia breviter memoravit.

VERS. 28.-- In monte Sinai. Qui rubus vel tentatio interpretatur: similis est autem rubo tentatio: asperum enim virgultum est et difficile capitur: sic corona bonae vitae in tentationibus et tribulationibus vix habetur. Unde: Beatus vir qui sustinet tentationem, quia, cum probatus fuerit, accipiet coronam vitae (Jac., II).

CHAPTER VII.

  • 315A

VERS. 1.-- For an offence. (ISICH.) He sometimes calls it an offence, etc., as far as but the oblation of the reins and of the little fin of the liver and of the thighs is holiness.

(ORIG. quaest. 5 in Levit.) Certain men say that it is an offence when we do not do what we ought: but a sin when we do not do what we ought not, yet this is not everywhere observed.

VERS. 2.-- His blood, etc. (ISICH.) Whatsoever things are done in the sacrifice of the peace offerings, he bids to be done in this also.

VERS. 7.-- As for sin, etc. (AUG., quaest. 20 in Levit.) Sin is the perpetration of evil, an offence the desertion of good. For as it is one thing to turn aside from evil, 315Band another to do good, so also it is one thing to abstain from good, and another to do evil, and let the former be an offence, the latter a sin. Or an offence is what is committed unknowingly, a sin what is committed knowingly.

(ORIG., ubi supra. ) As for sin, etc. In like manner the fat of the ram is commanded, as far as that is, the things which turn the sinner from the way of sin.

To the priest, etc. Christ, who was made a propitiation for us. In him therefore every sacrifice is ministered and carried out, and what is obtained from penance is referred to him. For he himself, that he might save us, came saying: I came not to call the just, but sinners (Matth. IX).

The victim of the holocaust. (ISICH.) Not good, namely, or of sheep, etc., as far as in regard of whom we have 315Cpatience in sufferings.

VERS. 9.-- He shall have the skin. The compass of riches, which they ought to offer to the true priest who wish to become a holocaust. Satan named the compass of riches a skin, saying: Skin for skin, and all that a man hath he will give for his life (Job II). This Job gave, that he might keep the skin, that is, patience, which is called a skin out of which many useful things are made.

And whatsoever in the gridiron, etc. Because these things are nearest to the theology of the incarnation, they are of necessity the offering priest's, that is, of Christ born, suffering, and who rose again.

VERS. 10.-- Or sprinkled with oil, etc. (ORIG. hom. 5 in Levit.) The saving sacrifice is commanded to be made in oil, etc., as far as another in the frying-pan, a third in the gridiron.

315D(ISICH.) He calls the sacrifice of fine flour sprinkled with oil, etc., as far as he assigned to the sons of Aaron.

VERS. 12.-- If the oblation shall be for thanksgiving. According to the letter the oblation is made for thanksgiving, when anyone delivered from great tribulation gives thanks, as Moses after the passage of the Red Sea (Exod. XV). Mystically too, he who shall not have committed the greater crimes pays the praises of God's mercy, who has kept himself untouched by horrible crimes.

And unleavened wafers anointed with oil. Bread, namely, thin and broad, which signifies the knowledge of the prophets. And by as much as there is a distance between the solidity of bread and the thinness of a wafer, by so much is there between the knowledge of the Old Testament and of the New; the loaves therefore are sprinkled with oil, the wafers 316Aare anointed, because the light and the grace of the New Testament is greater than that of the Old.

And fine flour baked, and little cakes of oil, etc. The divine wisdom, namely, which is baked by the fire of charity. For Christ, the wisdom of God, on account of his exceeding charity delivered himself to death.

VERS. 14.-- It shall be offered to the Lord, and it shall be the priest's, etc. LXX: And he shall offer of all his gifts a praise of God to the priest that poureth out the blood of the peace offering; it shall be his. For it is not of him that willeth, nor of him that runneth, but of God that sheweth mercy (Rom. IX). But to Christ ministering and perfecting the sacrifice the firstfruits are to be offered.

VERS. 15.-- Whose flesh shall be eaten the same day, etc. (ORIG., ubi supra.) Another food of the soul is of milk, 316Betc., as far as: Let there be, he says, new things and old.

Until the morning. The world to come, where there will be the true day which follows upon the night of this world: he does not will our life, through which we must be saved, to be left imperfect: for in the world to come it will not be lawful to work.

VERS. 16.-- If by vow or of free will, etc. Every sacrifice of the peace offerings, or peace offering, is voluntary, whence it was set above among the gifts. But the sacrifice of thanksgiving or of praise, although it be a kind of it, because it is reasonable, is not called voluntary: because, since we are rational, we owe in a manner a chief debt to the Creator, a reasonable sacrifice.

The same things in like manner. Because certain men after the end of this life have described another such life, bringing in a certain 316Cage of a thousand years in which Jerusalem is built and its temple, and the holy of holies shall be restored, and sacrifices shall be offered. But if anyone believes this, and in a manner eats it in his mind, hoping that he shall eat at that time, which is impossible, his oblation is not received, because it is polluted, and he also contracts sin, whence he says: Whatsoever soul shall have defiled itself with such food, etc.

But if anything, etc. (ORIG., ubi supra. ) The two days, on which it is lawful to feed on the flesh of the sacrifice, etc., as far as let us not therefore take to ourselves the knowledge of all things.

VERS. 17.-- The third day shall find. The world to come, in which is the general resurrection, which the resurrection of Christ signified. When the morning of the third day is come, then what is left over shall be consumed by fire: for whatsoever 316Dpeace offering we shall have left imperfect, or evil we shall have done, as wood, hay, or stubble, shall be consumed by fire: but we shall suffer loss (I Cor. III).

VERS. 19.-- The flesh which, etc. (ORIG., hom. 5 in Levit.) By the holy flesh the divine words are understood, as far as and he is polluted by another's uncleanness.

VERS. 21.-- Or of a beast. Unclean beasts are they who are intent on unclean and earthly actions, whence fornicators are called stallion horses.

VERS. 23.-- The fat of a sheep, etc. That is, neither of the strong, nor of the innocent, nor of the penitent is the good intention or desire to be depraved by a sinister interpretation, as though it were done through hypocrisy, lest anyone wish to destroy another's merit, 317Aand as it were by eating to lessen it.

(AUG., quaest. 21 in Levit. tom. III.) He said above, etc., as far as they chose for themselves the death of sin.

VERS. 24.-- For various uses. Not only by imitating the works of the good, but also by drawing those of the wicked to ourselves for an example. For we read that Herod celebrated the day of his birth, and defiled the banquet with blood (Matth. XIV): such a desire we have in use that we may beware of it, but on the contrary let us remember with mourning and affliction the day of our birth, in which we were in paradise. The Pharisee, growing proud, said: I give thee thanks, that I am not as the rest of men, etc. (Luc. XVIII.) Let us on the contrary say: God, have mercy on me, because I am not like thy servants, who lead an angelic 317Blife on earth.

VERS. 25.-- If any man the fat, etc. (ORIG.) As if to say: let no man scandalize one of these least ones, who believe in me, etc. Blood is forbidden to be eaten, as if: boast not against the broken branches, lest thou too be cut off, and they, if they shall not have continued in unbelief, be grafted in (Rom. XI). [Vulg. Rom 11:18 has noli gloriari adversus ramos, "boast not against the branches," without fractos, and 11:23 the future inserentur, "shall be grafted in"] But by the blood the people of the Jews is rightly understood: for it descended not from the faith nor from the spirit of Abraham, but only from his blood.

VERS. 29.-- He that offereth the victim. (ORIG. hom. 5 in Levit.) Another reading, etc., as far as and, conscious of his own salvation, he renders thanks to the Lord.

(ID., ibid. ) The sacrifice which is called a peace offering, etc., as far as who bear the vessels of the Lord.

VERS. 30.-- He shall hold in his hands, etc. (HIERON., epist. 317Cad Fabiolam, tom. I.) Of the peace offerings the priests offer the fat, etc., as far as that he may learn to despise what he sees himself cast out into the privy.

LXX: He that offereth the sacrifice of peace offerings to the Lord, shall offer his gift to the Lord of his peace offering. Expounding which he adds: He shall hold in his hands the fat of the victim. Or according to the LXX: his hands shall offer of it to the Lord: for he himself must work the things which pertain to his own salvation: because the justice of the just shall be upon him, and the impiety of the impious shall be upon him (Ezech. XVIII).

But the little breast shall be, etc. (ISID.) The breast is given to the priest, that he may have a clean thought and the knowledge of doctrine. The right arm, that he may have good works, and may go forth to the battle against the devil with armed hand, and what he shall have conceived in his heart, may perfect 317Dby the example of works. There is given also of the private part the jaw, that he may be strong in eloquence: there is given also the belly, that he may extinguish wantonness, and despise gluttony.

VERS. 32.-- Of the priest. (AUG.) Of Christ, figured in the priest. Whence the priest alone can perfect this sacrifice. For to begin the good is ours: to perfect it and to bring it to its end belongs to divine grace. It is not of him that willeth, nor of him that runneth, but of God that sheweth mercy, to direct our actions: whence he is also called the arm of God. Whence rightly he received the shoulder, or, according to the LXX, the arm.

VERS. 33.-- He that hath offered the blood, etc. It is Christ, who offers the true blood of the peace offerings and the fat, that is, the souls and the desires of those who are saved, who is 318Aof the sons of Aaron, a priest namely singular and in all things chief, to whom the right shoulder falls for his portion, that is, every good and perfect action.

VERS. 35.-- This is the anointing. (ISICH.) These things therefore seem to pertain to the anointing of the priests, etc., as far as but what he understood by the aforesaid anointing he shows in what follows.

VERS. 37.-- This is the law of the holocaust, etc. (ORIG., hom. 5.) When the lawgiver shall have set forth, etc., as far as in which the truth of the figures is preserved.

This is the law, etc. (ISICH.) Thou seest the differing ways of life which join men to God, namely honour, the perfect thing of wisdom, which is signified by the holocaust; or middling justice, which works the things that are unto salvation. For the law understood that the sacrifices of the peace offerings, whether by holocaust 318Bor by oblation, are rightly useful unto the priestly dignity. Whence on the day on which he gave commandment of the law of anointing, he added the things which are concerning the sacrifices, and at once briefly recounted them all.

VERS. 28.-- In mount Sinai. Which is interpreted a bramble or a temptation: and temptation is like to a bramble: for it is a rough shrub and is grasped with difficulty: so the crown of a good life is scarcely had amid temptations and tribulations. Whence: Blessed is the man that endureth temptation, because, when he shall have been proved, he shall receive the crown of life (Jac., II). [Vulg. Jas 1:12 has suffert, "suffereth," and quoniam for quia]

CAPUT VIII.

VERS. 2.-- Duos arietes. Christi passio significatur, qui est dux populi. Bene autem duo arietes immolantur, 318Cquia Christus bis nobis mysteria suae passionis commisit: primum quando agnum mysticum in coena comedit, secundo quando eadem mysteria in panis et vini consecratione constituit.

VERS. 3.-- Et congregabis. Convocavit Moyses omnem synagogam, quamvis Dominus elegisset et praecepisset, convocatur tamen synagoga: requiritur enim in ordinando sacerdote praesentia populi, ut sciant omnes et certi sint quia qui praestantior est ex omni populo, doctior, sanctior et virtute eminentior, ille eligitur ad sacerdotium. Lavit ergo eum et induit. Quali indumento? tunica, et praecinxit eum zona. Et iterum: Vestivit eum tunicam talarem (Eccli. XLV), vel, ut alibi legimus, interiorem (II Reg. XV). Video autem duabus tunicis indutum 318Dpontificem. Jesus autem prohibet apostolos duabus tunicis indui, cum Moyses et Jesus, id est lex et Evangelium convenire debeant (Matth. X). Dicunt alii quod Jesus praecepit duas tunicas non habere, non esse contrarium legi, sed perfectius lege: sicut et cum lex homicidium vetat (Exod. XX), Jesus vero iracundiam resecat (Matth. V); et cum lex prohibet adulterium, Jesus vero concupiscentiam abscidit. Ego non intra hujus intelligentiae angustiam, pontificalia sacramenta concludo: amplius mihi aliquid ex ista forma videtur ostendi. Sciebat pontifex ille, quem tunc ordinabat Moses, quia circumcisio esset spiritualis: servabat tamen et circumcisionem carnis, quia incircumcisus esse non poterat, habeba 319Aergo duas tunicas, unam carnalis mysterii, aliam intelligentiae spiritualis. Non enim poterat esse pontifex eorum qui tunc erant, nisi carnalia sacrificia offerret, quamvis spiritualia esse debere sciret. Convenienter ergo habebat duas tunicas. Apostoli vero qui dicturi erant: Si circumcidimini, Christus nihil vobis proderit (Gal. V), et litteralem observantiam penitus reprobaturi, merito duas tunicas habere prohibentur; sufficit eis una, et haec interior, eam vero quae foris est legis tunicam nolunt, quia unam Jesus et ipsam interiorem habere permittit.

VERS. 6.-- Cumque lavisset. (HIERON., ubi supra. ) Jam tunc purgationem mundi, etc., usque ad ut quod mente concipimus, opere perpetremus.

(ORIG., hom. 6 in Levit.) Lavet te Moyses, etc., 319Busque ad quali ordine pontifex constituatur.

VERS. 7.-- Vestivit. (HIERON., ibid. ) Discamus prius communes sacerdotum vestes, etc., usque ad quia in tela non potest fieri hujusmodi opus.

(ID.) Secunda est ex lino poderis, id est talaris duplici syndone, quam Josephus vocat byssinam, haec adhaeret corpori ita arcta et strictis manicis, ut nulla in veste ruga sit, et usque ad crura descendat. Tales solent habere milites lineas quas camisias vocant, aptas membris et astrictas, ut ad omnia expediti sint. Hac sacerdotes utuntur tunica, habentes vestitorum pulchritudinem, et nudorum celeritatem, quae dicitur linea.

Tertium cingulum, serpentis pelli quam in aestate exuit simillimum, ut longum marsupium in rotundum 319Ctextum, sub tegmine cocci, purpurae, hyacinthi, et byssi: propter decorem et fortitudinem, ita polymite distinctum, ut diversos flores et gemmas artificis manu non intextas, sed additas putes. Hoc balteo inter umbilicum et pectus linea tunica stringitur, qui quatuor digitorum habens latitudinem et magna ex parte ad crura descendens, cum expeditione ad sacrificia opus est, in laevum humerum retorquetur.

(HIERON.) Quartum est rotundum, etc., usque ad statim moriturus.

(ID.) Sextum Hebraice, etc., usque ad in humeris portet.

(ID.) Septimum parvulum est, etc., usque ad et una textura videntibus putaretur.

319D(ID.) Octava est lamina aurea, etc., usque ad totamque pontificis pulchritudinem Dei nomen coronet et protegat.

Mala autem Punica et tintinnabula in inferioribus posita fulgura demonstrant et tonitrua, vel terram et aquam et omnia elementa inter se consonantia, et sic sibi perplexa, ut sint in singulis omnia.

(HIERON.) Hebraei quatuor colores, etc., usque ad providentia penetrat universa.

(ORIG., hom. 6 in Levit.) In Exodo, ubi de sacerdotalibus mandatur vestibus, etc., usque ad ne sanctum detur canibus et magaritae ponantur ante porcos (Marc. VII).

Et inducens, etc. LXX: Induit tunicam et praecinxit 320Azonam et vestivit eum epomidem. Incarnationem Christi exsequitur. Totum enim hominem suscepit, corpus scilicet et animam; tunicam terrenum appellavit, quia de terra est; epomidem coelestem, quia ὑπωρίς poderis est. Et hyacinthina, qui est color coelestis: utrumque enim suscepit, et utrumque salvavit.

(HIERON.) In tunica hyacinthina, mala Punica, et tintinnabula: quia tanta debet esse pontificis scientia, ut gressus ejus et motus et omnia vocalia sint, et veritatem, quam mente concepit, habitu resonet; quidquid agit, quidquid loquitur, doctrina sit. Sine tintinnabulis, coloribus diversis, gemmis, floribusque virtutum nec in Ecclesiam ingreditur, nec pontifex est.

320BHumerale, etc. (ORIG.) Imposuit Moyses pontifici, superhumerale, quod est humerorum ex circumductione vestis ornatus: humeri autem laboris et operis indicta sunt. Vult ergo pontificem in operibus esse ornatum: non enim sufficit sola scientia, sed qui fecerit et docuerit hic magnus vocabitur (I Tim. III; Matth. V).

Superhumerale. (HIER.) Superhumerale et duos lapides smaragdinos vel onychinos, qui desuper humeros tegunt, interpretantur duo hemisphaeria, quorum aliud super terram, aliud sub terra; vel solem et lunam qui desuper rutilant.

(ID.) Duo lapides in superhumerali Christus et Ecclesia, duodecim apostolorum qui ad praedicationem missi sunt nomina continentes, vel littera et spiritus 320Cin quibus omnia legis mysteria; a dextris spiritus, a sinistris littera (II Cor. III). Per litteras ad verba, per verba venimus ad sensum. Pulcher ordo ipso habitu sacramenta significans: in humeris opera, in pectore ratio, unde et pectusculum comedunt sacerdotes.

VERS. 8.-- Quod astringens. (ORIG., ubi supra. ) Cinxit eum secundum facturam humeralis, etc., usque ad quia secundum opera sua cingulo virtutis utitur.

Aptavit rationali, etc. (HIERON.) Rationale in medio positum, terram indicat, quae instar puncti, cum omnia in se habeat, cunctis vallatur elementis.

(ORIG., ubi supra. ) Imposuit super eum logion, λογίον, etc., usque ad nihil enim post haec adjicitur capiti 320Dpontificis.

(HIERON., ubi supra. ) Rationale duplex, apertum et absconditum, etc., usque ad et his praecedentibus doctrina et veritas sequatur.

(ID.) Duodecim lapides, etc., usque ad nobis scientiam demonstrante.

Posuit laminam. (ID.) Nihil prodest omnium eruditio, nisi Dei scientia coronetur, quae semper est ei cum Patre: unde laminam super cidarim imposuit.

(ID.) Imperfecta sunt omnia nisi currui quaeratur auriga, et super creaturas Creator insistens regat ipse, qui condidit. Quod olim in lamina, nunc in signo crucis ostenditur: pretiosior est sanguis evangelii 321Aauro legis; tunc juxta Ezechielem signum gementibus figebatur in fronte thau (Ezech. VIII): nunc portantes crucem dicimus: Signatum est super nos lumen vultus, Domine, etc. (Psal. IV)

Auri lamina in fronte pontificis, et nomen Dei inscriptum; omnia quae super terram sunt Dei arbitrio gubernari significant. Justum enim erat ut pontifex Dei, creaturarum omnium typum portans in vestibus suis, indicaret cuncta Dei misericordia indigere, et cum sacrificaret, expiaretur conditio universalis: ubi non pro liberis, parentibus, et propinquis, sed pro omni creatura voce et habitu precaretur.

VERS. 10.-- Tulit, etc. Ipse est Filius Dei, qui ungit et qui ungitur: ungens verbo divinitatis, secundum 321Bquod cum Patre et Spiritu sancto habet omnia communia. A Patre ungitur secundum illud: Unxit te Deus, Deus tuus oleo laetitiae, etc. (Psal. XLIV). Unxit eum Spiritus sanctus secundum Isaiam: Spiritus Domini super me, eo quod unxerit me (Isa. LXI). Quae autem Pater et Spiritus sanctus, haec eadem facit et Filius. Ungit ergo secundum divinitatem, ungitur secundum humanitatem. Unde et Daniel oblatum illum videt, offerentem non videt, quia semetipsum offert. Unxit ergo Moyses et offert, Christi figuram gerens.

VERS. 11.-- Sanctificant. (ORIG.) Significat supernam creaturam sanctificatione nostra ditatam: id est, per carnem Dominicam ex nobis sumptam. Unde: ut notum fieret principibus et potestatibus per 321Cecclesiam, etc.

VERS. 12.-- Fundens, etc. Caput Christi divinitas. Sicut enim caput a pedibus non dividitur, sic nec divinitas Christi ab humanitate post unctionem (I Cor. XI). Unde ait: Unxit te, Deus Deus tuus, etc. (Psal. LIV.) Sic enim Deus ungitur et sanctificatur.

VERS. 13.-- Filios, etc. (ORIG., ubi supra. ) Attende differentiam minorum sacerdotum ad majores etc., usque ad etiamsi primi nomen acceperit.

Vestivitque, etc. Filiis Aaron similes tunicae et zonae vel baltei et cidares vel mitrae imponuntur, ipsum enim induunt, ipso cinguntur, ipsum portant in capite; lumbi enim praecinguntur veritate, quae est Christus, et induuntur lorica justitiae: Christus autem justitia est sicut et galea salutis, ipse enim 321Dest salutare Dei.

VERS. 14.-- Obtulit, etc. (ISICH.) Quae sit hujus legis virtus, etc., usque ad necessario autem filii Aaron tanquam communicantes ei sacrificio manus imponunt.

(AUG., quaest. 23 in Levit.) Cum sacrificia pro peccatis memoraret, etc., usque ad et sanguinem fusum ad basim altaris sacrificiorum.

Obtulit, etc. (ISICH.) Lex sacerdotem in multis Christum figurantem, etc., usque ad ovibus praecepit.

Obtulit, etc. Mysteriorum traditionem, et pascha mysticum, quod Christus celebravit cum discipulis suis (Matth. XXVI), significat aries: secundus nominatur, 322Aquia post agnum in coena immolatum, seipsum obtulit; unde Aaron super caput arietis manus imposuit cum filiis suis, quia communem coenam cum discipulis suis Christus celebravit; sed Moyses specialiter hoc sacrificium obtulit, quia plus aliis figuram Christi expressit, qui maxime sacrificium suum fecit.

VERS. 24.-- Tetigit extremum auriculae, etc. Si per Aaron Christum accipimus, extremum auriculae ejus sanguine tactum fuit, quia Patri usque ad mortem obedivit. Si sacerdotes Ecclesiae aures eorum tangendae sunt, pollicesque manuum et pedum, ut eminentiam actionum et gressuum Domino consecrent. Tetigit extremum, etc. Quis sit hujus sacrificii effectus unctio aurium et manuum pedumque 322Bdemonstrat: propter obedientiam quippe nostram actionesque bonas, et conversationem celebratur, ut inobedientia Adae carentes Unigeniti obedientiam consequamur.

Manus ejus, etc. Ut in bona actione confortemur. Confortantur enim quae unguntur, destruuntur quae non unguntur. Ubi enim unctio non fuit sanguinis immolati, percutiebatur Aegyptus. Sed prius sacerdos, et postea filii ejus ungebantur sanguine, quia Christus primus sanguinem accepit in coena, deinde discipulis dedit.

Fudit super altare. Cum enim bibit, et apostolis dedit bibere, intelligibilem sanguinem super altare, id est, corpus suum fudit. Ecclesia autem corpus Christi; unde Marcus: Et sumens, gratias agens dedit 322Ceis, et biberunt ex eo omnes, et dixit eis: Hic est sanguis meus novi testamenti, qui pro multis effundetur (Marc. XIV).

Adipem vero. Quid haec significent, in salutari sacrificio, vel pacificorum dictum est. LXX vero pro cauda, lumbum; pro intestinis, ventriculum; et pro reticulo jecoris, pinnam epatis ediderunt: non frustra autem etiam nunc in ariete commemorantur.

VERS. 26.-- Canistro. LXX, canistrum: consummationis, quae est lingua Domini, quae praecipuam perfectamque doctrinam introduxit. Non enim venit solvere legem, sed adimplere (Matth. XIII). Haec bene canistrum dicitur propter textum doctrinae ejus, cum multa in parabolis dixerat. Erat autem 322Det ferculum et mensa totius doctrinae. Recte ergo de canistro consummationis mysticum panem proponit.

Panem. Apostolicam doctrinam, legalemque, et propheticam. Lex enim, et prophetae, et apostoli memoriam ejus faciunt. Lex enim dicit: Hic est panis quem dedit vobis Dominus (Exod. XXVI). Propheta autem: Panis ejus dabitur, etc. (Isa. XXXIII.) Prophetica autem et legalis doctrina minus nutriunt quam apostolica. Unde Paulus: Posuit Deus in ecclesia primum apostolos, deinde prophetas (I Cor. XII). Apostoli autem legis et prophetarum ducunt testimonia, per quae eis collyrida et lagana sunt subjecta.

323AEt armum (GREG. lib. I Regist., ep. 24.) Sacerdos ex praecepto legis armum dextrum, etc., usque ad et in sequentibus Moyses dicit.

VERS 39.-- Tulit pectusculum. (AUG., quaest. 23 in Levit.) Superius quia generaliter praecipiebat, etc., usque ad a principio enim dicitur: Ipse tibi quae ad populum, tu illi quae ad Deum.

VERS. 30.-- Assumesque unguentum. (ID., quaest. 24.) Quaeritur post mortem Mosi quis ungebat sacerdotem, etc., usque ad obsequendo non excellendo.

Aspersit, etc. Quaecunque in nobis sunt bona ad eum tanquam caput nostrum reducenda sunt; unde: Pro eis ego sanctifico meipsum, ut sint et ipsi sanctificati in veritate. Aperte igitur, sanctificationem, 323Bpassionem suam dixit, quae sanctificatio datur nobis.

Et super filios (AUG.) Non apparebat quis filiorum summo sacerdoti succederet. Non enim primogenitum vel majorem definit Scriptura, nisi in aliquo divino judicio fiebat, quamvis ex contentione venisse videatur, ut postea plures fierent sacerdotes summi, quia contendentibus excellentioribus litis finiendae causa pluribus honor ipse tribuebatur.

VERS. 31.-- Panes quoque. Carnes cum panibus comedi praecepit, ut intelligamus corpus Christi, qui caro est, et panis qui de coelo descendit (Joan. VI), et hoc in ecclesia tantum debet celebrari.

VERS. 32.-- Quidquid autem. Hoc videmus in Ecclesia fieri, et quae inconsumpta remanserint ignibus tradi. Non dicit autem, quotquot diebus inconsumpta 323Cremanserint, ut semper, si remanserint, tradantur igni: innuens, ut si a comestione sacrificii deficimus, nec perscrutari valemus, utrum ea quae videmus, ipsum corpus Domini sint, non tamen relinquamus, sed igni tradamus, ut comedat Spiritus sanctus quod non possumus. Comedit autem, cum cogitamus ei possibilia esse quae non possumus.

VERS. 33.-- De ostio. Significat quia toto tempore praesentis vitae non est de tabernaculo recedendum, sed Dei servitio insistendum.

(AUG., quaest. 24 in Levit.) Ad ostium tabernaculi testimonii, etc., usque ad habitatio quippe hoc nomen accepit.

Septem enim, etc. (ISICH.). Quinquagesima septenario 323Din se multiplicato, etc., usque ad observantes custodias Domini, etc.

VERS. 36.-- Feceruntque, etc. Aaron conjungitur filiis, nec exit de janua tabernaculi. Ipse enim erat praedicandus, et in eis praedicaturus, sicut ipse consecrat et in membris consecratur.

CHAPTER VIII.

VERS. 2.-- Two rams. The passion of Christ is signified, who is the leader of the people. And rightly two rams are immolated, 318Cbecause Christ twice committed to us the mysteries of his passion: first when he ate the mystical lamb at the supper, secondly when he established the same mysteries in the consecration of bread and wine.

VERS. 3.-- And thou shalt gather together. Moses called together the whole synagogue, although the Lord had chosen and commanded, yet the synagogue is called together: for in the ordaining of a priest the presence of the people is required, that all may know and be certain that he who is more excellent out of all the people, more learned, more holy and more eminent in virtue, he is chosen to the priesthood. He washed him therefore and clothed him. With what garment? with a tunic, and he girded him with a girdle. And again: He clothed him with an ankle-length tunic (Eccli. XLV), or, as we read elsewhere, with an inner one (II Reg. XV). But I see the high priest clothed with two tunics 318D. Yet Jesus forbids the apostles to be clothed with two tunics, whereas Moses and Jesus, that is the law and the Gospel, ought to agree (Matth. X). Others say that Jesus's commanding them not to have two tunics is not contrary to the law, but more perfect than the law: as also when the law forbids homicide (Exod. XX), but Jesus cuts away anger (Matth. V); and when the law forbids adultery, but Jesus cuts off concupiscence. I do not shut up the pontifical sacraments within the narrowness of this understanding: something further seems to me to be shown by that form. That high priest, whom Moses was then ordaining, knew that circumcision was spiritual: yet he kept also the circumcision of the flesh, because he could not be uncircumcised; he had habeba 319Atherefore two tunics, one of the carnal mystery, another of the spiritual understanding. For he could not be the high priest of those who then were, unless he offered carnal sacrifices, although he knew that they ought to be spiritual. Fittingly therefore he had two tunics. But the apostles, who were to say: If you be circumcised, Christ shall profit you nothing (Gal. V), and who were utterly to reject the literal observance, are rightly forbidden to have two tunics; one suffices them, and this the inner one, but that which is outside, the tunic of the law, they will not have, because Jesus permits them to have one, and that the inner one.

VERS. 6.-- And when he had washed. (HIERON., ubi supra. ) Even then the cleansing of the world, etc., as far as that what we conceive in mind, we may carry out in deed.

(ORIG., hom. 6 in Levit.) Let Moses wash thee, etc., 319Bas far as in what order a high priest is appointed.

VERS. 7.-- He clothed. (HIERON., ibid. ) Let us learn first the common vestments of the priests, etc., as far as because a work of this kind cannot be made on the loom.

(ID.) The second is the poderis of linen, that is, the ankle-length garment of a double linen cloth, which Josephus calls the byssine; this clings to the body, so close and with sleeves so tight that there is no wrinkle in the garment, and it comes down to the legs. Such linen garments soldiers are wont to have, which they call shirts, fitted to the limbs and drawn tight, that they may be unencumbered for all things. This tunic the priests use, having the beauty of the clothed and the swiftness of the naked, and it is called the linen one.

The third is the girdle, most like to the skin which a serpent puts off in summer, as a long pouch woven into a round 319Cshape, under a covering of scarlet, purple, hyacinth and fine linen: for the sake of comeliness and of strength, so marked with embroidery that you would think the various flowers and gems were not woven in by the craftsman's hand, but added. By this belt the linen tunic is drawn tight between the navel and the breast, which belt, having the breadth of four fingers and coming down for the greater part to the legs, when there is need of dispatch for the sacrifices is thrown back over the left shoulder.

(HIERON.) The fourth is round, etc., as far as about to die at once.

(ID.) The sixth in Hebrew, etc., as far as he may bear on his shoulders.

(ID.) The seventh is a little one, etc., as far as and it would be thought by those who saw it to be one weaving.

319D(ID.) The eighth is the golden plate, etc., as far as and let the name of God crown and protect the whole beauty of the high priest.

But the pomegranates and the little bells set in the lower parts show forth lightnings and thunders, or the earth and the water and all the elements sounding together among themselves, and so interwoven with one another that all are in each.

(HIERON.) The Hebrews the four colours, etc., as far as his providence penetrates all things.

(ORIG., hom. 6 in Levit.) In Exodus, where command is given concerning the priestly vestments, etc., as far as lest the holy be given to dogs and pearls be set before swine (Marc. VII).

And bringing in, etc. LXX: He put on him the tunic and girded 320Ahim with the girdle and clothed him with the epomis. He follows out the incarnation of Christ. For he took up the whole man, that is, body and soul; the tunic he called earthly, because it is of the earth; the epomis heavenly, because ὑπωρίς is the poderis. And of hyacinth, which is the heavenly colour: for he took up both, and saved both.

(HIERON.) On the hyacinthine tunic, pomegranates and little bells: because so great ought the knowledge of the high priest to be, that his steps and his motions and all things be vocal, and that he sound forth by his bearing the truth which he has conceived in mind; whatsoever he does, whatsoever he speaks, let it be doctrine. Without little bells, without divers colours, gems and flowers of the virtues, he neither enters into the Church, nor is he a high priest.

320BThe shoulder-piece, etc. (ORIG.) Moses laid upon the high priest the ephod, which is the adornment of the shoulders from the drawing round of the vesture: but the shoulders are the tokens of labour and of work. He wills therefore that the high priest be adorned in works: for knowledge alone does not suffice, but he that shall do and teach, he shall be called great (I Tim. III; Matth. V).

The ephod. (HIER.) The ephod and the two stones of emerald or of onyx, which cover the shoulders from above, are interpreted the two hemispheres, of which one is above the earth, the other beneath the earth; or the sun and the moon which glow red from above.

(ID.) The two stones on the ephod are Christ and the Church, containing the names of the twelve apostles who were sent to preach, or the letter and the spirit 320Cin which are all the mysteries of the law; on the right the spirit, on the left the letter (II Cor. III). Through the letters we come to the words, through the words to the sense. A beautiful order, signifying the sacraments by the very garb: on the shoulders works, on the breast reason, whence also the priests eat the little breast.

VERS. 8.-- Which binding fast. (ORIG., ubi supra. ) He girded him according to the making of the shoulder-piece, etc., as far as because according to his works he uses the girdle of virtue.

He fitted to the rational, etc. (HIERON.) The rational, set in the midst, points to the earth, which, after the manner of a point, though it have all things in itself, is walled about by all the elements.

(ORIG., ubi supra. ) He laid upon him the logion, λογίον, etc., as far as for nothing after these things is added to the head 320Dof the high priest.

(HIERON., ubi supra. ) The rational is twofold, open and hidden, etc., as far as and let doctrine and truth follow upon these which go before.

(ID.) Twelve stones, etc., as far as showing knowledge to us.

He put the plate. (ID.) The learning of all things profits nothing, unless it be crowned by the knowledge of God, which is always his with the Father: whence he set the plate above the tiara.

(ID.) All things are imperfect unless a charioteer be sought for the chariot, and unless he himself who founded them, standing over the creatures, rule them. What was once shown in the plate is now shown in the sign of the cross: more precious is the blood of the Gospel 321Athan the gold of the law; then, according to Ezekiel, the sign thau was fixed on the forehead of them that mourned (Ezech. VIII): now, bearing the cross, we say: The light of thy countenance is signed upon us, O Lord, etc. (Psal. IV)

The plate of gold on the forehead of the high priest, and the name of God inscribed, signify that all things which are upon the earth are governed by the will of God. For it was just that the priest of God, bearing on his vestments the figure of all creatures, should declare that all things stand in need of the mercy of God, and that, when he sacrificed, the universal creation should be expiated: where he should pray by voice and by bearing not for his children, his parents, and his kindred, but for every creature.

VERS. 10.-- He took, etc. He himself is the Son of God, who anoints and who is anointed: anointing by the word of the divinity, according to 321Bwhich he has all things in common with the Father and the Holy Spirit. By the Father he is anointed according to that: God, thy God, hath anointed thee with the oil of gladness, etc. (Psal. XLIV). The Holy Spirit anointed him according to Isaiah: The Spirit of the Lord is upon me, because he hath anointed me (Isa. LXI). But the things which the Father and the Holy Spirit do, these same the Son also does. He anoints therefore according to the divinity, he is anointed according to the humanity. Whence also Daniel sees him offered, and does not see the one offering, because he offers himself. Moses therefore anointed and offers, bearing the figure of Christ.

VERS. 11.-- They sanctify. (ORIG.) It signifies the creature above enriched by our sanctification: that is, through the Lord's flesh taken from us. Whence: that it might be made known to the princes and powers through 321Cthe church, etc. [Vulg. Eph 3:10 has ut innotescat principatibus et potestatibus in caelestibus, "that the manifold wisdom of God may be made known to the principalities and powers in heavenly places"]

VERS. 12.-- Pouring, etc. The head of Christ is the divinity. For as the head is not divided from the feet, so neither is the divinity of Christ from the humanity after the anointing (I Cor. XI). Whence he says: God, thy God, hath anointed thee, etc. (Psal. LIV.) For thus God is anointed and sanctified.

VERS. 13.-- The sons, etc. (ORIG., ubi supra. ) Mark the difference of the lesser priests to the greater, etc., as far as even if he shall have received the name of the first.

And he clothed, etc. Like tunics and girdles or belts and tiaras or mitres are put upon the sons of Aaron, for they put on him, are girded with him, bear him on the head; for the loins are girded with truth, which is Christ, and they are clothed with the breastplate of justice: and Christ is justice, as also the helmet of salvation, for he himself 321Dis the salvation of God.

VERS. 14.-- He offered, etc. (ISICH.) What the power of this law is, etc., as far as but of necessity the sons of Aaron, as sharing with him in the sacrifice, lay on their hands.

(AUG., quaest. 23 in Levit.) When he was recounting the sacrifices for sins, etc., as far as and the blood poured out at the base of the altar of the sacrifices.

He offered, etc. (ISICH.) The law, the priest figuring Christ in many things, etc., as far as he commanded for sheep.

He offered, etc. The handing down of the mysteries, and the mystical pasch, which Christ celebrated with his disciples (Matth. XXVI), is signified by the ram: the second he is named, 322Abecause after the lamb immolated at the supper he offered himself; whence Aaron laid his hands upon the head of the ram together with his sons, because Christ celebrated the common supper with his disciples; but Moses in a special manner offered this sacrifice, because more than the others he expressed the figure of Christ, who chiefly made his own sacrifice.

VERS. 24.-- He touched the tip of the ear, etc. If by Aaron we understand Christ, the tip of his ear was touched with blood, because he obeyed the Father even unto death. If the priests of the Church, their ears are to be touched, and the thumbs of their hands and feet, that they may consecrate to the Lord the eminence of their actions and of their steps. He touched the tip, etc. What the effect of this sacrifice is, the anointing of the ears and of the hands and feet 322Bshows: for it is celebrated on account of our obedience and our good actions and our manner of life, that, lacking the disobedience of Adam, we may attain to the obedience of the Only-begotten.

His hands, etc. That we may be strengthened in good action. For the things that are anointed are strengthened, the things that are not anointed are destroyed.
