For where the anointing of the immolated blood was not, Egypt was struck. But first the priest, and afterwards his sons, were anointed with the blood, because Christ first received the blood at the supper, then gave it to the disciples.

He poured out upon the altar. For when he drank, and gave the apostles to drink, he poured out the intelligible blood upon the altar, that is, upon his own body. But the Church is the body of Christ; whence Mark: And taking it, giving thanks he gave 322Cto them, and they all drank of it, and he said to them: This is my blood of the new testament, which shall be shed for many (Marc. XIV).

But the fat. What these things signify has been said in the peace offering, or in that of the pacific victims. But the LXX gave, for the tail, the loin; for the entrails, the little belly; and for the caul of the liver, the fin of the liver: and not in vain are they even now commemorated in the ram.

VERS. 26.-- The basket. LXX, the basket: of the consummation, which is the tongue of the Lord, which brought in the chief and perfect doctrine. For he came not to destroy the law, but to fulfil it (Matth. XIII). This is rightly called a basket because of the weave of his doctrine, since he had said many things in parables. And it was 322Dboth the dish and the table of the whole doctrine. Rightly therefore he sets forth the mystical bread out of the basket of the consummation.

The bread. The apostolic doctrine, and the legal, and the prophetic. For the law, and the prophets, and the apostles make his memorial. For the law says: This is the bread which the Lord hath given you (Exod. XXVI). And the prophet: His bread shall be given, etc. (Isa. XXXIII.) [Vulg. Is 33:16 has panis ei datus est, "bread is given him"] But the prophetic and the legal doctrine nourish less than the apostolic. Whence Paul: God hath set in the church first apostles, then prophets (I Cor. XII). But the apostles draw the testimonies of the law and of the prophets, by which the little cakes and the wafers are set beneath them.

323AAnd the shoulder (GREG. lib. I Regist., ep. 24.) The priest by the precept of the law the right shoulder, etc., as far as and Moses says in what follows.

VERS 39.-- He took the little breast. (AUG., quaest. 23 in Levit.) Above, because he was commanding generally, etc., as far as for from the beginning it is said: Let him be to thee for the things which pertain to the people, thou to him for the things which pertain to God.

VERS. 30.-- And thou shalt take of the ointment. (ID., quaest. 24.) It is asked who anointed the priest after the death of Moses, etc., as far as by serving, not by excelling.

He sprinkled, etc. Whatsoever good things are in us are to be led back to him as to our head; whence: For them do I sanctify myself, that they also may be sanctified in truth. Openly therefore he called his passion sanctification, 323Bwhich sanctification is given to us.

And upon the sons (AUG.) It did not appear which of the sons should succeed the high priest. For Scripture does not appoint the firstborn or the elder, unless it was done in some divine judgment, although it seems to have come of contention, so that afterwards there were made several high priests, because, when the more excellent were contending, for the sake of ending the strife the honour itself was bestowed on several.

VERS. 31.-- The loaves also. He commanded the flesh to be eaten with the loaves, that we may understand the body of Christ, who is flesh, and the bread which came down from heaven (Joan. VI), and that this ought to be celebrated only in the Church.

VERS. 32.-- But whatsoever. We see this to be done in the Church, and the things which shall have remained unconsumed to be given to the fire. But he does not say, for however many days they shall have remained unconsumed, 323Cso that always, if they shall have remained, they be given to the fire: hinting that, if we fall short of the eating of the sacrifice, and are not able to search out whether the things which we see are the very body of the Lord, we should nevertheless not leave them, but give them to the fire, that the Holy Spirit may eat what we cannot. And he eats, when we consider that the things which we cannot do are possible to him.

VERS. 33.-- Of the door. It signifies that during the whole time of the present life one must not depart from the tabernacle, but must persevere in the service of God.

(AUG., quaest. 24 in Levit.) At the door of the tabernacle of the testimony, etc., as far as for the dwelling received this name.

For seven, etc. (ISICH.). The fiftieth, the number seven 323Dbeing multiplied into itself, etc., as far as keeping the watches of the Lord, etc.

VERS. 36.-- And they did, etc. Aaron is joined with his sons, and does not go out from the door of the tabernacle. For he himself was to be preached, and was to preach in them, as he himself consecrates and is consecrated in his members.

CAPUT IX.

VERS. 1.-- Octava die, etc. Octava dies post dedicationem significat diem Pentecostes, quo Spiritus sanctus advenit (Act. II). Septem vero a dedicatione dies innuunt septies septem dies, qui fuerunt a resurrectione usque ad Pentecosten, scilicet 49, addito quasi octavo ipso die Pentecostes.

Pentecoste quae contigit die resurrectionis, 324Agerit figuram futuri, et consummationis saeculi praesentis, quia septem volvitur diebus. Oportebat enim ut eadem die adveniret Spiritus, qua resurrexit Christus. Tunc ait vocari Aaron et filios ejus, et majores natu: tunc enim convocatum est Ecclesiae spectaculum, in qua est Aaron, id est, Christus et filii ejus qui altae sunt conversationis, et ideo sacerdotii ministri, et majores natu, id est, presbyteri: ex his enim corpus Ecclesiae consistit.

VERS. 2.-- Tolle de, etc. Accipit Aaron vitulum in holocaustum et arietem, et offert Domino. Tunc enim Christus sacrificium suum notificavit, cum Spiritus adveniens Parthos, Medos, Elamitas, et omnium gentium primitias ad apostolorum coenaculum colligit (Act. II).

324BVitulum offert Aaron et arietem, quando doctores Ecclesiae mysteria passionis annuntiant, et pro salute humani generis celebrata insinuant.

Tollite. Hircum obtulerunt apostoli die Pentecostes quando Petrus poenitentiam annuntians inquit: Convertimini et agite poenitentiam.

(AUG., quaest. 26 in Levit.) Accipite hircum ex capris unum, etc., usque ad ubi autem universi, ibi et singuli, sed non convertitur.

VERS. 6.-- Iste sermo, etc. Sermonem, scilicet rem imperatam. Hoc facite in meam commemorationem (Luc. XXII). Sermonem autem dicit, quia plus in sermone consideratur vel constitit hujus dispensatio mysterii, et maxime Dominico verbo ea quae 324Capparent in aliud majus et intelligibile tranferente. Unde et de manna ait: Hic est panis quem dedit vobis Dominus ad comedendum, hic est sermo quem constituit Dominus (Exod. XVI). In his ergo significat quia sanctum mysterium illis agentibus et Dominicam coenam celebrantibus advenit Spiritus sanctus. Erat enim dies Dominicus, in quo oportebat apostolos sacra celebrare mysteria: quorum sequentes traditiones Dominicam diem divinis conventibus deputamus. Similiter et Christum post resurrectionem per mysticam coenam et panis fractionem invenimus manifestum.

VERS. 7.-- Et immola pro peccato, etc. (AUG., quaest. 27 in Levit.). Alia littera, etc., usque ad non est necesse super holocaustum imponi.

324DOffer holocaustum, etc. Non dicit holocaustum consummationis, ut supra, quando oblatum est ut sanctificatione consummaretur, ut sacerdotio fungeretur, quo fungebatur. Notandum sane cum pro populo offeruntur sacrificia et pro peccato sacrificia esse jussa offerri, et holocaustum et sacrificium salutaris. Pro sacerdote vero oblatum fuisse pro peccato, et holocaustum consummationis, non salutaris. Sed consummationis oblatum est, quando sanctificati sunt sacerdotes. Et haec obtulit Moyses pro Aaron et filiis suis, postea vero Aaron jam sacerdotio fungens praecipitur pro se offerre vitulum pro peccato, et arietem in holocaustum non consummationis; tunc enim offerebatur ut sanctificatione consummaretur, et sacerdotio 325Afungi posset, quo cum fungebatur, non erat necesse iterum consummare.

VERS. 8.-- Statimque, etc. (ISICH.) Qua causa haec etiam hic sacrificia peragantur superius diximus, etc. usque ad unde in persona sacerdotis utrumque perspicimus oblatum.

(AUG., ubi supra ). Movet etiam quod cum faceret Aaron dona populi quae supra memorantur, etc. usque ad utrumque ex utroque animante, quasi pectuscula dixerit.

VERS. 15.-- Et pro peccato, etc. (ISICH.) Cujus haec figuram habeant, etc., usque ad et quae docerent Ecclesiam meditari non cessabant.

Extendens manum, etc. (AUG., Quaest. 28 in Levit.) Ad altare stans deserviebat, etc., usque ad qui ibi 325Bgradus prohibuit esse.

VERS. 22.-- Sicque completis et caetera. (ISICH.) Evidenter omnem figuram Ecclesiae vult exponere, etc., usque ad sequentia demonstrant.

VERS. 23.-- Ingressi autem Moyses et Aaron tabernaculum testimonii, etc. (ID.) Quia de intelligibili tabernaculo, etc., usque ad idoneus est ut praestet benedictionem.

Ingressi autem. Praemisit ingressi, et subdit egressi, quia sacerdos ingreditur tabernaculum, quando pro se vel pro populo orat, vel divinae contemplationi vacat; egreditur cum docet, vel benedicit populo, vel cum ad conscientiam redit perscrutando se, ne forte in praedicatione offendat.

Apparuitque gloria Domini. In specie ignis, sicut 325Cin die Pentecostes, unde subdit: Et egressus est ignis a Domino. Unde et Lucas: Apparuerunt illis dispertitae linguae, tanquam ignis.

Omni multitudini, etc. Cui simile Lucas ait: Facta autem hac voce convenit multitudo, et mente confusa est (Act. II); non solum convenit, sed expavit. Unde hic sequitur: Obstupebant autem et mirabantur, etc.

Qui erant super altare. Invenit quia necesse sit praedicta sacrificia, et eorum expositiones superponere intelligibili altari corporis Christi, ut illuc suspiret nostra intentio, et tunc nutrimenta intelligibilis ignis, id est Spiritus sancti, aderunt, de quo dictum est: Dominus Deus noster ignis consumens est (Deut. IV).

CHAPTER IX.

VERS. 1.-- On the eighth day, etc. The eighth day after the dedication signifies the day of Pentecost, on which the Holy Spirit came (Act. II). But the seven days from the dedication intimate seven times seven days, which were from the resurrection until Pentecost, namely 49, the very day of Pentecost being added as an eighth.

Pentecost, which fell on the day of the resurrection, 324Abears the figure of what is to come, and of the consummation of the present age, because it is rolled round through seven days. For it behoved that the Spirit should come on the same day on which Christ rose. Then he bids Aaron and his sons and the elders be called: for then was the assembly of the Church convoked, in which is Aaron, that is, Christ, and his sons who are of a lofty manner of life, and therefore ministers of the priesthood, and the elders, that is, the presbyters: for of these the body of the Church consists.

VERS. 2.-- Take of, etc. Aaron takes a calf for a holocaust, and a ram, and offers them to the Lord. For then Christ made his sacrifice known, when the Spirit, coming, gathered Parthians, Medes, Elamites, and the firstfruits of all nations to the upper room of the apostles (Act. II).

324BAaron offers a calf and a ram when the doctors of the Church announce the mysteries of the passion, and make known that they were celebrated for the salvation of the human race.

Take ye. The apostles offered the he-goat on the day of Pentecost, when Peter, announcing repentance, said: Be ye converted and do penance. [Vulg. Act 2:38 has Poenitentiam agite; these are the words of Ez 18:30]

(AUG., quaest. 26 in Levit.) Take ye one he-goat of the goats, etc., as far as but where all are, there also are the individuals, though it is not convertible.

VERS. 6.-- This word, etc. The word, that is, the thing commanded. Do this in commemoration of me (Luc. XXII). But he says the word, because more is looked to in the word, or because in the word stands the dispensation of this mystery, and chiefly by the Lord's word transferring the things which 324Cappear into something else, greater and intelligible. Whence of the manna also he says: This is the bread which the Lord hath given you to eat, this is the word which the Lord hath appointed (Exod. XVI). [Vulg. Ex 16:15-16 Iste est panis quem Dominus dedit vobis ad vescendum … hic est sermo quem praecepit Dominus] In these things, therefore, he signifies that the Holy Spirit came upon them as they were performing the holy mystery and celebrating the Lord's supper. For it was the Lord's day, on which the apostles were bound to celebrate the sacred mysteries: whose traditions we follow in appointing the Lord's day for the divine assemblies. In like manner we find Christ made manifest after the resurrection through the mystical supper and the breaking of bread.

VERS. 7.-- And offer for sin, etc. (AUG., quaest. 27 in Levit.). Another reading, etc., as far as it is not necessary that it be laid upon the holocaust.

324DOffer the holocaust, etc. He does not say the holocaust of consummation, as above, when it was offered that he might be consummated by sanctification, that he might exercise the priesthood which he was exercising. It is indeed to be noted that when sacrifices are offered for the people, sacrifices were commanded to be offered both for sin, and a holocaust, and a sacrifice of peace offerings. But for the priest there was offered a sacrifice for sin, and a holocaust of consummation, not of peace offerings. But that of consummation was offered when the priests were sanctified. And these things Moses offered for Aaron and his sons; but afterwards Aaron, now exercising the priesthood, is commanded to offer for himself a calf for sin, and a ram for a holocaust not of consummation; for then it was offered that he might be consummated by sanctification, and might be able to exercise the priesthood, 325Awhich, when he was exercising it, there was no need to consummate again.

VERS. 8.-- And forthwith, etc. (ISICH.) For what cause these sacrifices also are performed here we have said above, etc. as far as whence in the person of the priest we discern both to have been offered.

(AUG., ubi supra ). It moves one also that, when Aaron was performing the gifts of the people which are recalled above, etc., as far as both from each living creature, as though he had said little breasts.

VERS. 15.-- And for sin, etc. (ISICH.) Of what these bear the figure, etc., as far as and they ceased not to meditate the things which they were teaching the Church.

Stretching forth his hand, etc. (AUG., Quaest. 28 in Levit.) Standing at the altar he was serving, etc., as far as who there 325Bforbade there to be steps.

VERS. 22.-- And so, these being completed, and the rest. (ISICH.) Plainly he wishes to set forth the whole figure of the Church, etc., as far as the things that follow show.

VERS. 23.-- And Moses and Aaron entering into the tabernacle of the testimony, etc. (ID.) Because concerning the intelligible tabernacle, etc., as far as he is fit to bestow the blessing.

And entering in. He set entering in first, and subjoins going out, because the priest enters the tabernacle when he prays for himself or for the people, or gives himself to divine contemplation; he goes out when he teaches, or blesses the people, or when he returns to his conscience by searching himself, lest perchance he give offence in his preaching.

And the glory of the Lord appeared. In the form of fire, as 325Con the day of Pentecost, whence he subjoins: And there went out a fire from the Lord. Whence Luke also: There appeared to them parted tongues, as it were of fire.

To all the multitude, etc. To which Luke says the like: And this voice being made, the multitude came together, and was confounded in mind (Act. II); not only came together, but was terrified. Whence here there follows: And they were astonished and wondered, etc.

Which were upon the altar. He finds that it is necessary to set the aforesaid sacrifices, and the expositions of them, upon the intelligible altar of the body of Christ, that our intention may sigh thither, and then the nourishments of the intelligible fire, that is, of the Holy Spirit, will be at hand, of which it is said: The Lord our God is a consuming fire (Deut. IV). [Vulg. Dt 4:24 Dominus Deus tuus, "thy God"; the form with noster is Heb 12:29]

CAPUT X.

  • 325D

VERS. 1.-- Arreptisque Nadab. (AUG. quaest. 31 in Levit., tom. III.) Exeunte a Domino igne mortui sunt filii Aaron, etc., usque ad tormenta patientur.

Arreptisque, etc. (ISID., quaest. in Levit., tom. V.) Alienum ignem offerunt, etc., usque ad ne vitiis flagremus.

(ISICH.) Hic eos qui aliena docent, etc., usque ad unde vitam poterant promereri.

(AUG., ubi supra. ) Alia littera, etc., usque ad per quos nobis Scripturam dedit.

VERS. 4.-- Vocatis autem Moysi, Misaele et Elisaphan. מישאל Misael, Dei attrectatio. אלצפן Elisaphan, 326Asive Elzaphan, Dei illuminatio, vel Dei speculum. His jure praecipitur ut tollant Nadab et Abiu extra castra. Hi enim sanctos significant, qui a Deo illuminati ipsum manibus fidei attrectant, et Deum perfecta fide contemplantur. Unde bene filii Oziel dicuntur, qui significat Evangelium: unde patruelis Aaron fuisse dicitur, quia magna cognatio est Evangelii et Legis. Evangelium namque expositio est Legis: filii autem Evangelii haereticos vere mortuos a castris Ecclesiae detrudunt. Vel Misadai et Elisaphan primus attrectatio Dei, id est Misadai; Elisaphan interpretatur specula Dei mei. Quicunque scit attrectare Deum, et speculari Christi scilicet carnem et divinitatem, sicut Thomas qui manu contrectans carnem dicebat: Deus meus et Dominus meus (Joan. XX), potest ignem 326Balienae doctrinae auferre a facie sanctorum, id est sacerdotum. Tunicae sacerdotum, conversatio et vita qua sacerdotii induuntur habitu. Abjiciuntur ergo cum sacerdotio a consortio sacerdotum et etiam populi. Unde addit, extra tabernaculum, id est, ecclesiam populi, filii patrui Aaron haec faciunt.

Aaron, id est David, per quem prophetiae intelliguntur, quae Christum manifestum faciunt, et quodammodo ignorantibus pariunt. Frater autem prophetiae Evangelium est; quae enim in Evangeliis facta sunt, a prophetis praedicta sunt. Filii ergo fratris patris Aaron, id est Evangelii, tales ejiciunt. Unde: Si quis venit ad vos, et hanc doctrinam non affert, nolite eum recipere in domum (II Joan.).

VERS. 6.-- Capita, etc. Ad litteram. Quia nuper 326Cfuerant Deo consecrati, praecipit ut sancta vestimenta non deponant, sed parati servire Deo assistant.

Allegorice autem, ne in Christo divisionem personarum faciant. Caput enim Christi Deus est, Christus autem ex divinitate constat et humanitate; capitis enim, id est divinitatis tiara humanitas est, qua tecta fuit velut pileo divinitas; haec non est auferenda, nec divisio personarum in Christo praedicanda, sed duarum substantiarum una persona.

Capita vestra, etc. (AUG., quaest. 32 in Lev.) Ne scilicet cidares deponant, etc., usque ad unde addit:

Fratres vestri et omnis domus Israel plangant incendium. (ISICH.) Non ignoramus quid cidares significent, 326Detc., usque ad de qua: Unctio ejus docet vos de omnibus.

VERS. 9.-- Vinum et omne quod inebriare. (ID.) Usque nunc hanc legem Ecclesia custodit, etc., usque ad id est spiritualiter inspirasse.

Vinum et omne quod, etc. Ab iis quae statum mentis dejiciunt vel evertunt abstinere jubentur, quando tabernaculum ingrediuntur. Nos autem nunquam inebriemur, sed semper mente et corpore sobrii simus.

Quando intrabitis. (AUG., quaest. 33). Quando ergo eis licebat bibere, etc., usque ad ut hoc sit officium sacerdotum legitimum aeternum in progenies eorum.

327AVERS. 10.-- Ut habeatis scientiam discernendi. (AUG.) Hoc et ambigue positum est, etc., usque ad an potius ad utrumque referendum est?

VERS. 12.-- Tollite sacrificium, etc. (ISICH.) Quod pectusculum confidentiam, etc., usque ad quae autem ad pacifica et salutaria pertinent, totius populi sunt.

Tollite, etc. Arcana, scilicet et profunda Scripturae sacramenta, quae vulgus non capit, retinenda ac comedenda sunt perfectis; juxta altare, id est juxta Dominicae passionis fidem et auctoritatem.

VERS. 14.-- Pectusculum, etc. (AUG., quaest. 34.) Pectusculum. Quamvis singulis singula nomina data sint, etc., usque ad de pectusculo imponebatur altari.

327BVERS. 16.-- Inter haec. (ISICH.) Quia omne peccatum per poenitentiam deletur, et qui praedictum peccatum alterius doctrinae et blasphemiae commiserunt, salvati non sunt, causam mortis eorum legislator exponit.

Inter haec hircum, scilicet quia non comederunt filii Aaron quod pro peccato erat in loco sancto, id est poenitentiam peccati commissi in ecclesia peragi non fecerunt. Quibus hoc praeceptum est, quos Christus in Ecclesia vicarios suos constituit. Ipse enim oblationis suae sanguinem, quae pro nostro sanguine oblata est, in sanctum, id est in coelum, in conspectu Patris obtulit. Debet autem comedi hoc sacricium in loco sancto, id est ecclesia, in propitiationem peccatorum, unde addit: Quemadmodum praeceptum 327Cest mihi, id est sicut praecepi. Moyses enim gerit personam Christi. Exustum reperit, quia in Spiritum sanctum peccaverant, ab intelligibili igne Spiritus sancti oblatio pro poenitentia ablata est. Voluntarie enim peccantibus non relinquitur hostia pro peccato (Heb. X). Nam sicut vera poenitentia veniam promeretur, ita simulata Deum irritat: quia Spiritus sanctus disciplinae effugiet fictum (Sap. I). Hoc autem sancti Patres irremissibile peccatum, et blasphemiam in Spiritum sanctum dixerunt, in sceleribus usque ad finem vitae perseverare, de salute sua desperare, de potentia et misericordia Redemptoris diffidere.

(ADAMANT.) Non peccare solius Dei est, etc., usque ad nisi verum et sincerum.

327D(AUG., quaest. 36 in Levit.) Sane quoniam sex pecora praecipiuntur offerri pro populo, etc., usque ad quae omnia fuerant pro peccato.

VERS. 19.-- Respondit Aaron, etc. In persona Ecclesiae, cujus figuram gerit, sicut et Christi, videtur Aaron dicere hoc. Quasi, quia post adventum Salvatoris deputati sunt ministare, et pro poenitentia offerre, et propheticam vitam exponere. Aliter: Docentes perierunt et in eis haec Ecclesiae acciderunt, ut sacerdotum et magistrorum lugeret mortem, unde comedere hodie pro peccato non potuit, nec orare: Domino autem displicebat.

(AUG.) Respondit Aaron, etc. Si hodie obtulerunt quae pro peccato illorum sunt, etc., usque ad Hoc 328Aautem quare sit factum, respondit Aaron, et placuit Mosi.

Hodie. Quasi dicat: si ante remissionem Salvatoris hoc peccassent, forsitan ignosceretur, cum nondum mundo scientia manifestaretur: unde, Si non venissem, et locutus eis fuissem, peccatum non haberent; nunc autem excusationem non habent de peccato suo (Joan. XV).

CHAPTER X.

  • 325D

VERS. 1.-- And Nadab having taken. (AUG. quaest. 31 in Levit., tom. III.) Fire going out from the Lord, the sons of Aaron died, etc., as far as they will suffer torments.

And having taken, etc. (ISID., quaest. in Levit., tom. V.) They offer strange fire, etc., as far as lest we burn with vices.

(ISICH.) Here those who teach strange things, etc., as far as whereby they were able to earn life.

(AUG., ubi supra. ) Another reading, etc., as far as through whom he gave us the Scripture.

VERS. 4.-- And Moses having called Misael and Elisaphan. מישאל Misael, the touching of God. אלצפן Elisaphan, 326Aor Elzaphan, the illumination of God, or the mirror of God. To these it is with right commanded that they take up Nadab and Abiu outside the camp. For these signify the saints, who, being illumined by God, touch him with the hands of faith, and contemplate God with perfect faith. Whence they are well called the sons of Oziel, which signifies the Gospel: whence he is said to have been Aaron's cousin on the father's side, because great is the kinship of the Gospel and of the Law. For the Gospel is the exposition of the Law: and the sons of the Gospel thrust heretics, truly dead, from the camp of the Church. Or Misadai and Elisaphan: the first, the touching of God, that is Misadai; Elisaphan is interpreted the watchtower of my God. Whosoever knows how to touch God, and to behold, namely, the flesh and the divinity of Christ, as Thomas who, handling the flesh with his hand, said: My God and my Lord (Joan. XX), [Vulg. Jo 20:28 Dominus meus, et Deus meus, the order reversed] is able to take away the fire 326Bof strange doctrine from the face of the saints, that is, of the priests. The tunics of the priests: the manner of life and the life by which they are clothed with the habit of the priesthood. They are cast out therefore, together with the priesthood, from the fellowship of the priests and also of the people. Whence he adds, outside the tabernacle, that is, the church of the people: the sons of Aaron's uncle do these things.

Aaron, that is David, through whom the prophecies are understood, which make Christ manifest, and in a manner bring him forth to those who know him not. But the brother of prophecy is the Gospel; for the things which were done in the Gospels were foretold by the prophets. The sons therefore of the brother of Aaron's father, that is, of the Gospel, cast out such men. Whence: If any man come to you, and bring not this doctrine, receive him not into the house (II Joan.).

VERS. 6.-- Heads, etc. According to the letter. Because they had lately 326Cbeen consecrated to God, he commands that they lay not aside the holy vestments, but stand ready to serve God.

But allegorically, that they make not a division of persons in Christ. For the head of Christ is God, but Christ consists of divinity and humanity; for the tiara of the head, that is of the divinity, is the humanity, by which the divinity was covered as with a cap; this is not to be taken away, nor is a division of persons in Christ to be preached, but one person of two substances.

Your heads, etc. (AUG., quaest. 32 in Lev.) That is, lest they lay aside the mitres, etc., as far as whence he adds:

Let your brethren and all the house of Israel bewail the burning. (ISICH.) We are not ignorant what the mitres signify, 326Detc., as far as of which: His unction teacheth you of all things.

VERS. 9.-- Wine, and every thing that may make drunk. (ID.) Until now the Church keeps this law, etc., as far as that is, to have breathed spiritually.

Wine, and every thing that, etc. They are bidden to abstain from those things which cast down or overturn the state of the mind, when they enter the tabernacle. But let us never be made drunk, but let us always be sober in mind and in body.

When you shall enter. (AUG., quaest. 33). When therefore it was lawful for them to drink, etc., as far as that this may be the lawful office of the priests for ever in their generations.

327AVERS. 10.-- That you may have knowledge to discern. (AUG.) This too is set down ambiguously, etc., as far as or is it rather to be referred to both?

VERS. 12.-- Take the sacrifice, etc. (ISICH.) That the little breast is confidence, etc., as far as but the things that belong to the peace and welfare offerings are of the whole people.

Take ye, etc. The hidden things, that is, and the deep sacraments of Scripture, which the common sort does not grasp, are to be kept and eaten by the perfect; beside the altar, that is, beside the faith and the authority of the Lord's passion.

VERS. 14.-- The little breast, etc. (AUG., quaest. 34.) The little breast. Although several names have been given to the several things, etc., as far as it was laid upon the altar from the little breast.

327BVERS. 16.-- Among these. (ISICH.) Because every sin is blotted out through penance, and they who committed the aforesaid sin of another doctrine and of blasphemy were not saved, the lawgiver sets forth the cause of their death.

Among these the he-goat, that is, because the sons of Aaron did not eat that which was for sin in the holy place, that is, they did not cause the penance for the sin committed to be performed in the church. To whom this is commanded, whom Christ appointed as his vicars in the Church. For he himself offered the blood of his own oblation, which was offered for our blood, into the holy place, that is, into heaven, in the sight of the Father. But this sacrifice sacricium ought to be eaten in the holy place, that is, in the church, unto the propitiation of sins, whence he adds: As it hath been commanded 327Cto me, that is, as I have commanded. For Moses bears the person of Christ. He finds it burnt up, because they had sinned against the Holy Spirit: by the intelligible fire of the Holy Spirit the oblation for penance was taken away. For to them that sin wilfully there is not left a sacrifice for sin (Heb. X). For as true penance earns pardon, so feigned penance provokes God: because the Holy Spirit of discipline will flee the feigned (Sap. I). But this the holy Fathers have called the unremissible sin, and the blasphemy against the Holy Spirit: to persevere in crimes unto the end of life, to despair of one's own salvation, to distrust the power and the mercy of the Redeemer.

(ADAMANT.) Not to sin belongs to God alone, etc., as far as save what is true and sincere.

327D(AUG., quaest. 36 in Levit.) Indeed, since six beasts are commanded to be offered for the people, etc., as far as all which had been for sin.

VERS. 19.-- Aaron answered, etc. In the person of the Church, whose figure he bears, as also that of Christ, Aaron seems to say this. As it were, because after the coming of the Saviour they were appointed to minister, and to offer for penance, and to set forth the prophetic life. Otherwise: The teachers perished, and in them these things befell the Church, so that it mourned the death of the priests and the masters, whence it could not eat to-day for sin, nor pray: but it was displeasing to the Lord.

(AUG.) Aaron answered, etc. If to-day they offered the things which are for their sin, etc., as far as But 328Awhy this was done, Aaron answered, and it pleased Moses.

To-day. As if he said: if they had sinned this before the remission of the Saviour, perhaps it would be pardoned, since knowledge was not yet made manifest to the world: whence, If I had not come, and spoken to them, they would not have sin; but now they have no excuse for their sin (Joan. XV).

CAPUT XI.

VERS. 1.-- Locutus est Dominus. (ISICH.) Quidquid a Deo factum ipsa institutionis auctoritate mundum est. In animalibus igitur mores pinguntur hominum, et actus, et voluntates, ex quibus fiunt mundi vel immundi.

328B(ID.) Dixit Dominus ad Noe: Omne quod movetur et vivit, erit vobis in cibum (Gen. IX), etc. In Genesi quoque dictum est: Vidit Deus cuncta quae fecerat, et erant valde bona (Gen. I). Hic autem quaedam prohibentur, velut mala, et immunda decernuntur. Non sunt tamen ista contraria sibi: nam et Judaeorum gastrimargiam ad modicum Dominus reprimit, et spiritualis praeceptorum expositio quales debeant esse instruit. Lex enim nutrimentum est animae rationalis rationabiliter intellecta. Sicut autem in superioribus per oblationes figuravit offerentes, sic nunc per ea quae comeduntur exprimit comedentes.

VERS. 3.-- Omne quod habet, etc. (ISID.) In piscibus quidquid pinnulas et squamas non habet, 328Cabominabile est: aspera pro mundis habentur. Hispidi enim et hirsuti, firmi, et graves hominum mores approbantur. Qui sine his sunt, immundi dicuntur, quibus leves et lubrici, infirmi et effeminati mores adjiciuntur. Pinnulas quoque conjungit et squamas; quicunque enim pisces pinnati sunt et squamosi. Si quibus ignorantia solubilis est, his et cognitio sublimis et vita coelestis: qui autem tales non sunt, cum his nec cibum sumere.

Moraliter. (GREG.) Pisces qui pinnulas habent et squamas, dare saltus super aquas solent. In pinnatis ergo piscibus electae animae figurantur, quae solae in coelestis Ecclesiae corpus transeunt; quae pinnulis virtutum fultae per coeleste desiderium saliunt ad contemplationem, quamvis in se relabantur per carnis 328Dfragilitatem. Soli ergo in electorum corpus quasi cibus electus transeunt, qui dum imis serviunt, mentis saltibus superna conscendunt, ne semper in profundis curarum lateant, ubi nullam amoris summi, quasi liberi aeris, curam habent. Qui ergo rebus temporalibus occupantur, tunc bene exteriora disponunt, cum sollicite ad interiora refugiunt, nec perturbationis strepitus diligunt, sed apud semetipsos intus in tranquillitatis sinu requiescunt.

VERS. 10.-- Quidquid pinnulas, etc. Sunt etiam pisces testam pro pelle habentes, quorum squama auferri non potest, quia nec verbo Dei squamas ignorantiae deponunt, nec cultrum spiritus suscipiunt: et quamvis in mari baptismi inveniantur, vel 329Ain poenitentiae fluminibus, abominabiles sunt tamen in his quae moventur et vivunt in aquis, quia regenerationem et vitam, quam in baptismo acceperunt, vivendo male corrumpunt.

VERS. 11.-- Carnes. (RAB. in Levit. ) Cum suem prohibet, etc., usque ad quaerentes tenebras noctis.

VERS. 13.-- Haec sunt quae de avibus, etc. (ISICH.) Quae ad contemplativorum vitam pertinent, adjungit, etc., usque ad mulierculas peccatis oneratas.

VERS. 14.-- Vulturem juxta genus suum. (ID.) Qui bellis gaudet. Unde sequitur exercitum, expectans cadavera mortuorum: non debet enim alieno luctu pasci, qui dat operam contemplationi.

VERS. 16.-- Struthionem. Struthio avis est, sed volare non potest, et semper circa terram est, sicut 329Bquidam Deo militantes et saecularibus negotiis se implicantes (Job XXXIX; II Tim. II).

Noctuam. Hanc aiunt in nocte acute videre, et parum in die. Tales sunt qui Legis gloriantur contemplatione, et Evangelii lucem capere non valent. Nycticorax. In nocte tantum rapit. Tales sunt gentes noctis operibus inhiantes, quae sunt fornicatio, immunditia, impudicitia (Gal. V).

Larum. Larus est animal tam in aqua quam in terra habitans: volat enim et natat. Huic assimilantur qui circumcisionem et baptismum venerantur.

Accipitrem. Accipiter inter rapaces est aves, sed mansuescit, et rapinam cum hominibus operatur. Hunc imitantur qui mansueti videntur, sed cum potentibus et avaris atque rapacibus rapere nituntur.

329CMergulum et ibin. Haec longi colli sunt, et cibum ex profunditate terrae vel aquae (ut dicitur) trahunt, nec imitanda sunt. Qui enim contemplationi vacat ad superiora debet tendere, nec de inferioribus sollicitus esse.

VERS. 19.-- Upupam. Haec lugubris est et luctum amans. Saeculi autem tristitia mortem operatur. Qui autem Deum diligit, debet gaudere, sine intermissione orare, in omnibus gratias agere (I Thess. V).

Vespertilio. Quae circa terram volat, pennis pro pedibus utitur: quod alienum est ab eis qui contemplantur, ne contemplatio eorum in terrenis occupetur.

VERS. 20.-- Omne de volucribus quod graditur super 329Dquatuor pedes, etc. Qui contemplativis videntur esse similes, sed tanquam reptilia terrenis inhaerent concupiscentiis, etiamsi in quatuor pedibus ambulent, id est si evangelicam praedicationem simulent, quae vocat ad poenitentiam; quia non vere socialem amplectuntur vitam, voluptatibus servientes, abominabiles sunt.

VERS. 21.-- Quidquid autem ambulat, etc. Quia cum sint in carne, non secundum carnem ambulant (II Cor. X), sed a sordidis actionibus exsiliunt, et terrenis hominibus permiscentur, ut instruant et doceant: sicut Dominus cum peccatoribus et publicanis (Matth. IX). Habere nos oportet crura sublimiora pedibus, ne claudicemus utroque pede, sicut 330AJudaei in lege et prophetis; sed crura habeamus recta, sicut Cherubin, de quibus dicitur: Crura eorum recta, et pennati pedes eorum (Ezech. I).

VERS. 22.-- Brucus. Qui exsilit, sed minus quam attacus, vel locusta, quae magis exsiliunt. Tales sunt, qui cum peccatoribus conversantur, ad conversationem, non ad consensum vel imitationem.

Ophiomachus. Qui pugnat cum serpentibus, nobis autem pugna est cum antiquo serpente et angelis ejus (Apoc. XII).

VERS. 25.-- Ut portet. Sicut qui dicit malum bonum, et amarum dulce (Isa. V): laudando enim malum a quo prohibere debuerat, suum facit et portat.

VERS. 26.-- Omne animal quod. (ISICH.) Nullum peccatum praeterit lex; quaecunque in Evangelio prohibentur 330BLegis aenigmatibus exprimuntur, ut in ore duorum testium stet omne verbum (Matth. XVIII), et in utraque lege laudes Deum, et, sicut prohibet, fugias malum.

VERS. 27.-- Quod ambulat super manus, etc. Qui evangelicam conversationem migrat, sed manibus ambulat, turpis, scilicet, lucri causa manus imponens (I Tim. IV), hic recte omnibus bestiis comparatur, ut vel adulterum dicas, vel avarum, homicidam et similia: omnibus enim per pravam manus impositionem communicat.

VERS. 28.-- Qui portaverit hujusmodi cadavera. Falsa dicendo testimonia, dignum dicendo indignum, et mundum immundum.

VERS. 29.-- Mustela. Ingluviem. Mus enim pro 330Cventris ingluvie nulla vitat pericula.

Crocodilus. Qui durae cutis et malitiosus luto pascitur.

VERS. 30.-- Chamelaeon. Miserescit, et prae infirmitate mansuetum se fingit, cum crudele sit.

Talpa. Animal caecum de terra genitum. Creatur enim, ut aiunt, de terra compluta et lutulenta, sed prius corrupta.

VERS. 31.-- Qui tetigerit, etc. Si naturaliter essent immunda, immundus esset viventia etiam qui tangeret. Morticina talium actiones sunt, id est, dolus, furtum, duplicitas, et hujusmodi in quibus mors animae.

VERS. 32.-- Et super quod, etc. (ISICH.) Admirabili divisione utitur legislator, etc., usque ad tam 330Dvas ligneum quam vestimentum.

VERS. 34.-- Omnis cibus, etc. Cibum et potum opera bona intelligit, ideo dixit: Cibus quem comedetis, et liquens quod bibitur, etc. Quia malarum actionum et cibus et potus prohibetur, ne quis participet. Si ergo bonis actionibus, quae sunt in omni vase, id est in omni homine, quodlibet morticinum inciderit, necessario immunda actio erit. Si enim bonae actioni, superbia, dolus, hypocrisis vel quodlibet morticinum, id est, participanti mortem inferens inciderit, et hic immundus erit: bonam enim actionem mala incidens intentio polluit.

VERS. 34.-- Si fusa fuerit super eum aqua. (AUG., quaest. 37 in Levit.) De vase, scilicet quod 331Afactum est immundum de morticinis immundis si forte aquam habuit.

Aqua. Baptismi: est enim sermo de baptizatis; de infidelibus autem mentionem non facit. Unde Paulus: Scripsi vobis non commisceri fornicariis (I Cor. III), non utique fornicariis hujus mundi, etc.

VERS. 35.-- Sive clibani. Per hoc magistri designantur, in quibus cibi sacrae doctrinae coquuntur operatione et igne Spiritus sancti. Nec mirum: Moab enim lebes appellatur: Moab olla spei meae (Rom. II).

Chytropodes, etc. Quia qui docent non furari, furantur; et qui praedicant non moechari, moechantur: doctrina tamen munda est. Unde fontes et caetera.

331BVERS. 37.-- Si ceciderit. (ISICH.) Nihil indiscussum relinquit, etc., usque ad si quodlibet morticinum super eos ceciderit, fiunt immundi.

VERS. 39.-- Qui cadaver ejus tetigerit, immundus. (ISICH.) Iniquorum vitam debemus abominari, justorum imitari. Qui enim vitam malorum tetigerit, immundus erit, donec poenituerit.

VERS. 40.-- Qui comederit. Id est, quasi advocatus defenderit, et in se auxiliando transtulerit, lavet vestimenta sua, id est actiones pravas, donec poenituerit.

VERS. 41.-- Omne quod reptat super terram. Hoc superius legislator exposuit, sed ad frequentationem meditandae legis monendo auditorem, repetit.

VERS. 47.-- Et sciatis quid comedere. Non hi cibi 331Cnos mundant vel polluunt, sed nos ipsi: vivificamus enim quae comeduntur, et quemadmodum omnis cibus bonus (I Petr. II), et nihil rejiciendum, quod cum gratiarum actione percipitur; sic ea quae comeduntur, id est quae nutriunt animam, bonas scilicet actiones, nos vivificamus, si bene utimur: si male, mortificamus. Qui enim ex rapina facit eleemosynam mortificat eam, et aufert ei potentiam; et qui abstinent a cibis, fratribus vero detrahunt, mortificant abstinentiam.

CHAPTER XI.

VERS. 1.-- The Lord spoke. (ISICH.) Whatsoever was made by God is clean by the very authority of its institution. In the animals, therefore, are depicted the manners of men, and their acts, and their wills, from which they become clean or unclean.

328B(ID.) The Lord said to Noe: Every thing that moveth and liveth shall be meat for you (Gen. IX), etc. In Genesis also it was said: God saw all the things that he had made, and they were very good (Gen. I). But here certain things are forbidden as evil, and are decreed unclean. Yet these things are not contrary to one another: for the Lord both restrains for a little the gluttony of the Jews, and the spiritual exposition of the precepts instructs what manner of men they ought to be. For the Law is the nourishment of the rational soul when it is understood in a reasonable way. And as in the passages above he figured those who offer by the oblations, so now by the things that are eaten he expresses those who eat.

VERS. 3.-- Every thing that hath, etc. (ISID.) Among fishes whatsoever hath not fins and scales 328Cis abominable: rough things are held for clean. For bristly and shaggy, firm and grave manners of men are approved. They who are without these are called unclean, to whom smooth and slippery, weak and effeminate manners are attached. He joins fins also and scales; for whatsoever fishes are finned are also scaly. If to any their ignorance can be dissolved, to these belongs also lofty knowledge and a heavenly life: but they who are not such, with these not even to take food.

Morally. (GREG.) Fishes which have fins and scales are wont to give leaps above the waters. In finned fishes, therefore, are figured the elect souls, which alone pass over into the body of the heavenly Church; which, upheld by the fins of the virtues, leap through heavenly desire to contemplation, although they slip back into themselves through the frailty of the flesh. 328DThose alone, then, pass over into the body of the elect as chosen food, who, while they serve the lowest things, ascend to the things above by the leaps of the mind, lest they always lie hid in the depths of cares, where they have no care of the highest love, as of the free air. They therefore who are occupied with temporal things dispose their outward affairs well then, when they anxiously flee back to the inward ones, and love not the din of disturbance, but rest within themselves in the bosom of tranquillity.

VERS. 10.-- Whatsoever fins, etc. There are also fishes having a shell for a skin, whose scale cannot be taken away, because they neither lay aside the scales of ignorance at the word of God, nor receive the knife of the spirit: and although they be found in the sea of baptism, or 329Ain the rivers of penance, they are nevertheless abominable among those things which move and live in the waters, because by living ill they corrupt the regeneration and the life which they received in baptism.

VERS. 11.-- Flesh. (RAB. in Levit. ) When he forbids the swine, etc., as far as seeking the darkness of night.

VERS. 13.-- These are they which of the birds, etc. (ISICH.) He adds the things which belong to the life of contemplatives, etc., as far as little women laden with sins.

VERS. 14.-- The vulture after its kind. (ID.) Which rejoices in wars. Whence it follows an army, awaiting the carcasses of the dead: for he who gives his labour to contemplation ought not to be fed on another's grief.

VERS. 16.-- The ostrich. The ostrich is a bird, but it cannot fly, and is always about the ground, like 329Bcertain men who serve God and entangle themselves in secular businesses (Job XXXIX; II Tim. II).

The owl. They say that this sees keenly by night, and little by day. Such are they who glory in the contemplation of the Law, and are not able to receive the light of the Gospel. The night-raven. It seizes prey only by night. Such are the nations that gape after the works of night, which are fornication, uncleanness, immodesty (Gal. V).

The gull. The gull is an animal dwelling as much in water as on land: for it both flies and swims. To this are likened they who venerate both circumcision and baptism.

The hawk. The hawk is among the birds of prey, but it grows tame, and works its rapine together with men. Him do they imitate who seem gentle, but strive to seize together with the powerful and the covetous and the ravening.

329CThe didapper and the ibis. These are of long neck, and draw their food out of the depth of the earth or of the water (as it is said), and are not to be imitated. For he who gives himself to contemplation ought to stretch towards the higher things, and not to be solicitous about the lower.

VERS. 19.-- The hoopoe. This is mournful and loving of grief. But the sorrow of the world worketh death. But he who loves God ought to rejoice, to pray without ceasing, in all things to give thanks (I Thess. V).

The bat. Which flies about the ground, uses its wings for feet: which is foreign to those who contemplate, lest their contemplation be occupied in earthly things.

VERS. 20.-- Every one of the winged things that goeth upon 329Dfour feet, etc. They who seem to be like the contemplatives, but cling like creeping things to earthly concupiscences, even if they walk upon four feet, that is, if they feign the evangelical preaching, which calls to penance; because they do not truly embrace the social life, serving pleasures, they are abominable.

VERS. 21.-- But whatsoever walketh, etc. Because, although they are in the flesh, they walk not according to the flesh (II Cor. X), but leap away from filthy actions, and mingle with earthly men, that they may instruct and teach: as the Lord with sinners and publicans (Matth. IX). We ought to have legs loftier than our feet, lest we halt on either foot, as 330Athe Jews in the law and the prophets; but let us have straight legs, like the Cherubim, of whom it is said: Their legs were straight, and their feet winged (Ezech. I). [Vulg. Ez 1:7 et pedes eorum, pedes recti, et planta pedis eorum quasi planta pedis vituli; no received verse reads crura eorum recta]

VERS. 22.-- The bruchus. Which leaps, but less than the attacus, or the locust, which leap more. Such are they who associate with sinners, for association, not for consent or imitation.

The ophiomachus. Which fights with serpents; but for us the fight is with the ancient serpent and his angels (Apoc. XII).

VERS. 25.-- That he bear. Like one who calls evil good, and bitter sweet (Isa. V): for by praising the evil from which he ought to have restrained, he makes it his own and bears it.

VERS. 26.-- Every beast that. (ISICH.) The law passes over no sin; whatsoever things are forbidden in the Gospel 330Bare expressed by the riddles of the Law, that in the mouth of two witnesses every word may stand (Matth. XVIII), [Vulg. Mt 18:16 duorum, vel trium testium, "of two or three witnesses"] and that in both laws thou mayest praise God, and, as it forbids, mayest flee evil.

VERS. 27.-- That walketh upon hands, etc. He who transgresses the evangelical manner of life, but walks upon his hands, laying on hands, that is, for the sake of filthy lucre (I Tim. IV), is rightly compared to all beasts, so that thou mayest call him either an adulterer, or covetous, a murderer and the like: for by a wicked imposition of hands he communicates with them all.

VERS. 28.-- Whosoever shall carry carcasses of this kind. By speaking false testimonies, by calling the worthy unworthy, and the clean unclean.

VERS. 29.-- The weasel. Voracity. For the mouse for the 330Cvoracity of its belly shuns no dangers.

The crocodile. Which, of hard skin and malicious, is fed on mud.

VERS. 30.-- The chameleon. It shows pity, and out of weakness feigns itself tame, though it is cruel.

The mole. A blind animal begotten of the earth. For it is created, as they say, of earth rained upon and muddy, but first corrupted.

VERS. 31.-- He that toucheth, etc. If they were unclean by nature, he would be unclean who touched them even living. The carrion of such are their actions, that is, guile, theft, duplicity, and things of this kind, in which is the death of the soul.

VERS. 32.-- And upon what, etc. (ISICH.) The lawgiver uses a wonderful division, etc., as far as as well 330Da wooden vessel as a garment.

VERS. 34.-- All food, etc. By food and drink he understands good works, therefore he said: The food which you shall eat, and the liquid that is drunk, etc. Because both the food and the drink of evil actions is forbidden, lest anyone partake of them. If therefore any carrion whatever fall upon good actions, which are in every vessel, that is, in every man, the action will necessarily be unclean. For if upon a good action there fall pride, guile, hypocrisy, or any carrion whatever, that is, anything bringing death to him that partakes, this man also will be unclean: for an evil intention falling upon a good action defiles it.

VERS. 34.-- If water be poured upon it. (AUG., quaest. 37 in Levit.) Concerning the vessel, that is, which 331Awas made unclean by unclean carrion, if perchance it held water.

Water. Of baptism: for the discourse is about the baptized; but of the unbelieving he makes no mention. Whence Paul: I have written to you not to keep company with fornicators (I Cor. III), not indeed with the fornicators of this world, etc.

VERS. 35.-- Or ovens. By this the masters are designated, in whom the foods of sacred doctrine are cooked by the working and the fire of the Holy Spirit. Nor is it a wonder: for Moab is called a cauldron: Moab is the pot of my hope (Rom. II).

Pots with feet, etc. Because they who teach not to steal, steal; and they who preach not to commit adultery, commit adultery: yet the doctrine is clean. Whence fountains and the rest.

331BVERS. 37.-- If it fall. (ISICH.) He leaves nothing unexamined, etc., as far as if any carrion whatever fall upon them, they become unclean.

VERS. 39.-- He that toucheth its carcass shall be unclean. (ISICH.) We ought to abominate the life of the wicked, to imitate that of the just. For he who touches the life of the evil shall be unclean, until he has done penance.

VERS. 40.-- He that eateth. That is, has defended it as an advocate, and by helping has transferred it upon himself: let him wash his garments, that is, his wicked actions, until he has done penance.

VERS. 41.-- Every thing that creepeth upon the earth. This the lawgiver set forth above, but he repeats it, admonishing the hearer to the frequent meditation of the law.

VERS. 47.-- And that you may know what to eat. These foods 331Cdo not cleanse or pollute us, but we ourselves: for we quicken the things that are eaten, and as every food is good (I Petr. II), and nothing is to be rejected which is received with thanksgiving; so the things that are eaten, that is, which nourish the soul, namely good actions, we quicken, if we use them well: if ill, we mortify them. For he who makes an alms out of rapine mortifies it, and takes away its power; and they who abstain from foods, but disparage their brethren, mortify their abstinence.

CAPUT XII.

VERS. 2.-- Mulier si suscepto semine. (ISID., quaest. in Levit., tom. V.) Septimana est praesens vita, etc., usque ad nec in futuro purgabitur.

331DImmunda erit. (ISICH.) Quia scilicet sator immundus, ut haeres Adae, qui per transgressionem immundus, et ita uxori mistus. Postquam enim exiit de paradiso cognovit uxorem suam, et ita propagatur humanum genus.

Septem diebus. (ID.) Quia Adam, etc., usque ad quando Adam separatus a paradiso in hanc miseriam decidit.

Die octavo, etc. (ID.) Dies octavus, etc., usque ad Inobedientia autem immunditiam nobis sordemque contraxit.

VERS. 4.-- Triginta tribus diebus, etc. (ID.) septem additis, etc., usque ad incertum est enim an feminam, an marem pariant.

332A(AUG., quaest. 40 in Levit.) Quae differentia est inter septem dies, etc., usque ad in feminae octoginta.

Omne sanctum non tanget. Quid sanctuarium vult intelligere cum tabernaculum nonnisi sacerdotes debeant ingredi, et usque ad velum secundi: ultra velum autem, ubi erat arca, summus tantum sacerdos introibat? Forte ante tabernaculum, ubi altare sacrificiorum est, potuit dici sanctuarium. Nam saepe sanctus locus appellatur etiam atrium, cum dicitur: In loco sancto edent ea. Illuc forsitan intrabant mulieres dona sua offerentes, quae altari imponerent.

VERS. 6.-- Cumque expleti fuerint. Intentionem aperit, quia scilicet, pollutionem naturales motus non faciunt, sed peccatum, de quo dicit David: Ecce enim in iniquitatibus conceptus (Psal. L), etc., 332BMatrem naturam appellans, et immunditiam peccati Adae et Evae in totum genus humanum descendisse significans. Ideo Moyses, completis diebus purificationis holocaustum, et pro peccato praecepit immolari; evidenter ostendens quia imago Dei perfectionem tribuit generi nostro. Nam formationem dies mundationis ostendunt, perfectionem holocaustum.

(AUG., ubi supra. ) Nunquid peperisse peccatum est? etc., usque ad non ut dicerent super filio aut super filia, sed pro filio aut filia.

Notandum autem quam pauper Dominus nasci voluerit, ut non pro illo offerretur agnus et pullus columbinus aut turtur, sed par turturum aut duo pulli columbarum secundum Evangelium, quod scriptura Levitici tunc jussit offerri, si non habuit 332Cagnum manus offerentis, quod satis est magnum.

(BEDA, in Lev., tom. II.) Dominus noster non solum homo, etc., usque ad et post in coelestem Jerusalem bonorum operum victimis Domino commendata perveniet.

VERS. 7.-- A profluvio, etc. Tanquam aliquis quaerat, unde mundabitur quae legitime generavit? ait, a profluvio sanguinis sui, hoc est inobedientia, unde immunditia et poena profluxit, per meditationem et contemplationem legis, cujus negligentia hominibus immunditiam invexit.

CHAPTER XII.

VERS. 2.-- If a woman having received seed. (ISID., quaest. in Levit., tom. V.) The week is the present life, etc., as far as nor shall she be purged in the world to come.

331DShe shall be unclean. (ISICH.) Because, that is, the sower is unclean, as the heir of Adam, who through his transgression was unclean, and so was mingled with his wife. For after he went out of paradise he knew his wife, and so is the human race propagated.

Seven days. (ID.) Because Adam, etc., as far as when Adam, separated from paradise, fell down into this misery.

On the eighth day, etc. (ID.) The eighth day, etc., as far as But disobedience contracted for us uncleanness and filth.

VERS. 4.-- Three and thirty days, etc. (ID.) seven being added, etc., as far as for it is uncertain whether they will bear a female or a male.

332A(AUG., quaest. 40 in Levit.) What difference there is between the seven days, etc., as far as to eighty in the case of a female.

She shall touch no holy thing. What does he wish to be understood as the sanctuary, since none but the priests ought to enter the tabernacle, and that as far as the veil of the second: but beyond the veil, where the ark was, the high priest alone entered? Perhaps that before the tabernacle, where the altar of sacrifices is, could be called the sanctuary. For often the court also is called a holy place, when it is said: In the holy place they shall eat them. Thither perhaps the women entered, offering their gifts, which they were to lay upon the altar.

VERS. 6.-- And when they shall be expired. He opens his intention, namely, that natural motions do not make pollution, but sin, of which David says: For behold I was conceived in iniquities (Psal. L), etc., 332Bcalling nature the mother, and signifying that the uncleanness of the sin of Adam and Eve has come down into the whole human race. Therefore Moses, the days of purification being completed, commanded a holocaust, and a sacrifice for sin, to be immolated; plainly showing that the image of God bestows perfection upon our race. For the days of cleansing show the formation, the holocaust the perfection.

(AUG., ubi supra. ) Is it a sin to have borne a child? etc., as far as not that they should say over a son or over a daughter, but for a son or a daughter.

But it is to be noted how poor the Lord willed to be born, so that there was not offered for him a lamb and a young pigeon or a turtle, but a pair of turtles or two young pigeons according to the Gospel, which the scripture of Leviticus then commanded to be offered, if the hand of the offerer had not 332Ca lamb, which is great enough.

(BEDA, in Lev., tom. II.) Our Lord not only man, etc., as far as and afterwards it shall come into the heavenly Jerusalem, commended to the Lord by the victims of good works.

VERS. 7.-- From the issue, etc. As though someone should ask, from what shall she be cleansed who has brought forth lawfully? he says, from the issue of her blood, that is, from disobedience, whence uncleanness and punishment flowed forth, by the meditation and contemplation of the law, the neglect of which brought uncleanness upon men.

CAPUT XIII.

VERS. 1.-- Locutusque est Dominus. (ISID.) Semel loquitur Deus, et duo audiuntur: Spiritum audit 332DMoyses, ut sublimior; litteram, Aaron, ut inferior, qui litteram nuntiat populo. Unde ipse loquetur ad populum, et erit os tuum, tu autem eris in his quae ad Deum pertinent (Exod. IV).

VERS. 2.-- Homo. (ID.) Aliquando excellenter accipitur, qui Dei imaginem et naturae servat dignitatem. Aliquando peccator, in quo dignitas deformatur, sicut hic.

In cujus carne, etc. In carne et cute lepram portat, qui carnalia vel exteriora suadere certat, ut Corinthiani, qui putabant resurrectionem futuram in voluptate carnis: et in fide manentibus, quamvis carnaliter vivant, non imputari peccata.

Aut quasi lucens. Lepra alia nimis vel moderate 333Aobscura, nondum effervente passione, quae nimia indiget deliberatione. Alia nimis clara et perspicua, cujus aperta cognitio.

Plaga leprae. (ISID.) Lepra falsa doctrina; leprosi haeretici, qui unitatem verae fidei non habentes, varios doctrinae profitentur errores, vera falsis admiscentes et Ecclesiam, sicut lepra corpora, irradiando commaculant.

In corpore lepram gerit, qui Ecclesiae detrahit, sicut Carpocratiani, qui negant resurrectionem carnis, et Novatiani, qui nuptias damnant, et peccantibus auferunt poenitentiam.

Vel ad unum quemlibet filiorum ejus, etc. Cognatorum secundum spiritum, a quo habet sacerdotium, quibus inest perfecta et plena prophetia, ut discernere 333Bpossint subtilia et animae motus absconditos.

VERS. 3.-- Pilos. Cogitationes, quibus anima ornatur, mensque contegitur, a qua generantur, sicut caput capillos generat, quibus ornatur et tegitur. Unde capilli capitis vestri omnes numerati sunt (Matth. X). Inde Nazaraei caput non tondent (Num. VI), quia superfluas cogitationes non habent, sanctas autem deponere sanctum, damnum est.

Humilior carne, etc. Humilior seipso: unde recte veniam non meretur. Vinci enim ab alio majori, portabile est; bonum autem a Deo praeceptum non operari (dividit enim Dominus prout vult), ut seipso quispiam deterior fiat, hoc importabile est.

VERS. 4.-- Recludet eum. Secundo recluditur, ut studiose inspiciatur, et duplici probatione transacta 333Cdistrictum judicium fiat. Manifestum enim erat peccatum sacerdoti, sed animum consentire passioni nesciebat, sed conjiciebat: ideo iterum inspiciebat. Hinc Dominus post negationem, animum Petri quomodo se habeat tertio interrogat (Joan. XI), ut non cito judicemus, sed frequenter inquiramus. Septem diebus recluditur. Septenarius enim praesentis vitae plenitudinem signat, quae septem diebus agitur, quo impleto, peccatum et servitium cessabit. Unde septima in divinis Scripturis requies nominatur.

VERS. 6.-- Mundabit eum, quia scabies est. Sic Paulus eum qui in Corintho fornicatus est (quia manifesta erat passio) sanctorum tabernaculo removit, et Satanae tradidit; quem cum inspexisset secundo, et poenitere vidisset, suscipi jussit (I Cor. V).

333DVERS. 10.-- Cumque color albus in cute fuerit. Manifesta transgressio litterae legis, quae propria Judaeis sicut in sacrificiis et purgationibus et in hujusmodi. Est enim et communis gentibus de adulterio, rapina, avaritia, et similibus.

VERS. 12.-- Sin autem effloruerit. (ISICH.) Ostendit Judaicum populum partim leprosum, ut in parte transgressorem legis: unde eum sacerdos expellit tanquam immundum. Sed toto corpore leprosum Gentilem, qui totam transgreditur legem, non expellit; sed eum mundum judicat, quia legis jugo subjectus non erat.

(HIERON.) Sin autem effloruerit, etc. In hac lepra quasi florente et alba, diversa crimina designat. Quia 334Acum mundialis vitae voluptas quasi candida aestimatur et florens, tunc grave vitiorum contagium grassatur in corpore. Nam aut avaritiae aut libidinis patescunt maculae. Sed avaritiae crimen facile potest resecari, unde: Date eleemosynam et omnia munda erunt vobis. Libido quoque jejuniis et abstinentia mundatur. Sed omnibus per sacerdotem medela adhibetur. Alia enim sacrificiorum oblationibus mundatur; alia est, quae facilius; alia, quae extra castra posita mundatur; alia, quae omnino non mundatur.

VERS. 14.-- Caro vivens, etc. (ISICH.) Judaicum mandatum, quod vivere dicitur quantum ad mandatum, vel quia bene intellectum affert vitam.

VERS. 18.-- Caro enim, etc. (ISID.) In carne viva 334Blepram gestat, qui de anima, quae vita carnis est, aliquid falsum existimat. Sicut Luciferiani qui dicunt animam de carnis substantia propagatam; et Arabici qui animam cum corpore mori putant.

VERS. 19.-- Cicatrix, etc. (ID.) In cicatrice sanati vulneris lepram portant, qui post agnitionem fidei et medicinam Dei quam in Christo suscipiunt, rursus aliquod indicium erroris aut perfidiae veteris dogmatis ostendunt.

Alba. Quia ex vulnere legalis transgressionis; lex enim alba et a sole justitiae non calefacta (Act. XV).

Subrufa. Quia de praevaricatione evangelica, sicut idolothytum sanguinem, vel suffocatum comedere; utrumque autem fit contra Christum, cujus est Lex et Evangelium.

334CVERS. 20.-- Contaminabit. Quia in habitu est peccatum, et animae mala depastio per ulcus incubuit. Unde lepram ex vulnere efflorentem vocavit.

Caro et cutis quam ignis exusserat, etc. (ISICH.) Supradicta videtur repetere, sed partim eadem dicit, partim adjicit, alteram quippe passionem, sed eamdem ponit indicationem. Egit enim de ulcere quod aliqua aegritudine neglectae carnis generatur et proficit. Ulcus ergo carnis nostrae est voluptas, ebrietas, gastrimargia et hujusmodi, quae luxuriante corpore et in semetipso pruriente proveniunt. Nunc autem de plaga majori, id est exustione ignis. De quo dicitur: Ignis adversarios consumet (Isa. LXXIV).

VERS. 25.-- Quia plaga, etc. In cicatrice exustionis lepram habent Manichaei, qui in abstinentiae cruciatu 334Dcorpora sua exurunt, et per infidelitatem, non munditiam inde, sed lepram gignunt.

VERS. 29.-- Vir sive mulier. Qui scilicet in divinitate errat, sicut haeretici, qui non solum Patrem factorem mundi negant, sed etiam actorem malorum affirmant, ut Manichaeus.

Vel barba, etc. (ISID.) In barba lepram gerunt qui de apostolis et discipulis Christi perverse sentiunt, et falsa praedicare confingunt. Barba enim est ornamentum viri, et apostoli doctores corporis Christi.

(ISICH.) Sex species leprae invenimus legislatorem posuisse, etc., usque ad quia erroris sui perfidiam aperta pravitate defendunt.

(ID.) Diversos colores leprae supponit, etc., usque 335Aad linguam mendacem significat et imbecillitatem.

VERS. 31.-- Recludet eum septem diebus, et die, etc., non tam pro poena quam districtae probationis causa; tempus enim in omnibus servandum, districtius in his quorum lingua circa divinitatem se effrenat.

VERS. 34.-- Si die septimo. Quia verbum in quo delinquitur, si semel negat qui peccavit, dijudicari non oportet. Quis enim reum judicat per quod confessus est lingua, voluntate se protulisse negat?

Lotisque vestibus mundus erit, etc. (HIERON.) Alia ante baptismum commissa, etc., usque ad de quo dicitur: Qui peccat in Spiritum sanctum, non remittetur ei, etc.

VERS. 37.-- Capilli nigri, id est, si luxerit impietatem suam, et veram egerit poenitentiam. Lugentes 335Benim et poenitentes nigro habitu deformantur.

VERS. 38.-- Vir sive mulier, etc. (ISICH.) In praevaricatione divini dogmatis, etc., usque ad: quibus non vivant.

VERS. 40.-- Calvus ac mundus est. Simplex, non stultis; nemo enim est cui saltem pauci non remaneant capilli.

VERS. 44.-- Quicunque ergo maculatus, etc. (ISID.) Haeretici ab Ecclesia ejiciuntur donec purgentur. Dissutis tunicis, id est, manifestatis secretis sedere jubentur. Capite nudato, ut nuditas eorum a cunctis videatur. Ore clauso, ne ulterius impia loquantur.

VERS. 46.-- Habitabit extra castra. Sic Joannes intelligibilem leprosum ab intelligibili habitaculo excludit: 335CQui hanc doctrinam non affert, non recipiatis eum in domum, et ave ei non dixeritis (II Joan. 10).

(ISICH.) Ridicula sunt haec secundum litteram interpretata, etc., usque ad ne dicatur ei, Qui praedicas non furandum, furaris; peccatori autem dixit Deus: Quare tu enarras justitias meas? (Rom. II.)

VERS. 47.-- Vestis lanea, etc. (ID.) Lanea, pretiosa et animum calefacientia, etc., usque ad scilicet et bona opera.

VERS. 48-- In stamine, etc. (HIERON.) Stamen anima est: quae cum tentationis fuso in filiorum tenuitatem torquetur, firmioris virtutis soliditate constringitur. Stat ergo anima, ut tamen erecta, corporis sensus subjacet ut trama; alia autem sunt 335Dcorporis, alia animae peccata.

(ISICH.) Qui solam litteram attendunt, etc., usque ad sed et de ea quae circa virtutes evenire consuevit.

(ID.) Lepra, in veste linea, etc., usque ad in conversatione hujus vitae.

Pellis vel quidquid, etc. (ID.) Tentatio tribulationis, etc., usque ad nec sicut munda temere suscipiat.

VERS. 49.-- Si alba, etc. (ID.) LXX: Viridans atque flavescens, id est, superbia, quae ad modicum floret: unde, Sicut olera herbarum cito decident (Psal. XXXVI), aut cenodoxia. Vult enim Christus virtutes esse sine vana gloria.

VERS. 56.-- Abrumpet eum. Per poenitentiam 336Aabscindet passionem; superbia abrumpitur, admota humilitate; vana gloria, amota adulatione, et vana laude.

VERS. 57.-- Lepra volatilis. (GREG., hom. X in Ezech.) Aliquando nos mala egisse cognoscimus et vitamus, etc., usque ad per immoderatam continentiam ad iracundiae vitium transit.

CHAPTER XIII.

VERS. 1.-- And the Lord spoke. (ISID.) God speaks once, and two things are heard: Moses hears the Spirit, 332Das the loftier; Aaron the letter, as the lower, who announces the letter to the people. Whence he shall speak to the people, and shall be thy mouth, but thou shalt be in those things which pertain to God (Exod. IV). [Vulg. Ex 4:16 ipse loquetur pro te ad populum … tu autem eris ei in his quae ad Deum pertinent, "he shall speak for thee" and "thou shalt be to him"]

VERS. 2.-- A man. (ID.) Sometimes it is taken in an excellent sense, of him who keeps the image of God and the dignity of his nature. Sometimes of a sinner, in whom that dignity is deformed, as here.

In whose flesh, etc. He bears leprosy in flesh and skin who strives to persuade to carnal or outward things, as the Corinthians, who thought that the resurrection would be in the pleasure of the flesh: and that to those who remain in the faith, although they live carnally, sins are not imputed.

Or as it were shining. One leprosy is exceedingly, or moderately, 333Adark, the passion not yet boiling up, which needs much deliberation. Another is exceedingly clear and manifest, whose recognition is open.

The plague of leprosy. (ISID.) Leprosy is false doctrine; the lepers are heretics, who, not having the unity of the true faith, profess various errors of doctrine, mixing true things with false, and by their spreading defile the Church, as leprosy defiles bodies.

He bears leprosy in the body who disparages the Church, as the Carpocratians, who deny the resurrection of the flesh, and the Novatians, who condemn marriages, and take away penance from those who sin.

Or to any one of his sons, etc. Of kinsmen according to the spirit, from whom he has the priesthood, in whom there is a perfect and full prophecy, that they may be able to discern 333Bsubtle things and the hidden motions of the soul.

VERS. 3.-- The hairs. Thoughts, by which the soul is adorned and the mind is covered, and by which they are begotten, as the head begets the hairs of the head, by which it is adorned and covered. Whence the hairs of your head are all numbered (Matth. X). [Vulg. Mt 10:30 Vestri autem capilli capitis omnes numerati sunt; this word order is that of Lc 12:7] Hence the Nazarites do not shave the head (Num. VI), because they have no superfluous thoughts; but to lay aside holy ones is a loss to a holy man.

Lower than the flesh, etc. Lower than himself: whence rightly he does not merit pardon. For to be overcome by another and a greater is bearable; but not to work the good commanded by God (for the Lord divides as he wills), so that a man becomes worse than himself, this is unbearable.

VERS. 4.-- He shall shut him up. He is shut up a second time, that he may be diligently inspected, and, a double trial being gone through, 333Ca strict judgment may be made. For the sin was manifest to the priest, but he did not know that the mind consented to the passion, but conjectured it: therefore he inspected again. Hence the Lord, after the denial, asks a third time how Peter's mind stands (Joan. XI), that we may not judge quickly, but may inquire often. He is shut up for seven days. For the number seven marks the fullness of the present life, which is passed in seven days; and when it is fulfilled, sin and servitude shall cease. Whence in the divine Scriptures the seventh is named rest.

VERS. 6.-- He shall cleanse him, because it is a scab. So Paul removed from the tabernacle of the saints him who had committed fornication at Corinth (because the passion was manifest), and delivered him to Satan; and when he had inspected him a second time, and had seen him repent, he commanded him to be received (I Cor. V).

333DVERS. 10.-- And when the colour shall be white in the skin. A manifest transgression of the letter of the law, which is proper to the Jews, as in sacrifices and purgations and things of this kind. For there is also one common to the nations, concerning adultery, rapine, avarice, and the like.

VERS. 12.-- But if it shall flower forth. (ISICH.) He shows the Jewish people to be leprous in part, as being in part a transgressor of the law: whence the priest casts him out as unclean. But the Gentile, leprous in his whole body, who transgresses the whole law, he does not cast out; but judges him clean, because he was not subject to the yoke of the law.

(HIERON.) But if it shall flower forth, etc. In this leprosy, as it were flowering and white, he designates diverse crimes. Because 334Awhen the pleasure of worldly life is esteemed as white and flowering, then the grievous contagion of vices rages in the body. For either the spots of avarice or those of lust break out. But the crime of avarice can easily be cut away, whence: Give alms, and all things shall be clean unto you. [Vulg. Lc 11:41 et ecce omnia munda sunt vobis, "and behold, all things are clean unto you"] Lust also is cleansed by fastings and abstinence. But to all of them a remedy is applied through the priest. For one is cleansed by the oblations of sacrifices; another there is which is cleansed more easily; another which is cleansed after being placed outside the camp; another which is not cleansed at all.

VERS. 14.-- Living flesh, etc. (ISICH.) The Jewish commandment, which is said to live so far as regards the commandment, or because, well understood, it brings life.

VERS. 18.-- For the flesh, etc. (ISID.) He bears leprosy in living flesh 334Bwho judges anything false concerning the soul, which is the life of the flesh. As the Luciferians, who say that the soul is propagated from the substance of the flesh; and the Arabici, who think that the soul dies together with the body.

VERS. 19.-- A scar, etc. (ID.) They bear leprosy in the scar of a healed wound who, after the acknowledgment of the faith and the medicine of God which they receive in Christ, again show some token of error or of the perfidy of the old doctrine.

White. Because it comes from the wound of the transgression of the law; for the law is white and not warmed by the sun of justice (Act. XV).

Somewhat reddish. Because it comes from an evangelical prevarication, as to eat what is offered to idols, or blood, or what is strangled; and both are done against Christ, whose are the Law and the Gospel.

334CVERS. 20.-- It shall defile. Because the sin is in a settled habit, and the evil consuming of the soul has settled in through the ulcer. Whence he called it a leprosy flowering forth out of the wound.

The flesh and the skin which the fire had burned, etc. (ISICH.) He seems to repeat the things said above, but partly he says the same, partly he adds; for he sets down another passion, but the same indication. For he treated of the ulcer which is begotten by some sickness of neglected flesh, and grows. The ulcer, then, of our flesh is pleasure, drunkenness, gluttony and things of this kind, which come forth when the body runs riot and itches within itself. But now he treats of a greater plague, that is, of the burning of fire. Of which it is said: The fire shall consume the adversaries (Isa. LXXIV). [Vulg. Is 26:11 ignis adversarios tuos devoret, Heb 10:27 ignis aemulatio, quae consumptura est adversarios; no verse reads as printed, and Isaiah has sixty-six chapters]

VERS. 25.-- Because the plague, etc. In the scar of a burning the Manichees have leprosy, who in the torment of abstinence 334Dburn up their own bodies, and through unbelief beget thence not cleanness, but leprosy.

VERS. 29.-- A man or a woman. That is, who errs concerning the divinity, as the heretics, who not only deny the Father to be the maker of the world, but even affirm him to be the author of evils, as Manichaeus.

Or the beard, etc. (ISID.) They bear leprosy in the beard who think perversely of the apostles and disciples of Christ, and feign that they preached false things. For the beard is the ornament of a man, and the apostles are the teachers of the body of Christ.

(ISICH.) We find that the lawgiver set down six kinds of leprosy, etc., as far as because they defend the perfidy of their error with open depravity.

(ID.) He sets down diverse colours of leprosy, etc., as far 335Aas it signifies a lying tongue and weakness.

VERS. 31.-- He shall shut him up seven days, and on the day, etc., not so much for punishment as for the sake of a strict trial; for the time is to be observed in all cases, more strictly in those whose tongue runs unbridled about the divinity.

VERS. 34.-- If on the seventh day. Because the word in which one offends, if he who has sinned once denies it, ought not to be adjudged. For who judges a man guilty by that which he has confessed with his tongue, when he denies that he uttered it with his will?

And his garments being washed he shall be clean, etc. (HIERON.) Some things committed before baptism, etc., as far as of which it is said: He that sinneth against the Holy Spirit, it shall not be forgiven him, etc. [Vulg. Mt 12:32 qui autem dixerit contra Spiritum sanctum, non remittetur ei, "he that shall speak against"]

VERS. 37.-- Black hairs, that is, if he has mourned his impiety, and has done true penance. For they that mourn 335Band do penance are disfigured with a black habit.

VERS. 38.-- A man or a woman, etc. (ISICH.) In the prevarication of the divine doctrine, etc., as far as: whereby they may not live.

VERS. 40.-- He is bald and clean. Simple, not to the foolish; for there is no one to whom at least a few hairs do not remain.

VERS. 44.-- Whosoever therefore is spotted, etc. (ISID.) Heretics are cast out of the Church until they be purged. With their tunics unsewn, that is, their secrets made manifest, they are commanded to sit. With the head bared, that their nakedness may be seen by all. With the mouth closed, that they may not speak impious things any further.

VERS. 46.-- He shall dwell outside the camp. So John shuts out the intelligible leper from the intelligible dwelling: 335CHe that bringeth not this doctrine, receive him not into the house, and say not God speed to him (II Joan. 10). [Vulg. 2Jo 10 Si quis venit ad vos, et hanc doctrinam non affert, nolite recipere eum in domum, nec ave ei dixeritis]

(ISICH.) These things are ridiculous when interpreted according to the letter, etc., as far as lest it be said to him, Thou that preachest that men should not steal, stealest; but to the sinner God hath said: Why dost thou declare my justices? (Rom. II.)

VERS. 47.-- A woollen garment, etc. (ID.) Woollen, precious, and warming the mind, etc., as far as namely, and good works.

VERS. 48-- In the warp, etc. (HIERON.) The warp is the soul: which, when it is twisted by the spindle of temptation into the fineness of sons, is bound together by the solidity of a firmer virtue. The soul, then, stands, yet as erect; the sense of the body lies beneath as the woof; but some sins are 335Dof the body, others of the soul.

(ISICH.) They who attend to the letter alone, etc., as far as but also of that which is wont to come about concerning the virtues.

(ID.) Leprosy, in a linen garment, etc., as far as in the manner of life of this life.

A skin or whatsoever, etc. (ID.) The temptation of tribulation, etc., as far as nor let him rashly receive it as clean.

VERS. 49.-- If white, etc. (ID.) LXX: Greening and growing yellow, that is, pride, which flowers for a little: whence, As the herbs of the field they shall quickly fall (Psal. XXXVI), or vainglory. For Christ wills the virtues to be without vain glory.

VERS. 56.-- He shall break it off. Through penance 336Ahe shall cut off the passion; pride is broken off when humility is brought near; vain glory, when flattery and vain praise are taken away.

VERS. 57.-- A flying leprosy. (GREG., hom. X in Ezech.) Sometimes we recognise that we have done evil, and we avoid it, etc., as far as through immoderate continence he passes over to the vice of wrath.

CAPUT XIV.

VERS. 1.-- Locutusque est. (ISICH.) Quia maculas, etc., usque ad ipse enim omne peccatum curavit.

VERS. 4.-- Duos passeres. (ID.) Divinitatem, scilicet et humanitatem Christi. Quae munda, quia peccatum non habuit; et viva, quia ex peccato non fuit obnoxia mortis.

336BQuos vesci, etc. Quia, Verbum caro factum est, et nisi manducaverimus carnem Filii hominis, non habebimus vitam in nobis.

Lignum cedri. (ISICH.) Crucem, quae contra omnia fortis, sicut cedrus fortis et imputribilis.

Vermiculum. LXX: Coccinum tortum passionem, scilicet quia coccinum rubeum est, tortum passibilitate, et impassibilitate contextum.

Hyssopum. Gratiam Spiritus sancti, quae nostrae emundationi cooperatur. Unde: Asperges me, Domine, hyssopo, et mundabor, etc. (Psal. L.)

VERS. 5.-- Super aquas, etc. Baptismatis. Quicunque in Christo baptizati sumus, in morte ipsius baptizati sumus (Rom. VI).

VERS. 6.-- Tinget in sanguine, etc. Quia comprehenduntur 336Comnia in virtute baptismi. Vivens passer in sanguine occisi passeris tingitur, quia non in hominis passione tantum, sed Dei et hominis baptizamur.

VERS 7.-- Septies ut jure purgetur. Pro septiformi Spiritu, in quo omnis mundatio et remissio; vel ut purgetur anima a septem daemonibus.

Et dimittet passerem. Quia quodammodo tenetur sub contemplatione humani intellectus.

VERS. 8.-- Vestimenta sua. Opera et si quas virtutes habuerit, quia et has peccatum polluit.

Purificatusque, vere. Superius tamen dictum est lepram mundatam, sed sic intelligendum est, mundari aptam.

Extra tabernaculum. Consortium sanctorum, a 336Dquo excluduntur peccatores. Unde cum hujusmodi nec cibum sumere (I Cor. 5).

VERS. 9.-- Capillos. Cogitationes quae ornatum homini ut rationali, et multa disceptanti praebent: sicut capilli capitis.
