Et supercilia. Quae hominem contemplativum de sublimibus et divinioribus constituunt, quod supercilia in altioribus sita significant.

Totius corporis. Etiam verendarum partium, ut nulla remaneat veterum cogitationum, ut totus novus et quasi modo genitus veterem hominem exuat, novum induat. Quomodo enim rasis pilis succedunt novi, si novae cogitationes ad meliora pullulant veteribus abrasis.

337AVERS. 10.-- Die octavo. Quo Dominus surrexit, quo leprosus ingreditur castra, quae patriarcha Jacob vidit occurrentibus sibi angelis: unde locum castra Dei appellavit (Gen. XXXII).

Postquam. Prima die, qua egreditur sacerdos, extra castra immolari ad immundationem leprosi praecepit, octava die alia sacrificia substituit. Haec enim eamdem vim habent quam prima: quia omne tempus in hebdomadibus terminatur. Octava ergo et prima est quae subsequitur. Et notandum quod lepram, id est peccatum summopere vitandum ostendit, quod tam diligenter purgari praecepit.

Agnos immaculatos, etc. Omnia haec figurant sacrificium Salvatoris, qui pro nobis hostia fuit. Ex utroque sexu sacrificium fit, quia utrumque sexum 337Bsalvavit.

(HIER.) In duobus agnis duo adventus Christi, quibus credendum est a lepra peccatorum mundari, primo enim venit et passus est: secundo in gloria venturus est.

Anniculam. Anniculum jubet esse sacrificium tanquam perfectum: perfecta enim in praedictis animalibus annicula sunt: unde et tunc generationi apta sunt.

Et tres decimas. Qui enim mundatur sacrificio Unigeniti, debet fide sanctae Trinitatis signari, tres decimas similae, id est Trinitatem, Patrem et Filium, et Spiritum sanctum habentem in unaquaque persona perfectionem: denarius enim perfectus est.

Similae. Unigeniti. Quamvis enim Unigeniti passio 337Cfuerit, opus tamen est totius Trinitatis: unde in nomine Patris, Filii et Spiritus sancti baptizamur, dum in mortem Christi baptizamur.

(ISID.) Est lepra peccati, etc., usque ad et consequi veniam.

Olei sextarium. Quia fide intelligitur et agitur unumquodque praedictorum. Sextarius namque mensura est, nec plus nec minus capientis: quod enim plus est, effunditur, si minus est, non est sextarius. Sic fides ad mentis nostrae mensuram datur, nec capit plus aut minus quam a Deo distribuitur: quod enim plus est aut minus, extra fidem est.

Olei sextarium, pinguem Dei misericordiam et gratiam: et si clarissimam sanctimoniae faciem praeferre non valuit, lucernam fidei non exstinguat.

337DVERS. 14.-- Supra extremum. Ut auditum muniat, et cautum reddat: ne amplius vocem serpentis audiat.

Et pedis. Ut in conspectu Dei motio nostra et via dirigatur. His enim supradictis custoditis per sanguinem agni et fidem ejus, quae nobis per misericordiam et compassionem ipsius donatur, intelligibilis lepra fugatur.

VERS. 16.-- Et asperget, etc. Praesumit enim talis sacerdos apud Deum, quoniam mundare et dimittere peccata potuerit: unde septies aspergit, quia numerus est remissionis et gratiae.

VERS. 17.-- Quod autem, etc. Ut fides sacerdotis in operibus ostendatur. Non enim aliter vim suam 338Aexerit, nisi manu assumpta, id est, actione confirmata et signata. Mittit autem oleum in sinistram manum, et intingit digitum dextrum in oleo, ut per arma justitiae a dextris et sinistris conversetur, per gloriam et ignobilitatem, per infamiam et bonam famam.

VERS. 19.-- Et faciet sacrificium, etc. Per ea quae dicta sunt de delictis, possunt ea, quae pro peccato sunt, conjici: vicina enim sunt et communia tanquam in communi materia.

Holocaustum, etc. Christi sacrificium prius nos a delictis et peccatis mundat, et tunc nostrum conficitur holocaustum. Cum enim intelligibili emundatione corpus nostrum et animam sanctificantes facti sumus spirituales, tunc digni ad ascensionem altaris 338Befficimur: tunc enim plenam emundationem accipimus, nec alia indigemus. Sunt namque emundationum profectus, quaedam enim a pollutione leprae liberat; alia inducit in castra; alia plenissime mundatos ad altare praesentat.

VERS. 21.-- Quod si pauper est et non potest manus ejus, etc. (HIERON.) Hoc est, si divitias coelestium charismatum non habet, verbi gratia, si virginitatem servare non valuit, thorum custodiat immaculatum; si martyrii non potuit reportare tropaeum, se saltem servet invictum.

Quod si pauper est, etc. Mirabiliter condescendit et veniam tribuens paupertati, congrua pauperi sacrificia praecipit, ne obstupescat si majora viribus sacrificia 338Cexigantur. Qui tamen non habet panem aut pecuniam, unde emat agnum aut columbam aut turturem? Ad spiritualia ergo horum occasione vocamur, ut lepram, malitiam; poenitentiam, emundationem intelligamus. Poenitentiae sunt opera necessaria, quae valeant delere peccata. Unde: Facite ergo fructus dignos poenitentiae (Luc. III). Sunt autem plures qui non possunt solvere quod exigit districtio poenitentiae. Exigit enim orationem assiduam, eleemosynam, vigilias, sacrum jejunium, et ut possit dicere: Factae sunt lacrymae meae panes die ac nocte; et, Lavabo per singulas noctes lectum meum, lacrymis meis, etc. (Psal. XIV.) Et, Quia cinerem tanquam panem manducabam, etc. (Psal. CI.) Ignoscit his qui volunt, sed non possunt aliam viam poenitentiae, 338Did est, alia sacrificiorum genera exquirit.

VERS. 20.-- Ponet super extremum auriculae dextrae illius qui, etc. (ORIG., hom. 8 in Levit.) Vides quomodo ultimae et summae purificationis, etc., usque ad et per omnia Patris Filius reparetur.

VERS. 34.-- Chanaan. (ISICH.) Terra Chananaeorum Abrahae promissa est, etc., usque ad quod ibi patet Salutate Nympham et domesticam ejus ecclesiam (Col. IV).

VERS. 40.-- Extra civitatem. Non tantum extra domum, quia de universali Ecclesia sunt ejiciendi, quae est civitas Dei vivi.

VERS. 42.-- Lapidesque alios. Dogmata veritatis, de quibus dicitur: Lapides vivi volvuntur super terram 339A(Zach. IX); et alibi: Quicunque aedificaverit super fundamentum aurum et argentum, etc. (I Cor. III).

VERS. 44.-- Reversam lepram et parietes, etc. Id est, si, ejectis pravis dogmatibus, expositione subtili rursus impietatis doctrina inveniatur aspersa.

VERS. 48.-- Quod si introiens, etc. Memento quae legeris in mundatione leprosi. Manifeste enim apparet quia ecclesiam haereticorum exprimit; qui contra dispensationem incarnationis Christi agit: et ideo ad domum mundandum talia sacrificia adjicit, in quibus mysteria carnis et passionis Christi possint exprimi, et peccata in se commissa sanari.

VERS. 54.-- Ista est lex. Quia late leges leprae exposuit, necessario recapitulavit, prius genus, 339Bdeinde partes exponit.

Leprae. Impiae doctrinae, quae contingit etiam laicis, ideo additum est: Vir aut mulier in quo fuerit plaga leprae et eat. Si enim sacerdotes tantum intellexisset, mulierem non addidisset. Post haec lepram, quae est in virtutibus, posuit, haec enim sunt animae vestimenta. Inde quae est in Ecclesiae doctoribus, qui sunt parietes domus. Tandem cicatricis et erumpentium papularum memoriam facit: lepram veram describens, eam scilicet quae est in peccatis vel in dubiis vel manifestis.

CHAPTER XIV.

VERS. 1.-- And he spoke. (ISICH.) Because stains, etc., as far as for he himself healed every sin.

VERS. 4.-- Two sparrows. (ID.) The divinity, namely, and the humanity of Christ. Which are clean, because he had no sin; and alive, because out of sin he was not liable to death.

336BWhich to eat, etc. Because, The Word was made flesh, and unless we shall have eaten the flesh of the Son of man, we shall not have life in us. [Vulg. Jn 6:54 nisi manducaveritis carnem Filii hominis, et biberitis ejus sanguinem, non habebitis vitam in vobis, second person plural, and with the drinking of his blood]

Cedar wood. (ISICH.) The cross, which is strong against all things, as the cedar is strong and cannot rot.

Scarlet. The LXX: twisted crimson, that is, the passion; namely because crimson is red, and twisted, woven together of passibility and of impassibility.

Hyssop. The grace of the Holy Spirit, which works together toward our cleansing. Whence: Thou shalt sprinkle me, O Lord, with hyssop, and I shall be cleansed, etc. (Psal. L.) [Vulg. Ps 50:9 Asperges me hyssopo, et mundabor, without the vocative Domine]

VERS. 5.-- Over the waters, etc. Of baptism. Whosoever of us have been baptized in Christ, have been baptized in his death (Rom. VI).

VERS. 6.-- He shall dip in the blood, etc. Because all things are comprehended 336Cin the power of baptism. The living sparrow is dipped in the blood of the slain sparrow, because we are baptized not in the passion of a man only, but of God and man.

VERS 7.-- Seven times, that he may be rightly purged. For the sevenfold Spirit, in whom is all cleansing and remission; or that the soul may be purged of the seven demons.

And he shall let the sparrow go. Because in a certain manner he is held under the contemplation of the human understanding.

VERS. 8.-- His garments. His works, and whatever virtues he shall have had, because sin has defiled these too.

And being purified, truly. Yet above the leprosy was said to be cleansed; but it is to be understood thus: fit to be cleansed.

Outside the tabernacle. The fellowship of the saints, from 336Dwhich sinners are shut out. Whence with such a one not even to take food (I Cor. 5).

VERS. 9.-- The hairs. The thoughts, which furnish a man with adornment as a rational being and one that debates many things: even as the hairs of the head.

And the eyebrows. Which set a man in contemplation of things sublime and more divine, which the eyebrows, placed higher up, signify.

Of the whole body. Even of the parts of shame, that none of the old thoughts may remain, that being wholly new and as it were newly born he may put off the old man and put on the new. For just as, when the hairs are shaved, new ones follow, if new thoughts sprout up toward better things when the old have been shaved away.

337AVERS. 10.-- On the eighth day. On which the Lord rose, on which the leper enters the camps which the patriarch Jacob saw when the angels met him: whence he called the place the camps of God (Gen. XXXII).

After that. On the first day, on which the priest goes out, he commanded a sacrifice to be offered outside the camps for the cleansing of the leper; on the eighth day he appointed other sacrifices. For these have the same force as the first, because all time is bounded within weeks. The eighth, therefore, is also the first that follows. And it is to be noted that he shows leprosy, that is, sin, to be shunned with the utmost care, in that he commanded it to be purged so diligently.

Unspotted lambs, etc. All these things figure the sacrifice of the Savior, who was a victim for us. The sacrifice is made of either sex, because he saved 337Bboth sexes.

(HIER.) In the two lambs are the two comings of Christ, by which we must believe that we are cleansed from the leprosy of sins: for first he came and suffered; secondly he is to come in glory.

A yearling. He bids the sacrifice be a yearling, as being perfect: for among the aforesaid animals the yearlings are perfect, whence also they are then fit for generation.

And three tenths. For he who is cleansed by the sacrifice of the Only-begotten ought to be marked with faith in the holy Trinity: three tenths of fine flour, that is, the Trinity, Father and Son and Holy Spirit, having perfection in each several person: for ten is a perfect number.

Of fine flour. Of the Only-begotten. For although it was the passion of the Only-begotten, 337Cyet it is the work of the whole Trinity: whence we are baptized in the name of the Father and of the Son and of the Holy Spirit, while we are baptized into the death of Christ.

(ISID.) There is a leprosy of sin, etc., as far as and to obtain pardon.

A sextary of oil. Because by faith each of the aforesaid is understood and done. For the sextary is a measure, holding neither more nor less: for what is over is poured out; if it is less, it is not a sextary. So faith is given to the measure of our mind, and it holds neither more nor less than is distributed by God: for what is more or less is outside faith.

A sextary of oil, the rich mercy and grace of God: and if a man has not been able to display the most shining face of holiness, let him not put out the lamp of faith.

337DVERS. 14.-- Upon the tip. That he may fortify the hearing and make it wary, lest it hear the serpent's voice any more.

And of the foot. That in the sight of God our motion and our way may be directed. For when these aforesaid things are kept through the blood of the Lamb and faith in him, which is given us through his mercy and compassion, the intelligible leprosy is put to flight.

VERS. 16.-- And he shall sprinkle, etc. For such a priest presumes with God that he may be able to cleanse and remit sins: whence he sprinkles seven times, because it is the number of remission and of grace.

VERS. 17.-- But that which, etc. That the faith of the priest may be shown in works. For it does not otherwise put forth its own force 338Aexcept when the hand is taken up, that is, when it is confirmed and sealed by action. But he pours the oil into the left hand, and dips the right finger in the oil, that he may walk by the arms of justice on the right hand and on the left, by glory and ignobility, by ill fame and good report.

VERS. 19.-- And he shall make a sacrifice, etc. From the things that have been said of trespasses, those that are for sin may be gathered: for they are neighbouring and common, as being in a common matter.

A holocaust, etc. The sacrifice of Christ first cleanses us from trespasses and sins, and then our holocaust is completed. For when by the intelligible cleansing we have been made spiritual, sanctifying our body and our soul, then we are made worthy 338Bof the ascent of the altar: for then we receive full cleansing, and we need no other. For there are degrees of cleansings: one frees from the pollution of leprosy; another brings into the camps; another presents to the altar those who are most fully cleansed.

VERS. 21.-- But if he is poor and his hand is not able, etc. (HIERON.) That is, if he has not the riches of the heavenly graces: for example, if he has not been able to keep virginity, let him keep the marriage bed unspotted; if he could not carry off the trophy of martyrdom, let him at least keep himself unconquered.

But if he is poor, etc. Wonderfully he condescends, and granting pardon to poverty he appoints sacrifices suited to the poor man, lest he be struck dumb if sacrifices beyond his strength 338Cwere required. Yet he who has neither bread nor money, whence should he buy a lamb or a dove or a turtledove? By the occasion of these things, therefore, we are called to spiritual matters, that we may understand leprosy as malice, and penance as cleansing. The works of penance are necessary, such as have power to blot out sins. Whence: Bring forth therefore fruits worthy of penance (Luc. III). But there are many who cannot pay what the strictness of penance requires. For it requires assiduous prayer, almsgiving, watchings, holy fasting, and that a man be able to say: My tears have become my bread day and night; [Vulg. Ps 41:4 Fuerunt mihi lacrimae meae panes die ac nocte, "my tears have been my bread"] and, I will wash my bed every night, with my tears, etc. (Psal. XIV.) And, Because I did eat ashes like bread, etc. (Psal. CI.) He pardons those who will, but cannot; he seeks out another way of penance, 338Dthat is, other kinds of sacrifices.

VERS. 20.-- He shall put upon the tip of the right ear of him who, etc. (ORIG., hom. 8 in Levit.) Thou seest how of the last and highest purification, etc., as far as and that in all things the Son of the Father may be restored.

VERS. 34.-- Chanaan. (ISICH.) The land of the Chanaanites was promised to Abraham, etc., as far as which is plain there: Salute Nympha and the church that is in her house (Col. IV).

VERS. 40.-- Outside the city. Not only outside the house, because they are to be cast out of the universal Church, which is the city of the living God.

VERS. 42.-- And other stones. The doctrines of truth, of which it is said: Living stones are rolled upon the earth 339A(Zach. IX) [Vulg. Zech 9:16 lapides sancti elevabuntur super terram ejus, "holy stones shall be lifted up upon his land"]; and elsewhere: Whosoever shall have built upon the foundation gold and silver, etc. (I Cor. III) [Vulg. 1 Cor 3:12 Si quis autem superaedificat super fundamentum hoc, aurum, argentum, lapides pretiosos].

VERS. 44.-- The leprosy returned and the walls, etc. That is, if, when the depraved doctrines have been cast out, the teaching of impiety is found sprinkled over it again by a subtle exposition.

VERS. 48.-- But if going in, etc. Remember what thou hast read in the cleansing of the leper. For it plainly appears that it expresses the church of the heretics, which acts against the dispensation of the incarnation of Christ: and therefore for the cleansing of the house he adds such sacrifices as those in which the mysteries of the flesh and the passion of Christ can be expressed, and the sins committed in it healed.

VERS. 54.-- This is the law. Because he had set out the laws of leprosy at large, he necessarily recapitulated, expounding first the kind, 339Bthen the parts.

Of leprosy. Of impious teaching, which befalls laymen also, and therefore it was added: A man or a woman in whom shall be the stroke of leprosy, and let him go. [Vulg. Lv 13:29 Vir, sive mulier, in cujus capite vel barba germinaverit lepra] For if he had understood priests only, he would not have added the woman. After these things he set down the leprosy which is in the virtues, for these are the garments of the soul. Then that which is in the doctors of the Church, who are the walls of the house. Last of all he makes mention of the scar and of the breaking-out pustules, describing the true leprosy, namely that which is in sins, whether doubtful or manifest.

CAPUT XV.

(ISICH.) LXX sic, etc., usque ad in virum perfectum, etc.

339CVERS. 2.-- (GREG., lib. XXXIII Moral., cap. 15, tom. II.) Vir qui patitur, etc. Quid est sermo nisi semen? etc., usque ad quam perpetuae mercedis fructum.

(ISICH.) Quia multae species sunt pravae doctrinae, etc., usque ad si particeps fuerit doctrinae ejus sequens discipulos ipsius.

VERS. 9.-- Sagma super quo, etc. (ISICH.) LXX ἐπίσαγμα ὄνον, id est, stragulum asini. Per quod negligentior discipulus et molli corde commutandus intelligitur. Sagmata enim mollia sunt.

VERS. 13.-- Numerabit septem dies. (ID.) Quia per septiformem Spiritum datur remissio peccatorum, cujus septem donis indigent qui perfecte mundari 339Ddebent.

VERS. 14.-- Duos turtures. (ID.) Dignum doctoribus sacrificium. Turtur siquidem meditationem, columba frequenter generans et simplex, Spiritum sanctum designat. Oportet enim doctorem spiritualiter generare: digne ergo hoc sacrificium sacerdotibus et magistris dedicavit.

VERS. 15.-- Coram Domino ut emundetur. Secundum voluntatem et legem Dei, super quae peccavit, emundatur poenitendo, sacrificia orationum offerendo, et deinceps recte vivendo et perfecte sapiendo.

VERS. 16.-- Vir de quo egreditur, etc. (ISICH.) Nisi quis, etc., usque ad mundavit genus humanum.

(ID.) Quia multi, etc., usque ad doctorum suorum traditionibus.

340AVERS. 17.-- Vestem et pellem. Miseriam exprimit originalis peccati, quo genus humanum pollutum etiam insensata polluit. Mulieres exprimit quae libidinis causa miscentur viris ad temporalem usum, quem praestant, et vestimentum, pelles quoque veteratae projiciuntur, et muliebris consuetudo veterascente amicitia respuitur.

VERS. 19.-- Mulier, etc. Per menstrua pravae cogitationes designantur. Quae enim menstrua patitur, alienam carnem non tangit, et propria se polluit. Sic iniquae cogitationes et voluntates, quamvis opere non compleantur, apud Deum pro factis habentur et puniuntur.

Mulier quae redeunte. (ISICH.) Intendit tam mulieres, etc., usque ad quae corruptio humani generis 340Best.

Septem diebus, etc. Universitatem temporum significat, usque ad completionem, qua declaratum est Evangelium: separati enim erant homines a Deo usquequo conjungeret eos evangelica praedicatio.

Qui tetigerit vestimentum ejus, lavabit, etc. Sic oportet ab idololatria abstinere: ut nec idololatras nec eorum discipulos vel fautores, quibus quasi lectis vel vasis utuntur, contingamus, nec communionem cum eis habeamus.

VERS. 25.-- Mulier quae, etc. Quia fluxum seminis patientem, obscena et corrumpentia docentem diximus; et Judaica utentem doctrina de quo semen coitus egreditur; restant qui Gentilium doctrinam 340Cspargunt, quae opportune fluxus sanguinis dicitur, quasi seductio idololatriae: gaudent enim daemones sanguine.

VERS. 29.-- Offeret. Superius fluxum sanguinis patientem sumere duos turtures, aut columbas in sacrificium praecepit, quia magistri locum occupavit; et hic quoque eum qui patitur fluxum sanguinis quia et idololatriae magister fuit.

VERS. 31.-- Docebitis. Hoc neque de leprosis, neque his qui tangunt morticinia, sancivit.

Immunditias. Omne peccatum immunditia est animae, sed idololatria maxime.

Tabernaculum. Interiorem hominem, in quo imago Dei. Verbum quoque caro factum est, et habitabit in 340Dnobis.

VERS. 32.-- Ista. Quae sibi cohaerent, et communem intentionem habent, nectere consuevit, ut ostendat quomodo debeant intelligi, et quae cum quibus cognatione intellectus conjungantur.

Et qui, etc. Qui praecepta Judaica docet, et male docet bona, immundus est, dum quae ad litteram pertinent, extra tempus profert, sicut mundis utitur immundis, unde: Omnia munda mundis, etc.

VERS. 32.-- Et quae, etc. Hi sunt qui impietatem docent, cum genus humanum errore idololatriae sit detentum in menstruis, id est, in separatione malitiae.

CHAPTER XV.

(ISICH.) The LXX thus, etc., as far as unto a perfect man, etc.

339CVERS. 2.-- (GREG., lib. XXXIII Moral., cap. 15, tom. II.) The man that suffers, etc. What is speech but seed? etc., as far as than the fruit of an everlasting reward.

(ISICH.) Because there are many kinds of depraved teaching, etc., as far as if he shall have been a partaker of his teaching, following his disciples.

VERS. 9.-- The saddle upon which, etc. (ISICH.) The LXX ἐπίσαγμα ὄνον, that is, the coverlet of an ass. By which is understood the more negligent disciple, and one to be changed in his soft heart. For saddle-cloths are soft.

VERS. 13.-- He shall number seven days. (ID.) Because through the sevenfold Spirit remission of sins is given, of whose seven gifts they have need who are to be perfectly cleansed 339D.

VERS. 14.-- Two turtledoves. (ID.) A sacrifice worthy of doctors. For the turtledove signifies meditation, and the dove, frequently bearing young and simple, signifies the Holy Spirit. For a doctor must beget spiritually: worthily therefore did he dedicate this sacrifice to priests and to masters.

VERS. 15.-- Before the Lord, that he may be cleansed. According to the will and the law of God, against which he has sinned, he is cleansed by repenting, by offering the sacrifices of prayers, and thereafter by living rightly and by being perfectly wise.

VERS. 16.-- The man from whom there goeth out, etc. (ISICH.) Unless a man, etc., as far as he cleansed the human race.

(ID.) Because many, etc., as far as by the traditions of their teachers.

340AVERS. 17.-- The garment and the skin. He expresses the misery of original sin, by which the human race, being defiled, defiles even things without sense. He expresses the women who for lust's sake are mingled with men for the temporary use which they afford; and the garment and the worn-out skins are cast away, and the custom of women is spat out when the friendship grows old.

VERS. 19.-- The woman, etc. By the monthly courses depraved thoughts are signified. For she who suffers the monthly courses does not touch another's flesh, and defiles her own. So iniquitous thoughts and wills, although they are not fulfilled in deed, are accounted before God as deeds and are punished.

The woman who at the return. (ISICH.) He aims at women as well, etc., as far as which is the corruption of the human race 340B.

Seven days, etc. It signifies the whole of times, up to the completion in which the Gospel was declared: for men were separated from God until the evangelical preaching should join them.

Whosoever shall touch her garment, shall wash, etc. So must a man abstain from idolatry: that we touch neither idolaters nor their disciples or supporters, whom they use as beds or as vessels, nor have communion with them.

VERS. 25.-- The woman who, etc. Because we have said that the man suffering an issue of seed is he who teaches obscene and corrupting things, and that he who uses Judaic teaching is he from whom the seed of coition goes out; there remain those who scatter the teaching of the Gentiles, 340Cwhich is fitly called an issue of blood, being as it were the seduction of idolatry: for the demons rejoice in blood.

VERS. 29.-- He shall offer. Above he commanded that the man suffering an issue of blood take two turtledoves or doves for a sacrifice, because he had occupied the place of a master; and here likewise him who suffers an issue of blood, because he was a master of idolatry too.

VERS. 31.-- Ye shall teach. This he ordained neither of lepers nor of those who touch carcasses.

Uncleannesses. Every sin is an uncleanness of the soul, but idolatry most of all.

The tabernacle. The inward man, in whom is the image of God. The Word also was made flesh, and shall dwell in 340Dus. [Vulg. Jn 1:14 et habitavit in nobis, past tense, "and dwelt among us"]

VERS. 32.-- These. Things which cohere with one another and have a common purpose he is wont to bind together, that he may show how they ought to be understood, and which things are joined with which by kinship of understanding.

And he who, etc. He who teaches the Judaic precepts, and teaches good things badly, is unclean, while he brings forth out of season the things that pertain to the letter, just as he uses clean things as unclean, whence: All things are clean to the clean, etc.

VERS. 32.-- And she who, etc. These are they who teach impiety, since the human race is held in its monthly courses by the error of idolatry, that is, in the separation of malice.

CAPUT XVI.

  • 341A

VERS. 1.-- Locutusque est Dominus, etc. (ISICH.) Unum corpus legis facit, etc., usque ad unde post mortem eorum Dominus haec ad Moysen loquitur.

VERS. 2.-- Loquere ad Aaron, etc. (ID.) Quantum parcat nobis Deus, etc., usque ad quod praesentibus cavetur verbis.

Coram propitiatorio. (ISICH.) LXX: Coram facie propitiatorii, etc., usque ad vel quam ex carne Dominica percipimus.

VERS. 3.-- Vitulum pro peccato offeret. (ISICH.) Mirandus ordo. Cum enim dixisset, quia non est semper ingrediendum in sancta, ne temere et incircumspecte accedere praesumamus, quando et quomodo 341Bingrediamur, ostendit.

Vitulum offeret pro peccato. Initium praedicationis divinae Christus est, et passio ejus, si haec non fuisset et ab errore tam Gentili quam Judaico homines non liberasset, de divinitate Patris loqui non posset. Nemo enim novit Patrem nisi Filius et cui voluerit revelare (Matth. XI).

VERS. 4.-- Tunica linea vestietur, etc. Qui de Deo locuturus est, et ipsum secundum possibilitatem visurus, omnibus armetur virtutibus, quarum quatuor generales sunt, justitia, castitas, fortitudo, sapientia.

Feminalibus. (HIERON. epist. ad Fabiolam, tom. I.) Ubi refertur quomodo Moyses Aaron fratrem suum induerit, etc., usque ad morte moriamur.

341C(ID.) Secundum Hebraeos, etc., usque ad ideo lineis feminalibus femina vel femora velantur.

Cum lotus fuerit. (ISICH.) Aqua baptismi, etc., usque ad sed dispensationem Christi intelligere cupiens.

VERS. 5.-- Suscipietque ab universa multitudine, etc. (ID.) Christus pro nobis immolatus est, etc., usque ad unde Paulus: Mortificati estis per corpus Christi (Rom. VIII).

VERS. 7.-- Duos hircos, etc. (ISICH.) Quia tam pro peccatoribus quam pro justis, etc., usque ad dicens: Non erunt tibi alieni (Exod. XX).

(AUG., quaest. 55 in Levit., tom. III.) Duos hircos, Hircus immolandus, etc., usque ad allegorica significatio.

(ISICH.) Duos hircos stare faciet. Quia Christus 341DDeus et homo, etc., usque ad et alteram capro emissario Christo.

VERS. 10.-- In solitudinem. Id est in sinum Patris, quo nullus accedit nisi ipse et Spiritus sanctus. Abiit ergo, id est, operandi virtutem cohibuit, et portavit iniquitates nostras, non ut haberet, sed ut consumeret. Deus enim ignis consumens est.

In solitudinem, de qua dicitur: In terra deserta invia et inaquosa, etc. (Psal. LXII.) Ibi hostis locum non habet, ubi Deus manet. In coelum enim divinitas tempore passionis abiisse dicitur; non locum mutans, sed quodammodo virtutem cohibens, ut possent impii consummare passionem.

VERS. 11.-- Vitulum. Quando Christus pro Judaicae 342Aplebis liberatione offertur, vitulus dicitur: quia legis justificationem observabat. Unde ait: Non veni legem solvere, sed adimplere (Matth. V); qui factus est ex muliere, ut eos qui sub lege erant, redimeret (Gal. IV). Recte ergo dicitur vitulus, quasi mandatis legis subjugatus.

(ISICH.) Sacrificium Christi esse quidem carnis per virtutem divinitatis, et in beneplacito Patris, et propitiationem pro peccatis nostris: et ad subministrationem spiritus peragi legislator manifestat.

VERS. 12.-- De prunis altaris, etc. Spiritualibus verbis, quae Joannes, egresso Juda, in coena scripsit, quibus exponit quomodo ipse, cum sit in Patre et Pater in ipso Spiritum mittat; quomodo Pater agricola, ipse vitis, palmites apostoli, et alia multa, quae ad pietatem 342Baccendunt tanquam carbones ignis (Joan. XVIII).

Hauriens manu. LXX. Implebit manus suas thymiamate, compositionis subtilis, subtilia scilicet spiritualia orans, non terrena.

Thymiama orationem. Unde: Dirigatur oratio mea sicut incensum in conspectu tuo. Oravit enim Christus pro discipulis.

VERS. 13.-- Ut positis, etc. Aaron pro dignitate sacerdotii, Christum et alios sacerdotes significat. Christus super ignem, id est doctrinam, ponit aromata, id est, orationem; et vapor nebulae, id est, virtus orationis, operit oraculum, id est carnem, ex qua nobis propitiatio peccatorum in Christo, qui est et testimonium. Ponit autem et quisque sacerdos de Christo loquens, in igne doctrinae spiritualis, aromata 342Corationis, cujus virtus operit oraculum, quod est super testimonium, nec permittit eum quaerere de passione Christi, quae sunt supra virtutem quaerentis, et non morietur immoderatis, scilicet quaestionibus.

Et non morietur. Caro ejus non vidit corruptionem, quia vapore aromatum operiebatur virtute, scilicet orationis, de qua dicitur. Non dabis sanctum tuum videre corruptionem (Psal. XV).

VERS. 14.-- Contra propitiationem. Quia ad propitiationem nostram et illuminationem Christus passus est, qui propitiatorium dicitur, et oriens. Unde: Ecce vir, oriens, etc. (Psal. XV.) Et alibi: et visitavit nos oriens, etc. (Zachar. VI). Vel contra propitiatorium, id est, in conspectu Patris, septies, quia 342Dremissionem peccatorum et Spiritum sanctum nobis donavit.

VERS. 15.-- Cumque mactaverint, etc. Quia de communi massa hominum factus est Christus, recte hircus vocatur, quia hoc animal pilis asperum et hirsutum, et pro peccato genus humanum. Unde convenienter ait: Cumque mactaverit hircum pro peccato populi (Ose. I), id est humani generis: quod quondam non populus, nunc autem populus, ex quo ejus primitias assumpsit Christus. Ut ergo monstraret de Judaeis principaliter, sed et omnis naturae, quae in peccato est, primitias Christum portare, vitulum simul et hircum assumpsit, et quia unum sacrificium, et unus effectus, necessario 343Acommunia in hirco aguntur et vitulo. Cernis enim quia hircus occiditur ante Deum, id est Patrem: et sanguis infertur in interiora velaminis, id est, ad eumdem Patrem. Et expiatur sanctuarium, massa, scilicet, humani generis, quae ab initio sancta fuit, et tabernaculum Spiritus sancti.

VERS. 16.-- Tabernaculo testimonii, etc. Quod superius sanctuarium, hic tabernaculum testimonii vocat. Eadem enim ratione et sancta et tabernaculum testimonii, dicitur massa humani generis. Sic enim tabernaculum testimonii coelum est, recte sancta nominatur: coelestis est homo noster de quo Paulus ait: Sicut portavimus imaginem terrae, etc. (I Cor. XV). Hic recte sancta nominatur qui est in medio immunditiarum, quia passionibus carnis oppugnatur 343BChristus.

Sanctuarium. Genus humanum, carnem assumendo, ut in proprio sanctuario carnis rogaret per crucem.

VERS. 17.-- Donec egrediatur. Humano scilicet more spiritum emittens, et carnem in sepulcro relinquens.

VERS. 18.-- Et sumptum sanguinem. Quia passione sua cornua altaris, id est quatuor principales virtutes sanctificavit.

VERS. 19.-- Expiet. Christi sacrificio expiantur. Unde, ipsa autem coelestia majoribus hostiis expiantur (Heb. II), quam istis.

Ab immunditiis. Quantum ad coelorum virtutes non indigent expiari, quae sunt in sanctificatione 343Cperenni. Sed quia frequenter peccatorum nostrorum fumus ascendit, de quo dicitur: Ascendit fumus in ira ejus (Psal. XVII); et de Ninive, Malitia ejus ascendit coram me (Jonae I), recte ab immunditiis nostris expiat supernos choros, ut abominabilem auferat odorem. Ideo non simpliciter aspergit, sed septies, quia venia peccatorum nostrorum coelestia mundantur.

VERS. 20.-- Postquam. (AMBR. epist ad Simplicianum. )

Sicut duo sunt in agro, etc., usque ad quae facit animi tranquillitatem.

VERS. 21.-- Et posita utraque manu, etc. Manus impositio benedictionem ministrat in sacrificio, aut 343Dremissionem peccatorum, aut Spiritum sanctum; quae esse non possunt in eo super quem cadit fors diaboli. Multae quoque circumstantiae sunt quae illi sententiae contradicunt.

Et posita. Ut ostendatur quia verbum Dei impassibile, immortale, in carne pro nobis sufficiens sacrificium suscepit: huic recte omnia peccata imponuntur, quia peccata nostra pertulit in corpore suo. LXX: Imponet ea super caput haedi vivi. In quo significatur spinea corona capiti ejus impressa, in spinis enim significantur peccata.

Per hominem, etc. Christum qui est omnia, sui sacrificii sacerdos, sacrificium, cultus, et altare. Paratus secundum illud: Ego autem non contradico, retrorsum non abii: corpus meum dedi percutientibus, 344Aetc. (Isai. L). Christus ergo seipsum per haedum vivum immolans, ipse se per hominem paratum, id est propriam virtutem, mittit hircum viventem in solitudinem, immortalem scilicet, impassibilemque divinitatem ad invia nobis loca, et omni malitia deserta. Unde: Sciens Jesus quia omnia dedit ei Pater in manus, et quia a Deo exivit, et ad Deum vadit (Joan. XIII).

ORIG. in homil. super Leviticum hircum qui Domino immolatur, dicit significare eos qui ad fortem Domini destinantur, sicut Lazarus ille, qui in sinum Abrahae deportatur angelis (Luc. XVI). Hircum vero qui dimittitur in eremum, reprobos significare, vel Judaicum populum, qui a facie Domini pro peccatis suis abjiciuntur, sicut dives purpuratus in inferno sepultus. Hominem vero paratum, Christum 344Bvel angelos ejus, unde recipit unusquisque quod meretur.

VERS. 26.-- Illi vero qui dimiserint. (ORIG., hom. 9 in Lev.) Ascendit in cor tuum concupiscentia? etc., usque ad et hic in lavandis vestibus potest eadem figura servari.

VERS. 29.-- Mense autem septimo. (ORIG., ubi supra. In qua solemnitas repropitiationis, etc., usque ad id est abjectus et refutatus.

CHAPTER XVI.

  • 341A

VERS. 1.-- And the Lord spoke, etc. (ISICH.) He makes one body of the law, etc., as far as whence after their death the Lord speaks these things to Moses.

VERS. 2.-- Speak to Aaron, etc. (ID.) How greatly God spares us, etc., as far as which is guarded against by the present words.

Before the propitiatory. (ISICH.) The LXX: Before the face of the propitiatory, etc., as far as or which we receive from the Lord's flesh.

VERS. 3.-- He shall offer a calf for sin. (ISICH.) A wonderful order. For when he had said that entrance into the holy places is not always to be made, lest we presume to approach rashly and without circumspection, he shows when and how 341Bwe are to enter.

He shall offer a calf for sin. The beginning of the divine preaching is Christ, and his passion; and if this had not been, and had not freed men from error both Gentile and Judaic, one could not speak of the divinity of the Father. For no man knoweth the Father but the Son, and he to whom he shall be pleased to reveal him (Matth. XI). [Vulg. Mt 11:27 neque Patrem quis novit, nisi Filius, et cui voluerit Filius revelare, prefaced by nemo novit Filium, nisi Pater]

VERS. 4.-- He shall be clothed with a linen tunic, etc. He who is to speak of God, and to see him according to what is possible, must be armed with all the virtues, of which four are general: justice, chastity, fortitude, wisdom.

With linen breeches. (HIERON. epist. ad Fabiolam, tom. I.) Where it is related how Moses clothed his brother Aaron, etc., as far as let us die the death.

341C(ID.) According to the Hebrews, etc., as far as therefore with linen breeches the thighs or the loins are veiled.

When he shall have been washed. (ISICH.) With the water of baptism, etc., as far as but desiring to understand the dispensation of Christ.

VERS. 5.-- And he shall receive from the whole multitude, etc. (ID.) Christ was immolated for us, etc., as far as whence Paul: Ye are made dead by the body of Christ (Rom. VIII). [Vulg. Rom 7:4 mortificati estis legi per corpus Christi, "made dead to the law by the body of Christ"]

VERS. 7.-- Two he-goats, etc. (ISICH.) Because both for sinners and for the just, etc., as far as saying: There shall not be to thee strange gods (Exod. XX). [Vulg. Ex 20:3 Non habebis deos alienos coram me, "Thou shalt not have strange gods before me"]

(AUG., quaest. 55 in Levit., tom. III.) Two he-goats, The goat to be immolated, etc., as far as the allegorical signification.

(ISICH.) He shall make two he-goats to stand. Because Christ is 341DGod and man, etc., as far as and the other to the scapegoat, Christ.

VERS. 10.-- Into the wilderness. That is, into the bosom of the Father, to which none draws near save he himself and the Holy Spirit. He went away, therefore, that is, he restrained the power of working, and bore our iniquities, not that he might have them, but that he might consume them. For God is a consuming fire.

Into the wilderness, of which it is said: In a desert land, without way and without water, etc. (Psal. LXII.) There the enemy has no place, where God abides. For the divinity is said to have gone away into heaven at the time of the passion; not changing place, but in a certain manner restraining its power, that the impious might be able to accomplish the passion.

VERS. 11.-- A calf. When Christ is offered for the deliverance of the Judaic 342Apeople, he is called a calf: because he observed the justification of the law. Whence he says: I came not to destroy the law, but to fulfil (Matth. V); who was made of a woman, that he might redeem them that were under the law (Gal. IV). [Vulg. Gal 4:4-5 factum ex muliere, factum sub lege, ut eos qui sub lege erant, redimeret; Migne drops factum sub lege, which is the very clause the gloss's conclusion rests on] Rightly therefore is he called a calf, as being brought under the yoke of the commandments of the law.

(ISICH.) The lawgiver makes manifest that the sacrifice of Christ is indeed of the flesh, through the power of the divinity, and in the good pleasure of the Father, and a propitiation for our sins: and that it is accomplished unto the ministration of the Spirit.

VERS. 12.-- Of the coals of the altar, etc. With the spiritual words which John, when Judas had gone out, wrote down at the supper, in which he sets forth how he himself, being in the Father and the Father in him, sends the Spirit; how the Father is the husbandman, he himself the vine, the apostles the branches, and many other things which kindle to piety 342Blike coals of fire (Joan. XVIII).

Taking up with his hand. The LXX: He shall fill his hands with incense of fine composition, praying, that is, for things fine and spiritual, not earthly.

Incense, prayer. Whence: Let my prayer be directed as incense in thy sight. For Christ prayed for his disciples.

VERS. 13.-- That, being put, etc. Aaron, by the dignity of the priesthood, signifies Christ and the other priests. Christ puts the spices, that is, prayer, upon the fire, that is, upon doctrine; and the vapor of the cloud, that is, the power of prayer, covers the oracle, that is, the flesh, out of which comes to us the propitiation of sins in Christ, who is also the testimony. And every priest speaking of Christ also puts upon the fire of spiritual doctrine the spices 342Cof prayer, whose power covers the oracle, which is above the testimony, and does not permit him to inquire concerning the passion of Christ into things that are above the power of the inquirer, and he shall not die of immoderate questionings, that is.

And he shall not die. His flesh saw not corruption, because it was covered by the vapor of the spices, that is, by the power of prayer, of which it is said: Thou shalt not give thy holy one to see corruption (Psal. XV).

VERS. 14.-- Toward the propitiation. Because for our propitiation and enlightenment Christ suffered, who is called the propitiatory, and the East. Whence: Behold a man, the East, etc. (Psal. XV.) And elsewhere: and the East hath visited us, etc. (Zachar. VI). Or toward the propitiatory, that is, in the sight of the Father, seven times, because 342Dhe gave us the remission of sins and the Holy Spirit.

VERS. 15.-- And when they shall have slain, etc. Because Christ was made of the common mass of men, he is rightly called a he-goat, because this animal is rough and shaggy with hairs, and because the human race is for sin. Whence he fitly says: And when he shall have slain the he-goat for the sin of the people (Ose. I), that is, of the human race: which once was not a people, but now is a people, from which Christ took his firstfruits. That he might show, therefore, that Christ bears the firstfruits chiefly of the Jews, but also of every nature that is in sin, he took at once both the calf and the he-goat; and because there is one sacrifice and one effect, necessarily 343Athings common are done in the he-goat and in the calf. For thou seest that the he-goat is slain before God, that is, before the Father: and the blood is carried within the veil, that is, to the same Father. And the sanctuary is expiated, that is, the mass of the human race, which from the beginning was holy, and the tabernacle of the Holy Spirit.

VERS. 16.-- The tabernacle of the testimony, etc. What above he called the sanctuary, here he calls the tabernacle of the testimony. For by the same reasoning the mass of the human race is called both the holy place and the tabernacle of the testimony. For thus the tabernacle of the testimony is heaven, and is rightly named holy: heavenly is our man, of whom Paul says: As we have borne the image of the earth, etc. (I Cor. XV). [Vulg. 1 Cor 15:49 sicut portavimus imaginem terreni, "the image of the earthly man"] Here is he rightly named holy who is in the midst of uncleannesses, because Christ is assaulted by the passions of the flesh 343B.

The sanctuary. The race of mankind, by taking flesh, that in the proper sanctuary of his flesh he might make petition through the cross.

VERS. 17.-- Until he go out. After a human manner, that is, giving up the spirit, and leaving his flesh in the sepulchre.

VERS. 18.-- And having taken the blood. Because by his passion he sanctified the horns of the altar, that is, the four principal virtues.

VERS. 19.-- Let him expiate. They are expiated by the sacrifice of Christ. Whence, but the heavenly things themselves are expiated with greater victims (Heb. II), than these. [Vulg. Heb 9:23 ipsa autem caelestia melioribus hostiis quam istis, "with better victims than these," and with no verb expiantur]

From the uncleannesses. So far as concerns the powers of the heavens, they have no need to be expiated, being in perpetual sanctification 343C. But because the smoke of our sins frequently ascends, of which it is said: There went up a smoke in his wrath (Psal. XVII); and of Nineveh, Its malice is come up before me (Jonae I), he rightly expiates the choirs above from our uncleannesses, that he may take away the abominable odor. Therefore he does not sprinkle simply, but seven times, because by the pardon of our sins the heavenly things are cleansed.

VERS. 20.-- After that. (AMBR. epist ad Simplicianum. )

As there are two in the field, etc., as far as which makes the tranquillity of the mind.

VERS. 21.-- And having laid both hands, etc. The imposition of the hand ministers in the sacrifice either blessing, or 343Dthe remission of sins, or the Holy Spirit; which things cannot be in him upon whom the lot of the devil falls. There are also many circumstances which contradict that opinion.

And having laid. That it may be shown that the Word of God, impassible, immortal, took upon himself in the flesh a sufficient sacrifice for us: upon him rightly are all sins laid, because he bore our sins in his own body. The LXX: He shall lay them upon the head of the living kid. In which is signified the crown of thorns pressed upon his head, for by thorns sins are signified.

By a man, etc. Christ, who is all things, the priest of his own sacrifice, the sacrifice, the worship, and the altar. Prepared according to that word: But I do not contradict, I went not backward: I gave my body to the strikers, 344Aetc. (Isai. L). Christ therefore, immolating himself by the living kid, sends by the man prepared, that is, by his own power, the living he-goat into the wilderness, the immortal, that is, and impassible divinity, into places without way for us, and deserted by all malice. Whence: Jesus knowing that the Father had given him all things into his hands, and that he came from God and goeth to God (Joan. XIII).

ORIG., in homil. super Leviticum, says that the he-goat which is immolated to the Lord signifies those who are destined to the Lord's lot, as that Lazarus who is carried by angels into Abraham's bosom (Luc. XVI). But the he-goat which is let go into the desert signifies the reprobate, or the Judaic people, who are cast away from the face of the Lord for their sins, as the rich man clad in purple was buried in hell. And the man prepared signifies Christ 344Bor his angels, whence each receives what he deserves.

VERS. 26.-- But they who shall have let go. (ORIG., hom. 9 in Lev.) Has concupiscence come up into thy heart? etc., as far as and here in the washing of garments the same figure can be kept.

VERS. 29.-- In the seventh month. (ORIG., ubi supra. In which is the solemnity of re-propitiation, etc., as far as that is, cast away and refuted.

CAPUT XVII.

VERS. 1.-- Et locutus est, etc. Praesenti lege demonstratur quia non tantum Moysi, sed omnibus communiter loquitur Dominus. Omnes scire cupit quod omnibus expedit, legem scilicet temporalem esse, et 344Cexpleto suo tempore, observari supervacue. Expletio vero temporis perfecta, destructio loci in quo sacrificia praecepit offerri, intimando eum qui extra castra, sive foris, tantum extra tabernaculum testimonii sacrificaverit, interire de populis suis, ut scilicet, destructo tabernaculo et loco, sacrificia finem habeant. Sanctas vero actiones et quae aedificant humanam mentem, ut patrem et matrem honorare, et proximos diligere, et similia, loco non circonscripsit, quia semper prosunt, et cum Evangelio conveniunt. Quicunque ergo nunc figuras agunt destructi loci, sanguinem effundunt, et communem conversationem maculant, in communi peccant; sicut qui sanguinem fundunt, eorum quoque sanguinem fundunt, 344Dquos exemplo suo in errorem mittunt.

VERS. 3.-- Homo quilibet, etc. Quasi figurae legis temporales sunt, et in loco certo sunt, quo destructo non possunt manere.

Si occiderit ovem. (AUG., quaest. 56 in Levit.) Non de his dicit, etc., usque ad quidquid in hac re fecit, contra legem esse non potuit, quia dator est legis.

VERS. 7.-- Et nequaquam ultra immolabunt hostias suas daemonibus, etc. Cum a Domino dictum sit (Exod. XX): Non facies tibi idolum nec omnem similitudinem eorum quae in coelo sunt sursum, et in terra deorsum, et quaecunque subtus terram; conflatilem vitulum fecerunt, et ei sacrificium obtulerunt, abligurire cupientes sacrificium. Lex ergo, quasi paedagogus eorum, praecipit Deo sacrificare (Exod. XXXII), 345Aut in hoc occupati abstinerent se a sacrificio idololatriae. Talia tamen sancivit sacrificia, quibus mysteria significantur futura.

Cum quibus fornicati sunt. Idololatriam fornicationem vocat, quia Deus despondit sibi animas nostras. Dicitur ergo fornicatio ab illo discessio. Terret etiam eos qui in carne fornicantur, cum peccatum fornicationis comparat idolorum cultui et daemonum oblationi.

VERS. 8.-- Homo de domo Israel, etc. (AUG., ubi supra. ) Si idem repetitur mandatum, etc., usque ad frustra se Israelitas vel Judaeos jactant.

VERS. 10.-- De advenis. Gentilibus, qui non sunt conjuncti Judaeis, sed apud nos. Apud eos quippe fuimus, ut legem eorum et prophetas, et promissam 345Bhaereditatem habentes, non tamen ipsis sed Deo adhaerentes: propterea juxta LXX, homo homo dixit, ut ostendat tam eos qui in circumcisione, quam qui in praeputio adhaerent Deo, hoc debere mandatum servare.

Si comederit sanguinem, etc. Sanguinem, homicidium intelligimus; fratris odium, homicidium est: Qui enim odit fratrem suum, homicida est (I Joan. III). Abstinendum est igitur ab omni odio fratrum, quia super talem animam quae talem comedit sanguinem (secundum illud: Qui devorant plebem meam sicut escam panis (Psal. XIII) obfirmat faciem suam Deus, Christum, scilicet qui est imago Patris. Qui enim videt Filium, videt et Patrem (Joan. XIV). Ipse enim judex est de quo dicitur: Vultus autem 345CDomini super facientes mala, ut perdat de terra memoriam eorum (Psal. XXXIII). Ipse autem comedentem sanguinem perdit de populo suo, de communi scilicet hominum genere: quia sicut immisericordem et impium cum malignis spiritibus mittit in ignem aeternum, qui praeparatus est diabolo et angelis ejus (Matth. XXV). Ut autem ostenderet quantum debeamus esse invicem misericordes, quantum diligentes, ostendit quia pro expiatione nostra sanguinem suum fuderit dicens: Ego dedi illum vobis, sanguinem scilicet Christi.

VERS. 11.-- Anima carnis. (AUG., quaest. 57 in Lev.) Aliquid vitale est in sanguine, etc., usque ad cui tantum meritum acquirebat.

345DVERS. 13.-- Homo quicunque. (ISICH.) Multa Deo cura est de nobis, ne alteri noceamus: propterea percutienti maxillam jubet alteram dare; et auferenti pallium, tunicam dimittere; et non in judicio contendere, ne ex contentione velimus etiam nocere (Matth. V), sed patiendo ipsum corrigere. Ideo legislator sanguinis mandatum recapitulat, ut fraudes et astutias nocendi varias auferat, dicens: Sive venatione sive aucupio, etc.

Venatione. Fraude, quae ad sanguinem vitamque pertinet. Unde: Captabant in animam justi, et sanguinem innocentem condemnabant (Psal. XLIII). Et rursus: Anima nostra sicut passer, erepta est de laqueo venantium (Psal. CXXIII). Hoc est, si peccantem viderit 346Aeum, qui venationi et dolo ex infirmitate vel simplicitate expositus est.

Vel ovem, etc. Eum scilicet qui a terrenis se removet, quibus vesci licitum est: tales enim violenti et avari comedunt. Unde: Si invicem comeditis, videte ne ab invicem consumamini (Gal. V). Vel quoniam prius erant justi, recte inter munda computantur.

Operiat illum terra, etc. Peccata confitentium terra operit, tanquam inferius remanentia et per poenitentiam mortificata: quorum prohibitus est clamor ad coelos ascendere, quo ascendebat clamor Sodomorum, qui nolebant poenitere (Gen. XVIII).

VERS. 15.-- Anima quae comederit, etc. Superius dictum est: Quae tetigerit morticina a bestiis capta, 346Bid est, qui in talibus communicaverit: hic autem dicitur: Anima quae comederit; totum scilicet peccatum suum fecerit, et ex eo voluntatem suam expleverit.

CHAPTER XVII.

VERS. 1.-- And he spoke, etc. By the present law it is shown that the Lord speaks not to Moses only, but to all in common. He desires all to know what is expedient for all: namely, that the law is temporal, and that, its time being fulfilled, it is observed to no purpose. 344CBut the perfect fulfilment of the time is the destruction of the place in which he commanded the sacrifices to be offered, by intimating that he who shall have sacrificed outside the camps, or without, only outside the tabernacle of the testimony, shall perish from among his people: namely, that when the tabernacle and the place are destroyed, the sacrifices may have an end. But holy actions, and those which build up the human mind, such as to honor father and mother, and to love one's neighbours, and the like, he did not circumscribe by place, because they always profit, and agree with the Gospel. Whosoever therefore now enact the figures of the destroyed place, shed blood, and stain the common manner of life, and sin in what is common; and just as they shed blood, they shed also the blood of those 344Dwhom by their example they send into error.

VERS. 3.-- Any man whatsoever, etc. Inasmuch as the figures of the law are temporal, and are in a fixed place, which being destroyed they cannot remain.

If he shall have killed a sheep. (AUG., quaest. 56 in Levit.) He does not say this of those, etc., as far as whatever he did in this matter could not be against the law, because he is the giver of the law.

VERS. 7.-- And they shall no more immolate their victims to demons, etc. Whereas it had been said by the Lord (Exod. XX): Thou shalt not make to thyself an idol, nor any likeness of those things which are in heaven above, and in the earth beneath, and whatsoever is under the earth; [Vulg. Ex 20:4 Non facies tibi sculptile, neque omnem similitudinem quae est in caelo desuper, et quae in terra deorsum, nec eorum quae sunt in aquis sub terra] they made a molten calf, and offered sacrifice to it, desiring to lick up the sacrifice. The law therefore, as their schoolmaster, commanded them to sacrifice to God (Exod. XXXII), 345Athat being occupied in this they might abstain from the sacrifice of idolatry. Yet he ordained such sacrifices as signify future mysteries.

With whom they have committed fornication. He calls idolatry fornication, because God has betrothed our souls to himself. Departure from him is therefore called fornication. He terrifies also those who commit fornication in the flesh, when he compares the sin of fornication to the worship of idols and to the oblation offered to demons.

VERS. 8.-- A man of the house of Israel, etc. (AUG., ubi supra. ) If the same commandment is repeated, etc., as far as in vain do they boast themselves Israelites or Jews.

VERS. 10.-- Of the strangers. The Gentiles, who are not joined to the Jews, but are among us. For among them we were, as having their law and the prophets and the promised 345Binheritance, yet cleaving not to them but to God: therefore, according to the LXX, man, man, he said, that he may show that both those who are in circumcision and those who in uncircumcision cleave to God ought to keep this commandment.

If he shall have eaten blood, etc. By blood we understand murder; hatred of a brother is murder: For he that hateth his brother is a murderer (I Joan. III). We must therefore abstain from all hatred of the brethren, because against such a soul as eats such blood (according to that word: They who devour my people as the food of bread (Psal. XIII) God sets his face firmly, that is, Christ, who is the image of the Father. For he that seeth the Son, seeth the Father also (Joan. XIV). [Vulg. Jn 14:9 qui videt me, videt et Patrem, "he that seeth me, seeth the Father also"] For he himself is the judge, of whom it is said: But the countenance 345Cof the Lord is upon them that do evil things, to cut off the remembrance of them from the earth (Psal. XXXIII). And he destroys the eater of blood from his people, that is, from the common race of men: because as one without mercy and impious he sends him with the malignant spirits into the everlasting fire, which is prepared for the devil and his angels (Matth. XXV). And that he might show how merciful we ought to be to one another, how loving, he shows that for our expiation he poured out his own blood, saying: I have given it to you, the blood, namely, of Christ.

VERS. 11.-- The soul of the flesh. (AUG., quaest. 57 in Lev.) There is something vital in the blood, etc., as far as for whom it was acquiring so great a merit.

345DVERS. 13.-- Any man whatsoever. (ISICH.) God has much care of us, that we harm not one another: therefore he bids a man give the other cheek to him that strikes it; and to leave his coat to him that takes away his cloak; and not to contend in judgment, lest out of contention we should wish even to do harm (Matth. V), but by suffering to correct the man himself. Therefore the lawgiver recapitulates the commandment concerning blood, that he may take away the various frauds and cunning devices of doing harm, saying: Whether by hunting or by fowling, etc.

By hunting. By fraud, which touches blood and life. Whence: They hunted after the soul of the just, and condemned innocent blood (Psal. XLIII). [Vulg. Ps 93:21 future Captabunt … condemnabunt, "they will hunt … will condemn"] And again: Our soul, as a sparrow, hath been delivered out of the snare of the fowlers (Psal. CXXIII). That is, if he shall have seen sinning 346Athe man who through weakness or simplicity lies open to hunting and to guile.

Or a sheep, etc. Him, namely, who withdraws himself from earthly things, on which it is lawful to feed: for such men the violent and the greedy devour. Whence: If ye devour one another, take heed lest ye be consumed one of another (Gal. V). Or, because they were formerly just, they are rightly counted among clean things.

Let the earth cover it, etc. The earth covers the sins of those who confess, as things remaining beneath and mortified by penance: whose cry is forbidden to ascend to the heavens, whither the cry of the Sodomites ascended, who would not repent (Gen. XVIII).

VERS. 15.-- The soul that shall have eaten, etc. Above it was said: Whosoever shall touch the carcasses of beasts taken by wild beasts, 346Bthat is, whoever shall have had part in such things; but here it is said: The soul that shall have eaten; that is, who shall have made the whole sin his own, and shall have fulfilled his own will out of it.

CAPUT XVIII.

VERS. 2.-- Ego Dominus. Quasi dicat, Cognoscite vestrum creatorem; diligenter servate ejus praecepta.

VERS. 3.-- Juxta consuetudinem, etc. Intentionem totius legis aperit, ut a malis scilicet abstineamus et bona teneamus.

Neque in legitimis eorum, etc. Non enim solum a fornicatione, avaritia et superbia, sed etiam a divinationibus, et auguriis, et omnium gentium superstitionibus 346Cpraecipit abstinere.

VERS. 5.-- Vivet in eis. Ego Dominus. Quia in ipsis vera vita in Christum credentibus: quia qui videt Filium, et credit in eum, habet vitam aeternam (Joan. VI). Hoc in prioribus judiciis et praeceptis non addidit: lex enim non vivificat, nisi evangelium superveniat.

VERS. 6.-- Omnis homo ad proximam, etc. Prohibet ne astute ad peccantes in lege, cognationis occasione, accedamus, ut tandem turpia facta eorum cognoscentes prodamus; unde subdit: Ego Dominus.

Omnis homo. LXX: Homo ad omnem domesticam carnis ipsius non accedet, etc. Quemadmodum enim homo duplex, sic duplicia praecepta, interiorem et exteriorem hominem docentia. Lex exterior sanguineis 346Dmisceri prohibet, sicut gentes, quarum haereditatem accepturus erat Israel, qui adeo dissolutus et deditus carnis voluptatibus, ut unus multas uxores acciperet, ut sic saltem ab alienigenis abstinerent. Generationes ergo legislator exsequitur per ordinem, a quibus continere debent, ne confundantur generationes et dispereant haereditates. Talium conjunctionem turpitudinem appellat, ut abominandam horrendamque ostendat. Notandumque quod cum in principio hujus legis dixisset: Ego Dominus Deus vester, iteravit Ego Dominus, significans quia nobis carne conjunctus est, et nostrae cognationis particeps: tantam ergo cognationem offendere damnabile est.

347AEgo Dominus. Non manifestans peccata hominum, sed delens et tegens quod fuerit confessione deletum vel detectum

Turpitudinem, etc. (AUG., quaest. 58 in Lev.) Prohibet cum matre concumbere, etc., usque ad in charitate enim peccat, quae plenitudo legis est (Rom. XIII).

VERS. 9.-- Foris genita est. (ID., quaest. 59.) Ex matre, si de priori viro, etc., usque ad quod superius dixit obscure.

VERS. 11.-- Filiae uxoris, etc. (AUG., ubi supra. ) Non cum filia novercae ex priori viro videatur prohibere concubitum, cum ei cui prohibetur, soror non sit, vel ex patre vel ex matre, addidit quod ex eodem patre.

347BVERS. 14.-- Turpitudinem fratris patris, etc. (AUG., quaest. 61.) Quaeritur utrum hoc, etc., usque ad posset enim aliquis putare quod talem liceret sibi ducere et adulterium non timere.

Nec accedes, etc. LXX: Et ad uxorem ejus non introibis. Proinde hanc enim turpitudinem dicunt patris fratris ejus, id est patrui pudenda, scilicet, uxoris.

VERS. 16.-- Turpitudinem uxoris fratris, etc. In matre concupiscentia, actio intelligitur in filia, in nepte mors. Concupiscentia enim parit peccatum, peccatum mortem (Jac. I), vel aliud peccatum; sicut David ex concupiscentia adulteravit, et pro adulterio occidit.

VERS. 17.-- Ut reveles ignominiam ejus. Quasi 347Cfamiliare et cognatum est nobis peccatum. Unde invicem debemus condolere, et onera nostra portare, ut adimpleamus legem Christi, qui peccata nostra portavit.

VERS. 18.-- Sororem uxoris tuae, etc. (AUG., quaest. in Lev. ) Non prohibet, etc., usque ad ne faceret eam moechari.

In pellicatum illius non accipies nec revelabis, etc. LXX: In zelum illius, quasi hoc esset zelus inter sorores, quasi non sit et inter non sorores. An potius ne hoc animo fiat, ut in zelum sororis soror superducatur? In pace nos vocavit Deus, pacem cum omnibus habeamus (Rom. XII), maxime cum proximis. In Judaica quoque conversatione non decet 347Decclesiasticam suscipere, nec typicum pascha cum vero celebrare, nec circumcisionem cum baptismate. Unde Paulus: Si circumcidimini, Christus vobis nihil proderit (Gal. V). Mortificata ergo Judaica conversatione Ecclesiae debet conjungi, quae est soror legis, eosdem habet doctores, eosdem prophetas. Sed Judaica conversatio aemulatur, quia ad Ecclesiam transierunt divitiae ejus. Unde Paulus ait: Aemulantur vos non bene, sed excludere vos, etc. (Gal. IV). Uxor namque prior zelat, quia subintrat secunda. Unde Lia ait: Parumne tibi videtur quod praeripueris maritum mihi? (Gen. XXX.)

VERS. 19.-- Ad mulierem, etc. (AUG., quaest. 64 in Lev.) Cum hoc superius sufficienter prohibuisset, 348Aetc., usque ad sed concipiendae proli noxium prohibetur.

VERS. 20.-- Cum uxore, etc. (ISICH.) Hic prohibetur rursus adulterium, quod etiam in decalogo prohibetur: unde apparet illa ita esse prohibita, ut etiam mortuis viris non ducant uxores, quarum prohibet turpitudinem revelari.

VERS. 21.-- De semine, etc. (ID.) Convenienter haec subjunguntur, etc., usque ad nec in errore gentilium miscemur.

Nec pollues, etc. Non solum luxuriando, vel idolis immolando, sed et gentilium philosophiam in Ecclesiam introducendo. Quis enim consensus templo Dei cum idolis?

VERS. 23.-- Mulier non, etc. Non solum viris, 348Bsed etiam mulieribus lex posita est (II Cor. IX). Unde: Mulierem fortem quis inveniet? (Prov. XIII.) Sicut enim masculus animam fortem et constantem in virtutibus significat, sic mulier dignitatem suam custodientem designat: facta est enim in adjutorium viri (Gen. II): quae vero talis est, non debet jumentorum coitu maculari.

VERS. 24.-- Nec polluamini in omnibus his, etc. (ISICH.) Quanta pollutio sit in praedictis, poena pollutorum ostendit. Nec incaute praetereundum, quia ea quae invicem sibi conjuncta sunt, in unum colligit, ut ostendat quia qui in uno pollutus fuerit, in omnibus offendit (Jac. II).

Contaminatae sunt universae gentes, etc. Daemones, qui propter multitudinem dicuntur gentes universae. 348CQui cum omni peccato gaudeant, praecipue tamen fornicatione et idololatria, quia in his et corpus et anima maculatur, et totus homo, qui terra dicitur. Sed visitavit Deus terram, id est hominum genus.

VERS. 29.-- Omnis anima, etc. Qui enim post evangelicam praedicationem talia committunt, aliam mali medicinam non inveniunt; nec in sorte hominum, sed daemonum erunt.

VERS. 30.-- Custodite mandata mea. Quasi: Mea sunt mandata, non hominis; ad cujus imaginem facti estis; quam polluere non debetis: quia templum Dei estis, et Spiritus Dei habitat in vobis (I Cor. III).

CHAPTER XVIII.

VERS. 2.-- I am the Lord. As if he said: Know ye your creator; keep his commandments diligently.

VERS. 3.-- According to the custom, etc. He opens the purpose of the whole law, namely that we abstain from evils and hold fast to good things.

Neither in their ordinances, etc. For he commands them to abstain not only from fornication, avarice and pride, but also from divinations, and auguries, and the superstitions of all the nations 346C.

VERS. 5.-- He shall live in them. I am the Lord. Because in them is the true life for those who believe in Christ: because he that seeth the Son and believeth in him hath life everlasting (Joan. VI). This he did not add in the former judgments and precepts: for the law does not give life, unless the Gospel come upon it.

VERS. 6.-- No man to her that is near, etc. He forbids us to approach craftily, on the occasion of kinship, those who sin against the law, so that at last, knowing their shameful deeds, we should betray them; whence he adds: I am the Lord.

No man. The LXX: Man shall not approach to any that is of the household of his own flesh, etc. For as man is twofold, so the precepts are twofold, teaching the inward and the outward man. The outward law forbids that men be mingled with those of their own blood, 346Das the nations did, whose inheritance Israel was to receive — Israel being so dissolute and given over to the pleasures of the flesh that one man would take many wives, so that at least in this way they might abstain from women of another race. The lawgiver therefore goes through the generations in order, from which they ought to refrain, lest the generations be confounded and the inheritances perish. The conjunction of such persons he calls a shameful thing, that he may show it to be abominable and dreadful. And it is to be noted that, whereas at the beginning of this law he had said: I am the Lord your God, he repeated I am the Lord, signifying that he is joined to us in the flesh, and is a partaker of our kindred: to offend so great a kindred, therefore, is damnable.

347AI am the Lord. Not making manifest the sins of men, but blotting out and covering what has been blotted out or uncovered by confession

Shamefulness, etc. (AUG., quaest. 58 in Lev.) He forbids to lie with a mother, etc., as far as for he sins against charity, which is the fulness of the law (Rom. XIII).

VERS. 9.-- She is born abroad. (ID., quaest. 59.) Of the mother, if by a former husband, etc., as far as what he had said obscurely above.

VERS. 11.-- Of the wife's daughter, etc. (AUG., ubi supra. ) Let it not seem to forbid intercourse with the stepmother's daughter by a former husband, since she is not a sister to him to whom it is forbidden, either by the father or by the mother, he added that she is by the same father.

347BVERS. 14.-- The shamefulness of the father's brother, etc. (AUG., quaest. 61.) It is asked whether this, etc., as far as for someone might think that he was allowed to marry such a woman and need not fear adultery.

Neither shalt thou approach, etc. The LXX: And thou shalt not go in to his wife. Accordingly they say that this shamefulness is that of his father's brother, that is, of the uncle's private parts, namely of his wife.

VERS. 16.-- The shamefulness of thy brother's wife, etc. In the mother is understood concupiscence, in the daughter the act, in the granddaughter death. For concupiscence bringeth forth sin, and sin death (Jac. I), [Vulg. Jas 1:15 concupiscentia cum conceperit, parit peccatum: peccatum vero cum consummatum fuerit, generat mortem] or another sin; as David out of concupiscence committed adultery, and for the adultery killed.

VERS. 17.-- That thou mayest discover her disgrace. As it were, 347Csin is familiar and akin to us. Whence we ought to grieve with one another, and to bear our burdens, that we may fulfil the law of Christ, who bore our sins.

VERS. 18.-- The sister of thy wife, etc. (AUG., quaest. in Lev. ) He does not forbid, etc., as far as lest he should make her commit adultery.

Thou shalt not take for her rival, nor discover, etc. The LXX: For her jealousy, as if this were jealousy between sisters, as though it were not also between those who are not sisters. Or rather, lest it be done with this mind, that a sister be brought in upon a sister for jealousy? God hath called us in peace; let us have peace with all men (Rom. XII), [Vulg. 1 Cor 7:15 in pace autem vocavit nos Deus, conflated with Rom 12:18 cum omnibus hominibus pacem habentes] and chiefly with those nearest us. In the Judaic manner of life also it is not fitting 347Dto take up the ecclesiastical, nor to celebrate the typical pasch with the true, nor circumcision with baptism. Whence Paul: If ye be circumcised, Christ shall profit you nothing (Gal. V). When the Judaic manner of life has been mortified, therefore, she ought to be joined to the Church, who is the sister of the law, has the same doctors, the same prophets. But the Judaic manner of life is jealous, because its riches have passed over to the Church. Whence Paul says: They are zealous for you not well, but would exclude you, etc. (Gal. IV). For the first wife is jealous, because a second comes in. Whence Lia says: Doth it seem a small thing to thee that thou hast taken away my husband from me? (Gen. XXX.)

VERS. 19.-- To a woman, etc. (AUG., quaest. 64 in Lev.) Since he had sufficiently forbidden this above, 348Aetc., as far as but what is harmful to the offspring to be conceived is forbidden.

VERS. 20.-- With the wife, etc. (ISICH.) Here adultery is again forbidden, which is forbidden in the decalogue also: whence it appears that those women are so forbidden that, even when their husbands are dead, men may not marry them, whose shamefulness he forbids to be uncovered.

VERS. 21.-- Of thy seed, etc. (ID.) Fittingly are these things subjoined, etc., as far as nor are we mingled in the error of the Gentiles.

Neither shalt thou defile, etc. Not only by living wantonly, or by sacrificing to idols, but also by bringing the philosophy of the Gentiles into the Church. For what agreement hath the temple of God with idols?

VERS. 23.-- A woman shall not, etc. Not for men only, 348Bbut for women also is the law laid down (II Cor. IX). Whence: Who shall find a valiant woman? (Prov. XIII.) For as the male signifies a soul strong and constant in the virtues, so the woman designates one that keeps her own dignity: for she was made for the help of the man (Gen. II): and she who is such ought not to be stained by the coupling of beasts.

VERS. 24.-- Neither be ye defiled in any of these things, etc. (ISICH.) How great a defilement there is in the aforesaid things, the punishment of the defiled shows. Nor is it to be passed over incautiously that he gathers into one those things which are joined to one another, that he may show that he who shall have been defiled in one, offends in all (Jac. II).

All the nations are contaminated, etc. The demons, who because of their multitude are called all the nations. 348CAnd though they rejoice in every sin, yet chiefly in fornication and idolatry, because in these both the body and the soul is stained, and the whole man, who is called earth. But God has visited the earth, that is, the race of men.

VERS. 29.-- Every soul, etc. For they who after the evangelical preaching commit such things find no other medicine for their evil; nor shall they be in the lot of men, but of demons.

VERS. 30.-- Keep ye my commandments. As if to say: Mine are the commandments, not a man's; to whose image ye were made; which ye ought not to defile: because ye are the temple of God, and the Spirit of God dwelleth in you (I Cor. III).

CAPUT XIX.

348DVERS. 1, 2.-- Locutus est Dominus . . . Sancti estote. (ORIG., hom. 12 in Lev.) Quid significet hoc nomen quod est sanctum, etc., usque ad ut scilicet in saeculo conversationem habeatis.

VERS. 3.-- Unusquisque. Super fundamentum aedificat: quasi, estote sancti, ut sanctus habitet in vobis; et naturae benignitatem impendite honorando patrem et matrem.

Sabbata mea custodite. (ISICH.) Mandata legis volens recapitulare, id est, Non occides, Non furaberis, etc., etc., inquit: Sabbata mea custodite. Verum enim sabbatum est ab omni malo cessare. Unde Moyses: Omne opus servile non facietis in eo (Exod. XXXI).

Sabbata mea custodite. Volens nos Deus de intelligibili 349Arequie docere, et de mundi consummatione, quando impossibile est cibum parare vel operari, nec circa aliquid corporale occupari, ait per Moysen: Sabbata hodie Domino, non invenietis illud in agro (Exod. XVI), etc. Nota quia non ait, sabbatum, sed sabbata, quia intelligibilis requies non ab uno, sed ab omnibus carnalibus quieta est et absoluta: ideo in hoc loco prius sabbatorum nomen in divina Scriptura positum est, ut scias quia sabbatum plena et perfecta quies.

VERS. 5.-- Si immolaveritis, etc. Omnia fere legis capitula reiterat, ut eorum memoriam auditoribus infigat; sed maxime necessaria, sicut hoc mandatum quod superius proposuit, cum de sacrificiis ageret. Sacrificium salutare accepit, in quo quae ad 349Bsalutem pertinent necesse est operari, ut autem sit acceptabile, oportet immolari pietatem et pudicitiam. Hoc enim secundum Apostolum bonum est et acceptum coram Salvatore nostro Deo, qui vult omnes homines salvos fieri (I Tim. II). Comedere autem in die immolationis et altero, est in vita praesenti quaecunque ad salutem pertinent operari: hodie enim et cras pro praesenti saeculo accipiuntur; unde Jacobus: Qui dicitis: Hodie et crastino ibimus in civitatem, etc. (Jac. IV). Dies tertia, quia in ipsa Dominus a mortuis resurrexit, figuram gerit futuri saeculi et resurrectionis, in quo non licebit operari, sed operum suscipere retributionem. Qui ergo hic vivit tanquam illic operaturus sit, tanquam die tertia sacrificium manducaturus, a legislatore hoc facere 349Cprohibetur, quia virtutem imperfectam relinquit, tanquam ibi perficere possit.

VERS. 9.-- Cum messueris, etc. Ut dilectionem adinvicem habeamus, ut amatores et misericordes in pauperes simus, praesenti capitulo praecipitur. Debemus enim egentibus dare, nec debita violenter exigere, et reliquias bonorum nostrorum concedere, imitando Deum datorem omnium. Propterea metentes vel vindemiantes vult aliquid derelinquere pauperi et proselyto, ne datori omnium videatur ingratus, qui aliis divitias dedit, alios de aliorum reliquiis vivere voluit; ut sic probaret dispensationem divitum, et patientiam pauperum. Pauperes enim et proselytos non abjicit, quibus etiam sic victum providit. 349DUnde subdit: Ego Dominus Deus vester, scilicet qui divites facio, qui pro vobis pauper fio, qui propter miseriam inopum et gemitum pauperum aliquando exsurgam.

Cum messueris, etc. Aperte ostendit quia doctores Judaeorum totius legis interpretationem non capiunt, sed quaedam pauperi et proselyto derelinquunt, gentibus scilicet, quibus dicitur: Beati pauperes, etc. (Matth. V). Qui et proselyti sunt, quia cognationi Abrahae se ingerunt. Quod ergo dicitur: Non occides, Non moechaberis, metant Judaeorum doctores; quae autem spiritualem habent interpretationem, sicut emundationes, sacrificia et similia, cedant Ecclesiae doctoribus, et quae illis metentibus decidunt, id est quae ipsi non intelligunt.

350AVERS. 10.-- Neque in vinea. (ISICH.) Vinea Domini sabaoth, etc., usque ad ut experimento divinum eloquium comprobetur.

VERS. 11.-- Non facietis furtum, etc. (AUG., quaest. 86 in Levit.) Hoc in Decalogo positum est, etc., usque ad et detestanda talem sequuntur errorem.

Non mentiemini. Cognationem peccatorum ostendit. Multa putamus parva, quae sunt maxima, ut mendacium, perjurium; quae furto sunt conjuncta, et recte, quia haec furto cooperantur.

VERS. 12.-- Non perjurabis, etc. LXX: Non levabitis, etc., usque ad talia juramenta non debent fieri, vel si fiant, custodiri.

Nec pollues, etc. Maledicendo, quia jusjurandum 350Btransgredi non potes. Deus enim, per quem juras, in tantum vult bona, ut si mala minetur, non tamen inferat, si poenitentes videat, ut pietatem impendat.

VERS. 13.-- Non facies calumniam. (AUG., ubi supra. ) Omnia quae prohibentur non committi in proximum, ad hoc unum referenda sunt quod dictum est: Non nocebis proximo. Nam quod sequitur: Non rapies, nisi ad hoc referatur, ne rapiendo noceatur, evenit ut non rapiendo quis noceat, nam gladius insanienti rapiendus est.

VERS. 13.-- Nec vi opprimes, etc. Paulatim auditorem erudit, et quae videntur parva, magnis conjungit: ut qui definita malorum poena terrentur, abstineant a minoribus. Qui enim sociantur in mandato, 350Cet in poena. Qui enim in uno offenderit, omnium reus est (Jac. II). Ideo vim calumniae conjungit. Nec differt quomodo quis res suas perdat, vel fraude, vel potentia, vel quolibet alio modo.

Non morabitur, etc. Merces, scilicet qua forsitan vivit cum uxore et filiis et parentibus. Si differtur, deficit victus; etiamsi in crastino recipiat, vim et calumniam pertulit, quia interim unde viveret non habuit.

Opus mercenarii, etc. Merces praedicatoris ex verbo est praedicationis: quod non debet apud nos morari, id est otiosum esse, usque mane, in futurum scilicet saeculum, qui huic nocti succedit, in quo est lux inexstinguibilis. Ex proventu enim 350Ddiscipuli crescit merces magistri.

VERS. 14.-- Non maledices surdo. Surdo maledicere est absenti et non audienti derogare. Coram caeco offendiculum ponere, est discretam quidem rem agere, sed ei qui lumen discretionis non habet, scandali occasionem praebere.

VERS. 15.-- Non facies, etc. (ISICH). Summam et perfectam justitiam, etc., usque ad et surdum ac caecum irridere divitum et potentum sunt.

VERS. 16.-- Non eris criminator. (ISICH.) LXX: Non ambulabis in domo, etc., usque ad qui tanto crimini est conjunctus.

Non stabis contra. Quasi non te conjunges his qui proximis insidiantur, sicut multi, aliis sociati, faciunt quod per se non possunt.

351AVERS. 18.-- Non quaeras ultionem. Id est, ne ulciscendi libidine rapiaris dum arguis, ut laetari vel consolari velis de alieno malo, sed potius consulere illi quem arguis. Unde secundum LXX sequitur: Non irasceris filiis populi tui, etc. Sic enim recte ira definita est, quae sit ulciscendi libido.

VERS. 19.-- Jumenta tua, etc. Haec ad litteram videntur esse ridicula, nec a prophetis et spiritualibus viris sunt observata. Unde David dicit: Ponite filium meum Salomonem in mulam meam (Eccli. XIX). Oportebit autem, secundum litteram ea quae ex tali commistione nascebantur, abominari. Unde: Mulier quoque fortis inveniet lanam et linum, et operata est utile manibus suis (Prov. XXXI).

VERS. 20.-- Homo si dormierit, etc. (ISICH.) Cum 351Bprohiberet jumenta misceri cum jumentis alterius generis, eos scilicet qui sunt sub jugo Christi suscipere jugum legis: opportune hoc subdidit ostendens quia et ante adventum Christi quicunque exterius miscebatur Judaeis, indigebat sacrificio veri arietis.
