Nota quanta differentia libertatis et servitutis. In libera enim desponsata mors adulterii poena est, in ancilla autem nubili, et, secundum LXX, custodita homini, id est desponsata, sacrificium jubetur offerri, non mortem inferri. Spiritualiter tamen haec intelligenda sunt.

Nubilis. LXX: Custodita homini, id est Synagoga custodita legi, quae Christo non poterat desponderi, priusquam moreretur littera legis. Unde: Mortui estis legi per corpus Christi, ut sitis alterius (Rom. VII). 351CAncilla est Synagoga, secundum illud: Abraham duos filios habuit, unum de ancilla, et unum de libera (Gal. IV).

Vapulabunt ambo. LXX: Visitatio erit eis, id est liberabuntur a servitute legis, secundum quod filiis Israel de Aegypto liberandis dicitur: Visitans visitavi vos.

Et non morientur. Quamvis enim secundum litteram agerent, non secundum spiritum, quia legislatoris intentionem ignorabant, facilius tamen assequebantur veniam.

VERS. 23.-- Quando ingressi, etc. Permanet in proposito, ostendens fructum bonum ante Christi adventum non habuisse Judaeos, cum dicit fructum non esse edendum in triennio, nec mundum, sed 351Dquarto anno mundandum, nec dicitur quae sit mundatio. Spiritualiter ergo intelligendum est quod dicitur.

Quando ingressi fueritis terram. Terram vocat divinam Scripturam, quam primo Judaei susceperunt. Unde plantare ligna pomifera praecepti sunt. Lignorum autem plantatio, meditatio legis est et praedicatio, secundum quod dicitur: Ego plantavi, Apollo rigavit, etc. (I Cor. III). Bona quidem mandata erant tanquam ligna pomifera, sed illi quod debebant non faciebant, tantum sequentes litteram. Fructus ergo, id est operatio legis, triennio erat immunda, ab Abraham usque ad David, a David usque ad transmigrationem Babylonis; inde usque ad Christum, sicut 352AMatthaeus distinxit (Matth. I). Quarto anno fructum legis sanctum commendat et laudabilem, non tamen adhuc esse Judaeis esibilem. Fructus enim legis in quarto anno, id est in adventu Christi, sanctus et laudabilis, quem Sanctum sanctorum faciebat, cujus opera laudabilia et gloriosa. Unde et in paralytico grabatum portante glorificabant Deum (Matth. VIII), qui dedit talem potestatem hominibus: sed nondum edebat Judaeus, quia non conversus. In quinto vero anno, id est praedicantibus apostolis, fructum ejus ederunt quicunque crediderunt.

Et plantaveritis, etc. (GREG., lib. VIII Moral., cap. 35.) Ligna pomifera, etc., usque ad in praesentis vitae nocte gaudere.

VERS. 26.-- Non comedetis, etc. Totum Judaeorum 352Bet gentilium radit fermentum. Unde nunc prohibet a legis servitute, nunc a gentilium errore.

(AUG.) Non comedere super montes, est superbis cogitationibus non pasci, de quibus Isaias: Omnis mons et collis humiliabitur (Isa. XL). Non augurari volucribus, non attendere quae rapiunt semen pietatis. Comam in rotundum tondere, est cogitationes daemonibus consecrare. Barbam radere, est virtutis perfectionem corrumpere, ficte operando. Carnem non incidere, est delectationes non suscipere, quae et animae nocent et corpus corrumpunt. Stigmata, prava doctrina, quae animae infigitur et difficulter aboletur.

Non augurabimini, etc. Per partem totum significat, omnes hujusmodi superstitiones condemnans. Qui enim talia curat observare, Deum videtur infamare, 352Ctanquam plus scientiae avibus quam hominibus contulerit.

VERS. 27.-- Neque in rotundum attondebitis comam, etc. Sicut barbari faciunt: debet quisque esse contentus naturalibus ornamentis, ne Creatorem videatur accusare, tanquam impotentem opus suum perficere, nec in honore daemonum cicinnos nutrire et fovere, sicut student pagani puerorum capita daemonibus offerre, quod maxime hic videtur prohibere.

VERS. 28.-- Stigmata. Legislator etiam curam nostri decoris gerit, ne carne abutamur in his etiam quae corpus deturpant.

VERS. 29.-- Ne prostituas filiam tuam. Carnalem, vel animam. Unde Psal. XXI: Erue a framea, Deus, animam meam, et de manu canis Unicam meam. Haec 352Drecedendo a Deo fornicantur cum daemonibus.

VERS. 30.-- Sabbata mea custodite. Ab uno sabbato omne legitimum otium sabbatum appellatur. Sic ab una generali abstinentia studet legislator per singula currere, praecipiens nos ab omni malo abstinere. Unde addit: Et sanctuarium meum metuite. Sicut enim qui digne accedunt, sanctificant, sic qui inique praesumunt, et immundi accedunt, quantum est in se, polluunt, sicut Nadab et Abiu. Unde Apostolus: Inter vos multi infirmi et imbecilles, etc. (I Cor. XI.) Inde additur: Ego Dominus, sanctus, scilicet, et etiam in sanctis habitans et sanctorum vindicans injuriam.

VERS. 31.-- Ad magos. LXX: Ventriloquas, id est 353Apythones, vel falsos prophetas, qui de ventre cordis sui fabulas quasdam et fetentes doctrinas proferunt.

Neque ab ariolis, etc. LXX: Veneficis, qui daemonum scilicet nomina invocant, et aliquando corpus curant, ut animam interficiant.

VERS. 32.-- Coram cano capite, etc. In senibus est sapientia, et in multo tempore prudentia (Job XII). Hinc Paulus ait: Presbyteri duplici honore digni habeantur, maxime qui laborant in verbo et doctrina (I Tim. V).

VERS. 33.-- Si habitaverit advena, etc. Terra a Deo nobis data, est virtutum scilicet cultura, et bona doctrina. Si quis ergo ad fidem nostram converti voluerit, et civis terrae nostrae ascribi, non affligamus eum, nec imponamus grave jugum, sicut Pharisaei, 353Bde quibus dicitur: Alligant onera gravia et importabilia, digito autem suo nolunt ea movere (Matth. XXIII). Sit nobis sicut indigena, qui scilicet nutritus est in fide, et crevit in pietate. Hinc Petrus ad eos qui credentes de gentibus circumcidere volebant, ait: Quid tentatis Deum, imponere jugum super cervices discipulorum? (Act. XV.)

VERS. 35.-- Nolite facere, etc. In praecedentibus praecipit in judicio justitiam servari, hic in ponderibus et mensuris, et ne abutamur ad injustitiam justitiae terminis: haec enim termini sunt vendentibus et ementibus, sicut leges judicibus; ut ab omni injustitia contra proximum caveatur. Quod enim diviti agrorum possessiones et pecuniae thesauri, hoc pauperi mensura vini et tritici, et hujusmodi.

353C(ISICH.) Nolite facere, etc. In judicio mentis non debemus injuste agere, nec mensuras naturalis justitiae corrumpere, aliter nostra, aliter proximi peccata judicantes. In quo enim judicio judicaverimus, judicabitur et de nobis; et in qua mensura mensi fuerimus, remetietur nobis (Matth. VII).

VERS. 36.-- Statera justa, etc. (ID.) Stateras quoque divinas, et mensuras divinas justas habeamus; id est, leges divinae Scripturae sancte et juste custodiamus: nihil nostrum inferentes, nihil suum auferentes, ne nobis istud dicatur: Quare transgredimini mandatum Dei propter traditiones vestras? (Matth. XV).

CHAPTER XIX.

348DVERS. 1, 2.-- The Lord spoke . . . Be ye holy. (ORIG., hom. 12 in Lev.) What this name which is "holy" signifies, etc., as far as namely, that ye may have your manner of life in the world.

VERS. 3.-- Every one. He builds upon the foundation: as if to say, be ye holy, that the Holy One may dwell in you; and pay the kindness of nature by honouring father and mother.

Keep my sabbaths. (ISICH.) Wishing to recapitulate the commandments of the law, that is, Thou shalt not kill, Thou shalt not steal, etc., etc., he says: Keep my sabbaths. For the true sabbath is to cease from every evil. Whence Moses: Ye shall do no servile work therein (Exod. XXXI).

Keep my sabbaths. God, wishing to teach us concerning the intelligible 349Arest, and concerning the consummation of the world, when it is impossible to prepare food or to work, nor to be occupied about anything bodily, says through Moses: Sabbaths today to the Lord, ye shall not find it in the field (Exod. XVI), etc. [Vulg. Ex 16:25 sabbatum est Domini: non invenietur hodie in agro, the singular "sabbath" and a passive "shall not be found"] Note that he does not say, sabbath, but sabbaths, because the intelligible rest is quiet and set free not from one thing, but from all carnal things: therefore in this place the name of sabbaths is first set down in divine Scripture, that thou mayest know that the sabbath is a full and perfect rest.

VERS. 5.-- If ye shall offer, etc. He goes over again almost all the chapters of the law, that he may fix the memory of them in his hearers; but especially the most necessary ones, such as this commandment which he set forth above, when he was treating of the sacrifices. He took the saving sacrifice, in which it is necessary to work the things which pertain to 349Bsalvation; and that it may be acceptable, piety and chastity must be offered up. For this, according to the Apostle, is good and acceptable before God our Saviour, who will have all men to be saved (I Tim. II). But to eat on the day of the offering and on the next is to work, in the present life, whatsoever pertains to salvation: for today and tomorrow are taken for the present age; whence James: Ye who say: Today and tomorrow we will go into the city, etc. (Jac. IV). [Vulg. Jac 4:13 hodie aut crastino ibimus in illam civitatem, "today or tomorrow ... into that city"] The third day, because on it the Lord rose from the dead, bears the figure of the age to come and of the resurrection, in which it will not be lawful to work, but to receive the recompense of works. He therefore who lives here as though he were to work there, as though he were to eat the sacrifice on the third day, is forbidden by the lawgiver to do this, 349Cbecause he leaves virtue imperfect, as though he could perfect it there.

VERS. 9.-- When thou shalt reap, etc. It is enjoined in the present chapter that we have love one toward another, that we be lovers of the poor and merciful toward them. For we ought to give to the needy, and not to exact debts by violence, and to grant the leavings of our goods, imitating God the giver of all things. Therefore he wills that they who reap or gather the vintage should leave something for the poor man and for the stranger, lest he seem ungrateful to the giver of all, who gave riches to some, and willed others to live upon the leavings of others; that so he might prove the stewardship of the rich, and the patience of the poor. For he does not cast off the poor and the strangers, for whom even in this way he has provided food. 349DWhence he adds: I am the Lord your God, namely, who make men rich, who for you become poor, who because of the misery of the needy and the groaning of the poor will one day arise.

When thou shalt reap, etc. He plainly shows that the doctors of the Jews do not take in the interpretation of the whole law, but leave certain things to the poor man and the stranger, that is, to the Gentiles, to whom it is said: Blessed are the poor, etc. (Matth. V). Who are also strangers, because they thrust themselves into the kindred of Abraham. What therefore is said: Thou shalt not kill, Thou shalt not commit adultery, let the doctors of the Jews reap; but the things which have a spiritual interpretation, such as the cleansings, the sacrifices and the like, let them yield to the doctors of the Church, and the things which fall from those reapers, that is, the things which they themselves do not understand.

350AVERS. 10.-- Neither in the vineyard. (ISICH.) The vineyard of the Lord of hosts, etc., as far as that the divine utterance may be proved by experience.

VERS. 11.-- Ye shall not commit theft, etc. (AUG., quaest. 86 in Levit.) This is set down in the Decalogue, etc., as far as and detestable things follow such an error.

Ye shall not lie. He shows the kinship of sins. We reckon many things small which are greatest, such as lying, perjury; which are joined to theft, and rightly, because these work together with theft.

VERS. 12.-- Thou shalt not forswear thyself, etc. LXX: Ye shall not lift up, etc., as far as such oaths ought not to be made, or, if they are made, to be kept.

Neither shalt thou pollute, etc. By cursing, because thou canst not 350Btransgress an oath. For God, by whom thou swearest, so far wills good things that, if he threaten evils, he nevertheless does not bring them, if he see men repenting, that he may bestow his mercy.

VERS. 13.-- Thou shalt not do calumny. (AUG., ubi supra. ) All the things which are forbidden to be committed against a neighbour are to be referred to this one thing which was said: Thou shalt not hurt thy neighbour. For as to what follows: Thou shalt not seize, unless it be referred to this, that no hurt be done by seizing, it comes about that a man does hurt by not seizing, for the sword must be seized from a madman.

VERS. 13.-- Neither shalt thou oppress by force, etc. Little by little he instructs the hearer, and joins the things which seem small to great ones: so that they who are terrified by the appointed penalty of evils may abstain from the lesser. For they who are associated in the commandment, 350Care so also in the penalty. For he who has offended in one is guilty of all (Jac. II). Therefore he joins force to calumny. Nor does it make a difference in what way a man loses his goods, whether by fraud, or by power, or by any other way whatever.

It shall not tarry, etc. The wages, namely, on which perhaps he lives with wife and children and parents. If it is deferred, his living fails; even if he receive it on the morrow, he has suffered force and calumny, because meanwhile he had not that whereon to live.

The work of the hireling, etc. The wages of the preacher are from the word of preaching: which ought not to tarry with us, that is, to be idle, until the morning, namely in the age to come, which succeeds this night, in which there is unquenchable light. For from the increase of the disciple 350Dgrows the wages of the master.

VERS. 14.-- Thou shalt not curse the deaf. To curse the deaf is to disparage one who is absent and does not hear. To set a stumbling block before the blind is to do a thing that is indeed discreet, but to offer occasion of scandal to him who has not the light of discretion.

VERS. 15.-- Thou shalt not do, etc. (ISICH). The highest and perfect justice, etc., as far as and to mock the deaf and the blind belongs to the rich and the powerful.

VERS. 16.-- Thou shalt not be a slanderer. (ISICH.) LXX: Thou shalt not walk in the house, etc., as far as who is joined to so great a crime.

Thou shalt not stand against. As if to say, thou shalt not join thyself to those who lie in wait for their neighbours, as many do who, being allied with others, do what by themselves they cannot.

351AVERS. 18.-- Seek not revenge. That is, be not carried away by the lust of avenging while thou reprovest, so as to wish to rejoice or to be consoled at another's evil, but rather to take counsel for him whom thou reprovest. Whence according to the LXX it follows: Thou shalt not be angry with the children of thy people, etc. For thus has anger rightly been defined, as that which is the lust of avenging.

VERS. 19.-- Thy beasts, etc. [Vulg. Lv 19:19 Jumentum tuum, in the singular] These things to the letter seem to be ridiculous, nor were they observed by the prophets and by spiritual men. Whence David says: Set my son Solomon upon my mule (Eccli. XIX). [Vulg. 3 Rg 1:33 imponite Salomonem filium meum super mulam meam] But it will be needful, according to the letter, to abominate the things which were born of such mingling. Whence: The valiant woman also shall find wool and flax, and hath wrought a useful thing with her hands (Prov. XXXI). [Vulg. Pr 31:13 Quaesivit lanam et linum, et operata est consilia manuum suarum, "She sought wool and flax, and hath wrought by the counsel of her hands"]

VERS. 20.-- If a man shall sleep, etc. (ISICH.) Since 351Bhe had forbidden beasts to be mingled with beasts of another kind, that is, that those who are under the yoke of Christ should take up the yoke of the law: he opportunely subjoined this, showing that even before the coming of Christ whosoever was outwardly mingled with the Jews had need of the sacrifice of the true ram.

Note how great is the difference between liberty and servitude. For in a free woman betrothed the penalty of adultery is death, but in a bondwoman of marriageable age, and, according to the LXX, kept for a man, that is, betrothed, a sacrifice is commanded to be offered, not death to be inflicted. Yet these things are to be understood spiritually.

Of marriageable age. LXX: Kept for a man, that is, the Synagogue kept for the law, which could not be betrothed to Christ before the letter of the law died. Whence: Ye are dead to the law by the body of Christ, that ye may be another's (Rom. VII). [Vulg. Rom 7:4 mortificati estis legi, "ye are made dead to the law"] 351CThe bondwoman is the Synagogue, according to that: Abraham had two sons, one of the bondwoman, and one of the free (Gal. IV).

They shall both be beaten. LXX: There shall be a visitation for them, that is, they shall be freed from the servitude of the law, according to what is said to the children of Israel who were to be freed out of Egypt: Visiting I have visited you.

And they shall not die. For although they acted according to the letter, not according to the spirit, because they were ignorant of the lawgiver's intention, yet they the more easily obtained pardon.

VERS. 23.-- When ye are entered, etc. He continues in his purpose, showing that the Jews had not good fruit before the coming of Christ, when he says that the fruit is not to be eaten in the three years, nor is it clean, but 351Dis to be cleansed in the fourth year, nor is it said what the cleansing is. Spiritually therefore is that to be understood which is said.

When ye are entered into the land. By the land he means the divine Scripture, which the Jews first received. Whence they were commanded to plant fruit-bearing trees. But the planting of trees is the meditation of the law and preaching, according to what is said: I planted, Apollo watered, etc. (I Cor. III). The commandments were indeed good, like fruit-bearing trees, but they did not do what they owed, following only the letter. The fruit therefore, that is, the working of the law, was unclean for three years, from Abraham to David, from David to the transmigration of Babylon; thence to Christ, as 352AMatthew distinguished (Matth. I). In the fourth year he commends the fruit of the law as holy and praiseworthy, yet not as being edible for the Jews as yet. For the fruit of the law in the fourth year, that is, in the coming of Christ, is holy and praiseworthy, which the Holy of holies made, whose works are praiseworthy and glorious. Whence also, over the paralytic carrying his bed, they glorified God (Matth. VIII), who gave such power to men: but the Jew did not yet eat, because he was not converted. But in the fifth year, that is, when the apostles preached, whosoever believed ate the fruit of it.

And ye shall plant, etc. (GREG., lib. VIII Moral., cap. 35.) Fruit-bearing trees, etc., as far as to rejoice in the night of the present life.

VERS. 26.-- Ye shall not eat, etc. He shaves away the whole leaven of the Jews 352Band of the Gentiles. Whence now he forbids the servitude of the law, now the error of the Gentiles.

(AUG.) Not to eat upon the mountains is not to be fed with proud thoughts, of which Isaiah says: Every mountain and hill shall be brought low (Isa. XL). Not to augur by birds is not to attend to those things which snatch away the seed of piety. To shear the hair round is to consecrate one's thoughts to the demons. To shave the beard is to corrupt the perfection of virtue by working feignedly. Not to cut the flesh is not to take up delights, which both harm the soul and corrupt the body. The stigmata are wicked doctrine, which is fixed in the soul and is with difficulty done away.

Ye shall not augur, etc. By the part he signifies the whole, condemning all superstitions of this kind. For he who takes care to observe such things seems to defame God, 352Cas though he had conferred more knowledge upon birds than upon men.

VERS. 27.-- Neither shall ye round off the hair, etc. As the barbarians do: every man ought to be content with natural ornaments, lest he seem to accuse the Creator, as though unable to perfect his own work, nor in honour of the demons to nourish and cherish ringlets, as the pagans are eager to offer the heads of their boys to the demons, which he seems here chiefly to forbid.

VERS. 28.-- Stigmata. The lawgiver takes care also for our comeliness, that we may not misuse the flesh even in those things which disfigure the body.

VERS. 29.-- Prostitute not thy daughter. The carnal one, or the soul. Whence Psal. XXI: Deliver, O God, my soul from the sword, and my only one from the hand of the dog. These, 352Dby withdrawing from God, commit fornication with the demons.

VERS. 30.-- Keep my sabbaths. From the one sabbath every lawful rest is called a sabbath. So from one general abstinence the lawgiver is at pains to run through the particulars, commanding us to abstain from every evil. Whence he adds: And fear my sanctuary. For as they who approach worthily sanctify it, so they who presume wickedly, and approach unclean, so far as in them lies, pollute it, as Nadab and Abiu. Whence the Apostle: Among you are many weak and feeble, etc. (I Cor. XI.) Thence it is added: I am the Lord, holy, namely, and also dwelling in the holy ones and avenging the wrong done to the holy ones

VERS. 31.-- To the magicians. LXX: Ventriloquists, that is, 353Apythons, or false prophets, who out of the belly of their heart bring forth certain fables and stinking doctrines.

Neither to the soothsayers, etc. LXX: Poisoners, who, namely, invoke the names of the demons, and sometimes heal the body, that they may kill the soul.

VERS. 32.-- Before the hoary head, etc. In old men is wisdom, and in length of time prudence (Job XII). [Vulg. Job 12:12 In antiquis est sapientia, "In the ancients is wisdom"] Hence Paul says: Let the priests be accounted worthy of double honour, especially they who labour in the word and doctrine (I Tim. V).

VERS. 33.-- If a stranger dwell, etc. The land given us by God is the cultivation of the virtues, namely, and good doctrine. If therefore any man will be converted to our faith, and be enrolled a citizen of our land, let us not afflict him, nor lay upon him a heavy yoke, as the Pharisees did, 353Bof whom it is said: They bind heavy and insupportable burdens, but with their own finger they will not move them (Matth. XXIII). Let him be to us as one home-born, who, namely, has been nourished in the faith, and has grown in piety. Hence Peter says to those who wished to circumcise the believers of the Gentiles: Why tempt ye God, to put a yoke upon the necks of the disciples? (Act. XV.)

VERS. 35.-- Do not, etc. In the foregoing he commands that justice be kept in judgment, here in weights and measures, and that we do not misuse to injustice the boundary-stones of justice: for these are the boundary-stones of sellers and buyers, as the laws are of judges; that men may beware of every injustice against a neighbour. For what to the rich man are the possessions of fields and the treasures of money, that to the poor man is a measure of wine and of wheat, and the like.

353C(ISICH.) Do not, etc. In the judgment of the mind we ought not to act unjustly, nor to corrupt the measures of natural justice, judging our own sins in one way and our neighbour's in another. For with what judgment we shall have judged, it shall be judged also of us; and with what measure we shall have measured, it shall be measured again to us (Matth. VII). [Vulg. Mt 7:2 judicaveritis ... judicabimini ... mensi fueritis, remetietur vobis, in the second person: "with what judgment you judge, you shall be judged"]

VERS. 36.-- A just balance, etc. (ID.) Let us have divine balances also, and just divine measures; that is, let us keep the laws of divine Scripture holily and justly: bringing in nothing of our own, taking away nothing of theirs, lest this be said to us: Why do you transgress the commandment of God for your traditions? (Matth. XV).

CAPUT XX.

VERS. 1.-- Locutus est Dominus, etc. Frequenter 353Dlex eadem repetit, ut eorum memoriam infigat legenti: quaedam tamen nova interserit, ne superfluere videatur. Sed nova inquirendo jam dicta studiosius inquirantur. Unde superius quaedam prohibendo poenam tacuit, ut quaerentes quae vel qualis sit, in sequentibus diligenter investigemus. Cum enim superius Moloch prohiberet sacrificari, idipsum poenam addendo repetit: populus scilicet terrae lapidabit eum, non solum principes. Nec his contentus addit: Ego ponam faciem meam contra illum, etc.

VERS. 4.-- Quod si negligens, etc. Provocat alios contra illum, et terret, si neglexerint poenam tam studiose praeceptam, Deo ipso irascente et ferente sententiam.

354ANec voluerit, etc. (ISICH.) Malum est permittere etiam eos qui foris sunt, fornicari a Deo et adhaerere diabolo: sed multo magis de medio populi, hoc est enim totum gregem lupo concedere. Consentientes autem, qui scilicet possunt accusare, vel arguere, vel monere, et non faciunt, non effugient judicium. Ponit Deus faciem super illum, de quo dicitur: Vultus autem Domini super facientes mala, etc. (Psal. XXXIII).

VERS. 6.-- Anima quae declinaverit ad magos, etc. Grande peccatum est ad magos et ariolos declinare: hoc est enim a Deo recedere. Sunt autem magi intelligibiles, qui in nomine Domini falsa prophetant; sunt arioli deceptores et adulatores, qui veneficis verbis auditus multorum corrumpunt et a veritate 354Bavertunt.

VERS. 9.-- Qui maledixerit patri suo, etc. (ORIG., hom. 11 in Lev.) De his superius praecepta dedit, etc., usque ad ne puniat contemptorem servum.

(ID.) Nomen patris vel matris, etc., usque ad ad aliquam terrenam civitatem invertunt sensibus depravatis?

Sanguis ejus, etc. Quia ipse est sibi causa mortis. Si sic punitur, qui carnali patri maledixerit, quomodo punietur qui Christum blasphemaverit, nec obaudivit dicenti: Nisi quis renatus fuerit ex aqua et Spiritu sancto, etc. (Joan. III), morte scilicet morietur, alienatus a regno coelorum reus est: nihil enim grave, nec operosum, aut laboriosum ei praeceptum est.

354CVERS. 10.-- Si moechatus quis, etc. (AUG., quaest. 73 in Lev.) Hic aliquid voluit distare, etc., usque ad quam homo homini.

Si moechatus, etc. (ISICH.) Adulterium corporate malum est, etc., usque ad qui ei commisit hanc curam.

VERS. 11.-- Qui dormierit, etc. Haec superius dicta et exposita sunt, ubi dictum est: Non revelabis turpitudinem patris tui, etc. Sed hic aperte tan git eum qui idem quod proximus agit, nec peccati societate erubescens, prodit; in quo mortis poenam legislator sanxit. Novercae autem virum conjunxit, quia quod in patrem a filio delinquitur, hoc in filium a patre committitur, et similis utrobique impietas.

(ORIG., ubi supra. ) Supra dictum est, etc., usque 354Dad non solum pro qualitate, sed etiam pro quantitate.

VERS. 13.-- Qui dormierit cum masculo, etc. Hoc peccatum injuria naturae est: unde et morte mulctari decretum est. Spiritualiter autem eum qui fortis est animo et masculus (vir enim in divina Scriptura, perfectus in virtutibus nominatur) verbis pravis vel actionibus effeminare caveamus, ne cum ipso dissolvamur et simul moriamur.

VERS. 14.-- Qui supra uxorem, etc. Saevam huic delicto poenam decernit. Qui enim ex matre sunt geniti, his quos filia genuit, cum unus pater sit, incertum est utrum fratres sint an avunculi. Caveatur ergo tam incerta commistio. Sed quia omnis peccati 355Aconcupiscentia mater est, si qua nobis concupiscentia sit, actionem non conjungamus ei. Qui enim hoc facit, igne incenditur, et concupiscentia et actio ejus. Si enim facta carnis spiritu mortificaverimus, vivemus (Rom. VIII).

VERS. 15.-- Qui cum jumento, etc. Nimia contra eos exercetur districtio, qui in tantum iniqui et libidini dediti, ut inaudita scelera concupiscerent: unde viris vel mulieribus commistis quadrupedibus, mortis poenam indixit. Fidelibus quoque non erit haec lex superflua, si quadrupedia voluntates pravas ad terram pronas intelligamus: quae manus sicut homines non habent, quia omni humana actione carent, unde cognationes nobiscum non habent. Vir ergo vel mulier, quae non languore naturae, sed deliberatione 355Beis cohaeserit vel coierit, reus est mortis. Ipsi quoque mori dicuntur illis mortuis, neque enim per se subsistunt, nec ulla peccati substantia est, sed in peccatoribus operatur.

(AUG.) Qui cum jumento, etc. Quaeritur quomodo pecus reum sit, cum nec rationale nec legis capax sit? an quemadmodum transferuntur verba metaphorica ab animali ad animale; sicut dicitur ventus improbus, vel iratum mare, ita et hic translatum a rationali ad irrationale? Nam inde pecora jussa sunt interfici, quia tali flagitio contaminata indignam refricant memoriam facti.

VERS. 17.-- Qui acceperit sororem suam, etc. (ISICH.) Hoc tolerabile videbatur, quia Abraham de Sara dicit: Soror mea est ex patre, et non ex matre: 355Csed eos qui de Thare patre Abraham geniti sunt Moyses enumerans, nusquam Sarae fecit mentionem. Quod si sorori suae Abraham nupsit, ante tamen hoc quam Deum cognosceret, fecit. Bene ergo lex talem conjunctionem prohibuit.

Qui acceperit, etc. Tropologice turpitudinem sororis ex patre et non ex matre videt et accipit, qui turpem actionem a patre vel a matre (in qua scilicet deliquerunt) admiratur, et imitatur: non solum perscrutans quae non oportuit, sed conjungens se parentum pravitati quae soror ejus dicitur, quasi ab eisdem genita parentibus. Quia vero filii publicant parentum peccata faciendo similia, recte in conspectu populi infligitur poena; vel juxta LXX: In conspectu filiorum generis sui, eorum scilicet qui talia cogitant 355Dimitari: ut unius afflictio, multorum sit correctio.

Viderit turpitudinem, etc. (AUG., quaest. 75.) Id est, concumbendo cognoverit, sicut ibi: Cognovit Adam uxorem suam (Gen. IV), id est, ei mistus est. Quod autem ait: Portabunt iniquitatem suam, id est, ipsum peccatum poena erat.

VERS. 20.-- Qui coierit, etc. Intendit legislator continentiam suadere, ne more pecorum cum mulieribus continue misceamur, sed velut coacti, ad usum continendi paulatim veniamus. Mirum autem quod nunc tales de populo suo disperdi praecipit, cum superius eisdem legem purgationis indixerit. Contemplemur ergo menstruatam gentilium sapientiam a Deo remotam, de volatu avium, et cursu siderum, 356Aet mensuris coeli disputantem. Cum hac dormire, id est delectari, vel revelare et palam turpitudinem ejus proferre, fontemque totius erroris exponentem audire, impium est. Ideo talis interficietur de medio populi sui, ne nominetur homo qui imaginem Dei et seipsum ignorando Creatorem a se alienavit. Ipsa quoque interficietur, ne sapientia nominetur, quia sapientia hujus mundi stultitia est apud Deum (I Cor. III).

Turpitudinem, etc. Sororibus parentum filios misceri prohibet, ut generationes non confundantur, et parentibus reverentia exhibeatur. Secundum spiritum vero et parentum peccata debemus velare, et eorum qui eis sociati sunt, quasi fratres et sorores, quibus nudatis parentes latere non possunt. Unde addit: Ignominiam carnis suae nudabit. LXX: Sine 356Bfiliis morietur, id est, sine virtutibus, quae est pessima sterilitas.

Qui coierit, etc. (ISICH.) Ostendit quia causa filiorum, etc., usque ad superioribus similis est.

(AUG., quaest. 76. in Lev.) Qui coierit cum uxore patris, etc. Quicunque dormierit cum cognata sua, etc., usque ad id est nullo jure succedant parentibus.

VERS. 22.-- Custodite leges, etc. Non sufficit partem legis custodire, necesse habemus observare omnia praecepta et judicia.

VERS. 23.-- Nolite ambulare, etc. Non solum Judaeis, sed omnibus gentibus haec observanda sunt, si Deo servire volunt.

VERS. 24.-- Lacte. Deliciis beatitudinis, quibus nihil suavius, nihil dulcius, et sine omni labore et 356Cdolore erunt, sicut mel et lac, quae sine nostro labore praeparantur.

VERS. 26.-- Ego Dominus. Ne cogitetis dicentes: Quid comedemus, aut quid bibemus, aut quo operiemur? haec enim omnia gentes inquirunt (Matth. VI). Qui autem sublimiorem conversationem et retributionem sequuntur, separari debent a caeteris gentibus. Separate, etc. Jumenta vel pecora, populares: volatilia, contemplatores lex appellat. Omnis popularis mundus propter simplicitatem a pluribus judicatur. Similiter contemplativus, sed non ita est: popularis enim debet esse prudens in bono, simplex in malo, hic enim solus mundus est. Contemplativus quoque conscientiam debet habere mundam, et 356Dfidem non fictam. Qui haec non habent, ad vaniloquia convertuntur, non intelligentes neque quae loquuntur, neque de quibus affirmant (I Tim. I). Diligenter ergo mundi separandi sunt ab immundis, ut mundos sequamur, immundos fugiamus. Notandum vero quam discrete in quadrupedibus et volatilibus hic legislator distinguit, et non in reptilibus terrae. Cuncta enim quae moventur in terra immunda sunt, terrena autem sapiunt, quibus Deus venter est ipsorum (Philip. III).

Et quae, etc. (AUG., quaest. 76 in Lev.) Videtur dicere quod non sunt haec natura immunda, sed pro aliquo sacramenti signo, quia dicit quia ostendit eis immunda, tanquam immunda eis non essent nisi ostensa fuissent.

357ALapidibus. Quia vanis erroribus simplicem populum seducunt; unde ventrem nutriunt, et auditoribus placere cupiunt: quales hodie in Ecclesia multi sunt, de quibus Paulus ait: Hi enim non serviunt Christo, sed suo ventri, et per dulces sermones, et benedictiones seducunt corda innocentium (Rom. XVI).

CHAPTER XX.

VERS. 1.-- The Lord spoke, etc. Frequently 353Dthe law repeats the same things, that it may fix the memory of them in the reader: yet it inserts certain new things, lest it should seem superfluous. But by inquiring into the new things, the things already said are inquired into more studiously. Whence above, in forbidding certain things, it kept silence as to the penalty, so that we, asking what or of what kind it is, should diligently investigate it in what follows. For since above he forbade sacrifice to be made to Moloch, he repeats that same thing by adding the penalty: the people, namely, of the land shall stone him, not the princes alone. Nor content with these things he adds: I will set my face against him, etc.

VERS. 4.-- But if being negligent, etc. He stirs up others against him, and terrifies them, if they shall have neglected a penalty so studiously enjoined, God himself being angry and passing sentence.

354AAnd will not, etc. (ISICH.) It is an evil to allow even those who are without to commit fornication away from God and to cleave to the devil: but much more from the midst of the people, for this is to yield the whole flock to the wolf. But those who consent, who, namely, are able to accuse, or to reprove, or to admonish, and do not do it, shall not escape judgment. God sets his face upon him, of whom it is said: But the countenance of the Lord is upon them that do evil, etc. (Psal. XXXIII).

VERS. 6.-- The soul that shall turn aside to magicians, etc. It is a great sin to turn aside to magicians and soothsayers: for this is to withdraw from God. But there are intelligible magicians, who prophesy false things in the name of the Lord; there are soothsayers, deceivers and flatterers, who with poisonous words corrupt the hearing of many and turn them away 354Bfrom the truth.

VERS. 9.-- He that curseth his father, etc. (ORIG., hom. 11 in Lev.) Concerning these he gave commandments above, etc., as far as that he may not punish the contemptuous servant.

(ID.) The name of father or of mother, etc., as far as do they turn it, with depraved senses, to some earthly city?

His blood, etc. Because he himself is to himself the cause of death. If he is so punished who has cursed a carnal father, how shall he be punished who has blasphemed Christ, and did not obey him who says: Unless a man be born again of water and the Holy Ghost, etc. (Joan. III), he shall die the death, namely, and being estranged from the kingdom of heaven he is guilty: for nothing heavy, nor toilsome, or laborious has been enjoined upon him.

354CVERS. 10.-- If any man commit adultery, etc. (AUG., quaest. 73 in Lev.) Here he willed something to differ, etc., as far as than man to man.

If any man commit adultery, etc. (ISICH.) Adultery is a bodily evil, etc., as far as who committed this charge to him.

VERS. 11.-- He that sleepeth, etc. These things have been said and expounded above, where it was said: Thou shalt not uncover the nakedness of thy father, etc. But here he openly touches him who does the same as his neighbour, and, not blushing at the fellowship of the sin, betrays it; in which the lawgiver has established the penalty of death. But he joined the husband to the stepmother, because what is done wrong against a father by a son, this is committed against a son by a father, and the impiety is alike on either side.

(ORIG., ubi supra. ) It was said above, etc., as far 354Das not only according to the quality, but also according to the quantity.

VERS. 13.-- He that sleepeth with a male, etc. This sin is an injury to nature: whence also it was decreed that it be punished with death. But spiritually let us beware of making effeminate by wicked words or actions him who is strong in mind and male (for a man, in divine Scripture, is named one perfect in virtues), lest we be dissolved together with him and die together.

VERS. 14.-- He that upon his wife, etc. He decrees a savage penalty for this offence. For as to those who are begotten of the mother, and those whom the daughter has borne, since the father is one, it is uncertain whether they are brothers or uncles. Let so uncertain a mingling therefore be guarded against. But because concupiscence is the mother of every sin, 355Aif there be any concupiscence in us, let us not join action to it. For he who does this is burned with fire, both his concupiscence and his action. For if by the spirit we shall have mortified the deeds of the flesh, we shall live (Rom. VIII). [Vulg. Rom 8:13 si autem spiritu facta carnis mortificaveritis, vivetis, in the second person: "if you mortify ... you shall live"]

VERS. 15.-- He that with a beast, etc. Excessive strictness is exercised against those who were so wicked and given over to lust that they lusted after unheard-of crimes: whence upon men or women mingled with four-footed beasts he laid the penalty of death. Nor will this law be superfluous for the faithful, if by four-footed beasts we understand depraved wills bent down to the earth: which have not hands as men have, because they lack all human action, whence they have no kinship with us. A man therefore or a woman, who not by the weakness of nature but by deliberation 355Bshall have cleaved to them or come together with them, is guilty of death. They too are said to die when those are dead, for neither do they subsist by themselves, nor is there any substance of sin, but it works in sinners.

(AUG.) He that with a beast, etc. It is asked: how is the beast guilty, since it is neither rational nor capable of law? or is it as metaphorical words are transferred from one animal to another, as a wind is called wicked, or the sea angry, so here too a transfer is made from the rational to the irrational? For therefore were the beasts commanded to be killed, because, defiled by such an outrage, they rub up again an unworthy memory of the deed.

VERS. 17.-- He that taketh his sister, etc. (ISICH.) This seemed tolerable, because Abraham says of Sara: She is my sister by the father, and not by the mother: 355Cbut Moses, enumerating those who were begotten of Thare the father of Abraham, nowhere made mention of Sara. But if Abraham married his own sister, yet he did it before he knew God. Rightly therefore did the law forbid such a union.

He that taketh, etc. Tropologically, he sees and takes the nakedness of a sister by the father and not by the mother, who admires and imitates a base action of his father or of his mother (in which, namely, they went wrong): not only searching out the things which he ought not, but joining himself to the depravity of his parents, which is called his sister, as though begotten of the same parents. But because sons publish their parents' sins by doing the like, rightly is the penalty inflicted in the sight of the people; or according to the LXX: In the sight of the sons of his own race, namely of those who think to 355Dimitate such things: that the affliction of one may be the correction of many.

He shall have seen the nakedness, etc. (AUG., quaest. 75.) That is, shall have known her by lying with her, as in that place: Adam knew his wife (Gen. IV), that is, was mingled with her. But as to his saying: They shall bear their iniquity, that is, the sin itself was the penalty.

VERS. 20.-- He that shall come together, etc. The lawgiver intends to urge continence, that we may not after the manner of cattle be continually mingled with women, but that, as it were constrained, we may come by little and little to the habit of containing. But it is a wonder that he now commands such to be destroyed from among his people, when above he had appointed for the same a law of purification. Let us therefore contemplate the menstruous wisdom of the Gentiles, far from God, disputing of the flight of birds, and the course of the stars, 356Aand the measures of heaven. To sleep with her, that is, to take delight in her, or to uncover and bring forth openly her nakedness, and to listen to her setting forth the fountain of all error, is impious. Therefore let such a one be killed from the midst of his people, lest he be named a man who, by not knowing the image of God and himself, has estranged the Creator from himself. She too shall be killed, lest she be named wisdom, because the wisdom of this world is foolishness with God (I Cor. III).

The nakedness, etc. He forbids sons to be mingled with the sisters of their parents, that generations be not confounded, and that reverence be shown to parents. But according to the spirit we ought both to veil the sins of our parents, and those of them who are associated with them as brothers and sisters, since, when these are stripped bare, the parents cannot lie hid. Whence he adds: He shall uncover the shame of his own flesh. LXX: He shall die 356Bwithout children, that is, without virtues, which is the worst barrenness.

He that shall come together, etc. (ISICH.) He shows that for the sake of children, etc., as far as it is like the things above.

(AUG., quaest. 76. in Lev.) He that shall come together with his father's wife, etc. Whosoever shall have slept with his kinswoman, etc., as far as that is, let them by no right succeed their parents.

VERS. 22.-- Keep my laws, etc. It is not enough to keep a part of the law; we must needs observe all the precepts and judgments.

VERS. 23.-- Walk not, etc. Not to the Jews only, but to all nations are these things to be observed, if they will serve God.

VERS. 24.-- With milk. With the delights of blessedness, than which nothing is sweeter, nothing more pleasant, and they shall be without all labour and 356Csorrow, like honey and milk, which are prepared without our labour.

VERS. 26.-- I am the Lord. Think not, saying: What shall we eat, or what shall we drink, or wherewith shall we be covered? for after all these things do the nations seek (Matth. VI). [Vulg. Mt 6:31 Nolite ergo solliciti esse, dicentes: Quid manducabimus, "Be not therefore solicitous, saying: What shall we eat"] But they who follow a loftier manner of life and recompense ought to be separated from the rest of the nations. Separate, etc. Beasts of burden or cattle the law calls the common people: winged creatures, the contemplative. Every common man is judged clean by most, because of his simplicity. Likewise the contemplative, but it is not so: for the common man ought to be prudent in good, simple in evil, for he alone is clean. The contemplative also ought to have a clean conscience, and 356Dfaith unfeigned. They who have not these turn aside to vain talk, understanding neither the things they say, nor whereof they affirm (I Tim. I). Diligently therefore are the clean to be separated from the unclean, that we may follow the clean, and flee the unclean. But it is to be noted how discreetly this lawgiver distinguishes among the four-footed beasts and the winged creatures, and not among the creeping things of the earth. For all the things which move upon the earth are unclean, and they savour of earthly things, whose God is their belly (Philip. III).

And the things which, etc. (AUG., quaest. 76 in Lev.) He seems to say that these things are not by nature unclean, but for the sake of some sign of a mystery, because he says that he showed them to be unclean, as though they would not have been unclean to them unless they had been shown.

357AWith stones. Because with vain errors they seduce the simple people; whence they nourish the belly, and desire to please their hearers: such as many are today in the Church, of whom Paul says: For these serve not Christ, but their own belly, and by sweet words and blessings seduce the hearts of the innocent (Rom. XVI).

CAPUT XXI.

VERS. 1.-- Dixit quoque Dominus ad Moysen. . . . Ne contaminetur, etc. (ISICH.) Id est nihil omittat ex his quae expediat sacerdotem docere, etc., usque ad cohabitantium enim non poterit ignorare peccatum.

VERS. 2.-- Nisi tantum in consanguineis. (HIERON., epist. ad Fabiolam, tom. I.) Super omnem animam quae mortua est, etc., usque ad minoribus vero aliquid 357Bindulgetur.

VERS. 5.-- Non radent caput. (ID., ibid. ) Summus autem pontifex, etc., usque ad quibus Christus donavit ut lumen sint.

Non radent caput, etc. (ISICH.) Nimis aegre dolere sacerdotes de mortuis prohibet lex, etc., usque ad ut possit exhortari, arguere, increpare.

Non polluent nomen, etc. (ID.) Quod est magnum in gentibus. Vos autem, dicit Malachias, polluistis illud in eo quod dicitis: Mensa Domini deserta est (Malach. I). In peccato enim sacrificii nomen Dei polluunt sacerdotes.

VERS. 7.-- Scortum, etc. Sacerdos quilibet, nam de summo postea dicet, quod non nisi virginem 357Cducat uxorem.

Quae repudiata est, etc. Quia culpabilis invenitur, hanc non debet ducere sacerdos uxorem, nec praedicere ejus observantiam litteralem.

VERS. 8.-- Sint ergo sancti, etc. Si sacerdotes datam jam sibi a Deo sanctificationem custodiunt, sanctificant ipsum Deum: qui cum in natura sanctus sit, a participibus gloriae suae voluit sanctificari.

VERS. 9.-- Sacerdotis filia, etc. Grave est cujuslibet filiam fornicari, sed maxime sacerdotis, qui debet habere filios subditos in omni castitate.

Sacerdotis filia. Synagoga non solum uxor, sed et Dei filia appellatur; sed utrumque depravans, aliena facta est. Unde: Relinquetur filia Sion sicut 357Dumbraculum vineae, etc. (Isa. I). Et alibi: Fornicata est mater eorum (Ose. II): quod igne sit exusta, incendii monstrant vestigia.

VERS. 10.-- Pontifex, etc. Christus magnus Dominus noster, et magna virtus ejus, qui pro nobis factus est sacerdos et hostia.

Consecratae sunt. Alii sacerdotes in sanguine vituli et arietis consecrabant, vel consummabant, scilicet, manus suas: ipse vero proprio sanguine, crucifixis manibus.

Vestimenta non scindet, etc. (ISICH.) Ex quo enim Verbum caro factum est, etc., usque ad haec vere sancta sunt indumenta.

VERS. 11.-- Ad omnem mortuum, etc. (ISICH.) 358ALXX: Animam mortuam. Anima quae peccaverit ipsa morietur (Ezech. XVIII; Isa. LIII). Hac morte maxime gentes perierunt, quae nec legem nec doctrinam habuerunt; sed Christus peccata omnium tulit, et tamen impollutus mansit.

(AUG. quaest. 81 in Lev.) Ad omnem mortuum, etc. LXX: Super omnem animam mortuam non introibit. Quomodo dicat animam mortuam, etc., usque ad quod secundum non prohibuit.

VERS. 12.-- (ID., quaest. 82.) De sanctis non exibit. Dum scilicet eorum funera celebrantur, de sanctis prohibitus est exire, etc., usque ad quod nonnullis diebus non imponebatur incensum.

Super patre. (ORIG., hom. 14 in Lev.) Omnis homo in patre et matre pollutus est, etc., usque ad vel 358Bcoelestis matris libertatem indigna peccati servitute maculant.

(AUG., quaest. 85 in Levit.) Quaeri potest, etc., usque ad sicut de ipso Aaron Scriptura testatur.

Ego Dominus. (AUG. quaest. 84.) De Moyse etiam dictum est, etc., usque ad nec superflua judicata est visibilis sanctificatio, quia invisibilis praecessit.

VERS. 13.-- Virginem ducet uxorem. (HIERON., epist. ad Fabiolam. ) Vidua est cujus maritus, etc., usque ad unde: Despondi enim vos uni viro virginem castam, etc.

Nolo discipulam conjugemque pontificis quidquam habere veteris hominis. Si consurreximus cum Christo, quae sursum sunt sapiamus, praeteritorum obliti, 358Cfuturorum avidi. Infelix Simon qui post baptismum de antiquo matrimonio cogitavit, nec ad virginalem venerat puritatem, Petri consortio indignus fuit.

VERS. 14.-- Viduam autem et repudiatam, etc. (ORIG., hom. 12 in Levit.) Qui peccat, abjectus est; etiam si episcopus tacet, vel ignorantia, vel negligentia, non prodest hujusmodi gratia; animam abjectam Christus non ducit uxorem, nec pollutam, quae scilicet tantum peccatum voluit et optavit, quia magnus pontifex omnino puram requirit animam, quam sibi jungat et unum spiritum efficiat. Qui peccat ad mortem, abjectus est; qui venialiter, pollutus est.

Sordidam, etc. Quae vadit post amatores, scilicet daemones, qui animam pulchram, id est, ad imaginem Dei factam, adulterare cupiunt.

358DVERS. 17.-- Homo de semine. (ISICH.) Haec secundum litteram tantum, etc., usque ad valde per contumeliam refragatur.

VERS. 18.-- Si caecus (ISICH.) Lumen contemplationis ignorans, praesentis vitae tenebris pressus, qui dum venturam lucem diligenter non conspicit, nescit quo porrigat operis gressum.

Si claudus. (ID.) Qui scilicet videt quo pergere debeat, sed infirmitate mentis non potest facere quod desiderat.

Si parvo, vel grandi, vel torto naso. (ID.) Parvo naso est, qui ad tenendum mensuram discretionis idoneus non est: naso enim odores fetoresque dis cernimus. Nasus grandis et tortus, est discretionis 359Aimmoderata subtilitas, quae plus quam oportet vel decet excrescens, actionis confundit rectitudinem.

VERS. 19.-- Si fracto pede. (ISICH.) Qui viam Dei pergere non potest, et boni operis exsors est, non ut claudus, qui saltem cum infirmitate tenebat

VERS. 20.-- Si gibbus. (ID.) Terrena sollicitudine pressus ad superna non respiciens, inferiora tantum inspiciens.

Si lippus. Lippus, cujus ingenium ad cognitionem veritatis emicat, sed operatio carnalis obscurat. In lippis enim pupillae sanae sunt, sed humore defluente palpebrae grossescunt, cujus crebra infusione acies etiam pupillae vitiatur.

Si albuginem. (ID.) Albuginem habet qui veritatis lucem videre non sinitur, quia arrogantia sapientiae 359Bvel justitiae caecatur. Pupilla enim nigra videt, alba non videt: quia sensus humanae cogitationis si stultum se vel peccatorem intelligit, cognitionem intimae claritatis apprehendit: si candorem justitiae vel sapientiae sibi attribuit, a superna cognitione se excludit.

Si jugem scabiem. (ID.) Cui scilicet petulantia carnis sine cessatione dominatur in mente. In scabie namque fervor viscerum ad cutem trahitur, in quo luxuria signatur, quia similiter cordis tentatio ad operationem prosilit.

Si impetiginem in corpore. (GREG. Past., part. I, cap. 11.) Impetiginem habet in corpore, etc., usque ad quia per omnia vitia animum supplantat. Radix enim omnium malorum est cupiditas (I Tim. VI).

Vel herniosus. Alii, ponderosus, quia scilicet turpitudinem 359Cnon exercet in opere, sed adhuc continua cogitatione gravatur in mente: qui totis cogitationibus ad lasciviam defluit, tanquam in corde gestans pondus turpitudinis.

VERS. 21.-- Omnis qui habuerit maculam Quisquis cuilibet horum vitiorum subjicitur, panes Domino offerre prohibetur, ne aliena delicta diluere non valeat, quem adhuc propria delicta devastant. (AUG., quaest. 35 in Lev.) Ablata est omnis dubitatio, etc., usque ad a quo incensum necesse erat imponi.

Oportet sacerdotem esse animo et conversatione immaculatum, quem sicut Moses duodecim maculis juxta LXX mundum esse praecepit: sic Paulus duodecim conservare eum monuit dicens: Non superbum, non iracundum, non vinolentum, etc. (I Tim. II.) 359DEst enim Dei dispensator, qui subjectis debet virtutibus praecellere. Hoc quoque in Ecclesia fit si quis maculatus fuerit, et poenitendo cessaverit, sanctis communicare non prohibetur: ministrare autem, vel ad velum accedere et ad altare non licet. Velum enim et altare corpus Christi est, unde Paulus: Habemus altare de quo comedere non habent potestatem qui tabernaculo deserviunt (Hebr. XIII). Et alibi: Habentes fiduciam in introitu sanctorum in sanguine Christi, quam initiavit nobis viam novam et viventem per velamen, id est carnem ejus (Hebr. X).

Et contaminare non debet, etc. Inordinate agendo populum scandalizent, putantes superfluum esse Dei ministerium, et justitiam et judicium.

360AVERS. 24.-- Locutus est Moyses, etc. Moysi dignitatem commendat: aliis enim pauca, ipsi vero loquitur Deus omnia, quia justus est et imago Christi est. Nos quoque monet majoribus et perfectioribus attendere, qui continue fruuntur aeterna visione.

CHAPTER XXI.

VERS. 1.-- The Lord said also to Moses. . . . Let him not be defiled, etc. (ISICH.) That is, let him omit nothing of those things which it is expedient for a priest to teach, etc., as far as for he will not be able to be ignorant of the sin of those who dwell with him.

VERS. 2.-- But only in his kinsfolk. (HIERON., epist. ad Fabiolam, tom. I.) Over every soul that is dead, etc., as far as but to the lesser ones something is 357Ballowed.

VERS. 5.-- They shall not shave the head. (ID., ibid. ) But the high priest, etc., as far as to whom Christ granted that they should be light.

They shall not shave the head, etc. (ISICH.) The law forbids priests to grieve too bitterly over the dead, etc., as far as that he may be able to exhort, to reprove, to rebuke.

They shall not pollute the name, etc. (ID.) Which is great among the nations. But you, says Malachias, have polluted it in that you say: The table of the Lord is forsaken (Malach. I). [Vulg. Mal 1:12 Mensa Domini contaminata est, "The table of the Lord is defiled"] For in the sin of the sacrifice the priests pollute the name of God.

VERS. 7.-- A harlot, etc. Any priest whatsoever, for of the high priest he will speak afterwards, that he take to wife none but a virgin. 357C

Who is put away, etc. Because she is found blameworthy, the priest ought not to take her to wife, nor to preach beforehand the literal observance of her.

VERS. 8.-- Let them be therefore holy, etc. If the priests keep the sanctification already given them by God, they sanctify God himself: who, since he is holy in his nature, willed to be sanctified by the partakers of his glory.

VERS. 9.-- The daughter of a priest, etc. It is grievous that the daughter of anyone should commit fornication, but most of all of a priest, who ought to have sons subject in all chastity.

The daughter of a priest. The Synagogue is called not only the wife but also the daughter of God; but depraving both, she became an alien. Whence: The daughter of Sion shall be left as 357Da covert of a vineyard, etc. (Isa. I). And elsewhere: Their mother hath committed fornication (Ose. II): that she was burned with fire, the traces of the burning show.

VERS. 10.-- The high priest, etc. Christ, our great Lord, and great is his power, who for us was made priest and victim.

Are consecrated. Other priests used to consecrate, or consummate, in the blood of a calf and of a ram, namely, their own hands: but he in his own blood, with his hands crucified.

He shall not rend his garments, etc. (ISICH.) For from the time that the Word was made flesh, etc., as far as these are truly holy vestments.

VERS. 11.-- To any dead man, etc. (ISICH.) 358ALXX: A dead soul. The soul that shall have sinned, the same shall die (Ezech. XVIII; Isa. LIII). By this death the nations chiefly perished, which had neither law nor doctrine; but Christ bore the sins of all, and yet remained undefiled.

(AUG. quaest. 81 in Lev.) To any dead man, etc. LXX: Over any dead soul he shall not go in. How he calls it a dead soul, etc., as far as which he did not forbid according to the second.

VERS. 12.-- (ID., quaest. 82.) He shall not go out from the holy places. While, namely, their funerals are being celebrated, he was forbidden to go out from the holy places, etc., as far as that on some days the incense was not laid on.

Over his father. (ORIG., hom. 14 in Lev.) Every man is polluted in father and mother, etc., as far as or 358Bthey stain the liberty of their heavenly mother with the unworthy servitude of sin.

(AUG., quaest. 85 in Levit.) It can be asked, etc., as far as as Scripture testifies of Aaron himself.

I am the Lord. (AUG. quaest. 84.) Of Moses also it was said, etc., as far as nor was the visible sanctification judged superfluous, because the invisible went before.

VERS. 13.-- He shall take a virgin to wife. (HIERON., epist. ad Fabiolam. ) A widow is she whose husband, etc., as far as whence: For I have espoused you to one husband, a chaste virgin, etc.

I would not have the disciple and spouse of a high priest keep anything of the old man. If we have risen with Christ, let us savour the things that are above, [Vulg. Col 3:1-2 si consurrexistis cum Christo, quae sursum sunt quaerite ... quae sursum sunt sapite, in the second person] forgetful of the past, 358Ceager for what is to come. The unhappy Simon, who after baptism thought of his old marriage, and had not come to virginal purity, was unworthy of Peter's fellowship.

VERS. 14.-- But a widow and one put away, etc. (ORIG., hom. 12 in Levit.) He who sins is cast off; even if the bishop keep silence, whether from ignorance or from negligence, such a man's favour profits nothing; a soul that is cast off Christ does not take to wife, nor a polluted one, which, namely, has willed and desired so great a sin, because the great high priest requires a wholly pure soul, which he may join to himself and make one spirit. He who sins unto death is cast off; he who sins venially is polluted.

A foul one, etc. Who goes after her lovers, namely the demons, who desire to commit adultery with the beautiful soul, that is, the soul made to the image of God.

358DVERS. 17.-- A man of the seed. (ISICH.) These things according to the letter only, etc., as far as he strives against it very reproachfully.

VERS. 18.-- If blind (ISICH.) Not knowing the light of contemplation, pressed down by the darkness of the present life, who, while he does not diligently look upon the light to come, knows not whither to direct the step of his work.

If lame. (ID.) Who, namely, sees whither he ought to go, but by the weakness of his mind cannot do what he desires.

If with a little, or a great, or a crooked nose. (ID.) He is of a little nose who is not fit to hold the measure of discretion: for by the nose we distinguish odours and stenches. A great and crooked nose is the immoderate subtlety of discretion, which, 359Agrowing more than is fitting or seemly, confounds the rightness of action.

VERS. 19.-- If with a broken foot. (ISICH.) Who cannot go the way of God, and has no share in good work, not like the lame man, who at least with his weakness was holding

VERS. 20.-- If crook-backed. (ID.) Pressed down by earthly care, not looking back to the things above, gazing only on the lower things.

If blear-eyed. The blear-eyed is he whose wit flashes out to the knowledge of the truth, but whose carnal working darkens it. For in the blear-eyed the pupils are sound, but with the humour flowing down the eyelids grow thick, and by its frequent flooding even the keenness of the pupil is impaired.

If he have a white speck. (ID.) He has a white speck who is not permitted to see the light of truth, because he is blinded by arrogance of wisdom 359Bor of justice. For the black pupil sees, the white does not see: because the sense of human thought, if it understands itself to be foolish or a sinner, lays hold of the knowledge of the inward brightness: if it attributes to itself the whiteness of justice or of wisdom, it shuts itself out from the knowledge that is above.

If a continual scab. (ID.) Over whom, namely, the wantonness of the flesh rules without ceasing in the mind. For in a scab the heat of the inward parts is drawn to the skin, in which lust is signified, because in like manner the temptation of the heart leaps forth into action.

If a ringworm in the body. (GREG. Past., part. I, cap. 11.) He has a ringworm in the body, etc., as far as because through all vices it supplants the mind. For the root of all evils is covetousness (I Tim. VI).

Or one that hath a rupture. Others, burdened, because, namely, he does not practise baseness 359Cin deed, but is still weighed down in mind by continual thought: who with all his thoughts flows down to wantonness, as though bearing in his heart the weight of baseness.

VERS. 21.-- Every one that hath a blemish Whosoever is subject to any of these vices is forbidden to offer bread to the Lord, lest he be unable to wash away others' offences, whom his own offences still lay waste. (AUG., quaest. 35 in Lev.) All doubt has been taken away, etc., as far as by whom it was necessary that the incense be laid on.

A priest ought to be unspotted in mind and in manner of life, whom, as Moses commanded him to be clean from twelve blemishes according to the LXX, so Paul admonished to keep twelve, saying: Not proud, not angry, not given to wine, etc. (I Tim. II.) 359DFor he is the steward of God, who ought to excel his subjects in virtues. This also is done in the Church: if any man has been spotted, and by repenting has ceased, he is not forbidden to communicate with the holy ones: but to minister, or to approach to the veil and to the altar, is not lawful. For the veil and the altar are the body of Christ, whence Paul: We have an altar whereof they have no power to eat who serve the tabernacle (Hebr. XIII). And elsewhere: Having confidence in the entering into the holies by the blood of Christ, a new and living way which he hath dedicated for us through the veil, that is, his flesh (Hebr. X).

And he must not defile, etc. By acting disorderly let them scandalize the people, thinking the ministry of God, and justice and judgment, to be superfluous.

360AVERS. 24.-- Moses spoke, etc. He commends the dignity of Moses: for to others God speaks few things, but to him all things, because he is just and is the image of Christ. He admonishes us also to give heed to the greater and more perfect, who continually enjoy the eternal vision.

CAPUT XXII.

VERS. 2.-- Quae ipsi offerunt, etc. Quae obtulerunt scilicet sacerdotibus, per eos Domino offerenda. Nota sanctificationis genus quod fit voto et devotione offerentis; sed utrum homines eodem modo seipsos sanctificare dicantur, cum in aliqua re seipsos vovent, observandum est.

VERS. 3.-- Omnis homo qui accesserit, etc. Quasi, non solum a ministerio et mystico officio abstineat, 360Bsed nec tangat quae oblata sunt, mundus enim ad munda debet accedere.

VERS. 4.-- Qui fuerit leprosus, etc. Non sensibilem leprosum abominatur, qui invitus patitur, sed spiritualem: quod ibi exponit: Qui tetigerit immundum super mortuo, sive, juxta LXX: Omnem immunditiam animae, id est peccatum.

Ex quo, etc. (HIERON., epist. ad Fabiolam. ) Si quis sacerdotum semine fluxerit, usque ad nullius alterius more teneatur.

VERS. 9.-- Custodiant, etc. Praecepta Domini custodientibus ea causa sunt justificationis et salutis; transgredientibus vero, peccati et mortis, et praecipue in perceptione sanctorum. Qui enim manducat et bibit indigne, judicium sibi manducat et bibit, etc. 360C(I Cor. XI.)

VERS. 10.-- Inquilinus sacerdotis, etc. (ISICH.) Qui non pure nec diligenter in Ecclesia versatur, tanquam migraturus, scilicet gloriae aut quaestus causa, tanquam mercenarius memoratur: qui non est pastor, cujus non sunt oves propriae (Joan. X).

VERS. 12.-- Cuilibet ex populo nupta. (ID.) Non sufficit in fide Christi nutriri: permanere et crescere in ea debemus; nec alienigenarum cuilibet, id est alienae a Deo doctrinae, vel legi vel actioni commisceri: nec quia de patribus sumus fidelibus, ideo de sanctis praesumamus, sed ubi et ipsi fideles persistamus.

VERS. 14.-- Qui comederit de sanctificatis, etc. (ISICH.) Sancta sanctorum, etc., usque ad Unde Salomon: 360DCum sederis ut comedas cum principe, diligenter attende quae posita sunt ante te (Prov. XXIII).

Addet quintam, etc. LXX: Super ipsum. Quinta ergo non aliunde, sed de ipso et cum ipso, vel super ipsum addi jubetur: hic est sermo prolatus ab ipso Christo super Dominicum mysterium. Ipse nos ab ignorantia removet, nec carnale vel terrenum aliquis putet. Hic bene quinta dicitur, quia spiritus et sermo quem tradit quinque sensus nostros componit; nec solum gustum producit ad mysterium, sed auditum et visum, tactumque et odoratum, ut nihil terrenum de his quae superna sunt suspicemur.

Et dabit sacerdoti in sanctuarium. (ISICH.) Sanctificationem mystici sacrificii, et a sensibilibus ad intelligibilia 361Atranslationem vero sacerdoti oportet dari: quia per ejus virtutem et sermonem sanctificata sunt quae videntur, ut omnes excedant sensus.

VERS. 15.-- Filiorum Israel. Fidelem populum significat, qui per hoc mysterium Deum videt: et quicunque Deo consecrat in Dominicum sacrificium, sancta sanctorum sunt, quia ex eis conficitur corpus Christi. Ipse enim est primitiae nostrae conspersionis: ideo recte ex nostris subsistit primitiis.

VERS. 17.-- Locutus est Dominus, etc. (ISICH.) Non solum sacerdotes, etc., usque ad aliter enim non suscipietur.

VERS. 18.-- Homo de domo Israel, etc. Ad litteram quoque, si offeras bovem vel ovem aut capram, aut aliquid hujusmodi, sacerdotibus primitias dando, 361Bvel pauperes pascendo, vel divina aedificia constituendo, vel aliquid quod pertineat ad ipsum Deum faciendo (ideo enim holocaustum dicitur), impolluta sit haec oblatio; non tamen pollutionem nominat, ut spiritualem intelligamus pollutionem quae est ex rapina, injustitia, avaritia, et hujusmodi.

VERS. 21.-- Homo qui obtulerit, etc. Praecepit oblationem populi mundam esse: adjungit quales ministri debeant esse et offerre.

Pacificorum, etc. Quae valeant ad salutem et pacem: etsi non possit perfecte philosophari, sicut qui omnia reliquit, qui studet virginitati et hujusmodi. Stella enim differt a stella in claritate (I Cor. XV).

VERS. 22.-- Si caecum fuerit, etc. Qui Deum ignorat et ejus ministerium, nec aliis potest exponere; 361Cunde circa se et circa alios, et circa ministerium, negligenter agit. Unde: Videte quoniam omnes nesciverunt, universi canes muti, non valentes latrare (Isa. LVI).

Si fractum. Pede vel manu: cujus actio scilicet vel voluntas debilis est vel distorta, non est dignus salute et sacris altaribus.

Si cicatricem. Detractorem notat: pessimus enim hic moribus et asperrimum vulnus non facile mitigatur.

Si papulas. LXX: linguam incisam: qui scilicet propter sordidam vitam perdidit docendi fiduciam; unde David: Peccatori dixit Deus: Quare tu enarras justitias meas? etc. (Psal. XLIX.)

Scabiem vel impetiginem. Fornicator significatur, 361Dqui maculat corpus suum. Qui enim fornicatur, in corpus suum peccat (I Cor. VI).

Non offeretis ea Domino. LXX: Non offerent haec Domino: tales enim ministerio altaris non sunt offerendi. Sacrificium pacificorum non addit sicut superius, ut de ministris altaris hoc dici intelligamus qui holocaustum debent esse, et vitam perfectam et consummatam agere. Aure et cauda amputatis, etc. Aure amputatus est, qui inobediens est, qui verbis turpibus delectatur, bona aversatur.

Voluntarie, etc. Talis enim vel mandata operatur, et dicitur bos; vel simplex est, et ovis appellatur. Ideo non omnino adversamur, aut in parte inimici deputamus, sed docemus et exhortamur.

362AVel tusis, etc. Qui illatis tentationibus cedit, et animae fortitudinem perdit. Non enim oportet haec pati, nec cedere hosti affligenti. Unde: Utquid tristis incedo, dum affligit me inimicus (Psal. XLI)?

Ablatisque. Qui una actione prava quasi cultro seipsum abscindit a conjunctione Dei, privatus prole virtutis et signis virilitatis: alter enim est qui paulatim conteritur.

VERS. 27.-- Bos ovis et capra. (ISICH.) Mater omnium natura est, etc., usque ad unde Salomon: Ante mortem non laudes hominem (Eccli. XI).

VERS. 28.-- Non immolabuntur. (ISICH.) Legale pascha significat, quia haec Moyses in die paschatis tradidit immolanda Judaeis, dicens: Immolabis pascha Domino Deo tuo; oves, etc. (Deut. XVI): hoc 362Bpascha celebrare non possunt qui verum susceperunt.

Una die, etc. (ID.) Quia vetus pascha tempus suum habuit, etc., usque ad: In crastino Christum crucifixerunt.

CHAPTER XXII.

VERS. 2.-- Which they themselves offer, etc. Which they offered, namely, to the priests, to be offered by them to the Lord. Note the kind of sanctification which comes about by the vow and devotion of the offerer; but whether men are said in the same way to sanctify themselves, when in some matter they vow themselves, is to be observed.

VERS. 3.-- Every man that approacheth, etc. As if to say, let him not only abstain from the ministry and the mystical office, 360Bbut let him not even touch the things which have been offered, for the clean must approach clean things.

VERS. 4.-- Who shall be a leper, etc. He does not abominate the leper who is perceived by the senses, who suffers unwillingly, but the spiritual leper: which he there sets forth: He that toucheth what is unclean by reason of the dead, or, according to the LXX: Every uncleanness of the soul, that is, sin.

From whom, etc. (HIERON., epist. ad Fabiolam. ) If any of the priests have an issue of seed, as far as let him be held by the manner of no other.

VERS. 9.-- Let them keep, etc. To those who keep the precepts of the Lord they are a cause of justification and of salvation; but to those who transgress, of sin and of death, and chiefly in the receiving of the holy things. For he that eateth and drinketh unworthily, eateth and drinketh judgment to himself, etc. 360C(I Cor. XI.)

VERS. 10.-- The sojourner of the priest, etc. (ISICH.) He who is not purely nor diligently occupied in the Church, as one about to migrate, namely for the sake of glory or of gain, is spoken of as a hireling: who is not the shepherd, whose own the sheep are not (Joan. X).

VERS. 12.-- Married to any one of the people. (ID.) It is not enough to be nourished in the faith of Christ: we ought to abide and to grow in it; nor to be mingled with any of the aliens, that is, with a doctrine or a law or an action alien from God: nor, because we are of faithful fathers, are we therefore to presume upon the holy things, but let us persist where they too were faithful.

VERS. 14.-- He that eateth of the sanctified things, etc. (ISICH.) The holy of holies, etc., as far as Whence Solomon: 360DWhen thou sittest to eat with a prince, consider diligently what is set before thee (Prov. XXIII).

He shall add the fifth part, etc. LXX: Upon it. The fifth part therefore is commanded to be added not from elsewhere, but from it and with it, or upon it: this is the word brought forth by Christ himself upon the Lord's mystery. He himself removes us from ignorance, and let no man think it carnal or earthly. Here it is rightly called a fifth part, because the spirit and the word which he delivers composes our five senses; nor does it bring forth the taste only to the mystery, but the hearing and the sight, and the touch and the smell, that we may suspect nothing earthly of the things which are above.

And he shall give it to the priest in the sanctuary. (ISICH.) The sanctification of the mystical sacrifice, and the passage from things sensible to things intelligible, 361Amust indeed be given to the priest: because by his power and word the things which are seen are sanctified, so that they surpass all the senses.

VERS. 15.-- Of the children of Israel. He signifies the faithful people, who through this mystery see God: and whosoever consecrates to God for the Lord's sacrifice, they are the holy of holies, because of them the body of Christ is made. For he himself is the firstfruits of our lump: therefore he rightly subsists of our firstfruits.

VERS. 17.-- The Lord spoke, etc. (ISICH.) Not only the priests, etc., as far as for otherwise it will not be received.

VERS. 18.-- A man of the house of Israel, etc. To the letter also, if thou offer an ox or a sheep or a she-goat, or anything of this kind, by giving firstfruits to the priests, 361Bor by feeding the poor, or by setting up divine buildings, or by doing anything that pertains to God himself (for therefore is it called a holocaust), let this oblation be undefiled; yet he does not name the defilement, that we may understand the spiritual defilement which is from rapine, injustice, avarice, and the like.

VERS. 21.-- The man that offereth, etc. He has commanded that the oblation of the people be clean: he adds what kind the ministers ought to be, and to offer.

Of peace offerings, etc. Such as avail unto salvation and peace: even if a man cannot perfectly philosophize, like him who has left all things, who is zealous for virginity and the like. For star differeth from star in brightness (I Cor. XV).

VERS. 22.-- If it be blind, etc. He who does not know God and his ministry, nor can set it forth to others; 361Cwhence about himself and about others, and about the ministry, he acts negligently. Whence: See that they have all been without knowledge, all dumb dogs, not able to bark (Isa. LVI). [Vulg. Is 56:10 Speculatores ejus caeci omnes; nescierunt universi: canes muti non valentes latrare, "His watchmen are all blind, they are all ignorant"]

If broken. In foot or in hand: whose action, namely, or will is weak or twisted, he is not worthy of salvation and of the holy altars.

If it have a scar. He marks the detractor: for this man is the worst in his manners, and a most bitter wound is not easily assuaged.

If it have pimples. LXX: a cut tongue: he, namely, who because of a sordid life has lost his confidence in teaching; whence David: God said to the sinner: Why dost thou declare my justices? etc. (Psal. XLIX.)

Scab or ringworm. The fornicator is signified, 361Dwho stains his own body. For he that committeth fornication sinneth against his own body (I Cor. VI).

You shall not offer them to the Lord. LXX: They shall not offer these to the Lord: for such are not to be offered to the ministry of the altar. He does not add the sacrifice of peace offerings as above, that we may understand this to be said of the ministers of the altar, who ought to be a holocaust, and to lead a perfect and consummated life. The ear and the tail being cut off, etc. He is cut off in the ear who is disobedient, who delights in base words, turns away from good things.

Voluntarily, etc. For such a man either works the commandments, and is called an ox; or is simple, and is called a sheep. Therefore we do not altogether oppose them, or reckon them on the enemy's side, but we teach and exhort them.

362AOr bruised, etc. He who yields to the temptations brought upon him, and loses the strength of his soul. For it behoves us not to suffer these things, nor to yield to the enemy that afflicts us. Whence: Wherefore do I go sorrowful, whilst the enemy afflicteth me (Psal. XLI)? [Vulg. Ps 41:10 quare contristatus incedo, dum affligit me inimicus, "why do I go about mourning"]

And taken away. He who by one depraved action cuts himself off, as with a knife, from union with God, deprived of the offspring of virtue and of the signs of manhood: for it is another who is worn away little by little.

VERS. 27.-- The ox, the sheep and the goat. (ISICH.) Nature is the mother of all, etc., as far as whence Solomon: Praise not a man before his death (Eccli. XI).

VERS. 28.-- They shall not be sacrificed. (ISICH.) It signifies the legal pasch, because Moses delivered these things to be sacrificed by the Jews on the day of the pasch, saying: Thou shalt sacrifice the pasch to the Lord thy God; sheep, etc. (Deut. XVI): [Vulg. Dt 16:2 Immolabisque Phase Domino Deo tuo de ovibus, et de bobus, "And thou shalt sacrifice the Phase ... of sheep and of oxen"] this 362Bpasch they cannot celebrate who have received the true one.

In one day, etc. (ID.) Because the old pasch had its own time, etc., as far as: On the morrow they crucified Christ.

CAPUT XXIII.

VERS. 1.-- Hae sunt feriae Domini. (ISICH.) Si subtiliter attendas praesentia, etc., usque ad requiescit sabbato intelligibili.

VERS. 4.-- Hae sunt ergo. Non Domino, sicut aliae omnes, sed Domini proprie, quibus hoste devicto genus humanum salvavit, et a captivitate reduxit (Exod. II).

VERS. 5.-- Quartadecima die, etc. (ISICH.) Interrogemus Judaeos, etc., usque ad in qua pascha nostrum immolatus est Christus, etc.

362CPhase Domini est. Quia passus est Dominus, non mox tempora finivit, sed reliquias novissimae horae et vespertinae reliquit, ut in omnem terram exeat sonus eorum (Psal. XVIII), scilicet apostolorum, et in fines orbis terrae verba eorum. Ad vesperum quoque, vel in vespere medio, pascha proprium praeveniens crucem suam celebravit, quia super mysticum, verum pascha discipulis tradidit. Nox ergo erat jam quando Judas, accepta buccella, ad prodendum exivit: sequenti die pascha complevit. Unde quinta decima die legislator hanc festivitatem constituit, et solemnitatem azymorum praedicavit.

VERS. 6.-- Septem diebus, etc. Si vere pascha celebrare volumus, septem diebus, id est semper, ab omni malitia azymi esse debemus, et maxime a 362DJudaico errore. Unde ait Apostolus: Non in fermento veteri neque in fermento malitiae et nequitiae, sed in azymis sinceritatis et veritatis (I Cor. V).

VERS. 7.-- Omne opus, etc. Qui facit peccatum servus est peccati (Joan. VIII). Non debemus servire peccato, cum pascha celebramus, in quo a peccato nos liberavit Christus.

Servile, etc. Non permittit agere servilia, aliis diebus quibus nos prohibuit, unde subdit: sed offeretis sacrificium in igne Domino.

VERS. 8.-- Sacrificium, etc. LXX: holocaustomata, ubi innuitur quod ab omnibus cessare jubet servilibus; praeterea non solum primam, sed et septimam nominavit, ut totam comprehenderet vitam 363Ahumanam, quia initium, haec finis, in quo omnia possunt comprehendi.

VERS. 11.-- Altero die. Potuit dicere secunda azymorum: sed ne ad sequentem diem nostram duceret intentionem, scilicet in crastinum primae, dixit, altera die significans resurrectionem quae successit passioni, sicut dies diei.

VERS. 12.-- Atque in eodem die. Resurrectionis, id est, elevationis manipuli, id est, quando resurrectionem celebramus, Dominici sacrificii oblivisci non debemus, ex quo oblatus est manipulus.

VERS. 13.-- Duae decimae. Oportet enim perfectam humanitatem et perfectam divinitatem in unum convenire: in oleo, id est, per eam quam circa nos habuit misericordiam; sic enim sacrificium odor suavitatis 363BDomino invenitur, si quae de eo digna sunt intelligimus.

Liba quoque. Ecce intelligibilis oblatio Melchisedech, quae in pane et vino perficitur (Gen. XIV); inde quarta pars hinc offertur, ut per quartam partem evangelica doctrina significetur; per libationem vero sermo Dominicus, quo ait: Hic est sanguis meus, qui pro multis effundetur in remissionem peccatorum (Matth. XXVI).

VERS. 14.-- Panem. Oblatio praesentium donorum, quae est Christi mysterium, reconciliavit nos Deo, et cibum praeparavit novum.

Polentam. LXX: χίδρα νέα, id est sata nova tosta non manducabitis, vel manibus contrita et emundata: sic enim χίδρα, chidra, alii interpretantur, quia 363CChristi praecepta actionem et correctionem quaerunt (Matth. XI).

VERS. 16.-- Quinquaginta dies, et sic, etc. Haec dies Dominica est, in qua Pentecostes festivitatem celebramus, quam Judaei nec secundum litteram, nec secundum spiritum celebrant, qui nec dies secundum legem numerant.
