Offeretis novum sacrificium, novum non vetus, non saeculare. Adveniens siquidem Spiritus totam vitam humanam renovavit, quia Christi institutionem confirmavit, et vitae confirmationem in illis perfecit: unde, De meo accipiet, et annuntiabit vobis (Joan. XVI). Ideo non in alia die advenit, sed in qua resurrectio fuit, et oblatus manipulus intelligibilis, ut ostendat quia nova conversatio resurrectione Christi et adventu 363DSpiritus sancti perfecta est.

VERS. 17.-- Habitaculis vestris, etc. Quia est habitaculum praecipuum, de quo dicitur: Beati qui habitant in domo tua, Domine (Psal. LXXXII). Et alibi: Unam petii a Domino, hanc requiram, ut inhabitem, etc. (Psal. XXVI).

Panes primitiarum. Primus enim Dominus Evangelium cum lege perfecit et docuit. Audistis quia dictum est antiquis: Non occides: Ego autem dico vobis: Omnis qui irascitur fratri suo, reus erit judicio (Matth. V): hinc enim legem conjunxit Evangelio. Hinc Lucas ait: Quae coepit Jesus facere et docere, etc. (Act. I).

De duabus decimis, etc. De doctrina scilicet, quae 364Aperfectam divinitatem et perfectam humanitatem docet, nec aliter possunt coqui nisi per talem doctrinam.

VERS. 18.-- Septem agnos. Pro septem donis Spiritus sancti, quae vocat agnos, quia Spiritus sanctus unum est cum Christo, de quo scriptum est: Ecce agnus Dei, ecce qui tollit peccata mundi, ideo nemo potest dicere Dominus Jesus, nisi in Spiritu sancto (Joan. I; I Cor. XII).

Et arietes duos. Quibus inimicus ventilatur. Haec sunt superiora mandata, et virtutes, quas aliae sequuntur ut oves, verbi gratia: Vade et vende omnia quae habes, et da pauperibus (Matth. IX).

Cum libamentis, etc. Quae sunt scientia et correctio; unde: Deo autem gratias, qui odorem scientiae 364Bsuae manifestat per nos, quia Christi bonus odor sumus, etc. (II Cor. II).

VERS. 19.-- Duosque agnos, etc. Mandata pacis et sanctimoniae, unde: Pacem sequimini cum omnibus et sanctimoniam, etc. (Heb. XII). Haec quia apostolico sermone dicta sunt, praecipit offerri cum panibus primitiarum, qui doctrinam Christi significant. Unde in sequentibus: Cumque elevaverit eos sacerdos cum panibus primitiarum (Joan. X).

VERS. 20.-- Cedent in usum, etc. In usum ejus sacerdotis, id est, Christi, qui est sacerdos noster et Dominus noster: ipsius sunt panes primitiarum et agni mandatorum, ipse in Paulo loquebatur, ipse omnia quae dicta sunt obtulit; primus pacem discipulis annuntiavit, et sanctimoniam custodivit, sicut Sanctus 364Csanctorum.

VERS. 21.-- Celeberrimum. In quo gentium vocatio non modo praedicta est, sed impleta: quando Parti et Medi et Elamitae, et caeterae gentes audierunt apostolos loquentes variis linguis magnalia Dei (Act. II).

VERS. 22.-- Postquam, etc. Eadem superius dixisse videtur, sed ibi: Non complebitis missionem vestram; hic autem ait: Non secabitis eam usque ad solum, et differentiam innuit ipsa mutatio verborum.

Segetem terrae, etc. Conversationis, cujus messio post resurrectionem Christi, et adventum Spiritus sancti, non tamen omnem scientiam, quam in futuro sumus habituri, tradit verbum praedicationis. Sunt 364Denim quae nunc non possunt nobis revelari (Matth. VIII), unde in praedictis festivitatibus hanc legem interposuit.

Pauperibus et peregrinis. LXX: pauperi et proselyto, id est Christo, qui non habuit ubi caput reclinaret, et in propria venit, et sui eum non receperunt. Hic solos intelligit qui remanent; unde: O altitudo sapientiae et scientiae Dei, quam incomprehensibilia sunt judicia ejus, et investigabiles viae ejus! (Rom. XI.)

VERS. 24.-- Mense septimo prima die mensis. Septenarius consummationem vel specialem vel generalem significat. Hinc Deus die septimo requievit (Gen. II); et sua requie nostram significavit; unde: Qui ingressus est in illam requiem, requievit ab operibus 365Asuis, sicut Deus a suis (Heb. IV). Initium vero consummationis temporum, adventus Domini est, unde: Filioli, novissima hora est (I Joan. II). Hoc tempus appellat primam septimi mensis, sicut principium consummationis nobis etiam requies est, quos ab operibus malis cessare fecit, et a jugo gravi liberavit; unde: Venite ad me, omnes qui laboratis, et onerati estis, etc., et discite a me quia mitis sum et humilis corde, et invenietis requiem animabus vestris (Matth. XI).

Clangentibus tubis. Quia praedicat Salvatoris resurrectionem, quae memoriale est generalis resurrectionis, quae secundum Apostolum erit in voce archangeli, et in tuba Dei, etc.

VERS. 25.-- Omne opus servile non facietis in eo. Quasi [sicut] non est opus servilia agendi: ante fecistis, sed 365Bsicut exhibuistis membra vestra iniquitati ad iniquitatem, ita nunc exhibete servire justitiae in sanctificationem (Rom. VI): hoc est enim verum holocaustum.

VERS. 27.-- Decimo die, etc. (ISICH.) Propitiationem et a peccatis liberationem acquiri in humilitate et afflictione animae ex his intelligimus. Hanc debemus agere a vespere, id est a poenitentia incipere quam fecit Christus initium praedicationis suae (Matth. IV): poenitentiae superaedificanda est vita bona et virtutibus consummata. Unde adjecit: Et offeretis holocaustum Domino.

VERS. 28.-- Dies propitiationis. (ISICH.) Duo significat, quia in hoc tempore propitiationem suscipiemus, 365Cet ideo in illo ab omni malo abstinere debemus.

VERS. 31.-- Nihil ergo. Semper a malis et superfluis significat esse cessandum. Unde et Dominus nec causam dicere concedit, nec cogitare quid comedatur, aut quid bibatur, aut induatur, verum enim et sabbatum sabbatorum annuntiavit.

VERS. 32.-- Affligetis. Recapitulatio est humilitatis, quae est fundamentum et basis virtutum. Unde Dominus ait: Quicunque se humiliaverit sicut parvulus iste, hic est major in regno coelorum (Matth. XVIII).

VERS. 34.-- A quintodecimo die. (ISICH.) Hoc numero inchoatur festivitas, etc., usque ad aedificatio non manufacta in coelis habetur (II Cor. V).

VERS. 36.-- Dies quoque. (ID.) Nota eadem de 365Dprimo et de octavo dici, quia scilicet resurrectionis dies secunda est, et initium futuri saeculi. Primus, in Christo est conversationis principium et futurorum exordium; octavus vero praesenti succedit saeculo, quod significat in septenario. Praeterea sancti habent requiem et vitam futuram in hac vita. Unde apostolis dictum est: Regnum Dei intra vos est, etc.

Coetus. LXX: egressus, quia hinc exitus, ibi habitatio, unde: Cupio dissolvi et esse cum Christo (Philipp. I).

VERS. 37.-- Hae sunt feriae Domini, etc. (ID.) Legislatori consonat Paulus, etc., usque ad vel in periculis, vel voluntarie promittimus.

VERS. 39.-- A quinto decimo, etc. Quintusdecimus ex septem et octo compositus, Evangelicam praedicationem 366Asignificat, quae consistit ex praesenti vita et futura, hanc componit, illam praeparat.

Die primo et die octavo. (ID.) Qui est octavus, etc., usque ad quia fidelibus legem facientibus requies in futuro servatur.

VERS. 40.-- Die prima fructus. (ID.) Fructus est Christus, etc., usque ad in tempore suo.

Spatulasque palmarum, etc. Juvenescentia justitiae germina, quia justus ut palma florebit; quia vero de palma nihil respuitur, nec de his qui juste vivunt, addit, et ramos ligni densarum frondium.

Et ramos ligni. Omnes scilicet virtutum fructus, sed maxime misericordiam, quam oportet esse continuam et permanentem. Unde: Oleum de capite tuo non deficiet (Eccle. IX).

366BDe torrente, etc. De praesenti scilicet vita, quae fluit tanquam torrens, tentationibus et tribulationibus ebulliens, unde: De torrente in via bibet, propterea et exaltabit caput (Psal. CIX), in cruce, scilicet, pendens.

CHAPTER XXIII.

VERS. 1.-- These are the feasts of the Lord. (ISICH.) If thou attend subtly to things present, etc., as far as he rests in the intelligible sabbath.

VERS. 4.-- These therefore are. Not to the Lord, as all the others are, but the Lord's properly, in which, the enemy being overcome, he saved the human race, and brought it back from captivity (Exod. II).

VERS. 5.-- On the fourteenth day, etc. (ISICH.) Let us ask the Jews, etc., as far as on which Christ our passover was sacrificed, etc.

362CIt is the Phase of the Lord. Because the Lord suffered, he did not straightway end the times, but left the remnants of the last and evening hour, that their sound might go out into all the earth (Psal. XVIII), that is, the sound of the apostles, and their words unto the ends of the world. At evening also, or at mid-evening, forestalling his cross he kept his own passover, because upon the mystical passover he delivered the true one to the disciples. It was night therefore already when Judas, having received the morsel, went out to betray him: on the following day he fulfilled the passover. Whence on the fifteenth day the lawgiver appointed this festival, and proclaimed the solemnity of the unleavened bread.

VERS. 6.-- Seven days, etc. If we would truly keep the passover, for seven days, that is, always, we ought to be unleavened of all malice, and most of all of 362Dthe Jewish error. Whence the Apostle says: Not with the old leaven, nor with the leaven of malice and wickedness, but with the unleavened bread of sincerity and truth (I Cor. V).

VERS. 7.-- All work, etc. Whosoever committeth sin is the servant of sin (Joan. VIII). We ought not to serve sin, when we keep the passover, in which Christ set us free from sin.

Servile, etc. He does not permit servile things to be done on the other days on which he forbade us, whence he adds: but you shall offer a sacrifice in fire to the Lord.

VERS. 8.-- A sacrifice, etc. LXX: holocaustomata, where it is hinted that he commands them to cease from all servile things; moreover he named not only the first, but the seventh also, that he might take in the whole of 363Ahuman life, because that is the beginning, this the end, in which all things can be comprehended.

VERS. 11.-- On the next day. He could have said, the second day of the unleavened bread: but lest he should draw our attention to the following day, that is, to the morrow of the first, he said, on the next day, signifying the resurrection which came after the passion, as day comes after day.

VERS. 12.-- And on the same day. Of the resurrection, that is, of the lifting up of the sheaf, that is, when we keep the resurrection, we ought not to forget the Lord's sacrifice, from which the sheaf was offered.

VERS. 13.-- Two tenth parts. For it behoves the perfect humanity and the perfect divinity to come together into one: in oil, that is, through the mercy which he had toward us; for so the sacrifice is found an odour of sweetness 363Bto the Lord, if we understand of him the things that are worthy.

The cakes also. Behold the intelligible oblation of Melchisedech, which is accomplished in bread and wine (Gen. XIV); thence a fourth part is offered here, that by the fourth part the evangelical doctrine may be signified; but by the drink offering the Lord's word, in which he says: This is my blood, which shall be shed for many unto the remission of sins (Matth. XXVI).

VERS. 14.-- Bread. The oblation of present gifts, which is the mystery of Christ, has reconciled us to God, and has prepared new food.

Parched corn. LXX: χίδρα νέα, that is, you shall not eat new grain roasted, or rubbed with the hands and cleansed: for so others interpret χίδρα, chidra, because 363Cthe precepts of Christ require action and correction (Matth. XI).

VERS. 16.-- Fifty days, and so, etc. This is the Lord's day, on which we keep the festival of Pentecost, which the Jews keep neither according to the letter nor according to the spirit, who do not even number the days according to the law.

You shall offer a new sacrifice, new, not old, not worldly. For the Spirit at his coming renewed the whole of human life, because he confirmed the institution of Christ, and perfected in them the confirmation of life: whence, He shall receive of mine, and shall show it to you (Joan. XVI). Therefore he came not on another day, but on that on which the resurrection was, and the intelligible sheaf was offered, that he might show that the new manner of life was perfected by the resurrection of Christ and the coming 363Dof the Holy Spirit.

VERS. 17.-- Your dwellings, etc. Because there is a chief dwelling, of which it is said: Blessed are they that dwell in thy house, O Lord (Psal. LXXXII). And elsewhere: One thing I have asked of the Lord, this will I seek after, that I may dwell, etc. (Psal. XXVI).

The loaves of the firstfruits. For the Lord first perfected and taught the Gospel together with the law. You have heard that it was said to them of old: Thou shalt not kill: But I say to you: Whosoever is angry with his brother shall be in danger of the judgment (Matth. V): for hereby he joined the law to the Gospel. Hence Luke says: Which Jesus began to do and to teach, etc. (Act. I).

Of two tenth parts, etc. That is, of the doctrine which 364Ateaches the perfect divinity and the perfect humanity, and they cannot be baked otherwise than by such doctrine.

VERS. 18.-- Seven lambs. For the seven gifts of the Holy Spirit, which he calls lambs, because the Holy Spirit is one with Christ, of whom it is written: Behold the Lamb of God, behold him who taketh away the sins of the world, therefore no man can say, the Lord Jesus, but in the Holy Spirit (Joan. I; I Cor. XII).

And two rams. By which the enemy is winnowed away. These are the higher commandments, and the virtues, which others follow as sheep, for example: Go and sell all that thou hast, and give to the poor (Matth. IX). [Vulg. Mt 19:21 Vade, vende quae habes, et da pauperibus; the omnia is Lc 18:22, and Matthew has the saying at ch. XIX, not IX]

With the drink offerings, etc. Which are knowledge and correction; whence: But thanks be to God, who manifesteth the odour of his knowledge 364Bby us, because we are the good odour of Christ, etc. (II Cor. II). [Vulg. 2 Cor 2:14 odorem notitiae suae; Migne's scientiae is the very word the gloss expounds]

VERS. 19.-- And two lambs, etc. The commandments of peace and of holiness, whence: Follow peace with all men, and holiness, etc. (Heb. XII). Because these were spoken in the apostolic word, he commands them to be offered with the loaves of the firstfruits, which signify the doctrine of Christ. Whence in what follows: And when the priest shall have lifted them up with the loaves of the firstfruits (Joan. X).

VERS. 20.-- They shall fall to the use, etc. To the use of that priest, that is, of Christ, who is our priest and our Lord: his are the loaves of the firstfruits and the lambs of the commandments, he spoke in Paul, he offered all the things that have been said; he first announced peace to the disciples, and kept holiness, as the Holy 364Cof holies.

VERS. 21.-- Most solemn. In which the calling of the nations was not only foretold, but fulfilled: when Parthians and Medes and Elamites, and the rest of the nations, heard the apostles speaking in divers tongues the wonderful works of God (Act. II).

VERS. 22.-- After that, etc. He seems to have said the same things above, but there: You shall not complete your reaping; and here he says: You shall not cut it down to the ground, and the very change of words hints at a difference.

The corn of the land, etc. Of the manner of life, whose reaping is after the resurrection of Christ and the coming of the Holy Spirit; yet the word of preaching does not deliver all the knowledge which we are to have in the time to come. For there are things 364Dwhich cannot now be revealed to us (Matth. VIII), whence among the aforesaid festivals he set this law between.

To the poor and the strangers. LXX: to the poor man and the proselyte, that is, to Christ, who had not where to lay his head, and came unto his own, and his own received him not. Here he understands only those who remain; whence: O the depth of the wisdom and of the knowledge of God! how incomprehensible are his judgments, and how unsearchable his ways! (Rom. XI.) [Vulg. Rom 11:33 O altitudo divitiarum sapientiae et scientiae Dei, "the depth of the riches of the wisdom"]

VERS. 24.-- In the seventh month, on the first day of the month. The number seven signifies a consummation, either particular or general. Hence God rested on the seventh day (Gen. II); and by his rest he signified ours; whence: He that is entered into that rest, hath rested from his own works, 365Aas God did from his (Heb. IV). [Vulg. Heb 4:10 Qui enim ingressus est in requiem eius, etiam ipse requievit ab operibus suis, sicut a suis Deus, "into his rest"] But the beginning of the consummation of the times is the coming of the Lord, whence: Little children, it is the last hour (I Joan. II). This time he calls the first day of the seventh month, since the beginning of the consummation is a rest to us also, whom he has made to cease from evil works, and has freed from the heavy yoke; whence: Come to me, all you that labour and are burdened, etc., and learn of me, because I am meek and humble of heart, and you shall find rest to your souls (Matth. XI).

With trumpets sounding. Because it proclaims the resurrection of the Saviour, which is a memorial of the general resurrection, which according to the Apostle shall be in the voice of an archangel and in the trumpet of God, etc.

VERS. 25.-- You shall do no servile work therein. As if [sicut] there were no need of doing servile things: before, you did them, but 365Bas you have yielded your members to iniquity unto iniquity, so now yield them to serve justice unto sanctification (Rom. VI): for this is the true holocaust.

VERS. 27.-- On the tenth day, etc. (ISICH.) From these things we understand that propitiation and deliverance from sins are gained in humility and affliction of soul. This we ought to keep from the evening, that is, to begin from penance, which Christ made the beginning of his preaching (Matth. IV): upon penance a good life, consummated in virtues, is to be built. Whence he added: And you shall offer a holocaust to the Lord.

VERS. 28.-- A day of propitiation. (ISICH.) It signifies two things, that in this time we shall receive propitiation, 365Cand therefore in it we ought to abstain from all evil.

VERS. 31.-- Nothing therefore. He signifies that there must always be a ceasing from evil and superfluous things. Whence the Lord neither allows a cause to be pleaded, nor allows us to think what shall be eaten, or what drunk, or what put on; for he proclaimed a true sabbath too, and a sabbath of sabbaths.

VERS. 32.-- You shall afflict. It is a recapitulation of humility, which is the foundation and base of the virtues. Whence the Lord says: Whosoever shall humble himself as this little child, he is the greater in the kingdom of heaven (Matth. XVIII).

VERS. 34.-- From the fifteenth day. (ISICH.) With this number the festival is begun, etc., as far as a building not made with hands is had in the heavens (II Cor. V).

VERS. 36.-- The day also. (ID.) Note that the same things are said of 365Dthe first and of the eighth, namely because the day of the resurrection is the second, and the beginning of the age to come. The first is, in Christ, the beginning of the manner of life and the opening of things to come; but the eighth succeeds the present age, which he signifies in the number seven. Moreover the saints have rest and the life to come in this life. Whence it was said to the apostles: The kingdom of God is within you, etc.

An assembly. LXX: a going out, because hence is the departure, there the dwelling, whence: I desire to be dissolved and to be with Christ (Philipp. I).

VERS. 37.-- These are the feasts of the Lord, etc. (ID.) Paul accords with the lawgiver, etc., as far as whether in dangers, or of our own will, we promise.

VERS. 39.-- From the fifteenth, etc. The fifteenth, composed of seven and eight, signifies the evangelical preaching, 366Awhich consists of the present life and the life to come; the one it composes, the other it prepares.

On the first day and on the eighth day. (ID.) Which is the eighth, etc., as far as because for the faithful who keep the law a rest is reserved in the time to come.

VERS. 40.-- On the first day, the fruits. (ID.) The fruit is Christ, etc., as far as in his own time.

And the branches of palm trees, etc. The youthful shoots of justice, because the just shall flourish like the palm tree; and because nothing of the palm is rejected, nor anything of those who live justly, he adds, and the boughs of thick trees.

And the boughs of trees. Namely all the fruits of the virtues, but chiefly mercy, which ought to be continual and abiding. Whence: The oil shall not fail from thy head (Eccle. IX). [Vulg. Eccle 9:8 et oleum de capite tuo non deficiat, "let not the oil depart from thy head"; Migne's future indicative turns the precept into the promise the gloss reads]

366BOf the torrent, etc. Namely of the present life, which flows like a torrent, boiling up with temptations and tribulations, whence: He shall drink of the torrent in the way, therefore shall he lift up the head (Psal. CIX), hanging, that is, upon the cross.

CAPUT XXIV.

VERS. 2.-- Praecipe filiis Israel. (ISICH.) Candelabrum evangelica vita, etc., usque ad ubi misericordes abscondunt thesauros.

VERS. 3.-- Usque in mane, futuri saeculi: scilicet tunc enim non erit tempus orandi, vel eleemosynam dandi, unde: Ne dicas amico tuo, Vade et revertere, et cras tibi dabo: nescitis enim quid pariat superventura dies (Prov. III, XXVII).

VERS. 4.-- Super candelabrum, etc. Christus, qui 366Cest lumen verum et illuminat omnem hominem venientem. Et peccatum non fecit, nec inventus est dolus, etc. (Isa. LIII). Super hunc ardet lucerna, id est Spiritus sanctus, qui in igneis linguis super discipulos apparuit: huic purissimum oleum, id est mundam vitam debemus offerre, scientes quia non omnes possunt incendere illum (scilicet ignem), sed solus Christus et filii ejus, qui plenus Spiritu ait, Spiritus Domini super me (Isa. LXI). Qui etiam ait, Ignem veni mittere in terram, et quid volo nisi ut accendatur? (Luc. XII.) Hunc insufflans, quasi incendens ait: Accipe Spiritum sanctum, etc. (Joan. XX). Haec autem lucerna non accenditur, nisi per vitam et conversationem mundam, vel accensa cito exstinguitur, unde: Spiritum nolite exstinguere, etc. (I Thes. V).

366DVERS. 5.-- Accipies quoque. (BEDA, lib. I de Tabernac., cap. 7, tom. II.) Duodecim panes in mensa tabernaculi, etc., usque ad ut per labores et opera bona vitae temporalis ad eam properemus.

Coques ex ea duodecim. (ORIG., hom. 13 in Exod.) Sermo Domini panis est, sed multiformis, etc., usque ad panis super mensam ponitur mundam, scilicet super mentem sanctam et puram.

VERS. 10.-- Ecce autem, etc. (ISICH.) Mulier Israelitis, etc., usque ad qui in Christo blasphematur.

Ecce autem. (ORIG., hom. 14 in Lev.) Duo litigant: unus ex integro Israelita, etc., usque ad interdura enim qui foras mittitur, intus est; et qui foras est, intus videtur retineri.

367AVERS. 12.-- Miseruntque eum, etc. Moyses non ex auctoritate sua sententiam profert contra blasphemantem, sed Dei praecepto reservans, mittit in carcerem: lex enim odit eum qui blasphemat, contra quem frequenter Trinitatem praedicat, sed non manifeste sicut Evangelium; ideo illum ad judicium Evangelii reservavit. Qui non credidit in Filium, jam judicatus est (Joan. XXXI), et hujusmodi.

VERS. 15.-- Homo qui, etc. Locutio est tanquam dicatur: homo ille aut ille, id est quicunque homo. Non sicut quidam putant, homo homo ita dici, tanquam laudabiliter, quasi homo; sed homo homo, id est qualiscunque, velut etiam similis pecori, sed qui vere sit homo, quem sensum non esse verum, sed locutionis hoc esse Scripturarum aperte hic ostenditur, 367Bcum in eo dicitur qui culpatur, non qui laudatur.

Homo, etc. (AUG., quaest. 87 in Lev.) Si maledixerit Deum suum, peccatum accipiet. Nominans autem nomen Domini morte moriatur; quasi aliud sit maledicere Deum suum, etc., usque ad notandum est locutionis genus.

(ISICH.) LXX: Homo homo, etc., usque ad sicut filius Israelitidis nominavit et blasphemavit (Josue VII).

VERS. 17.-- Qui percusserit et occiderit hominem. LXX: Qui percusserit animam hominis, etc., id est qui ad iniquitatem traxerit. Animam vero, animalem hominem intelligit, qui non percipit quae sunt Spiritus Dei.

367C(AUG., quaest. 88 in Lev.) Qui percusserit animam hominis et mortuus fuerit, etc. Non ait, etc., usque ad hoc est enim animam hominis esse percussam.

VERS. 20.-- Fracturam, etc. LXX: Contritionem pro contritione, id est, si docendo, suadendo, scandalizando, aliquam virtutem proximi contriverit.

Dentem pro dente. Virtutem loquendi. Dentes enim pro verbis accipiuntur, unde: Filii hominum, dentes eorum arma et sagittae, etc. (Psal. LIX). Et pulchriores sunt oculi ejus vino, et dentes ejus lacte candidiores (Gen. XLIX). Clariora enim et apertiora sunt verba Evangelii lacte legis.

VERS. 21.-- Qui percusserit, etc. Haec secundum litteram Judaeis erant necessaria, qui non poterant capere: Si quis percusserit te in dexteram maxillam, 367Dpraebe illi et alteram (Matth. V). Sed cum essent malevoli, mala inferre volebant, et non pati. Ideo metu alternae poenae erant a scelere revocandi.

VERS. 23.-- Et eduxerunt, etc. Experimento ipso egem confirmatam ostendit, ut et tu blasphemare imeres, et peccatum fugeres, quod non solum princeps, sed tota multitudo vindicat. Omnes enim communiter blasphemos debent punire, qui omnium auctori non metuerunt detrahere, et omnes ab eo separare.

CHAPTER XXIV.

VERS. 2.-- Command the children of Israel. (ISICH.) The candlestick is the evangelical life, etc., as far as where the merciful hide their treasures.

VERS. 3.-- Until morning, of the age to come: for then there shall be no time for praying, or for giving alms, whence: Say not to thy friend, Go and come again, and to-morrow I will give thee: for you know not what the day to come may bring forth (Prov. III, XXVII). [Vulg. Prv 3:28 Ne dicas amico tuo: Vade, et revertere: cras dabo tibi, and Prv 27:1 ignorans quid superventura pariat dies; Migne's nescitis puts the second clause in the plural]

VERS. 4.-- Upon the candlestick, etc. Christ, who 366Cis the true light and enlighteneth every man that cometh. And he did no sin, neither was guile found, etc. (Isa. LIII). [Vulg. Is 53:9 eo quod iniquitatem non fecerit, neque dolus fuerit in ore eius; the words printed here are those of 1 Pt 2:22 qui peccatum non fecit, nec inventus est dolus in ore eius] Upon him burns the lamp, that is, the Holy Spirit, who appeared in fiery tongues upon the disciples: to him we ought to offer the purest oil, that is, a clean life, knowing that not all men can kindle it (that is, the fire), but Christ alone and his sons, who, full of the Spirit, says, The Spirit of the Lord is upon me (Isa. LXI). Who also says, I am come to cast fire upon the earth, and what will I but that it be kindled? (Luc. XII.) Breathing this upon them, as though kindling it, he says: Receive the Holy Spirit, etc. (Joan. XX). But this lamp is not kindled save by a clean life and manner of living, or, once kindled, is quickly quenched, whence: Extinguish not the Spirit, etc. (I Thes. V).

366DVERS. 5.-- Thou shalt take also. (BEDA, lib. I de Tabernac., cap. 7, tom. II.) Twelve loaves upon the table of the tabernacle, etc., as far as that through the labours and good works of the temporal life we may hasten to it.

Thou shalt bake twelve of it. (ORIG., hom. 13 in Exod.) The word of the Lord is bread, but manifold, etc., as far as the bread is set upon a clean table, that is, upon a holy and pure mind.

VERS. 10.-- And behold, etc. (ISICH.) An Israelite woman, etc., as far as who is blasphemed in Christ.

And behold. (ORIG., hom. 14 in Lev.) Two strive together: the one wholly an Israelite, etc., as far as for sometimes he who is put forth is within; and he who is without seems to be held within.

367AVERS. 12.-- And they put him, etc. Moses does not of his own authority pronounce sentence against the blasphemer, but, reserving him to God's command, casts him into prison: for the law hates him who blasphemes, against whom it frequently preaches the Trinity, but not openly as the Gospel does; therefore it reserved him to the judgment of the Gospel. He that hath not believed in the Son is already judged (Joan. XXXI), and the like. [Vulg. Jo 3:18 qui autem non credit, iam iudicatus est, "he that doth not believe"; in Filium belongs to Jo 3:36 qui autem incredulus est Filio, and John has twenty-one chapters, not thirty-one]

VERS. 15.-- The man who, etc. It is a manner of speech, as though it were said: this man or that man, that is, whatsoever man. Not, as some think, that man, man is so said in praise, as who should say "a man indeed"; but man, man, that is, of whatever sort, even like to a beast, yet one who is truly a man; and that this sense is not the true one, but that this belongs to the manner of speech of the Scriptures, is here plainly shown, 367Bsince it is said of him who is blamed, not of him who is praised.

Man, etc. (AUG., quaest. 87 in Lev.) If he shall have cursed his God, he shall bear his sin. But he that nameth the name of the Lord, let him die the death; as though it were one thing to curse one's God, etc., as far as the kind of speech is to be noted.

(ISICH.) LXX: Man, man, etc., as far as as the son of the Israelite woman named and blasphemed (Josue VII).

VERS. 17.-- He that striketh and killeth a man. LXX: He that striketh the soul of a man, etc., that is, he who has drawn him to iniquity. But by the soul he understands the animal man, who perceiveth not the things that are of the Spirit of God.

367C(AUG., quaest. 88 in Lev.) He that striketh the soul of a man, and he shall have died, etc. He does not say, etc., as far as for this is for the soul of a man to have been stricken.

VERS. 20.-- A fracture, etc. LXX: Bruise for bruise, that is, if by teaching, by persuading, by giving scandal, he shall have bruised some virtue of his neighbour.

Tooth for tooth. The virtue of speaking. For teeth are taken for words, whence: The sons of men, their teeth are weapons and arrows, etc. (Psal. LIX). And his eyes are more beautiful than wine, and his teeth whiter than milk (Gen. XLIX). For the words of the Gospel are brighter and plainer than the milk of the law.

VERS. 21.-- He that striketh, etc. These things according to the letter were necessary for the Jews, who could not take in: If any man shall strike thee on thy right cheek, 367Dturn to him also the other (Matth. V). But since they were ill-willed, they wished to inflict evils, and not to suffer them. Therefore they had to be called back from wickedness by the fear of a like punishment.

VERS. 23.-- And they brought forth, etc. By the very trial he showed the law confirmed, that thou also mightest fear to blaspheme, and mightest flee sin, which not the prince only but the whole multitude avenges. For all in common ought to punish blasphemers, who have not feared to disparage the author of all, and to sever all men from him.

CAPUT XXV.

VERS. 1, 2.-- Locutusque est Dominus, etc. Quando ingressi, etc. (ISICH.) Terra, conversatio bona, de 368Aqua dicitur: Spiritus tuus bonus deducet me in terram, etc. (Psal. CXLII).

VERS. 3.-- Sex annis, etc. Dum sumus in hac vita, et in visibili creatura sumus, quam significat senarius. Sex enim diebus creata sunt omnia quae videntur: septimo autem anno sabbatum erit terrae Domini. Cum enim post praesens tempus sabbatum venerit, de quo Paulus ait: Relinquitur sabbatismus populo Dei, quem dies septima significat, in qua requievit Deus ab omnibus operibus suis, scilicet visibilibus (Hebr. IV), jam non erit operandi tempus.

VERS. 3.-- Seres agrum, etc. Per agrum et vineam, cum illum seminari, et illam amputari prohibuit, omne genus agri intelligendum est: neque enim in oliveto vel quolibet alterius generis agro aliquid 368Boperandum est.

VERS. 5.-- Quae sponte, etc. Eorum scilicet quae hic facimus, in futuro fructum manducabimus; unde: Labores manuum tuarum quia manducabis, beatus es et bene tibi erit (Psal. CXXVII).

VERS. 6.-- Sed erunt, etc. Non prohibet Dominum vesci eis quae sponte sunt, sed reponere in usum proprium.

VERS. 8.-- Numerabis quoque. Interrogemus Judaeos, si septimum diem (ut aiunt) legislator honoravit, et ab operibus vacare praecepit, quare quinquagesimum annum celebrari praecepit: septem enim hebdomadae annos quadraginta novem faciunt. Non ergo quinquagesimo vacare oporteret, nisi mysterium lex significaret, quod Judaeorum infirmitas 368Cnon caperet. Duplex enim a Deo visibilium omnium significatur consummatio; una cujusque singularis, altera omnium communis, quando tempus operandi non erit; in superioribus primam, in his insinuat secundam.

Septem. In hebdomada dierum humanum saeculum dividitur. Cum igitur hic numerus multiplicatus in semetipso fuerit, et tota completa aetas saeculi, et tempus devolutum ad finem pervenerit, succedit quinquagesimus quasi quadragesimo nono anno, in quo erit resurrectio, unde subdit: Et clangens buccina mense septimo, etc. Unde Apostolus: In tuba Dei descendent de coelo, et mortui qui in Christo sunt resurgent incorrupti (Thes. IV). Oportet enim suscitantem praecedere tubam: ideo in quadragesimo et 368Dnono anno, in quo communis est consummatio, tuba canere praecepit.

VERS. 9.-- Mense septimo. Quia quasi septimo mense aetatis mundi Christus venit; primus mensis, qui in paradiso fuit; secundus usque ad diluvium; tertius usque ad Abraham; quartus usquequo descensum est in Aegyptum; quintus usque ad transmigrationem, sextus usque ad adventum Christi; septimus ipse adventus.

VERS. 10.-- Sanctificabisque annum. Quasi in sanctimonia exspectetur et cogitetur, pacem enim debemus sequi et sanctimoniam (Hebr. XII), sine qua nemo videbit Deum.

Unusquisque. Patriarcharum et prophetarum est 369Aquaedam familia hospitalis, cui praeest Abraham. Est et alia castitatis, cui praeest Joseph. Est et alia mansuetudinis, cui praeest Moyses (Gen. XVIII, XXXIX; Num. XII).

VERS. 14.-- Quando vendes, etc. Quisque vendere potest vel emere. Unde Paulus: Omnia vestra sumus sicut et vos nostra (I Cor. III). Hoc secundum litteram moraliter informat, ut in contractibus proximis compatiamur, ne quis nimium molestus de pauperum necessitatibus negotietur, ne studeat possessiones aliorum acquirere, sed indigentibus commodare, et commodata paulatim de fructibus agrorum recipere.

Juxta numerum (ISICH.). Id est secundum virtutem cujusque. Anni enim aetatem significant, in qua 369Bmanifestatur quid possit quisque secundum corpus.

VERS. 16.-- Quanto plures (ID.). Id est, si potest multum operari, multum fructum exiget; si multum non potest, parum rependet; feret enim hic trigesimum, iste sexagesimum, ille centesimum fructum (Matth. XIII).

VERS. 18.-- Facite praecepta. Ne quis audita moderatione vendendi et emendi segnior fieret, et condescensio ipsa noceret, caute adjungit: Facite praecepta, etc. Neminem siquidem vult vacare, sed secundum virtutem fructificare. Virtutem quoque conscientia cujusque examinat.

Et judicia, etc. (ID.) Non corrumpentes intentionem vel operationem eorum; velut si quis eleemosynam tribuit, non alii noceat ut alii prosit: praeceptum 369Cenim quod eleemosynam fieri jussit, justam esse voluit.

Quod si dixeritis, etc. (ID.) Quia per duos annos terram vacare praeceperat, ne conturbarentur tanquam solliciti quid comederent aut quid biberent, ait: Dabo benedictionem meam, etc. (Matth. VI). Et notandum quia cum Christus dicat: Nolite cogitare de crastino, lex jubet ne cogitent de tribus annis.

VERS. 21.-- Dabo benedictionem. (ID.) Christum. Unde benediximus vobis in nomine Domini, id est, Christo, qui est nomen Patris. Qui Patrem nobis nominavit, et manifestavit; hic in sexto anno venit, id est, in perfectione, quam significat senarius; et ut hominem reformaret, quem formaverat die sexto, quod ibi sextus dies, hic sextus annus dicitur, quia 369Dinter formationem nostram et reformationem multa tempora defluxerunt.

VERS. 23.-- Terra quoque. (AUG., Quaest. 90 in Lev.) Alii codices habent, etc., usque ad quisquis habet, uti debet.

VERS. 25.-- Si attenuatus. (ISICH.) Quae sit nostra substantia, etc., usque ad docet quomodo restituat.

Potest redimere. (ISICH.) Secundum illud, etc., usque ad ut quodammodo communis sit.

VERS. 28.-- Usque ad annum Annus, quia omnes horas in se habet, et dies et menses, et circuitus omnes, perfectionem significat. Peccatum aut perfectum et diuturnum non nisi perfecta poenitentia 370Aemendatur, in qua jejunium, oratio, cilicium, eleemosyna, et lacrymae peraguntur: per haec enim plena remissio acquiritur.

VERS. 29.-- Qui vendiderit domum. (ID.) Non exprimitur venditor, nec proximus, nec propinquus dicitur. Non enim unus est, sed totus populus: nec de cognatione Judaeorum, sed Sodomae et Gomorrhae.

Qui vendiderit domum, etc. In superiori capitulo spiritualem cujusque conversationem exposuit, hic generaliter de synagoga vel Ecclesia tractare intendit, quia Ecclesia gentium libertatem accipit, synagoga in perpetuum servit, exceptis Levitis, id est apostolis, et qui adhaerent eis.

(ORIG., hom. 15 in Lev.) Qui vendiderit domum, etc. De Jacob scriptum est, etc., usque ad semper 370Bpoenitentiam recipiunt, et semper redimuntur.

VERS. 31.-- Agrorum jure. (ISICH.) Quia in Evangelio proximi sunt, non legi. Ideo semper possunt redimi, et ex quo Christus venit usque ad secundum adventum per partes redimuntur.

LXX: Ad agrum terrae aestimabuntur, de quo scilicet dicitur: Simile est regnum coelorum thesauro abscondito in agro, id est Evangelio, in quo thesaurus est mysterium Christi.

VERS. 32.-- Aedes Levitarum. (ORIG., hom. 15 in Lev.) Si sacerdotalis fuerit domus vel Levitica, etc., usque ad satisfactionem scilicet desiderii.

VERS. 34.-- Suburbana. (ISICH.) Alii agri, id est conversationes virtutum, et doctrinae. Hae separatae sunt a civitatibus eorum quae per ipsos consistunt, 370Cet salvantur, et non venduntur, quia gratis accipiuntur, gratis dantur.

VERS. 35.-- Si attenuatus fuerit. (ID.) Mirare legislatoris sapientiam et pietatem; in humilibus enim sublimia exponit, secundum litteram vero dat regulam pietatis.

Qui eduxi. Quasi dicat: Sicut de dominio Aegyptiorum vos liberavi, et abjectis peccatoribus in terram Chananaeorum jam mandatam induxi, ita nolo vos sub dominio peccatorum teneri, neque peccatorum societatem amplecti, sed cum poenitere voluerint, et cohabitatione et facultatis largitione sustentetis.

VERS. 39.-- Si paupertate. Multa pietate littera plena est, sed spiritualis intellectus plenior.

370DVERS. 40.-- Sed quasi mercenarius. Uterque subditus est, sed tamen hic liber est: sic qui per vos conversus est, subjectus quidem est, sed dignitate gratiae divinae frater est.

VERS. 43.-- Ne affligas, etc., quia in quo judicas, judicaberis: et eadem mensura qua mensi fueritis, remetietur vobis (Matth. XVII).

VERS. 44.-- Servus et ancilla. (ISICH.) Ostendit quid distet inter eos, quos ex infidelibus Deo salvamus, quosque jam fideles, et nobis similes vocationis et conversationis dignitate pro tempore virtutibus attenuatos corrigimus: hi enim usque ad jubilaeum apud nos operantur, illi autem servi sunt perpetui.

371AVERS. 45.-- Qui ex his. (ISICH.) Judaeorum cognati haeretici, qui eadem nobiscum praedicant mysteria, quasi sint in terra nostra: sed non recte et perfecte credunt.

Hos habebitis. (ID.) Quos de gentibus aut de Judaeis aut de haereticis convertimus, quasi servos et ancillas in perpetuum acquirimus: quia pro eis doctrinae pecuniam expendimus, et filiis nostris, id est discipulis docendos tradere possumus, sicut Paulus Tito Cretenses, Timotheo Ephesios docendos commisit.

VERS. 46.-- Fratres autem. Quasi non eadem dignitate apud vos erunt, quos de impiis convertistis, et quos lapsos reparastis.

VERS. 47.-- Si invaluerit. (ID.) Id est, si noviter fidei vestrae conjunctus, Gentilis, vel Judaeus, vel haereticus, 371Bvirtutibus claruerit, ut possit arguere et exhortari.

Si invaluerit, etc. Secundum litteram quantum diligat libertatem ostendit. Ideo enim Israelitam alienis non permittit servire, sed praecipit redimi.

Qui voluerit. Turpe est hominem in fide ab infantia nutritum, talium subire magisterium, qui repente magistri facti sunt, unde Hieronymus: Heri in foro, hodie in altari; heri socius histrionum, hodie consecrator virginum.

VERS. 49.-- Patruus, etc. (ID.). Patrueles sunt, qui nos in fide genuerunt, fratres eorum consimiles praedicatores, filii fratrum discipuli consimilium.

Consanguineus. (ID.) Qui remotior patruo vel patrueli, sed propinquior affini. Qui enim suorum, et 371Cmaxime domesticorum curam non habet, fidem abnegavit (I Tim. V).

Affinis. (ID.) In cognatione fidei incarnationis Christi, qui de carne ejus et ossibus sumus.

Sin autem. (ID.) Pro libertate nostra et gloria Spiritus sanctus agit, et quemcunque servum peccati virtutibus redimi praecipit.

Et ipse. (ID.) Qui pauper factus est in peccato, cum prius virtute et doctrina dives esset, recte seipsum liberat dum exhortando, consulendo ad virtutem revocat.

VERS. 50.-- Supputatis. (ID.) Id est tractando apud se qua virtute, etc., usque ad ideo Dominus ait illi: Hodie salus huic domui facta est.

VERS. 51.-- Reddet. (ID.) Secundum ea quae peccavit, 371Doportet pretium reddere. Quod gula peccavit, jejunium corrigat; quod garrulitas admisit, silentium deleat, et similiter contrariis curantur contraria.

CHAPTER XXV.

VERS. 1, 2.-- And the Lord spoke, etc. When you shall have entered, etc. (ISICH.) The land is a good manner of life, of 368Awhich it is said: Thy good spirit shall lead me into the land, etc. (Psal. CXLII).

VERS. 3.-- Six years, etc. While we are in this life, we are in the visible creation also, which the number six signifies. For in six days were made all the things that are seen: but in the seventh year there shall be a sabbath of the land of the Lord. For when after the present time that sabbath shall have come, of which Paul says: There remaineth a sabbath-rest for the people of God, which the seventh day signifies, on which God rested from all his works, that is, from the visible ones (Hebr. IV), there shall then be no time for working.

VERS. 3.-- Thou shalt sow thy field, etc. By the field and the vineyard, when he forbade the one to be sown and the other to be pruned, every kind of land is to be understood: for neither in an oliveyard nor in a field of any other kind is anything 368Bto be worked.

VERS. 5.-- The things that grow of themselves, etc. That is, of the things which we do here we shall eat the fruit in the time to come; whence: For thou shalt eat the labours of thy hands, blessed art thou, and it shall be well with thee (Psal. CXXVII).

VERS. 6.-- But they shall be, etc. He does not forbid the Lord [Dominum; read Dominus, "the Lord does not forbid"] to feed upon the things that grow of themselves, but to lay them up for his own use.

VERS. 8.-- Thou shalt also number. Let us ask the Jews, if the lawgiver (as they say) honoured the seventh day and commanded that men rest from works, why he commanded the fiftieth year to be kept: for seven weeks make forty-nine years. It would not therefore behove them to rest in the fiftieth, unless the law signified a mystery which the weakness of the Jews 368Ccould not take in. For a twofold consummation of all visible things is signified by God; one particular to each man, the other common to all, when there shall be no time for working; in the passages above he intimates the first, in these the second.

Seven. The age of man is divided into a week of days. When therefore this number shall have been multiplied by itself, and the whole age of the world completed, and the time rolled onward shall have come to its end, the fiftieth succeeds as it were to the forty-ninth year, in which shall be the resurrection, whence he adds: And thou shalt sound the trumpet in the seventh month, etc. Whence the Apostle: In the trumpet of God they shall come down from heaven, and the dead who are in Christ shall rise incorruptible (Thes. IV). [Vulg. 1 Th 4:15 in tuba Dei descendet de caelo: et mortui qui in Christo sunt, resurgent primi, singular "he shall descend" and "shall rise first"; incorrupti is drawn from 1 Cor 15:52] For the trumpet must go before him that raises: therefore in the forty-ninth year, 368Din which is the common consummation, he commanded the trumpet to sound.

VERS. 9.-- In the seventh month. Because Christ came as it were in the seventh month of the world's age; the first month is that which was in paradise; the second, unto the flood; the third, unto Abraham; the fourth, until the going down into Egypt; the fifth, unto the transmigration; the sixth, unto the coming of Christ; the seventh is that coming itself.

VERS. 10.-- And thou shalt sanctify the year. As though it were to be awaited and thought upon in holiness, for we ought to follow peace and holiness (Hebr. XII), without which no man shall see God.

Every one. Of the patriarchs and the prophets there is 369Aa certain household of hospitality, over which Abraham presides. There is another of chastity, over which Joseph presides. And there is another of meekness, over which Moses presides (Gen. XVIII, XXXIX; Num. XII).

VERS. 14.-- When thou shalt sell, etc. Every man can sell or buy. Whence Paul: We are all yours, as you also are ours (I Cor. III). [Vulg. 1 Cor 3:22 omnia enim vestra sunt, "for all things are yours"; the phrasing printed here is that of 2 Cor 1:14 gloria vestra sumus, sicut et vos nostra] This, according to the letter, instructs morally, that in our contracts we have compassion on our neighbours, that no man be too troublesome in trafficking upon the necessities of the poor, that he study not to acquire the possessions of others, but to lend to the needy, and to receive back what is lent little by little out of the fruits of the fields.

According to the number (ISICH.). That is, according to each man's strength. For the years signify the age in which 369Bit is made plain what each man can do according to the body.

VERS. 16.-- The more (ID.). That is, if he can work much, much fruit shall be required; if he cannot do much, he shall repay little; for this one shall bear the thirtieth fruit, that one the sixtieth, and that other the hundredth (Matth. XIII).

VERS. 18.-- Do my precepts. Lest any man, having heard of this moderation in selling and buying, should grow the slower, and the very condescension should do harm, he cautiously adds: Do my precepts, etc. For he will have no man idle, but bear fruit according to his strength. And each man's conscience examines his strength.

And my judgments, etc. (ID.) Not corrupting their intention or their working; as, if a man gives an alms, let him not hurt one that he may profit another: for the precept 369Cwhich bade alms be given willed that it should be just.

But if you shall say, etc. (ID.) Because he had commanded the land to rest for two years, lest they should be troubled, as men anxious what they should eat or what they should drink, he says: I will give my blessing, etc. (Matth. VI). And it is to be noted that, whereas Christ says: Think not of the morrow, the law bids them not think of three years. [Vulg. Mt 6:34 Nolite ergo solliciti esse in crastinum, "be not solicitous for to-morrow"; the gloss turns on cogitare, thinking, which it carries over to the three years]

VERS. 21.-- I will give my blessing. (ID.) Christ. Whence we have blessed you in the name of the Lord, that is, in Christ, who is the name of the Father. He named the Father to us, and made him manifest; he came in the sixth year, that is, in perfection, which the number six signifies; and that he might reform the man whom he had formed on the sixth day. What is there the sixth day is here called the sixth year, because 369Dbetween our forming and our reforming many ages have flowed away.

VERS. 23.-- The land also. (AUG., Quaest. 90 in Lev.) Other copies have, etc., as far as whosoever has, ought to use.

VERS. 25.-- If being impoverished. (ISICH.) What our substance is, etc., as far as he teaches how he may restore it.

He may redeem. (ISICH.) According to that, etc., as far as that it may in a manner be common.

VERS. 28.-- Until the year The year, because it has in itself all the hours, and the days and the months, and all the circuits, signifies perfection. But a sin that is perfect and long continued is not amended save by a perfect penance, 370Ain which fasting, prayer, sackcloth, almsgiving and tears are gone through: for by these full remission is obtained.

VERS. 29.-- He that shall have sold a house. (ID.) The seller is not expressed, nor is he called a neighbour or a kinsman. For he is not one man, but the whole people: nor of the kindred of the Jews, but of Sodom and Gomorrha.

He that shall have sold a house, etc. In the chapter above he set forth the spiritual manner of life of each man; here he means to treat generally of the synagogue and of the Church, because the Church of the nations receives liberty, while the synagogue serves for ever, save only the Levites, that is, the apostles, and those who cleave to them.

(ORIG., hom. 15 in Lev.) He that shall have sold a house, etc. Of Jacob it is written, etc., as far as they always 370Breceive penance, and are always redeemed.

VERS. 31.-- By the law of the fields. (ISICH.) Because in the Gospel they are near, not in the law. Therefore they can always be redeemed, and from the time that Christ came unto the second coming they are redeemed by parts.

LXX: They shall be valued according to the field of the land, of which, that is, it is said: The kingdom of heaven is like unto a treasure hidden in a field, that is, the Gospel, in which the treasure is the mystery of Christ.

VERS. 32.-- The houses of the Levites. (ORIG., hom. 15 in Lev.) If the house shall have been priestly or Levitical, etc., as far as the satisfying, that is, of desire.

VERS. 34.-- The suburbs. (ISICH.) Other fields, that is, the manners of life of the virtues, and of doctrine. These are set apart from their cities, which subsist through them 370Cand are saved; and they are not sold, because they are received freely and freely given.

VERS. 35.-- If he shall have been impoverished. (ID.) Marvel at the wisdom and the piety of the lawgiver; for in lowly things he sets forth lofty ones, while according to the letter he gives a rule of piety.

Who brought you out. As though he said: As I freed you from the dominion of the Egyptians, and, the sinners being cast out, brought you into the land of the Chanaanites already promised, so I will not have you held under the dominion of sinners, nor embrace the fellowship of sinners; but when they shall be willing to repent, that you sustain them both by dwelling with them and by the bestowing of your substance.

VERS. 39.-- If through poverty. The letter is full of much piety, but the spiritual understanding is fuller.

370DVERS. 40.-- But as a hired servant. Each of them is subject, yet this one is free: so he who has been converted through you is indeed subject, but by the dignity of divine grace he is a brother.

VERS. 43.-- Afflict him not, etc., because in what thou judgest, thou shalt be judged: and with the same measure that you shall have measured, it shall be measured back to you (Matth. XVII). [Vulg. Mt 7:2 In quo enim iudicio iudicaveritis, iudicabimini: et in qua mensura mensi fueritis, remetietur vobis, "with what judgment you judge"; Matthew has the saying at ch. VII, not XVII]

VERS. 44.-- Bondman and bondwoman. (ISICH.) He shows what difference there is between those whom out of unbelievers we save unto God, and those already faithful and like to us in the dignity of calling and of manner of life, whom we correct when for a time they are impoverished in virtues: for these labour with us until the jubilee, but those are servants for ever.

371AVERS. 45.-- They that are of these. (ISICH.) The heretics are kinsmen of the Jews, who preach the same mysteries with us, as though they were in our land: but they do not believe rightly and perfectly.

These you shall have. (ID.) Those whom we convert from among the nations or from the Jews or from the heretics, we acquire for ever as bondmen and bondwomen: because for them we spend the money of doctrine, and we can deliver them to our sons, that is, to our disciples, to be taught, as Paul committed the Cretans to Titus and the Ephesians to Timothy to be taught.

VERS. 46.-- But your brethren. As though they shall not be of the same dignity with you, those whom you have converted from among the ungodly, and those whom, being fallen, you have restored.

VERS. 47.-- If he shall have grown strong. (ID.) That is, if one newly joined to your faith, a Gentile, or a Jew, or a heretic, 371Bshall have grown famous in virtues, so that he can reprove and exhort.

If he shall have grown strong, etc. According to the letter he shows how much he loves liberty. For therefore he does not permit an Israelite to serve strangers, but commands that he be redeemed.

Whosoever shall will. It is shameful that a man nourished in the faith from infancy should come under the mastership of such as have suddenly been made masters, whence Jerome: Yesterday in the market place, to-day at the altar; yesterday a companion of stage players, to-day a consecrator of virgins.

VERS. 49.-- His uncle, etc. (ID.). Cousins are they who begot us in the faith; their brethren are preachers like to them; the sons of the brethren are the disciples of the like.

A kinsman. (ID.) One more remote than an uncle or a cousin, but nearer than a connexion by marriage. For he that hath not care of his own, and 371Cespecially of those of his house, hath denied the faith (I Tim. V). [Vulg. 1 Tim 5:8 Si quis autem suorum, et maxime domesticorum curam non habet, fidem negavit, "But if any man have not care"; Migne's Qui enim turns the condition into a general maxim, and abnegavit strengthens the denial]

A connexion by marriage. (ID.) In the kinship of the faith of the incarnation of Christ, we who are of his flesh and of his bones.

But if. (ID.) The Holy Spirit acts for our liberty and our glory, and commands that whatsoever servant of sin there be, he be redeemed by virtues.

And he himself. (ID.) He who has been made poor in sin, though before he was rich in virtue and doctrine, rightly frees himself while by exhorting and counselling he calls himself back to virtue.

VERS. 50.-- The reckoning being made. (ID.) That is, by considering with himself by what virtue, etc., as far as therefore the Lord says to him: This day is salvation come to this house.

VERS. 51.-- He shall render. (ID.) According to the things in which he has sinned, 371Dhe must render the price. What gluttony has sinned, let fasting correct; what talkativeness has committed, let silence blot out; and in like manner contraries are cured by contraries.

CAPUT XXVI.

VERS. 1.-- Non facietis. Cultum idolorum tam visibilium quam invisibilium prohibet.

Sculptile. (ISICH.) In aedificiis domorum et possessionibus agrorum aliquam vestri memoriam sculpere cupientes.

Nec insignem. (ID.) Cumulum auri et argenti aut pretiosorum lapidum in quibus seducuntur avari, sic ut Gentiles in idolis.

VERS. 2.-- Custodite. (ISICH.) Ab omni malo abstinendo, 372Aet omni bono vacando: hoc est enim verum sabbatum.

VERS. 3.-- Si in praeceptis Quid fieri, quid vitari deberet, praedixit. In fine quae observantibus praemia, quae praevaricantibus supplicia debeantur enumerat.

Si in praeceptis. (ID.) Haec ad litteram stare non possunt, etc., usque ad quae in hoc capite significantur.

(ORIG., hom. 16 in Lev.) Si lex carnalis est, etc., usque ad, Joannes venit in spiritu et in virtute Eliae.

Mandata mea. Quale est illud: Qui sabbatum non custodit ab omni synagoga lapidetur, et hujusmodi.

Dabo vobis pluvias, etc. Et dabo, et superfluum est, consuetudine locutionis nostrae, sed more scripturarum additum.

372BTemporibus. (ORIG.) Tempore opportuno, non ebrio, non in aliis occupato: prudenter ergo conjiciat doctor cui ministret pluviam, et tritici mensuram, et cui debet lac dare, non det escam

VERS. 4.-- Terra gignet. LXX: Ligna campi dabunt fructum suum. (ID.) Motus animae, qui altiores sunt motibus terrae, id est, carnis nostrae, quorum fructus sunt charitas, justitia, mansuetudo et similia.

Pomis arbores. (ORIG.) Non potest arbor, etc., usque ad de qua dicitur: Lignum vitae est his qui apprehenderunt eam.

VERS. 5.-- Apprehendet messium tritura. (ORIG.) Anima quae germinat ex verbo Dei, etc., usque ad cum conservis in tempore dispensandum remaneat.

372CApprehendet. Panis enim cor hominis confirmat, vinum laetificat (Psal. CIII). Quae de continentia, de observantiis et de custodiis mandatorum dicuntur, frumentum videntur, ex quo conficitur panis qui corda confortat. Ea vero quae ad scientiam pertinent et occultorum exploratione laetificant mentem, vino et vindemiae comparantur. Cor enim laetatur cum obscura [occulta] explanantur.

Vindemiam. Fructum animae. Vindemia enim arbor est. Haec autem simul habere, id est corpore et anima fructificare, magnae benedictionis est.

(ISICH.) Congregans enim anima virtutes suas mox desiderat alias. Unde: Quae retro sunt obliviscens, ad priora me extendo (Philip. III).

Vindemia. (ORIG.) Quasi dicamus, etc., usque ad 372Dut de spiritu metamus vitam aeternam.

Panem vestrum. Scilicet qui de coelo descendit, et dat vitam mundo; non qui plus abundat malis quam bonis.

Absque pavore. Si corpus stabile fuerit, et anima fructificaverit, securi erimus in terra nostra, quia caro non concupiscet adversus spiritum (Galat. IV).

VERS. 6.-- Et gladius non. (ISICH.) Quia pax erit in finibus vestris; modo ex concupiscentiis sunt bella et lites.

VERS. 7.-- Inimicos. (ID.) Occisi jejunio, oratione, et similibus; unde: Hoc genus non ejicitur nisi cum oratione et jejunio (Matth. XVII).

Corruent. LXX: In conspectu vestro morte vestra, 373Ascilicet mortificatis membris vestris, quae sunt super terram, id est, fornicatione, immunditia, et hujusmodi (Col. III).

VERS. 8.-- Persequentur. (ORIG.) Sicut quinarius sapientes indicat et insipientes, etc., usque ad ne credentes decipiant.

(ISICH.) LXX, etc., usque ad persequi potest et vincere.

Et centum. Qui non tantum subtilem scientiam, sed et vitam perfectam habent, qui spiritualiter deculpantur, et ideo centum dicuntur, scilicet qui centenos afferunt fructus.

VERS. 9.-- Respiciam. Cujus respectus, salus. Respexit Dominus Petrum, et flevit amare. Si sol segetem non respiciat, manet infructuosa. Segetem 373Bcordis nostri respicit Deus, et radiis verbi sui nos illuminat, auget et multiplicat ut magni efficiamur; sicut magnus factus est Isaac, et magnus Moses, et magnus Joannes.

Crescere. In operibus bonis, ut parva magna. Unde: Quod uni ex minimis meis fecistis, etc. (Matth. XXV.)

Multiplicabimini. Ut pauca fiant magna vel multa. Unde: Si quod est aliud mandatum in hoc verbo restauratur: Diliges proximum tuum sicut teipsum (Rom. XIII).

VERS. 10.-- Comedetis. (ORIG.) Vetus lex et prophetae, etc., usque ad et induimus novum.

VERS. 11.-- Ponam. Si praedicta habemus, abjecto veteri homine innovatum est cor nostrum: Venit ad nos Deus et habitat in nobis (Col. III).

373CNon abjiciet, etc. (ORIG.) Audeo et dico quia anima Dei Christus est, sicut et verbum Dei sapientia Dei et virtus Dei (I Cor. I). Tale est ergo ac si dicat: non abjiciet vos Filius meus.

Anima. (AUG., quaest. 93 in Lev.) Animam suam Deus vocat, etc., usque ad qui dicunt animam non habuisse Jesum.

VERS. 12.-- Ambulabo. (ISICH.) Homo factus. Unde: Hic est Deus noster, et non reputabitur alius ad eum; post haec in terra visus est, et cum hominibus conversatus est (Baruc. III).

VERS. 14.-- Quod si. (ISICH.) Obedientium bona praecedenti capitulo expressit: quae autem sint praevaricatorum mala, praesenti capitulo ostendit, quorum quaedam ad litteram Judaei passi sunt: omnia 373Dvero secundum spiritum.

VERS. 16.-- In egestate. (ID.) Verbi Dei. Unde: Durus est hic sermo, quis potest eum audire? (Joan. VI.) Quia intellectum legis transgrediebantur, ut evangelicum testamentum irritum facerent, obscure loquebatur eis, ne intelligerent.

VERS. 17.-- Fugietis, nemine. (ID.) Quia gentiles non persequentur ad expellendum, sed festinantes salutem eorum, secundum illud: Sequor autem, si quomodo comprehendam (Philip. III).

Sin autem. (ID.) Hujusmodi distinctionibus frequenter utitur, etc., usque ad plenam et integram vindictam significat.

VERS. 18.-- Addam correptionem. LXX: Et apponam 374Acastigare vos septies, etc. Etiam hic superfluum est. Septies autem pro omni numero est accipiendum.

VERS. 19.-- Et conteram superbiam duritiae vestrae. Quia dicitis: Semen Abrahae sumus, et nemini servivimus unquam (Joan. III): ideo gentibus servietis.

VERS. 25.-- Gladium, spiritus, quod est verbum Dei, quod per praedicatores Judaeos convincit, ne Vetus Testamentum habere cum Evangelio contradicant.

In urbes mittam. Prophetas in quibus fideles munitiones et salutem inveniunt, inimici fugientes capiuntur, illic moriuntur apertis de Christo prophetis resistere non valentes.

374BVERS. 26.-- Ita ut decem, etc. (ISICH.) Panem doctrinae non habent, etc., usque ad ideo non satiantur qui his panibus nutriuntur.

VERS. 27.-- Sin autem, etc. (ID.) Non solum legislator est Moyses, etc., usque ad et auctorem legis blasphemaverunt.

VERS. 29.-- Carnes filiorum. Id est discipulos, quos vobis assumitis in filios, ad eamdem doctrinam verbis validis, et animam corrumpentibus inducatis.

VERS. 30.-- Cadetis inter ruinas. Cadit enim populus cum Pharisaeis spiritualiter cadentibus, quos quasi idola admirabatur populus traditionibus eorum intentus.

VERS. 31.-- Urbes vestras. Ad litteram vel libros prophetarum, in quibus sanctitatis fructum non inveniunt.

374CVERS. 34.-- Tunc placebunt terrae sabbata, id est requies, scilicet vobis feriantibus et a lege remotis, cum in lege et prophetis voluntatem vestram non inveniatis.

VERS. 36.-- Terrebit eos. Sicut folium fructum, sic littera abscondit spiritum: aufer folium, accipe fructum.

Sonitus folii volantis. Hyperbole: quia etiam levissima quaeque formidabunt.

Cadent nullo persequente. Non enim persequimur eos, sed sequimur ut revocemus.

VERS. 37.-- Corruent. Disputantibus nobis cum eis, diversa proferunt testimonia alternatim: sed alternis conturbabuntur verbis, alter alterum conturbat 374Ddum subvenire videtur.

VERS. 41.-- Incircumcisa mens eorum. Quia incircumcisi corde, vitiorum polluti praeputio, non enim quae in carne est circumcisio (Rom. II).

Tunc orabunt pro impietatibus suis. LXX: Peccata sua placita habebunt, poenas scilicet quas pro peccatis patientur.

VERS. 42.-- Quod pepigi cum Jacob. (ISICH.) Nota quod temporis et generis ordinem mutavit, etc., usque ad conjunguntur vero tanquam cognata et convenientia.

Terrae quoque memor ero. (ISICH.) Scripturae scilicet, quam mandabo ejectis Judaeis, qui eam praevaricati 375Asunt vel praevaricabantur, et prave interpretabantur.

VERS. 44.-- Attamen etiam cum essent in terra hostili. Bene in fine capituli blanditur, et quos minis terruerat promissis consolatur.

Non penitus. Caecitas ex parte contingit in Israel, donec plenitudo gentium subintraret; et caetera (I Tim. II).

Nec sic despexi. Qui salutem omnium volo: ideo post tantas iniquitates, etiam Judaeorum reliquias salvo.

VERS. 45.-- Haec sunt. (ISICH.) Usque ad certum tempus, etc., usque ad non solvendo, sed implendo.

In monte Sinai. Non in Sion. Unde: Videbitur Deus deorum in Sion (Psal. LXXXIII). Et alibi : Ex 375BSion species decoris ejus. Deus manifeste veniet (Psal. XLIX). Et in Malachia: Subito veniet ad templum suum dominator quem vos quaeritis (Malac. III).

CHAPTER XXVI.

VERS. 1.-- You shall not make. He forbids the worship of idols, both visible and invisible.

A graven thing. (ISICH.) Desiring to carve some memorial of yourselves in the buildings of your houses and the holdings of your fields.

Nor a remarkable stone. (ID.) A heap of gold and silver or of precious stones, in which the covetous are led astray, even as the Gentiles are in idols.

VERS. 2.-- Keep. (ISICH.) By abstaining from all evil, 372Aand being at leisure for every good: for this is the true sabbath.

VERS. 3.-- If in my precepts He has foretold what ought to be done, and what avoided. At the end he enumerates what rewards are due to those who keep them, and what punishments to the transgressors.

If in my precepts. (ID.) These things cannot stand according to the letter, etc., as far as the things that are signified in this chapter.

(ORIG., hom. 16 in Lev.) If the law is carnal, etc., as far as, John came in the spirit and power of Elias.

My commandments. Such as this: He that keepeth not the sabbath, let him be stoned by the whole synagogue, and the like.

I will give you rain, etc. And I will give: the and is superfluous by the custom of our speech, but is added after the manner of the Scriptures.

372BIn their seasons. (ORIG.) At a fitting time, not when a man is drunken, not when he is busied about other things: let the teacher therefore judge prudently to whom he should minister the rain and the measure of wheat, and to whom he ought to give milk, let him not give meat.

VERS. 4.-- The ground shall bring forth. LXX: The trees of the field shall give their fruit. (ID.) The motions of the soul, which are higher than the motions of the earth, that is, of our flesh, whose fruits are charity, justice, meekness and the like.

The trees with fruit. (ORIG.) A tree cannot, etc., as far as of which it is said: She is a tree of life to them that have laid hold on her.

VERS. 5.-- The threshing of the harvest shall reach. (ORIG.) The soul that buds forth from the word of God, etc., as far as there remains what is to be dispensed to the fellow servants in due time.

372CShall reach. For bread strengtheneth man's heart, wine maketh it glad (Psal. CIII). The things that are said of continence, of observances and of the keeping of the commandments seem to be wheat, out of which is made the bread that strengthens hearts. But the things that pertain to knowledge, and gladden the mind by the searching out of hidden things, are likened to wine and to the vintage. For the heart is made glad when dark [occulta] things are explained.

The vintage. The fruit of the soul. For the vintage is a tree. But to have these together, that is, to bear fruit in body and in soul, is a matter of great blessing.

(ISICH.) For the soul, gathering her virtues, soon desires others. Whence: Forgetting the things that are behind, I stretch forth myself to those that are before (Philip. III).

The vintage. (ORIG.) As if we should say, etc., as far as 372Dthat of the spirit we may reap life everlasting.

Your bread. That is, which came down from heaven, and gives life to the world; not that which abounds more to the evil than to the good.

Without fear. If the body shall have been steadfast, and the soul shall have borne fruit, we shall be secure in our own land, because the flesh shall not lust against the spirit (Galat. IV). [Vulg. Gal 5:17 caro enim concupiscit adversus spiritum, "for the flesh lusteth against the spirit"; Migne prints a negative the Apostle does not, and cites Galatians IV for V]

VERS. 6.-- And the sword shall not. (ISICH.) Because there shall be peace in your borders; now from concupiscences come wars and strifes.

VERS. 7.-- Your enemies. (ID.) Slain by fasting, by prayer and the like; whence: This kind is not cast out but with prayer and fasting (Matth. XVII).

They shall fall. LXX: In your sight by your death, 373Athat is, your members being mortified which are upon the earth, that is, fornication, uncleanness and the like (Col. III).

VERS. 8.-- They shall pursue. (ORIG.) As the number five points to the wise and the foolish, etc., as far as lest they deceive believers.

(ISICH.) LXX, etc., as far as he can pursue and conquer.

And a hundred. They who have not only subtle knowledge but a perfect life also, who are spiritually cleared of blame, and are therefore called a hundred, namely those who bring forth a hundredfold fruit.

VERS. 9.-- I will look upon you. Whose look is salvation. The Lord looked upon Peter, and he wept bitterly. If the sun look not upon the corn, it remains without fruit. God looks upon the corn 373Bof our heart, and enlightens us with the rays of his word, increases and multiplies us that we may be made great; as Isaac was made great, and Moses great, and John great.

To increase. In good works, that small things may become great. Whence: What you have done to one of the least of mine, etc. (Matth. XXV.) [Vulg. Mt 25:40 Quamdiu fecistis uni ex his fratribus meis minimis, mihi fecistis, "as long as you did it to one of these my least brethren, you did it to me"]

You shall be multiplied. That few things may become great or many. Whence: If there be any other commandment, it is restored in this word: Thou shalt love thy neighbour as thyself (Rom. XIII).

VERS. 10.-- You shall eat. (ORIG.) The old law and the prophets, etc., as far as and we have put on the new.

VERS. 11.-- I will set. If we have the things aforesaid, the old man being cast off our heart has been made new: God comes to us and dwells in us (Col. III). [Vulg. has no such verse; compare Jo 14:23 ad eum veniemus, et mansionem apud eum faciemus and 2 Cor 6:16 inhabitabo in illis; Colossians III has nothing of the kind]

373CHe shall not cast off, etc. (ORIG.) I dare to say that the soul of God is Christ, as also the word of God is the wisdom of God and the power of God (I Cor. I). It is therefore as though he said: my Son shall not cast you off.

Soul. (AUG., quaest. 93 in Lev.) God calls it his own soul, etc., as far as who say that Jesus had no soul.

VERS. 12.-- I will walk. (ISICH.) Being made man. Whence: This is our God, and no other shall be accounted of beside him; after this he was seen upon the earth, and conversed with men (Baruc. III). [Vulg. Bar 3:36 et non aestimabitur alius adversus eum, "shall not be esteemed against him", and 3:38 in terris visus est, "upon the earth" in the plural]

VERS. 14.-- But if. (ISICH.) In the preceding chapter he set forth the good things of the obedient: but what are the evils of transgressors he shows in the present chapter, of which the Jews suffered some according to the letter: but all 373Daccording to the spirit.

VERS. 16.-- In want. (ID.) Of the word of God. Whence: This saying is hard, who can hear it? (Joan. VI.) Because they were transgressing the understanding of the law, that they might make void the evangelical testament, he spoke to them darkly, lest they should understand.

VERS. 17.-- You shall flee, no man. (ID.) Because the gentiles will not pursue them to drive them out, but hastening after their salvation, according to that: But I follow after, if I may by any means lay hold (Philip. III).

But if. (ID.) He frequently uses distinctions of this kind, etc., as far as it signifies a full and entire vengeance.

VERS. 18.-- I will add correction. LXX: And I will proceed 374Ato chastise you seven times, etc. Here too it is superfluous. But seven times is to be taken for every number.

VERS. 19.-- And I will break the pride of your hardness. Because you say: We are the seed of Abraham, and we have never served any man (Joan. III): therefore you shall serve the nations.

VERS. 25.-- A sword, of the spirit, which is the word of God, which by preachers convicts the Jews, lest they gainsay the having of the Old Testament together with the Gospel.

I will send into your cities. The prophets, in whom the faithful find fortifications and salvation, while the enemies fleeing are taken, and there die, not being able to resist the prophets who speak openly of Christ.

374BVERS. 26.-- So that ten, etc. (ISICH.) They have not the bread of doctrine, etc., as far as therefore they are not filled who are fed with these loaves.

VERS. 27.-- But if, etc. (ID.) Moses is not only a lawgiver, etc., as far as and they blasphemed the author of the law.

VERS. 29.-- The flesh of your sons. That is, the disciples whom you take to yourselves for sons, that you may lead them into the same doctrine by words that are strong and that corrupt the soul.

VERS. 30.-- You shall fall among the ruins. For the people falls together with the Pharisees who fall spiritually, whom the people, intent upon their traditions, admired as idols.

VERS. 31.-- Your cities. According to the letter, or else the books of the prophets, in which they find no fruit of holiness.

374CVERS. 34.-- Then shall the sabbaths of the land please, that is, its rest, namely while you keep holiday and are removed from the law, since in the law and the prophets you find not your own will.

VERS. 36.-- It shall terrify them. As the leaf hides the fruit, so the letter hides the spirit: take away the leaf, receive the fruit.

The sound of a flying leaf. A hyperbole: because they shall dread even the very lightest things.

They shall fall when none pursueth. For we do not pursue them, but follow after them that we may call them back.

VERS. 37.-- They shall fall. When we dispute with them, they bring forward divers testimonies by turns: but they shall be confounded by one another's words, and the one confounds the other 374Dwhile he seems to be helping him.

VERS. 41.-- Their uncircumcised mind. Because they are uncircumcised in heart, defiled with the foreskin of vices, for neither is that circumcision which is in the flesh (Rom. II).

Then shall they pray for their impieties. LXX: They shall hold their sins acceptable, that is, the punishments which they shall suffer for their sins.

VERS. 42.-- Which I made with Jacob. (ISICH.) Note that he has changed the order of time and of birth, etc., as far as but they are joined together as kindred and fitting.

I will remember the land also. (ISICH.) That is, the Scripture, which I will entrust to the Jews when they are cast out, who have transgressed 375Ait, or were transgressing it, and interpreted it perversely.

VERS. 44.-- And yet even when they were in the enemies' land. Well does he speak kindly at the end of the chapter, and console with promises those whom he had frightened with threats.

Not utterly. Blindness in part befalleth in Israel, until the fulness of the nations should come in; and the rest (I Tim. II). [Vulg. Rom 11:25 caecitas ex parte contigit in Israel, donec plenitudo gentium intraret, past contigit and simple intraret; the passage is Romans XI, not I Timothy II]

Neither have I so despised them. I who will the salvation of all: therefore after so great iniquities I save even the remnant of the Jews.

VERS. 45.-- These are. (ISICH.) Until a certain time, etc., as far as not by destroying, but by fulfilling.

In mount Sinai. Not in Sion. Whence: The God of gods shall be seen in Sion (Psal. LXXXIII). And elsewhere: Out of 375BSion is the loveliness of his beauty. God shall come manifestly (Psal. XLIX). And in Malachias: Suddenly shall come to his temple the ruler whom you seek (Malac. III). [Vulg. Mal 3:1 et statim veniet ad templum suum Dominator quem vos quaeritis, "and presently he shall come"]

CAPUT XXVII.

VERS. 2.-- Homo qui. (ISICH.) Plena sunt haec verba prudentia: quae oporteat agi, vel minime, superius praecepit; nunc vel ex toto, vel ex parte agentium dignitatem exponit, quid perfecta vita, quid mediocris, quid incipiens cognitionis Dei habeat differentias quoque aetatum describit.

Animam. (ISICH.) Aquila et Theodotio, admirabile. Symmachus, si se separaverit. LXX: honorem animae suae.

A vigesimo. (ID.) Quando pubes, fortis, etc., 375Cusque ad de qua dicitur: Estote perfecti sicut et Pater vester perfectus est (Matth. V).

Siclos. (ISICH.) Vel didrachmas, sexagenarium fructum, etc., usque ad unde: Vidua eligatur non minus sexaginta annorum (I Tim. V).

VERS. 5.-- A quinto. (ID.) Qui sensatus est, et proficere incoepit, sed conversatione jejunii non potest ornari.

Usque. (ID.) Qui proficere coepit sensibus quinque: provectus enim hic est ad virtutem, et perfectionem. Viginti enim annorum fortes sunt atque valentes.

Masculus dabit viginti siclos. (ID.) Vel didrachmas, id est quadraginta drachmas; id est obolos quadringentos. Quadragenarius vero jejunii et orationum 375Dproventum significat. Tot enim diebus jejunavit Moses (Exod. XXXIV), et Elias (III Reg. XIX), et ipse Christus, et post a diabolo est tentatus (Matth. IV).

VERS. 7.-- Femina decem. (ID.) Vel didrachmas, id est viginti drachmas vel ducentos obolos, custodiam scilicet decem mandatorum, quae duplicantur secundum litteram et spiritum, in quibus non est praeceptum jejunium, quia provectos decet necdum perfectos.

Ab uno. Unius mensis est, qui nunc in fide genitus est, quibus dicitur: Quasi modo geniti infantes rationabiles sine dolo, lac concupiscite, etc. (I Petr. II).

Quinque. Vel didrachmae, scilicet ut masculus 376Aduplices sensus habeat, et quae sensibiliter audit, intelligibiliter suscipiat. Sic enim ad perfectionem ducitur, propter quod centum obolorum est pretium ejus: centenarius autem perfectus est.

Pro femina. (ID.) Pro femina tres didrachmae, etc., usque ad quasi jam participes evangelicae conversationis.

Sexagenarius. Qui excellentiam conversationis promisit, sicut virginitatem, vel mundi abrenuntiationem, sed infirmatus est.

Dabit quindecim. Vel didrachmas, quae sunt triginta drachmae. Tres enim fructus bona terra reddidit (Matth. XIII): centenarium, id est virginem esse vel mundo abrenuntiare; sexagenarium, scilicet continentiam; tricenarium, id est casti conjugii 376Bconversationem.

VERS. 8.-- Si pauper. Qui multum peccavit, ideo interpretatus est Aquila, si excessit.

Stabit. Si enim quemlibet nobis subditum Dei servitio obtulerimus, in ministerium nostrum recipere non debemus, promittentes pro eo vel meliorem, vel minus bonum.

Quantum ille aestimaverit. Id est quantum viderit eum posse satisfactionis imponet: sed si locuples est, ut distribuat pecuniam, si sanus jejunet, oret, laboret.

VERS. 9.-- Animal. Quia differentias oblationum quae ex nobis ipsis sunt et gradus exposuit: jam eorum quae nobis tanquam pecora subditi sunt, quando sit oblatio sancta exponit.

376CSanctum erit. Secundum legem immaculatum et mundum: veluti si filium, discipulum, servum, vel quemlibet nobis subditum Deo obtulerimus.

VERS. 10.-- Quod. Ut sit duplex oblatio, et inimicus erubescat, qui oblationem mutare suggerebat.

VERS. 11.-- Animal immundum. (ISICH.) Quae non simul, etc., usque ad ut possit Domino offerri.

Sciendum est quod aliud est sanctificari, aliud est sanctum esse, aliud Deo offerri, aliud Dei esse. Quod enim sanctificatur et offertur sanctum incipit esse, quod non erat, et paulatim augmentatur. Quod sanctum est et Dei est, non eget augmento, quia perfectum est. Bonum est ergo aliorum doctrina et 376Dauxilio sanctificari, sed melius est talem esse, qui seipsum, et alios possit sanctificare, quod paucorum est. Sanctificat ergo domum Domino, qui familiam suam, et cognationem tam sensibilem quam intelligibilem pure, et sancte vult conversari, ut sit Ecclesia Dei. Si autem per se non est idoneus hoc facere, sacerdos debet considerare utrum bona an mala sit. Ex diversis enim constat personis, et secundum quod possunt, debet injungere eis.

VERS. 13.-- Quintam partem. Mentem, scilicet, quae regit quinque sensus, sine qua virgines fatuae ad nuptias non suscipiuntur (Matth. XXV).

VERS. 14.-- Homo si voverit. (ISICH.) In sequentibus dicit: Omne quod Domino consecratur, sive 377Ahomo, sive animal, sive ager, redimi non poterit; hic autem domum et agrum redimi praecipit. Sed forsan hoc secundum diversas intentiones offerentium decernit, tanquam ibi non habeat voluntatem ulterius habendi, quod penitus consecravit.

VERS. 15.-- Sin autem ille, etc. (ISICH.) Redimere, est per doctrinam et formulam vitae familiam liberare, et quidquid sanctimoniae obviat diluere. Adjiciet vero admonitioni et aedificationi sacerdotis intelligentiam, et frequentem commemorationem.
