VERS. 16.-- Quod si agrum. Id est, Scripturam divinam, cujus vitis Christus, Pater agricola (Joan. XIV). In hoc absconditur thesaurus, id est, salutaris praedicatio: in hoc diversa est cujusque possessio, modus scilicet sciendi, quem sibi quisque obtinere 377Bpotuit.

Si triginta modiis, etc. (ID.) In triginta modiis hordei Vetus Testamentum. In quinquaginta siclis vel didrachmis juxta LXX spiritualem et perfectam doctrinam intellige. Sed quando haec regula, vel perfecte danda, vel minuenda sit, per sequentia intellige: Si statim ab anno incipientis jubilaei, etc.

VERS. 17.-- Si statim ab anno. (ID.) Id est, si gratiae viam, etc., usque ad offerens sacerdoti, ab ipso possidebitur.

VERS. 19.-- Addet quintam partem. Doctrinae sacerdotis proprium intellectum: doctrina enim et instructione sacerdotis prius gubernamur, sed postea proficere, et intellectum nostrum adjicere debemus.

VERS. 20.-- Sin autem, etc. (ID.) Ille consecrat 377Cagrum et redimit agrum, etc., usque ad ejus enim lucrum est qui continue gubernat.

VERS. 22.-- Et non de possessione. Quia neque de custodia majorum suorum, neque ipsius emptoris fuerat

VERS. 23.-- Supputabit sacerdos. Recte sacerdos cum eo qui fratrem ad poenitentiam monuit, veram redemptionem supputat, qualiter scilicet poenitere debeat. Ille enim districte ejus actionem et conversationem novit, qui correxit: hic autem ut sacerdos perfectam redemptionem.

Juxta annorum numerum. Ex his scilicet quae de poenitentia praedicat Scriptura: ipsa enim aestimatio est jubilaei vel remissionis.

Et dabit ille qui voverat eum Domino. Non dicit 377Ddabunt, sed dabit. Etsi enim communi consilio sacerdotis et ejus qui ad poenitentiam monuit, aestimationis definitio datur, ille tamen offeret eum qui ad poenitendum excitavit: huic enim obediet, qui et ipse poenitenti obligatur, ut quod coepit perficiat.

VERS. 24.-- In jubilaeo autem revertetur ad priorem Dominum, etc. Quia non statim, cum poenitentia datur, vel definitur, penitus mundatur, sed ea peracta; ideo recte ait: In jubilaeo autem, etc., peracta enim poenitentia et remissione suscepta, conversationis suae agrum suscipiet.

VERS. 25.-- Omnis aestimatio siclo sanctuarii pondera. Ut omnis definitio sanctis fiat ponderibus, id est de sacra Scriptura venientibus, exteriora enim 378Apondera, ut philosophorum, vel haereticorum, quibus veritates et perfectiones hominum pensant, nec sancta, nec justa sunt.

Siclus viginti. Id est decem mandata secundum litteram et secundum spiritum intellecta. Quicunque autem aut legem aut Evangelium renuit, non habet didrachma, nec ejus pondera sunt sancta.

VERS. 26.-- Primogenita quae. (ISICH.) Primogenita tanquam pecora nostra, sunt qui nobis obediunt, filii, servi, et discipuli. Sed horum primogenita sunt, qui sic obediunt, ut aliis praebeant exemplum et magisterium, de quibus alibi dicitur: Omne masculinum adaperiens vulvam sanctum Domino vocabitur (Lucae II). Aperit enim vulvam generandorum filiorum, qui secundum Deum incipit obedire. Unde 378BPaulus de Timotheo ait: Qui est filius meus dilectus in Domino, ipse vos commonefaciet vias meas, etc. (I Cor. IV).

Nemo sanctificare poterit et vovere. Non quia sanctificatione indigna sunt, sed quia sancta sunt, nec egent sanctificari, ut filius, qui sic patrem et matrem honorat, ut etiam exemplum aliis fiat, tanquam Deo offerens honorem, qui hoc praecepit (Exod. XX). Servus quoque qui etiam dominis carnalibus in simplicitate cordis non ad oculum servit, ipsi Deo servitutem impendit (Eph. VI).

VERS. 27.-- Quod si immundum. (ISICH.) Peccator, qui nec legem sicut oportet meditatur,nec distinguere conatur. Quicunque ergo vehementer obediens est patri, domino aut magistro: nec tamen legem 378Cmeditatur aut distinguit, primogenitus quidem in pecoribus est, sed immundus est.

Et addet quintam partem pretii. Doctrinam ei cum intellectu offerens, et sapienter regens. Insensati enim patris, domini aut magistri est, obedientiam eos, qui ad se pertinent, subditos non docere, et eorum vitam ut operentur secundum legem non disponere. Addat igitur subjectioni et doctrinae intellectum: ipse enim solus in praesenti mundus est. Ideo ait: Addet quintam partem pretii. Habet enim obedientia pretium: ideo non dixit: aestimabit eum sacerdos, sicut supra.

VERS. 28.-- Omne quod Domino consecratur, sive homo. (ISICH.) Aliud est anathematizare, etc., usque ad id est, conversatio nostrae sortis.

378DQuidquid semel fuerit consecratum. (ID.) Jure belli acquisitum non potest aliquis inimicum exspoliare, etc., usque ad nec augmentum, nec profectum suscipiunt.

VERS. 30.-- Domini. (ID.) Ejus scilicet qui mandata legis operatur, etc., usque ad quia quam retributionem exspectare debeat nescit.

VERS. 31.-- Si quis. (ID.) Secundum litteram multa obscura et insolubilia legislator interposuit, etc., usque ad et poma arborum secundum tempus suum.

VERS. 32.-- Sub pastoris virga. (ID.) Virga crucis, etc., usque ad et ab illo solo, qui sanctos discernit, cognoscuntur.

379AVERS. 33.-- Non eligetur nec, etc. Quia nullus est qui respui possit, et quod magis eligendum sit, non est vestrae discretionis.

Si quis mutaverit, etc. (ISICH.) Paulus et Barnabas aliquando dissenserunt, etc., usque ad scribens ad Timotheum ait: Assumens Marcum, deduc tecum: est enim mihi utilis in ministerium (II Tim. IV).

Sanctificabitur Domino, etc. (ID.) Cum omnium cursus divinus sit, et altus, et supernae conversationi proximus, frustra conatis hunc subducere, 380Aillum inducere, et Deo quasi meliorem consecrare.

VERS. 34.-- Haec sunt praecepta quae mandavit, etc. (ID.) Discipuli nostri quando sunt perfecti, nihil nostri egent magisterii: ideo ait immundorum primogenita esse redimenda, mundorum vero nequaquam, sed nec Domino consecrata, nec ea quae novissime sunt dicta: perfecta enim demonstrata sunt, quia intentionem divinam coelestemque gerunt.

CHAPTER XXVII.

VERS. 2.-- The man who. (ISICH.) These words are full of prudence: above he commanded what things ought to be done and what not; now he sets forth the dignity of those who act, whether wholly or in part, what a perfect life has, what a middling one, what one that is beginning in the knowledge of God; and he describes also the differences of the ages.

A soul. (ISICH.) Aquila and Theodotion, a wonderful thing. Symmachus, if he shall have separated himself. LXX: the honour of his soul.

From the twentieth. (ID.) When he is grown, strong, etc., 375Cas far as of which it is said: Be you perfect as your Father also is perfect (Matth. V). [Vulg. Mt 5:48 Estote ergo vos perfecti, sicut et Pater vester caelestis perfectus est, "your heavenly Father"]

Sicles. (ISICH.) Or didrachmas, the sixtyfold fruit, etc., as far as whence: Let a widow be chosen of no less than sixty years (I Tim. V).

VERS. 5.-- From the fifth. (ID.) He who has understanding, and has begun to make progress, but cannot be adorned with the manner of life of fasting.

Unto. (ID.) He who has begun to make progress in the five senses: for this man is advanced toward virtue and perfection. For they that are of twenty years are strong and vigorous.

The male shall give twenty sicles. (ID.) Or didrachmas, that is, forty drachmas; that is, four hundred obols. But the number forty signifies the yield of fasting and of prayers. 375DFor so many days did Moses fast (Exod. XXXIV), and Elias (III Reg. XIX), and Christ himself, and afterward he was tempted by the devil (Matth. IV).

VERS. 7.-- The female ten. (ID.) Or didrachmas, that is, twenty drachmas or two hundred obols, namely the keeping of the ten commandments, which are doubled according to the letter and the spirit, in which fasting is not commanded, because it befits those who are advancing and not yet perfect.

From one. He is of one month who is now begotten in the faith, to whom it is said: As newborn babes, reasonable, without guile, desire milk, etc. (I Petr. II). [Vulg. 1 Pt 2:2 sicut modo geniti infantes, rationabile sine dolo lac concupiscite, where rationabile belongs to the milk and not to the infants]

Five. Or didrachmas, namely that the male 376Amay have his senses doubled, and may take in intelligibly the things he hears sensibly. For so is he led to perfection, on account of which his price is a hundred obols: and the number a hundred is perfect.

For a female. (ID.) For a female three didrachmas, etc., as far as as being already partakers of the evangelical manner of life.

A man of sixty. He who has promised an excellence of life, such as virginity, or the renouncing of the world, but has grown weak.

He shall give fifteen. Or didrachmas, which are thirty drachmas. For the good ground rendered three fruits (Matth. XIII): the hundredfold, that is, to be a virgin or to renounce the world; the sixtyfold, namely continence; the thirtyfold, that is, the manner of life of chaste 376Bwedlock.

VERS. 8.-- If he be poor. He who has sinned much; therefore Aquila rendered it, if he has gone beyond.

He shall stand. For if we have offered any one subject to us to the service of God, we ought not to take him back into our own ministry, promising in his stead either a better or a less good man.

As much as he shall have valued. That is, he shall lay upon him as much satisfaction as he shall have seen him able to bear: but if he is wealthy, that he distribute his money; if he is sound, that he fast, pray, labour.

VERS. 9.-- A beast. Because he has set forth the differences and the degrees of the oblations which are of ourselves: now he sets forth, concerning those who are subject to us as cattle, when their oblation is holy.

376CIt shall be holy. According to the law unspotted and clean: as if we have offered to God a son, a disciple, a servant, or any one subject to us.

VERS. 10.-- Which. That the oblation may be twofold, and the enemy be put to shame, who was suggesting that the oblation be changed.

VERS. 11.-- An unclean beast. (ISICH.) Which not at the same time, etc., as far as that it may be able to be offered to the Lord.

It is to be known that it is one thing to be sanctified, another to be holy, another to be offered to God, another to be God's. For that which is sanctified and offered begins to be holy, which it was not, and is increased little by little. That which is holy and is God's needs no increase, because it is perfect. It is good therefore to be sanctified by the doctrine and 376Dhelp of others, but it is better to be such a man as can sanctify himself and others, which belongs to few. He therefore sanctifies a house to the Lord who wills his household and his kindred, both the sensible and the intelligible, to live purely and holily, that it may be the Church of God. But if of himself he is not fit to do this, the priest ought to consider whether it be good or evil. For it consists of divers persons, and he ought to enjoin upon them according to what they are able.

VERS. 13.-- The fifth part. The mind, namely, which rules the five senses, without which the foolish virgins are not received to the marriage (Matth. XXV).

VERS. 14.-- If a man shall vow. (ISICH.) In what follows he says: Everything that is consecrated to the Lord, whether 377Aman, or beast, or field, cannot be redeemed; but here he commands that a house and a field be redeemed. But perhaps he decides this according to the differing intentions of those who offer, as though there he has no will to have any further what he has utterly consecrated.

VERS. 15.-- But if he, etc. (ISICH.) To redeem is, by doctrine and by a rule of life, to set the household free, and to wash away whatever stands against holiness. But he shall add to the admonition and edification of the priest his own understanding, and frequent remembrance.

VERS. 16.-- But if a field. That is, the divine Scripture, whose vine is Christ, and the Father the husbandman (Joan. XIV). In this the treasure is hidden, that is, the saving preaching: in this each man's possession is different, that is, the measure of knowing which each has been able to obtain 377Bfor himself.

If thirty bushels, etc. (ID.) In the thirty bushels of barley, the Old Testament. In the fifty sicles or didrachmas, according to the LXX, understand the spiritual and perfect doctrine. But when this rule is to be given in full, or to be lessened, understand by what follows: If from the year of the beginning of the jubilee, etc.

VERS. 17.-- If from the very year. (ID.) That is, if the way of grace, etc., as far as offering to the priest, he shall be possessed by him.

VERS. 19.-- He shall add the fifth part. His own understanding, to the priest's doctrine: for we are first governed by the doctrine and instruction of the priest, but afterward we ought to make progress and to add our own understanding.

VERS. 20.-- But if, etc. (ID.) He consecrates 377Cthe field and redeems the field, etc., as far as for the gain is his who governs continually.

VERS. 22.-- And not of the possession. Because it had been neither of the keeping of his forefathers, nor of the buyer himself.

VERS. 23.-- The priest shall reckon. Rightly does the priest, together with him who admonished the brother to penance, reckon the true redemption, that is, in what manner he ought to repent. For the one who corrected him knows strictly his action and his manner of life: but the other, as a priest, knows the perfect redemption.

According to the number of years. That is, out of the things which the Scripture preaches concerning penance: for that valuation is of the jubilee, or of remission.

And he shall give it who had vowed him to the Lord. He does not say 377Dthey shall give, but he shall give. For although the determining of the valuation is given by the common counsel of the priest and of him who admonished to penance, yet he who stirred him up to repentance shall offer him: for to this man he will be obedient, who himself also is bound to the penitent, that he may finish what he has begun.

VERS. 24.-- But in the jubilee it shall return to its former Lord, etc. Because he is not utterly cleansed straightway, when the penance is given or determined, but when it has been gone through; therefore he rightly says: But in the jubilee, etc., for when the penance has been gone through and remission received, he shall receive the field of his own manner of life.

VERS. 25.-- Every valuation, weights by the sicle of the sanctuary. That every determination be made by holy weights, that is, by such as come from Holy Scripture; for outward 378Aweights, such as those of the philosophers or of the heretics, by which they weigh the truths and perfections of men, are neither holy nor just. [Vulg. Lv 27:25 Omnis aestimatio siclo sanctuarii ponderabitur, "shall be weighed by the sicle of the sanctuary"; Migne's pondera leaves the clause without its verb]

A sicle of twenty. That is, the ten commandments understood according to the letter and according to the spirit. But whosoever refuses either the law or the Gospel has not the didrachma, nor are his weights holy.

VERS. 26.-- The firstborn which. (ISICH.) The firstborn, as being our cattle, are they who obey us, sons, servants and disciples. But the firstborn of these are they who so obey as to furnish others with an example and a teaching, of whom it is said elsewhere: Every male opening the womb shall be called holy to the Lord (Lucae II). For he opens the womb of the sons to be begotten who begins to obey according to God. Whence 378BPaul says of Timothy: Who is my beloved son in the Lord, he shall put you in mind of my ways, etc. (I Cor. IV). [Vulg. 1 Cor 4:17 qui est filius meus carissimus et fidelis in Domino: qui vos commonefaciet vias meas, "my dearest and faithful son"]

No man shall be able to sanctify and to vow. Not because they are unworthy of sanctification, but because they are holy, and need not be sanctified: as a son who so honours father and mother that he becomes an example to others also, offering honour as it were to God, who commanded this (Exod. XX). So too a servant, who serves even carnal masters in simplicity of heart and not to the eye, pays his service to God himself (Eph. VI).

VERS. 27.-- But if it be unclean. (ISICH.) A sinner, who neither meditates on the law as he ought, nor endeavours to distinguish. Whosoever therefore is vehemently obedient to father, master or teacher, and yet does not meditate on the law 378Cor distinguish, is indeed a firstborn among the cattle, but he is unclean.

And he shall add the fifth part of the price. Offering him doctrine together with understanding, and ruling him wisely. For it belongs to a senseless father, master or teacher not to teach obedience to those who belong to him and are subject to him, and not to order their life that they may work according to the law. Let him therefore add understanding to subjection and to doctrine: for that alone is clean in the present time. Therefore he says: He shall add the fifth part of the price. For obedience has a price: therefore he did not say, the priest shall value him, as above.

VERS. 28.-- Everything that is consecrated to the Lord, whether man. (ISICH.) It is one thing to anathematize, etc., as far as that is, the manner of life of our lot.

378DWhatsoever hath once been consecrated. (ID.) What is acquired by the right of war, no man can despoil the enemy of it, etc., as far as they receive neither increase nor advancement.

VERS. 30.-- Of the Lord. (ID.) That is, of him who works the commandments of the law, etc., as far as because he knows not what reward he ought to look for.

VERS. 31.-- If any man. (ID.) According to the letter the lawgiver has set between many dark and insoluble things, etc., as far as and the fruits of the trees in their own season.

VERS. 32.-- Under the shepherd's rod. (ID.) The rod of the cross, etc., as far as and they are known by him alone who discerns the saints.

379AVERS. 33.-- It shall not be chosen, nor, etc. Because there is none that can be rejected, and what is the more to be chosen is not of your discerning.

If any man shall change it, etc. (ISICH.) Paul and Barnabas once disagreed, etc., as far as writing to Timothy he says: Taking Mark, bring him with thee: for he is profitable to me for the ministry (II Tim. IV). [Vulg. 2 Tim 4:11 Marcum assume, et adduc tecum, "take Mark, and bring him with thee"]

It shall be sanctified to the Lord, etc. (ID.) Since the course of them all is divine, and lofty, and near to the heavenly manner of life, in vain for those who strive to withdraw this one, 380Ato bring in that one, and to consecrate him to God as though better.

VERS. 34.-- These are the precepts which he commanded, etc. (ID.) When our disciples are perfect, they have no need of our teaching: therefore he says that the firstborn of the unclean are to be redeemed, but those of the clean by no means; nor the things consecrated to the Lord, nor those that were last named: for they have been shown to be perfect, because they bear a divine and heavenly intention.

The Latin is Migne's printing of Liber Leviticus (PL 113, coll. 295D–380A), by Anselm of Laon and his school, from the Corpus Corporum transcription. Parenthetical references in small type stand on Migne's page — editorial identifications inherited from the edition he reprinted or supplied by his shop, not necessarily the author's own; the author's own citations run in the prose itself. Every gilt column mark is an address: migne.app/pl/113/295d resolves to the first.
